`DESIGN.md`:

```md
# Design direction

This file records the UI direction for Remote Package Installer NG so future
UI work has an anchor instead of re-deriving taste from scratch.

**Direction:** modern minimalist, clean, professional.
**Brand soul:** comfort and ease. The user is sending large files to a game
console over their home network; the app's job is to make that feel boring
(in the best way) and obvious at every step.

## Dials (antislop Liveliness Toolkit)

- **ENERGY 1** — calm. No racing counters, no celebratory states. A finished
  transfer is a quiet green label.
- **RHYTHM 1** — steady. The layout does not reflow on its own; things move
  only when the user acts.
- **MOTION 1** — minimal. The only moving parts are spinners and progress
  bars, because they report real work. No decorative animation, no hover
  transitions, no gradients or glow. Anything that moves must be reporting
  engine state.

## Color

One semantic color per meaning, per theme, centralized in
`crates/rpi-ng/src/palette.rs`:

| meaning | signals |
|---|---|
| `ok` (green) | connected, completed, server OK, upload rate |
| `warn` (amber) | needs user attention: paused, unknown outcome, warnings |
| `error` (red) | failed/invalid items, errors, incompatible console |
| `info` (blue) | work in progress: connecting, preparing, transferring |
| `installing` (violet) | the console is installing the package |
| `neutral` (gray) | no signal: queued, cancelled, no console |

Rules:

- Every colored text must pass WCAG AA (>= 4.5:1) on its background in BOTH
  themes; the palette module's unit tests pin this. If a color change breaks
  that test, the color is wrong, not the test.
- Color is a signal, never decoration. If a piece of text does not map to one
  of the six meanings, it gets the normal text color.
- No hardcoded RGB values in views. Everything goes through the palette.

## Typography and layout

- egui default fonts and sizes; no custom fonts. The tool should feel native,
  not branded.
- Monospace only for machine identifiers: URLs, content IDs, log lines.
- Layout follows content need: the queue toolbar wraps into two rows below
  1050px of available width so it never collides at the minimum window size
  (860x560); the threshold is pinned by measured layout tests. The top bar
  does the same below 960px, and the console status chip truncates at 320px
  with the full text on hover, so a long backend identity or incompatibility
  reason can never collide with the right-aligned controls. The file-server
  strip wraps below 940px for the same reason (session totals vs live
  rates).
- Empty, loading and error states are real screens, not afterthoughts
  ("Drop PKG files here", spinners with a label, actionable error text).

## Copy

- Plain, short, specific. "Start queue", "Check File Server", not "Get
  Started".
- Every number shown comes from the engine. No illustrative or fake data.
- Errors say what to do next, not just what went wrong ("check the firewall
  allows this app on the local network").
- No em dashes in user-facing strings; use periods, colons, semicolons or
  parentheses instead.

```

`MIGRATION.md`:

```md
# Migration Guide

How to move to Remote Package Installer NG from other tools and workflows.
This is a living document — file an issue when something here is wrong or
missing.

## From the original flatz workflow (npm `serve` + curl)

If you drove the original payload by hand (`docs/BASELINE.md` §1), everything
you did manually is now automated:

| Then | Now |
| --- | --- |
| `serve -p 8080 folder` | Automatic: the app runs its own file server when you connect |
| `curl /api/install` with hand-built URLs | Add PKGs → **Start queue** |
| `curl /api/get_task_progress` in a loop | Automatic progress, speed and ETA |
| `curl /api/pause_task` / `resume_task` | **Pause** / **Resume** buttons |
| Guessing whether it finished | Completion requires console evidence (transfer + copy phase) |
| Manual IP every time | Auto-discovery + remembered consoles |
| Editing JSON by hand | GUI |

Your console-side setup does **not** change: same installer app, same port
12800, same foreground requirement.

## From Loopayeh/pkg-sender

- Your console keeps running the same backends (RPI on PS4; LoopDPI receiver
  on PS5 — the PS5 receiver is not yet supported by this app, see
  `docs/PS5_ROADMAP.md`).
- Discovery approach is equivalent (bounded subnet sweep + verification), but
  this app does not listen for the `PKGSENDER` UDP beacon yet — the PS4 side
  has no beacon to listen to anyway (`docs/RESEARCH.md` §3.5).
- What you gain: a persistent recoverable queue, PC-side package validation,
  and evidence-based completion. What you lose today: PS5 sending,
  disc-image copying (`/api/files/pull`), and the console-side browser UI.

## From DirectPackageInstaller / web senders

- URL/debrid-hosting downloads (AllDebrid, RealDebrid, Google Drive, …) are
  **not implemented** — this app intentionally handles local files first
  (`docs/RESEARCH.md` §4). Download with your usual tool, then add the files.
- GoldHEN Payload-Server injection is not implemented either; use the
  installer app on the console instead.

## From PS4-Vault / library managers

This app is an installer, not a library manager: it will not scan your whole
drive for a catalog, manage cover art, or bulk-rename. Those workflows remain
with those tools; you can still drag files from anywhere into the queue.

## Where your data lives

- Queue and settings are stored per-user:
  - Linux: `~/.local/share/rpi-ng/`
  - Windows: `%APPDATA%\rpi-ng\` (per `directories` crate)
  - macOS: `~/Library/Application Support/rpi-ng/`
- `queue.json` and `settings.json` are plain JSON with atomic writes and a
  `.bak` fallback — you can inspect, back up, or delete them freely.

## Console-side tips that still matter

- Keep the installer app **in the foreground** on the PS4 while sending
  (`docs/BASELINE.md` §4.2) — the console suspends background apps.
- If the app was minimized mid-install, progress may pause; re-open it and
  reconnect — this app reconciles tasks instead of resubmitting.

```

`README.md`:

```md
# Remote Package Installer NG

**Put PS4 games on your console over your home network — no USB stick, no FTP.**

A free, open-source desktop app for **Windows**, **macOS** and **Linux**. You pick the game files (`.pkg`) on your computer, and the app sends them straight to your PS4 — with real progress, pause and resume.

[Download](https://github.com/AldySan/Remote-Package-Installer-NG/releases) · [How to use](#how-to-use) · [Report a problem](https://github.com/AldySan/Remote-Package-Installer-NG/issues)

## What you need

- A PS4 with [GoldHEN](https://github.com/GoldHEN/GoldHEN) — you'll use the **Package Installer** inside it. ([RPI NextGen](https://github.com/AldySan/ps4_remote_pkg_installer_nextgen) works too.)
- Your PS4 and your computer on the **same network** — same Wi-Fi or same router.
- Your own `.pkg` game files, saved anywhere on your computer.

No accounts, no console-side setup, and nothing ever leaves your own network.

## How to use

1. **On the PS4**, open GoldHEN and start **Package Installer**. Keep it on screen the whole time — the PS4 pauses apps that go to the background.
2. **On your computer**, download the app from the [Releases](https://github.com/AldySan/Remote-Package-Installer-NG/releases) page and unpack it:
   - Windows → `RemotePackageInstallerNG-vX.Y.Z-Windows-x64.zip`
   - Mac → `RemotePackageInstallerNG-vX.Y.Z-macos-universal.zip` (works on every Mac)
   - Linux → `RemotePackageInstallerNG-vX.Y.Z-Linux-x64.tar.gz` (contains a `linux/` folder with optional menu-shortcut files if you want the app in your app launcher)
3. Run the app. It looks for your PS4 on its own. If nothing shows up, type the PS4's IP address instead — you'll find it on the console under *Settings → Network → View Connection Status*.
4. Add your `.pkg` files (one or many, whole folders too) and press **Start**.
5. When the app says an item is **Done**, the game appears on your PS4's home screen.

You can line up as many files as you like — they install one by one, in order, with speed and remaining time shown for each. Pause or cancel any of them. If a transfer fails, you choose what happens: continue with the rest or pause the queue (in *Settings*). If the app or the console gets interrupted, it checks with the PS4 what actually finished instead of blindly sending everything again.

## Common problems

- **A blue "Windows protected your PC" window appears** — normal for unsigned free apps. Click *More info*, then *Run anyway*.
- **macOS says the app "cannot be verified"** — right-click the app, choose *Open*, then *Open* again. Needed only the first time.
- **No PS4 shows up** — make sure the installer is open on the console (not sitting in the background) and both machines are on the same network. Typing the IP manually helps; with some GoldHEN versions you need to add the port, like `192.168.1.20:9090`.
- **A firewall message appears on your computer** — allow it. The PS4 downloads the files from your computer, so the app needs to accept that connection.
- **An install stops halfway** — the PS4 probably put the installer app in the background. Open it again on the console, then reconnect in the app — it will figure out where things actually stand and continue from there.
- **Anything else** — [open an issue](https://github.com/AldySan/Remote-Package-Installer-NG/issues) and describe what you did and what happened. This is an early release; reports genuinely help.

## Good to know

- The app **does not come with games** and does not download anything. You bring your own `.pkg` files — it just delivers them to the console.
- The app **checks GitHub for a newer release** on startup (and when you press *Check for updates*). That request carries nothing but the app's name and version — no accounts, no identifiers — and you can turn it off in *Settings*.
- **It's portable:** your settings and queue are stored as small JSON files **next to the program itself** (same folder as the executable), so everything travels together — copy the folder, and your setup comes with it. The activity log tells you the exact location on every start.
- While you're connected, the app shows a live **file-server line** with the in/out transfer rates (Mbps), the totals for the session and a **Check File Server** button — a one-click self-test that tells you whether the app's own server is fine or whether something between the two machines (firewall, network) is the problem.
- Installing happens through the console's own installer. The app doesn't modify or "hack" your PS4.
- **PS4 only.** PS5 is not supported.

## For developers

Written in Rust (egui + tokio). Building from source, tests, architecture and protocol documentation: see [docs/BUILDING.md](docs/BUILDING.md) and the [docs folder](docs/).

## Contributors

- [AldySan](https://github.com/AldySan)

## License

GPL-3.0 — see [LICENSE](LICENSE).

The original [ps4_remote_pkg_installer](https://github.com/flatz/ps4_remote_pkg_installer) ships no license, so nothing was copied from it — the protocol here was re-implemented from reading its source. Discovery and probing patterns were informed by [pkg-sender](https://github.com/Loopayeh/pkg-sender) (MIT), also re-implemented from scratch.

```

`crates/rpi-ng-core/examples/debug_server.rs`:

```rs
use std::net::SocketAddr;

#[tokio::main]
async fn main() {
    let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let path = dir.path().join("game.pkg");
    std::fs::write(&path, vec![7u8; 4096]).unwrap();
    let server = rpi_ng_core::fileserver::FileServer::start(SocketAddr::from(([127, 0, 0, 1], 0)))
        .await
        .unwrap();
    let id = server.register(path, Some("game.pkg".into()));
    let url = server.url_for(id).unwrap();
    println!("url: {url}");

    let client = reqwest::Client::builder().no_proxy().build().unwrap();
    let resp = client.head(&url).send().await.unwrap();
    println!("HEAD status: {}", resp.status());
    let resp = client.get(&url).send().await.unwrap();
    println!("GET status: {}", resp.status());
    let b = resp.bytes().await.unwrap();
    println!("GET body len: {}", b.len());
    let resp = client
        .get(&url)
        .header("Range", "bytes=100-199")
        .send()
        .await
        .unwrap();
    println!("RANGE status: {}", resp.status());
}

```

`crates/rpi-ng-core/src/discovery.rs`:

```rs
//! Bounded LAN discovery of RPI-compatible consoles.
//!
//! The original PS4 payload has **no beacon** (`docs/BASELINE.md` §4.5), so
//! discovery is a bounded TCP sweep of the RPI ports across each interface's
//! real subnet, followed by API verification of every hit. Nothing is reported
//! as a console unless the RPI probes answer (`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §4.6).

use std::{
    collections::HashSet,
    net::{IpAddr, Ipv4Addr, SocketAddr},
    time::Duration,
};

use futures_util::stream::{self, StreamExt};

use crate::{
    model::{ConsoleCandidate, DiscoverySource},
    rpi::RpiClient,
    settings::Settings,
};

/// Default RPI-family ports, probed in this order (pkg-sender's order —
/// 12800 first, 9090 as the GoldHEN-family fallback).
pub const PROBE_PORTS: [u16; 2] = [12800, 9090];

const CONCURRENCY: usize = 128;

/// Bounded concurrency for RPI verification of discovery hits.
const VERIFY_CONCURRENCY: usize = 8;

fn to_u32(ip: &Ipv4Addr) -> u32 {
    u32::from_be_bytes(ip.octets())
}

fn from_u32(v: u32) -> Ipv4Addr {
    Ipv4Addr::from(v.to_be_bytes())
}

/// One IPv4 subnet derived from a real interface address + netmask
/// (never a /24 assumption).
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct LanNetwork {
    pub interface: String,
    pub network: u32,
    pub broadcast: u32,
    pub prefix: u32,
}

impl LanNetwork {
    pub fn from_interface(name: &str, addr: &IpAddr, mask: &IpAddr) -> Option<Self> {
        let (addr, mask) = match (addr, mask) {
            (IpAddr::V4(a), IpAddr::V4(m)) => (a, m),
            _ => return None,
        };
        if addr.is_loopback() {
            return None;
        }
        let a = to_u32(addr);
        let m = to_u32(mask);
        Some(Self {
            interface: name.to_string(),
            network: a & m,
            broadcast: a | !m,
            prefix: m.count_ones(),
        })
    }

    /// Usable host addresses (skips network + broadcast; caps at `max`).
    pub fn hosts(&self, max: usize) -> Vec<IpAddr> {
        if self.prefix >= 31 {
            return vec![
                IpAddr::V4(from_u32(self.network)),
                IpAddr::V4(from_u32(self.broadcast)),
            ];
        }
        let mut out = Vec::new();
        let total = self
            .broadcast
            .saturating_sub(self.network)
            .saturating_sub(1);
        let n = total.min(max as u32);
        for i in 0..n {
            out.push(IpAddr::V4(from_u32(self.network + 1 + i)));
        }
        out
    }

    pub fn truncated(&self, max: usize) -> bool {
        if self.prefix >= 31 {
            return false;
        }
        ((self.broadcast as u64) - (self.network as u64) - 1) > max as u64
    }
}

/// Enumerate local IPv4 networks from real interfaces.
pub fn local_networks() -> Vec<LanNetwork> {
    let mut nets = Vec::new();
    if let Ok(ifaces) = if_addrs::get_if_addrs() {
        for iface in ifaces {
            let (ip, mask) = match iface.addr {
                if_addrs::IfAddr::V4(v4) => (IpAddr::V4(v4.ip), IpAddr::V4(v4.netmask)),
                if_addrs::IfAddr::V6(_) => continue,
            };
            if let Some(net) = LanNetwork::from_interface(&iface.name, &ip, &mask) {
                nets.push(net);
            }
        }
    }
    nets
}

/// Discover consoles on the LAN: remembered hosts first, then a bounded TCP
/// sweep + API verification. Never busy-polls; a single bounded pass.
pub async fn discover(settings: &Settings) -> Vec<ConsoleCandidate> {
    let mut candidates: Vec<ConsoleCandidate> = Vec::new();
    let mut seen_ips: HashSet<IpAddr> = HashSet::new();

    // 1. Remembered hosts (usually the answer) — verified concurrently so a
    // single unreachable host does not stall the whole pass.
    let remembered: Vec<SocketAddr> = settings.remembered.iter().map(|rc| rc.addr).collect();
    let uniq: Vec<SocketAddr> = remembered
        .into_iter()
        .filter(|a| seen_ips.insert(a.ip()))
        .collect();
    let hits: Vec<Option<ConsoleCandidate>> = stream::iter(
        uniq.into_iter()
            .map(|addr| async move { verify_addr(addr, DiscoverySource::Remembered).await }),
    )
    .buffered(VERIFY_CONCURRENCY)
    .collect()
    .await;
    candidates.extend(hits.into_iter().flatten());

    // 2. Bounded subnet sweep.
    if settings.discovery_enabled {
        let max_per_net = settings.discovery_max_hosts as usize;
        let mut targets: Vec<SocketAddr> = Vec::new();
        for net in local_networks() {
            for ip in net.hosts(max_per_net) {
                for port in PROBE_PORTS {
                    targets.push(SocketAddr::new(ip, port));
                }
            }
        }

        let timeout = settings.discovery_timeout;
        let hits: Vec<Option<SocketAddr>> =
            stream::iter(targets.into_iter().map(|addr| async move {
                match tokio::time::timeout(timeout, tokio::net::TcpStream::connect(addr)).await {
                    Ok(Ok(_)) => Some(addr),
                    _ => None,
                }
            }))
            .buffer_unordered(CONCURRENCY)
            .collect()
            .await;

        // Dedup by IP (first port hit wins), skipping already-verified ones.
        let mut ordered: Vec<SocketAddr> = Vec::new();
        let mut hit_ips: HashSet<IpAddr> = HashSet::new();
        for addr in hits.into_iter().flatten() {
            if seen_ips.contains(&addr.ip()) {
                continue;
            }
            if hit_ips.insert(addr.ip()) {
                ordered.push(addr);
            }
        }

        // 3. Verify each hit speaks the RPI dialect — concurrently (bounded),
        // in discovery order.
        let verified: Vec<Option<ConsoleCandidate>> = stream::iter(
            ordered
                .into_iter()
                .map(|addr| async move { verify_addr(addr, DiscoverySource::SubnetProbe).await }),
        )
        .buffered(VERIFY_CONCURRENCY)
        .collect()
        .await;
        candidates.extend(verified.into_iter().flatten());
    }

    candidates
}

/// Verify one address; the first port that answers the RPI probes wins.
///
/// The address's own port is probed first: a remembered console may live on a
/// custom port (manual entry), and only probing the two standard ports would
/// silently fail re-validation for it — the "validated again on use" contract
/// of `settings.remembered`. Subnet-sweep addresses already carry a standard
/// port, so their behavior is unchanged.
pub async fn verify_addr(addr: SocketAddr, source: DiscoverySource) -> Option<ConsoleCandidate> {
    let ip = addr.ip();
    let mut ports: Vec<u16> = Vec::with_capacity(PROBE_PORTS.len() + 1);
    if !PROBE_PORTS.contains(&addr.port()) {
        ports.push(addr.port());
    }
    ports.extend_from_slice(&PROBE_PORTS);
    for port in ports {
        let target = SocketAddr::new(ip, port);
        let client = RpiClient::new(target, Duration::from_millis(1500));
        if let Ok(probe) = client.probe().await {
            return Some(ConsoleCandidate {
                addr: target,
                kind: probe.kind,
                source,
            });
        }
    }
    None
}

```

`crates/rpi-ng-core/src/engine.rs`:

```rs
//! Engine: the single orchestrator that owns the tokio runtime, the queue, the
//! console connection, the file server and all background tasks.
//!
//! UI (or any frontend) talks to it through `Command` in / `Event` out —
//! see `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §4.1. The engine never touches UI code.

use std::{
    net::{IpAddr, SocketAddr},
    path::{Path, PathBuf},
    sync::{Arc, Mutex},
    time::{Duration, Instant},
};

use tokio::sync::{mpsc, watch};

use crate::{
    discovery,
    fileserver::FileServer,
    model::{
        BackendIdentity, ConsoleCandidate, ConsoleGeneration, ConsoleInfo, ConsoleStatus,
        ConsoleTarget, Event, ItemId, ItemState, LogLevel, PartId, ProgressView, QueueItem,
        ServerInfo, Snapshot, unix_ms,
    },
    pkg::{self, parse_pkg_file},
    queue::Queue,
    rpi::{RpiClient, RpiError, types::V2Job},
    settings::{FailurePolicy, RememberedConsole, Settings},
};

/// Commands from the frontend to the engine.
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub enum Command {
    /// Add files and/or directories (dirs walked per `recursive`).
    AddPaths {
        paths: Vec<PathBuf>,
        recursive: bool,
    },
    Remove(ItemId),
    Move {
        id: ItemId,
        delta: i32,
    },
    /// Begin/continue processing the queue in order.
    Start,
    /// Pause the active console task and stop auto-advance.
    Pause,
    /// Resume the paused console task and auto-advance.
    Resume,
    /// Resume one specific paused item (per-row action).
    ResumeItem(ItemId),
    /// Requeue a finished/failed/cancelled/unknown item.
    Retry(ItemId),
    /// User-verified completion for an `Unknown` item (honest escape hatch).
    MarkCompleted(ItemId),
    /// Cancel an item (best-effort remote stop + unregister).
    Cancel(ItemId),
    ClearFinished,
    /// Probe and connect to a console at this address.
    Connect(SocketAddr),
    Disconnect,
    Discover,
    SetSettings(Box<Settings>),
    Shutdown,
}

/// Handle held by the frontend.
pub struct EngineHandle {
    runtime: tokio::runtime::Runtime,
    cmd_tx: mpsc::UnboundedSender<Command>,
    evt_rx: std::sync::mpsc::Receiver<Event>,
}

impl EngineHandle {
    /// Send a command (never blocks).
    pub fn send(&self, cmd: Command) {
        let _ = self.cmd_tx.send(cmd);
    }

    /// Drain pending events (non-blocking).
    pub fn poll_events(&self) -> Vec<Event> {
        let mut out = Vec::new();
        while let Ok(ev) = self.evt_rx.try_recv() {
            out.push(ev);
        }
        out
    }

    /// Deterministic shutdown: cancels work, stops the server, flushes state,
    /// then drops the runtime. Blocks until the engine is fully stopped.
    ///
    /// Safe to call from any thread (GUI main thread, test harness, async
    /// context) — the runtime is shut down on a dedicated thread because
    /// dropping a tokio runtime inside an async context panics.
    pub fn shutdown(self) {
        let _ = self.cmd_tx.send(Command::Shutdown);
        let runtime = self.runtime;
        let handle = std::thread::Builder::new()
            .name("rpi-ng-shutdown".into())
            .spawn(move || {
                runtime.shutdown_timeout(Duration::from_secs(5));
            });
        if let Ok(h) = handle {
            let _ = h.join();
        }
    }
}

/// Spawn the engine with the given settings (persisting under the default
/// data dir — see [`crate::settings::data_dir`]).
pub fn spawn(settings: Settings) -> EngineHandle {
    spawn_with(settings, None)
}

/// Spawn the engine with an explicit data directory (used by tests and
/// headless embedding; `None` = resolve the default data dir at startup).
///
/// **v0.0.2 regression fixed here:** `None` used to be passed through as
/// "no data dir", which made every settings/queue save a silent no-op —
/// settings appeared to save but never survived a restart. `None` now
/// resolves (and creates/migrates) the real data dir, exactly like the
/// desktop app expects.
pub fn spawn_with(settings: Settings, data_dir: Option<PathBuf>) -> EngineHandle {
    let runtime = tokio::runtime::Builder::new_multi_thread()
        .worker_threads(2)
        .enable_all()
        .build()
        .expect("tokio runtime");
    let (cmd_tx, cmd_rx) = mpsc::unbounded_channel::<Command>();
    let (evt_tx, evt_rx) = std::sync::mpsc::channel::<Event>();

    let settings = Arc::new(Mutex::new(settings));
    let data_dir = Arc::new(match data_dir {
        Some(dir) => Some(dir),
        None => crate::settings::init_data_dir(),
    });
    runtime.spawn(async move {
        let (internal_tx, mut internal_rx) = mpsc::unbounded_channel();
        let mut inner = EngineInner::new(settings.clone(), evt_tx, internal_tx, data_dir.clone());
        // While the file server runs, re-snapshot at a steady cadence so the
        // live transfer rates in the UI keep updating (and decay to zero when
        // a transfer finishes) even between poll ticks.
        let mut rate_ticker = tokio::time::interval(Duration::from_millis(250));
        rate_ticker.set_missed_tick_behavior(tokio::time::MissedTickBehavior::Skip);
        let mut cmd_rx = cmd_rx;
        loop {
            tokio::select! {
                cmd = cmd_rx.recv() => {
                    match cmd {
                        Some(Command::Shutdown) | None => break,
                        Some(c) => inner.handle(c).await,
                    }
                }
                Some(msg) = internal_rx.recv() => {
                    inner.handle_internal(msg).await;
                }
                _ = rate_ticker.tick(), if inner.has_server() => {
                    // Rates only — no queue work, no console traffic.
                }
            }
            inner.emit_snapshot();
        }
        inner.shutdown().await;
    });

    EngineHandle {
        runtime,
        cmd_tx,
        evt_rx,
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Internals
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

// No `Debug`: the SubmitResult variant carries an `RpiClient`, which has no
// Debug impl, and this enum is internal plumbing that is never formatted.
#[allow(clippy::large_enum_variant)]
enum Internal {
    ScanDone {
        files: Vec<PathBuf>,
    },
    ParseDone {
        results: Vec<(ItemId, PartId, Result<crate::model::PkgInfo, pkg::PkgError>)>,
    },
    ProbeDone {
        addr: SocketAddr,
        result: Result<ConsoleTarget, RpiError>,
    },
    DiscoveryDone(Vec<ConsoleCandidate>),
    SubmitResult {
        item: ItemId,
        /// The client the request went out on, sent back so a late answer
        /// can still clean up on the console that actually owns the task
        /// (self.client may point somewhere else by then).
        client: RpiClient,
        use_v2: bool,
        /// Console the request was submitted to (`None` = submitted while
        /// no console was recorded — defensive only).
        console: Option<SocketAddr>,
        result: Result<crate::rpi::types::InstallResponse, RpiError>,
    },
    SubmitRetry {
        item: ItemId,
        urls: Vec<String>,
    },
    ProgressUpdate {
        item: ItemId,
        result: Result<ProgressView, RpiError>,
    },
    /// v2 poller: the console's job entry, or `None` when the job vanished
    /// from the registry (console app restarted / slot reclaimed).
    V2JobUpdate {
        item: ItemId,
        result: Result<Option<V2Job>, RpiError>,
    },
}

struct ActiveTask {
    item_id: ItemId,
    task_id: i64,
    /// v2 job id when the backend tracks jobs (NextGen-class).
    job_id: Option<i64>,
    poll_cancel: watch::Sender<bool>,
}

struct EngineInner {
    settings: Arc<Mutex<Settings>>,
    data_dir: Arc<Option<PathBuf>>,
    evt: std::sync::mpsc::Sender<Event>,
    internal_tx: mpsc::UnboundedSender<Internal>,
    queue: Queue,
    console: Option<ConsoleTarget>,
    console_status: ConsoleStatus,
    client: Option<RpiClient>,
    server: Option<Arc<FileServer>>,
    running: bool,
    active: Option<ActiveTask>,
    /// (timestamp, transferred) of the last progress sample per active item.
    last_sample: Option<(Instant, u64)>,
    /// Consecutive transient poll failures while a task is active.
    poll_failures: u32,
    /// Consecutive full-transfer samples without a copy phase report.
    copy_phase_stalls: u32,
    scanning: bool,
    /// A discovery sweep is in flight (guards against stacking sweeps).
    discovering: bool,
}

const MAX_SUBMIT_ATTEMPTS: u32 = 3;
const MAX_WALK_FILES: usize = 2000;

impl EngineInner {
    fn new(
        settings: Arc<Mutex<Settings>>,
        evt: std::sync::mpsc::Sender<Event>,
        internal_tx: mpsc::UnboundedSender<Internal>,
        data_dir: Arc<Option<PathBuf>>,
    ) -> Self {
        let (queue, queue_repairs) = Queue::load_in_reported(data_dir.as_deref());
        let inner = Self {
            settings,
            data_dir,
            evt,
            internal_tx,
            queue,
            console: None,
            console_status: ConsoleStatus::None,
            client: None,
            server: None,
            running: false,
            active: None,
            last_sample: None,
            poll_failures: 0,
            copy_phase_stalls: 0,
            scanning: false,
            discovering: false,
        };
        // A hand-edited queue.json was repaired on load — say so instead of
        // silently dropping entries the user may have just added by hand.
        for warning in queue_repairs.warnings() {
            inner.log(LogLevel::Warn, warning);
        }
        inner
    }

    // -- plumbing ----------------------------------------------------------

    fn log(&self, level: LogLevel, msg: impl Into<String>) {
        let _ = self.evt.send(Event::Log(level, msg.into()));
    }

    fn emit_snapshot(&self) {
        let server = self.server.as_ref().map(|s| {
            let (tx_bps, rx_bps) = s.stats().rates();
            let (tx_total, rx_total, _) = s.stats().totals();
            ServerInfo {
                bind: s.bind,
                base_url: s.base_url.clone(),
                tx_bps,
                rx_bps,
                tx_total,
                rx_total,
            }
        });
        let snap = Snapshot {
            items: self.queue.items().to_vec(),
            console: ConsoleInfo {
                status: self.console_status.clone(),
                target: self.console.clone(),
            },
            server,
            engine_busy: self.queue.any_active() || self.scanning,
        };
        let _ = self.evt.send(Event::Snapshot(snap));
    }

    fn persist_queue(&mut self) {
        if let Err(e) = self.queue.save_in(self.data_dir.as_deref()) {
            self.log(LogLevel::Error, format!("queue save failed: {e}"));
        }
    }

    fn settings(&self) -> Settings {
        self.settings.lock().unwrap().clone()
    }

    /// True while the local file server is up (gates the rate ticker).
    fn has_server(&self) -> bool {
        self.server.is_some()
    }

    /// Mark in-flight items `Unknown` (outcome unobservable from here) and
    /// persist. Shared by an explicit `Disconnect` and by a successful
    /// connect that switches consoles: both stop observing the console that
    /// owns those tasks. Paused items keep their task id and stay resumable.
    /// Returns how many items were parked.
    fn park_inflight_unknown(&mut self) -> usize {
        let inflight: Vec<ItemId> = self
            .queue
            .items()
            .iter()
            .filter(|i| {
                matches!(
                    i.state,
                    ItemState::Submitting | ItemState::Transferring | ItemState::Installing
                )
            })
            .map(|i| i.id)
            .collect();
        for id in &inflight {
            if let Some(it) = self.queue.get_mut(*id) {
                it.state = ItemState::Unknown;
                it.error = Some(
                    "Connection closed while this item was in flight: the outcome is unknown; reconnect to re-attach, then verify on the console".into(),
                );
                it.updated_at = unix_ms();
            }
        }
        if !inflight.is_empty() {
            self.persist_queue();
        }
        inflight.len()
    }

    // -- command handling ---------------------------------------------------

    async fn handle(&mut self, cmd: Command) {
        match cmd {
            Command::AddPaths { paths, recursive } => self.cmd_add_paths(paths, recursive).await,
            Command::Remove(id) => self.cmd_remove(id).await,
            Command::Move { id, delta } => {
                if self.queue.move_item(id, delta) {
                    self.persist_queue();
                }
            }
            Command::Start => {
                self.running = true;
                self.maybe_start_next().await;
            }
            Command::Pause => {
                self.running = false;
                let ids = self
                    .active
                    .as_ref()
                    .map(|a| (a.task_id, a.item_id, a.job_id));
                if let Some((task_id, item_id, job_id)) = ids {
                    // Best-effort remote pause; the poller keeps watching so a
                    // completion or console-side error is still observed.
                    self.pause_remote(task_id, job_id).await;
                    if let Some(item) = self.queue.get_mut(item_id) {
                        item.state = ItemState::Paused;
                        item.updated_at = unix_ms();
                    }
                    self.persist_queue();
                    self.log(
                        LogLevel::Info,
                        "Paused (console pause was best-effort; auto-advance off)",
                    );
                }
            }
            Command::Resume => {
                // Prefer the actively monitored task; otherwise resume the
                // first paused item that still carries a console task id
                // belonging to THIS console (e.g. after a reconnect).
                let current = self.console.as_ref().map(|c| c.addr);
                let target = self.active.as_ref().map(|a| a.item_id).or_else(|| {
                    self.queue
                        .items()
                        .iter()
                        .find(|i| {
                            i.state == ItemState::Paused
                                && i.task_id.is_some()
                                && task_belongs_to(i.task_owner, current)
                        })
                        .map(|i| i.id)
                });
                if let Some(id) = target {
                    self.resume_item(id).await;
                }
                self.maybe_start_next().await;
            }
            Command::ResumeItem(id) => {
                self.resume_item(id).await;
                self.maybe_start_next().await;
            }
            Command::Retry(id) => {
                if self.queue.requeue(id) {
                    // Retry expresses "process this now" — it also un-parks a
                    // queue that was paused by the failure policy.
                    self.running = true;
                    self.persist_queue();
                    self.log(LogLevel::Info, format!("Requeued item #{id}"));
                    self.maybe_start_next().await;
                }
            }
            Command::MarkCompleted(id) => {
                if let Some(item) = self.queue.get_mut(id)
                    && item.state == ItemState::Unknown
                {
                    item.state = ItemState::Completed;
                    item.error = None;
                    item.updated_at = unix_ms();
                    self.persist_queue();
                    self.log(
                        LogLevel::Info,
                        format!("Item #{id} marked completed by user (was unknown)"),
                    );
                }
            }
            Command::Cancel(id) => self.cmd_cancel(id).await,
            Command::ClearFinished => {
                let removed_parts: Vec<PartId> = self
                    .queue
                    .items()
                    .iter()
                    .filter(|i| i.state.is_finished())
                    .flat_map(|i| i.parts.iter().map(|p| p.id))
                    .collect();
                let removed = self.queue.clear_finished();
                if let Some(server) = &self.server {
                    for pid in removed_parts {
                        server.unregister(pid);
                    }
                }
                self.persist_queue();
                self.log(
                    LogLevel::Info,
                    format!("Cleared {removed} finished item(s)"),
                );
            }
            Command::Connect(addr) => self.cmd_connect(addr).await,
            Command::Disconnect => {
                self.console = None;
                self.client = None;
                self.console_status = ConsoleStatus::None;
                self.stop_poller();
                if let Some(server) = self.server.take() {
                    server.shutdown();
                }
                // Items that were in flight can no longer be observed. Mark
                // them Unknown (never auto-resubmitted); reconcile() re-attaches
                // to console tasks on the next connect. Paused items keep their
                // task id and are resumable explicitly.
                let parked = self.park_inflight_unknown();
                if parked > 0 {
                    self.log(
                        LogLevel::Warn,
                        format!("Disconnected with {parked} in-flight item(s) marked unknown"),
                    );
                } else {
                    self.log(LogLevel::Info, "Disconnected");
                }
            }
            Command::Discover => {
                // One sweep at a time: the UI already disables "Scan again"
                // while running, but the startup auto-sweep can overlap a
                // manual click (or rapid clicks) — stacking subnet sweeps is
                // pure duplicate probing work.
                if self.discovering {
                    return;
                }
                self.discovering = true;
                let settings = self.settings();
                let tx = self.internal_tx.clone();
                self.log(LogLevel::Info, "Scanning the local network for consoles…");
                tokio::spawn(async move {
                    let found = discovery::discover(&settings).await;
                    let _ = tx.send(Internal::DiscoveryDone(found));
                });
            }
            Command::SetSettings(s) => {
                let result = {
                    let mut guard = self.settings.lock().unwrap();
                    *guard = *s;
                    // Persist into the engine's data dir (test/headless runs
                    // must not touch the user's real settings).
                    guard.save_in(self.data_dir.as_deref())
                };
                if let Err(e) = result {
                    self.log(LogLevel::Error, format!("settings save failed: {e}"));
                }
                // Echo back so the UI's cached copy stays in sync.
                let _ = self.evt.send(Event::SettingsChanged(self.settings()));
            }
            Command::Shutdown => unreachable!("handled by the supervisor loop"),
        }
    }

    async fn cmd_add_paths(&mut self, paths: Vec<PathBuf>, recursive: bool) {
        self.scanning = true;
        let tx = self.internal_tx.clone();
        tokio::task::spawn_blocking(move || {
            let files = collect_pkg_files(&paths, recursive);
            let _ = tx.send(Internal::ScanDone { files });
        });
    }

    async fn cmd_remove(&mut self, id: ItemId) {
        // If active: stop first (best effort remote cleanup).
        let was_active = self.active.as_ref().map(|a| a.item_id) == Some(id);
        if was_active {
            self.stop_active_remote().await;
        }
        if let Some(item) = self.queue.remove(id) {
            if let Some(server) = &self.server {
                for part in &item.parts {
                    server.unregister(part.id);
                }
            }
            self.persist_queue();
            self.log(
                LogLevel::Info,
                format!(
                    "Removed item #{} ({})",
                    item.id,
                    item.primary_part()
                        .map(|p| p.file_name.clone())
                        .unwrap_or_default()
                ),
            );
        }
        if was_active {
            // The monitored task is gone — keep the queue moving like Cancel
            // does (v0.0.4 fix: the queue used to sit idle until the user
            // pressed Start queue again).
            self.maybe_start_next().await;
        }
    }

    async fn cmd_cancel(&mut self, id: ItemId) {
        if self.active.as_ref().map(|a| a.item_id) == Some(id) {
            let task_id = self.active.as_ref().unwrap().task_id;
            self.stop_active_remote().await;
            if let Some(item) = self.queue.get_mut(id) {
                item.state = ItemState::Cancelled;
                item.updated_at = unix_ms();
                self.persist_queue();
                self.log(
                    LogLevel::Warn,
                    format!("Cancelled item #{id}; remote stop of task {task_id} was best-effort; verify on the console if it still appears"),
                );
            }
        } else if let Some(item) = self.queue.get_mut(id)
            // Transferring/Installing items are the monitored active task and
            // are handled by the branch above. Everything else is cancellable
            // locally — including Submitting (v0.0.5 fix: cancelling during
            // the submit window used to be a silent no-op, and the console's
            // late answer then started the task anyway). The late SubmitResult
            // already best-effort-stops the ghost task on the console it was
            // sent to.
            && !matches!(item.state, ItemState::Transferring | ItemState::Installing)
        {
            let task_id = item.task_id;
            let job_id = item.job_id;
            let owner = item.task_owner;
            item.state = ItemState::Cancelled;
            item.updated_at = unix_ms();
            self.persist_queue();
            // A paused/unknown item may still hold a live console task:
            // clean it up best-effort too — but only on the console the task
            // actually belongs to (ids collide across consoles).
            if let Some(task_id) = task_id {
                if task_belongs_to(owner, self.console.as_ref().map(|c| c.addr)) {
                    self.stop_remote(task_id, job_id).await;
                    self.log(
                        LogLevel::Warn,
                        format!("Cancelled item #{id}; remote stop was best-effort; verify on the console if it still appears"),
                    );
                } else {
                    self.log(
                        LogLevel::Warn,
                        format!(
                            "Cancelled item #{id}; its console task belongs to another \
                             console; connect to that one to stop it remotely"
                        ),
                    );
                }
            } else {
                self.log(LogLevel::Info, format!("Cancelled item #{id}"));
            }
        }
        self.maybe_start_next().await;
    }

    async fn cmd_connect(&mut self, addr: SocketAddr) {
        self.console_status = ConsoleStatus::Probing;
        self.log(LogLevel::Info, format!("Connecting to {addr}…"));
        let tx = self.internal_tx.clone();
        tokio::spawn(async move {
            let client = RpiClient::new(addr, Duration::from_secs(6));
            let result = client.probe().await.map(|outcome| ConsoleTarget {
                addr,
                generation: ConsoleGeneration::Ps4,
                kind: outcome.kind,
                caps: outcome.caps,
                label: None,
                backend: outcome.identity.map(|sys| BackendIdentity {
                    name: sys.name,
                    version: sys.version,
                }),
            });
            let _ = tx.send(Internal::ProbeDone { addr, result });
        });
    }

    // -- internal message handling ------------------------------------------

    async fn handle_internal(&mut self, msg: Internal) {
        match msg {
            Internal::ScanDone { files } => {
                self.scanning = false;
                if files.len() >= MAX_WALK_FILES {
                    self.log(
                        LogLevel::Warn,
                        format!("Scan capped at {MAX_WALK_FILES} files (some packages may be missing); add the rest from another folder"),
                    );
                }
                let (added, skipped) = self.queue.add_paths(files);
                for s in skipped {
                    self.log(LogLevel::Warn, format!("Skipped: {s}"));
                }
                self.log(LogLevel::Info, format!("Added {} item(s)", added.len()));
                self.persist_queue();
                // Kick metadata parsing in the background.
                let parts: Vec<(ItemId, PartId, PathBuf)> = self
                    .queue
                    .items()
                    .iter()
                    .filter(|i| added.contains(&i.id))
                    .flat_map(|i| i.parts.iter().map(|p| (i.id, p.id, p.path.clone())))
                    .collect();
                let tx = self.internal_tx.clone();
                tokio::task::spawn_blocking(move || {
                    let results = parts
                        .into_iter()
                        .map(|(item, part_id, path)| (item, part_id, parse_pkg_file(&path)))
                        .collect();
                    let _ = tx.send(Internal::ParseDone { results });
                });
            }
            Internal::ParseDone { results } => {
                for (item_id, part_id, result) in results {
                    if let Some(item) = self.queue.get_mut(item_id)
                        && let Some(part) = item.parts.iter_mut().find(|p| p.id == part_id)
                    {
                        match result {
                            Ok(info) => {
                                part.pkg = Some(info);
                                part.invalid_reason = None;
                            }
                            Err(e) => {
                                part.invalid_reason = Some(e.to_string());
                            }
                        }
                    }
                }
                self.finalize_parse().await;
                self.persist_queue();
                self.maybe_start_next().await;
            }
            Internal::ProbeDone { addr, result } => match result {
                Ok(target) => {
                    // A successful connect while another console session is
                    // still live is an implicit disconnect of that session.
                    // Without this, the poller kept watching the OLD console
                    // (it holds the old client) while every remote control
                    // call and the status chip pointed at the NEW one — and
                    // the file-server swap below cuts the old console's
                    // download anyway. First connects and reconnects after an
                    // explicit Disconnect are unaffected (no live session).
                    if self.console.is_some() {
                        self.stop_poller();
                        let parked = self.park_inflight_unknown();
                        if parked > 0 {
                            self.log(
                                LogLevel::Warn,
                                format!(
                                    "Switched consoles with {parked} in-flight item(s): outcome unknown; reconnect to the previous console to re-attach"
                                ),
                            );
                        }
                    }
                    self.console = Some(target.clone());
                    self.console_status = ConsoleStatus::Connected;
                    self.client = Some(RpiClient::new(addr, Duration::from_secs(15)));
                    let backend = target
                        .backend
                        .as_ref()
                        .map(|b| format!(" ({b})"))
                        .unwrap_or_default();
                    let v2 = if target.caps.api_v2 { ", v2 API" } else { "" };
                    self.log(
                        LogLevel::Info,
                        format!("Connected to {addr} ({}{v2}){backend}", target.kind),
                    );
                    self.remember_console(addr);
                    self.start_server_for(addr).await;
                    self.reconcile().await;
                    self.maybe_start_next().await;
                }
                Err(e) => {
                    self.console_status = ConsoleStatus::Incompatible(e.to_string());
                    self.log(LogLevel::Error, format!("Cannot use {addr}: {e}"));
                }
            },
            Internal::DiscoveryDone(found) => {
                self.discovering = false;
                if found.is_empty() {
                    self.log(LogLevel::Warn, "No consoles found on the local network");
                } else {
                    self.log(
                        LogLevel::Info,
                        format!("Discovery: {} console(s)", found.len()),
                    );
                }
                let _ = self.evt.send(Event::DiscoveryDone(found));
            }
            Internal::SubmitResult {
                item,
                client,
                use_v2,
                console,
                result,
            } => {
                match result {
                    Ok(resp) => {
                        let task_id = resp.task_id;
                        // The console may have answered after a disconnect or a
                        // user cancel: never let a late response overwrite those
                        // decisions.
                        let still_submitting = self
                            .queue
                            .get(item)
                            .map(|i| i.state == ItemState::Submitting)
                            .unwrap_or(false);
                        if !still_submitting {
                            if let Some(it) = self.queue.get_mut(item)
                                && it.state == ItemState::Unknown
                                && task_id > 0
                            {
                                // Disconnected (or switched consoles) while the
                                // request was in flight: remember the ids — with
                                // the console they belong to — for a later
                                // reconcile on THAT console. The task itself is
                                // left running: we stopped observing it, not
                                // controlling it (same semantics as parking an
                                // in-flight transfer; only an explicit Cancel
                                // stops a console task).
                                it.task_id = Some(task_id);
                                it.job_id = resp.job_id;
                                it.task_owner = console;
                                it.updated_at = unix_ms();
                                self.persist_queue();
                                self.log(
                                    LogLevel::Info,
                                    format!(
                                        "Item #{item}: console answered after the disconnect \
                                         (task {task_id}); id kept for a later re-attach"
                                    ),
                                );
                            } else if task_id > 0 {
                                // Cancelled/removed meanwhile: best-effort
                                // cleanup through the SAME client the request
                                // went out on. self.client may by now point at
                                // a different console (or nothing), and firing
                                // the old task id there could stop an unrelated
                                // task — ids are console-scoped and collide
                                // across machines.
                                stop_via(&client, use_v2, task_id, resp.job_id).await;
                                self.log(
                                    LogLevel::Warn,
                                    format!(
                                        "Item #{item}: cancelled while the request was in \
                                         flight; remote stop of task {task_id} was \
                                         best-effort on the console it was sent to; verify \
                                         there if it still appears"
                                    ),
                                );
                            }
                            return;
                        }
                        if task_id == -1 {
                            // Both dialects use task_id -1 for "already
                            // installed": the console reports the outcome
                            // directly, no task to track.
                            if let Some(it) = self.queue.get_mut(item) {
                                it.task_id = None;
                                it.job_id = resp.job_id;
                                it.task_owner = console;
                                it.console_title = resp.title.clone();
                                it.state = ItemState::Completed;
                                it.error = None;
                                it.updated_at = unix_ms();
                            }
                            self.persist_queue();
                            self.log(
                                LogLevel::Info,
                                format!("Item #{item}: console reports already installed"),
                            );
                            self.maybe_start_next().await;
                            return;
                        }
                        let job_id = resp.job_id;
                        if let Some(it) = self.queue.get_mut(item) {
                            it.task_id = Some(task_id);
                            it.job_id = job_id;
                            it.task_owner = console;
                            it.console_title = resp.title.clone();
                            it.state = ItemState::Transferring;
                            // The submit cycle succeeded (possibly after
                            // transient retries); a later failure + Retry
                            // must start with a full retry budget again.
                            it.attempts = 0;
                            it.updated_at = unix_ms();
                        }
                        self.poll_failures = 0;
                        self.copy_phase_stalls = 0;
                        self.last_sample = None;
                        self.spawn_poller(item, task_id, job_id);
                        self.persist_queue();
                        self.log(
                            LogLevel::Info,
                            format!("Console accepted item #{item} (task {task_id}); downloading"),
                        );
                        // A pause may have arrived while the request was in
                        // flight (there was no task id to pause yet): apply it now.
                        if !self.running {
                            self.pause_remote(task_id, job_id).await;
                            if let Some(it) = self.queue.get_mut(item) {
                                it.state = ItemState::Paused;
                                it.updated_at = unix_ms();
                            }
                            self.persist_queue();
                            self.log(
                            LogLevel::Info,
                            "Console accepted the request; paused right away (pause arrived during submit)",
                        );
                        }
                    }
                    Err(e) => {
                        // A backend that answered "success" without a task id did
                        // accept the install — the outcome is simply untrackable.
                        // Reporting that as Failed would be dishonest; mark it
                        // Unknown and let the user verify on the console.
                        if matches!(e, RpiError::MissingTaskId) {
                            if let Some(it) = self.queue.get_mut(item) {
                                it.state = ItemState::Unknown;
                                it.error = Some(
                                "The console accepted the install but did not return a task id (fork dialect); verify on the console, then mark completed or retry".into(),
                            );
                                it.updated_at = unix_ms();
                            }
                            self.persist_queue();
                            self.log(
                            LogLevel::Warn,
                            format!("Item #{item}: install accepted without a task id; outcome unknown"),
                        );
                            self.maybe_start_next().await;
                            return;
                        }
                        let attempts = self.queue.get(item).map(|i| i.attempts).unwrap_or(0);
                        let transient = e.is_transient();
                        if transient && attempts + 1 < MAX_SUBMIT_ATTEMPTS {
                            if let Some(it) = self.queue.get_mut(item) {
                                it.attempts = attempts + 1;
                                it.error = Some(e.to_string());
                            }
                            // Never schedule a retry that would POST an empty
                            // URL list: without the local file server there is
                            // nothing to install (v0.0.4 fix).
                            let Some(urls) = self.rebuild_urls(item) else {
                                if let Some(it) = self.queue.get_mut(item) {
                                    it.state = ItemState::Failed;
                                    it.error = Some(
                                        "the local file server is not running; reconnect, then retry"
                                            .into(),
                                    );
                                    it.updated_at = unix_ms();
                                }
                                self.persist_queue();
                                self.log(
                                    LogLevel::Error,
                                    format!(
                                        "Item #{item}: cannot rebuild package URLs: the local file server is not running",
                                    ),
                                );
                                self.on_transfer_failure().await;
                                return;
                            };
                            let backoff = Duration::from_secs(1u64 << attempts);
                            self.log(
                                LogLevel::Warn,
                                format!(
                                    "Submit failed ({e}); retrying in {backoff:?} (attempt {}/{})",
                                    attempts + 2,
                                    MAX_SUBMIT_ATTEMPTS
                                ),
                            );
                            let tx = self.internal_tx.clone();
                            tokio::spawn(async move {
                                tokio::time::sleep(backoff).await;
                                let _ = tx.send(Internal::SubmitRetry { item, urls });
                            });
                        } else {
                            if let Some(it) = self.queue.get_mut(item) {
                                it.state = ItemState::Failed;
                                it.error = Some(e.to_string());
                                it.updated_at = unix_ms();
                            }
                            self.persist_queue();
                            self.log(LogLevel::Error, format!("Item #{item} failed: {e}"));
                            self.on_transfer_failure().await;
                        }
                    }
                }
            }
            Internal::SubmitRetry { item, urls } => {
                // Only retry if the item is still submitting and console alive.
                let still = matches!(
                    self.queue.get(item).map(|i| i.state),
                    Some(ItemState::Submitting)
                );
                if still && self.console.is_some() {
                    self.submit(item, urls).await;
                }
            }
            Internal::ProgressUpdate { item, result } => {
                self.handle_progress(item, result).await;
            }
            Internal::V2JobUpdate { item, result } => {
                self.handle_v2_job_update(item, result).await;
            }
        }
    }

    /// Post-parse validation: multipart consistency, duplicate content ids,
    /// invalid flagging.
    async fn finalize_parse(&mut self) {
        // Multipart consistency + invalid flagging.
        let ids: Vec<ItemId> = self.queue.items().iter().map(|i| i.id).collect();
        for id in ids {
            let Some(item) = self.queue.get_mut(id) else {
                continue;
            };
            if item.state.is_finished() {
                continue;
            }
            // All parts parsed?
            let any_invalid = item.parts.iter().any(|p| p.invalid_reason.is_some());
            let all_parsed = item.parts.iter().all(|p| p.pkg.is_some());
            if any_invalid || !all_parsed {
                if matches!(item.state, ItemState::Queued | ItemState::Preparing) {
                    let reason = item
                        .parts
                        .iter()
                        .filter_map(|p| p.invalid_reason.clone())
                        .next()
                        .unwrap_or_else(|| "metadata unavailable".into());
                    item.state = ItemState::Invalid;
                    item.error = Some(reason);
                }
                continue;
            }
            // Content id consistency across pieces.
            let cids: Vec<String> = item
                .parts
                .iter()
                .filter_map(|p| p.pkg.as_ref().map(|x| x.content_id.clone()))
                .collect();
            if cids.len() > 1 && cids.windows(2).any(|w| w[0] != w[1]) {
                item.error = Some(
                    "Multipart pieces have different content ids; grouping looks wrong".into(),
                );
                if matches!(item.state, ItemState::Queued | ItemState::Preparing) {
                    item.state = ItemState::Invalid;
                }
                continue;
            }
            // Missing piece detection: indices must be 0..n-1 consecutive.
            if item.is_multipart() {
                let idxs: Vec<u32> = item.parts.iter().filter_map(|p| p.part_index).collect();
                let expected: Vec<u32> = (0..idxs.len() as u32).collect();
                if idxs != expected {
                    item.error =
                        Some("Multipart pieces are not consecutive; a part seems missing".into());
                    if matches!(item.state, ItemState::Queued | ItemState::Preparing) {
                        item.state = ItemState::Invalid;
                    }
                }
            }
        }

        // Duplicate content ids among live items → mark later ones invalid.
        for (_first, dup) in self.queue.duplicates_by_content_id() {
            if let Some(item) = self.queue.get_mut(dup)
                && matches!(item.state, ItemState::Queued | ItemState::Preparing)
            {
                item.state = ItemState::Invalid;
                item.error = Some(format!(
                    "Duplicate content id: same package is already queued (item #{_first})"
                ));
                self.log(
                    LogLevel::Warn,
                    format!("Duplicate content id on item #{dup}"),
                );
            }
        }
    }

    // -- submission ----------------------------------------------------------

    /// Re-register an item's parts with the file server and return their URLs
    /// in install order.
    ///
    /// Part ids are *server-scoped*: a fresh server (new connect, restart)
    /// starts counting from zero again, while a restored queue may carry ids
    /// from an earlier server. The registration id the server hands back is
    /// therefore written back into the queue — building URLs from stale ids
    /// would silently stall the queue (`url_for` would miss).
    fn rebuild_urls(&mut self, item: ItemId) -> Option<Vec<String>> {
        let server = self.server.clone()?;
        let queue_item = self.queue.get_mut(item)?;
        let mut urls = Vec::with_capacity(queue_item.parts.len());
        for part in &mut queue_item.parts {
            let id = server.register(part.path.clone(), Some(part.file_name.clone()));
            part.id = id;
            urls.push(server.url_for(id)?);
        }
        Some(urls)
    }

    /// Ensure parts are registered, then POST the install request.
    /// v2 backends install through `/api/v2/install`; legacy through
    /// `/api/install`. The submitting console is recorded with the result so
    /// a late answer can only ever act on the console it belongs to.
    async fn submit(&mut self, item: ItemId, urls: Vec<String>) {
        let Some(client) = self.client.clone() else {
            return;
        };
        let use_v2 = self.v2_mode();
        let console = self.console.as_ref().map(|c| c.addr);
        let tx = self.internal_tx.clone();
        tokio::spawn(async move {
            let result = if use_v2 {
                client.v2_install(&urls).await
            } else {
                client.install_direct(&urls).await
            };
            let _ = tx.send(Internal::SubmitResult {
                item,
                client,
                use_v2,
                console,
                result,
            });
        });
    }

    /// True while an install request is in flight or waiting out a retry
    /// backoff. The single-active-task invariant must hold across the whole
    /// submit window — `self.active` is only set once the console *answers*,
    /// so between `submit()` and `SubmitResult` this is the only guard
    /// (v0.0.5 fix: a second Start/Retry/parse-finish in that window used to
    /// submit the next item too, leaving two console tasks running and the
    /// first item's poller silently replaced).
    fn submit_in_flight(&self) -> bool {
        self.queue
            .items()
            .iter()
            .any(|i| i.state == ItemState::Submitting)
    }

    /// Start the next queued item if the engine is running, connected, the
    /// server is up and no task is active. Invalid items are skipped in a loop.
    async fn maybe_start_next(&mut self) {
        loop {
            if !self.running
                || self.active.is_some()
                || self.submit_in_flight()
                || self.console.is_none()
                || self.server.is_none()
                || !matches!(self.console_status, ConsoleStatus::Connected)
            {
                return;
            }
            let Some(id) = self.queue.next_queued() else {
                return;
            };
            // Prepare: files still exist.
            let missing: Option<PathBuf> = {
                let item = self.queue.get(id).expect("queued item exists");
                item.parts.iter().find_map(|p| {
                    if std::fs::metadata(&p.path).is_err() {
                        Some(p.path.clone())
                    } else {
                        None
                    }
                })
            };
            if let Some(path) = missing {
                if let Some(item) = self.queue.get_mut(id) {
                    item.state = ItemState::Invalid;
                    item.error = Some(format!("file vanished: {}", path.display()));
                    item.updated_at = unix_ms();
                }
                self.persist_queue();
                self.log(LogLevel::Error, format!("Item #{id}: source file is gone"));
                continue; // try the next queued item
            }
            let Some(urls) = self.rebuild_urls(id) else {
                self.log(
                    LogLevel::Error,
                    "Could not register package files with the local server; install cannot proceed",
                );
                return;
            };
            if let Some(item) = self.queue.get_mut(id) {
                item.state = ItemState::Submitting;
                item.updated_at = unix_ms();
            }
            self.persist_queue();
            self.log(
                LogLevel::Info,
                format!("Submitting item #{id} ({})", urls.len()),
            );
            self.submit(id, urls).await;
            return;
        }
    }

    // -- progress -------------------------------------------------------------

    fn spawn_poller(&mut self, item: ItemId, task_id: i64, job_id: Option<i64>) {
        let Some(client) = self.client.clone() else {
            return;
        };
        let (cancel_tx, mut cancel_rx) = watch::channel(false);
        self.active = Some(ActiveTask {
            item_id: item,
            task_id,
            job_id,
            poll_cancel: cancel_tx,
        });
        let interval = self.settings().poll_interval;
        let tx = self.internal_tx.clone();
        tokio::spawn(async move {
            loop {
                tokio::select! {
                    _ = tokio::time::sleep(interval) => {},
                    _ = cancel_rx.wait_for(|&c| c) => break,
                }
                if job_id.is_some() {
                    // v2: one jobs request covers every tracked item; find
                    // this item's job by id (task id as a safety net).
                    let result = client.v2_jobs().await.map(|jobs| {
                        jobs.into_iter()
                            .find(|j| Some(j.id) == job_id || j.bgft_task_id == task_id)
                    });
                    let _ = tx.send(Internal::V2JobUpdate { item, result });
                } else {
                    let result = client.task_progress(task_id).await;
                    let _ = tx.send(Internal::ProgressUpdate { item, result });
                }
            }
        });
    }

    fn stop_poller(&mut self) {
        if let Some(active) = self.active.take() {
            let _ = active.poll_cancel.send(true);
        }
        self.last_sample = None;
    }

    async fn handle_progress(&mut self, item: ItemId, result: Result<ProgressView, RpiError>) {
        let Some(active) = self.active.as_ref() else {
            return;
        };
        if active.item_id != item {
            return; // stale poll for a task we no longer track
        }
        match result {
            Ok(mut pv) => {
                self.poll_failures = 0;
                // Speed from delta. The legacy DTO reports no speed of its own,
                // so the previous stored sample is the only history — blending
                // with it is what makes the readout an EMA instead of a
                // per-poll spike (v0.0.5 fix: the old code blended with
                // `pv.speed_bps`, which is always 0 in this dialect).
                //
                // The sample baseline only advances when the delta is actually
                // used (or regressed): refreshing it on every poll froze the
                // readout at 0 for poll intervals below the 400 ms threshold,
                // because consecutive samples never got far enough apart.
                let now = Instant::now();
                match self.last_sample {
                    Some((_, b0)) if pv.transferred < b0 => {
                        // Counter regressed (task restarted): reseed.
                        self.last_sample = Some((now, pv.transferred));
                    }
                    Some((t0, b0)) => {
                        let dt = now.duration_since(t0).as_secs_f64();
                        if dt >= 0.4 {
                            let prev_bps = self
                                .queue
                                .get(item)
                                .and_then(|i| i.progress)
                                .map(|p| p.speed_bps)
                                .unwrap_or(0);
                            pv.speed_bps = blend_speed(prev_bps, pv.transferred - b0, dt);
                            self.last_sample = Some((now, pv.transferred));
                        }
                        // else: too soon after the last used sample — keep the
                        // older baseline so the delta keeps accumulating.
                    }
                    None => self.last_sample = Some((now, pv.transferred)),
                }

                let total = pv.total;
                let transferred = pv.transferred;
                let copy = pv.local_copy_percent;
                let err = pv.raw_error;

                let Some(it) = self.queue.get_mut(item) else {
                    return;
                };
                it.progress = Some(pv);
                it.updated_at = unix_ms();

                if err != 0 {
                    // Console-side failure with evidence.
                    let gloss =
                        crate::rpi::explain_error_code(err).unwrap_or("unknown console error");
                    it.state = ItemState::Failed;
                    it.error = Some(format!("console error 0x{err:X}: {gloss}"));
                    self.on_transfer_failure().await;
                    return;
                }

                if copy >= 100 {
                    it.state = ItemState::Completed;
                    it.error = None;
                    self.log(
                        LogLevel::Info,
                        format!("Item #{item} completed (transfer + copy phases finished)"),
                    );
                    self.stop_poller_and_continue().await;
                    return;
                }

                if total > 0 && transferred >= total {
                    it.state = ItemState::Installing;
                    self.copy_phase_stalls += 1;
                    if self.copy_phase_stalls == 30 {
                        self.log(
                            LogLevel::Warn,
                            "Transfer finished but the console does not report the copy phase yet; keep the console awake and check its notifications",
                        );
                    }
                } else if it.state != ItemState::Paused {
                    // Keep the user's Paused state: the console still reports
                    // progress for a paused task, but that must not un-pause
                    // the item in the UI. Terminal transitions above
                    // (completion / console error) still apply.
                    it.state = ItemState::Transferring;
                    self.copy_phase_stalls = 0;
                }
            }
            Err(e) => {
                self.handle_poll_error(item, e).await;
            }
        }
    }

    /// Error handling shared by the legacy and v2 pollers: transient vs
    /// permanent errors, and the "task no longer known" terminal case.
    async fn handle_poll_error(&mut self, item: ItemId, e: RpiError) {
        let transient = e.is_transient();
        let item_gone = matches!(
            &e,
            RpiError::BackendError { code, .. } if *code == 0x8099_0019
        );
        if item_gone {
            if let Some(it) = self.queue.get_mut(item) {
                it.state = ItemState::Unknown;
                it.error = Some(
                    "The console no longer knows this task (it may have completed or been removed); verify on the console, then retry or mark completed"
                        .into(),
                );
            }
            self.stop_poller();
            self.persist_queue();
            self.maybe_start_next().await;
            return;
        }
        if transient {
            self.poll_failures += 1;
            if self.poll_failures == 3 {
                self.console_status = ConsoleStatus::Lost;
                self.log(
                    LogLevel::Error,
                    format!("Console stopped answering while installing ({e}). Keep the installer app in the foreground on the console; reconnect to resume monitoring."),
                );
            }
        } else {
            if let Some(it) = self.queue.get_mut(item) {
                it.error = Some(format!("progress error: {e}"));
            }
            self.log(LogLevel::Warn, format!("Progress error: {e}"));
        }
    }

    /// v2 poller: fold a job entry (or its absence) into the item state.
    ///
    /// Terminal job states come straight from the console's own task manager
    /// (`taskman.c`), so they count as evidence: `completed` requires the
    /// console to have observed full transfer without error, `failed` carries
    /// the console error code. A job missing from the list means the console
    /// app restarted or reclaimed the slot — the outcome is genuinely unknown.
    async fn handle_v2_job_update(
        &mut self,
        item: ItemId,
        result: Result<Option<V2Job>, RpiError>,
    ) {
        let Some(active) = self.active.as_ref() else {
            return;
        };
        if active.item_id != item {
            return; // stale poll for a job we no longer track
        }
        let job = match result {
            Ok(job) => job,
            Err(e) => {
                self.handle_poll_error(item, e).await;
                return;
            }
        };
        let Some(job) = job else {
            if let Some(it) = self.queue.get_mut(item) {
                it.state = ItemState::Unknown;
                it.error = Some(
                    "The console no longer lists this job (its app may have restarted); verify on the console, then retry or mark completed"
                        .into(),
                );
            }
            self.stop_poller();
            self.persist_queue();
            self.maybe_start_next().await;
            return;
        };
        self.poll_failures = 0;

        use crate::rpi::types::v2_states as st;
        let pv = v2_job_progress(&job);

        let Some(it) = self.queue.get_mut(item) else {
            return;
        };
        it.progress = Some(pv);
        it.updated_at = unix_ms();
        if it.task_id.is_none() && job.bgft_task_id > 0 {
            it.task_id = Some(job.bgft_task_id);
            it.task_owner = self.console.as_ref().map(|c| c.addr);
        }
        if it.job_id.is_none() {
```
