```rs
            it.job_id = Some(job.id);
            it.task_owner = self.console.as_ref().map(|c| c.addr);
        }

        let transferred_done = job.length_total > 0 && job.transferred >= job.length_total;
        match job.state.as_str() {
            st::COMPLETED | st::ALREADY_INSTALLED => {
                it.state = ItemState::Completed;
                it.error = None;
                self.log(
                    LogLevel::Info,
                    format!("Item #{item} completed (console job state: {})", job.state),
                );
                self.stop_poller_and_continue().await;
            }
            st::FAILED => {
                let gloss =
                    crate::rpi::explain_error_code(job.error).unwrap_or("unknown console error");
                it.state = ItemState::Failed;
                it.error = Some(format!("console error 0x{:X}: {gloss}", job.error));
                self.log(
                    LogLevel::Error,
                    format!("Item #{item} failed: console job error 0x{:X}", job.error),
                );
                self.on_transfer_failure().await;
            }
            st::STOPPED => {
                it.state = ItemState::Cancelled;
                it.error = Some("Console reports the job was stopped".into());
                self.log(
                    LogLevel::Warn,
                    format!("Item #{item}: console reports the job was stopped"),
                );
                self.stop_poller_and_continue().await;
            }
            st::PAUSED => {
                // Console-side pause (dashboard or this app): keep Paused.
                it.state = ItemState::Paused;
            }
            _ => {
                if transferred_done {
                    it.state = ItemState::Installing;
                } else {
                    it.state = ItemState::Transferring;
                }
            }
        }
    }

    /// Stop the active poller, then auto-advance if enabled.
    async fn stop_poller_and_continue(&mut self) {
        self.stop_poller();
        self.persist_queue();
        if self.settings().auto_advance {
            self.maybe_start_next().await;
        }
    }

    /// A transfer failed. Honor the failure policy: keep the queue moving, or
    /// park it until the user explicitly restarts (Start queue / Retry).
    async fn on_transfer_failure(&mut self) {
        self.stop_poller();
        self.persist_queue();
        match self.settings().on_failure {
            FailurePolicy::Continue => {
                if self.settings().auto_advance {
                    self.maybe_start_next().await;
                }
            }
            FailurePolicy::Pause => {
                self.running = false;
                self.log(
                    LogLevel::Warn,
                    "Queue paused: a package failed to transfer (setting: pause on \
                     failure). Retry the item or press Start queue to continue.",
                );
            }
        }
    }

    // -- remote control --------------------------------------------------------

    /// Resume one paused item: ask the console to continue its task and
    /// re-attach a poller when this item is not the monitored one. Honors the
    /// single-active-task invariant.
    async fn resume_item(&mut self, id: ItemId) {
        let Some((task_id, job_id, owner)) = self
            .queue
            .get(id)
            .map(|i| (i.task_id, i.job_id, i.task_owner))
        else {
            self.log(
                LogLevel::Warn,
                format!("Item #{id} has no console task to resume; use Retry instead"),
            );
            return;
        };
        let Some(task_id) = task_id else {
            self.log(
                LogLevel::Warn,
                format!("Item #{id} has no console task to resume; use Retry instead"),
            );
            return;
        };
        // Task ids are console-scoped: resuming on a different console would
        // aim at that console's own (unrelated) task with the same id.
        if !task_belongs_to(owner, self.console.as_ref().map(|c| c.addr)) {
            self.log(
                LogLevel::Warn,
                format!(
                    "Item #{id} belongs to a different console; reconnect to that one to \
                     resume it"
                ),
            );
            return;
        }
        let is_monitored = self.active.as_ref().map(|a| a.item_id) == Some(id);
        if !is_monitored && self.active.is_some() {
            self.log(
                LogLevel::Warn,
                "Another item is already being monitored; wait for it to finish first",
            );
            return;
        }
        if self.submit_in_flight() {
            // Resuming now would race the in-flight submit for the
            // single-active-task slot (v0.0.5 fix).
            self.log(
                LogLevel::Warn,
                "An install request is still in flight; wait for the console to answer first",
            );
            return;
        }
        self.running = true;
        self.resume_remote(task_id, job_id).await;
        if let Some(item) = self.queue.get_mut(id) {
            item.state = ItemState::Transferring;
            item.updated_at = unix_ms();
        }
        if !is_monitored {
            self.spawn_poller(id, task_id, job_id);
        }
        self.persist_queue();
        self.log(
            LogLevel::Info,
            format!("Resumed item #{id} (task {task_id})"),
        );
    }

    /// The connected backend speaks the v2 job API right now.
    fn v2_mode(&self) -> bool {
        self.console
            .as_ref()
            .map(|c| c.caps.api_v2)
            .unwrap_or(false)
    }

    /// Pause the console-side task. The endpoint is chosen by the CURRENT
    /// backend capabilities: an item carrying a v2 job id may be resumed
    /// after reconnecting to a legacy-only backend, and vice versa.
    async fn pause_remote(&mut self, task_id: i64, job_id: Option<i64>) {
        if let Some(client) = self.client.clone() {
            let _ = match (self.v2_mode(), job_id) {
                (true, Some(job)) => client.v2_job_op(job, "pause").await,
                _ => client.pause_task(task_id).await,
            };
        }
    }

    async fn resume_remote(&mut self, task_id: i64, job_id: Option<i64>) {
        if let Some(client) = self.client.clone() {
            let _ = match (self.v2_mode(), job_id) {
                (true, Some(job)) => client.v2_job_op(job, "resume").await,
                _ => client.resume_task(task_id).await,
            };
        }
    }

    /// Stop a console-side task or job (best-effort), aiming at the current
    /// console.
    async fn stop_remote(&mut self, task_id: i64, job_id: Option<i64>) {
        if let Some(client) = self.client.clone() {
            stop_via(&client, self.v2_mode(), task_id, job_id).await;
        }
    }

    /// Best-effort console-side cleanup for the active task.
    async fn stop_active_remote(&mut self) {
        if let Some(active) = self.active.take() {
            let _ = active.poll_cancel.send(true);
            self.stop_remote(active.task_id, active.job_id).await;
        }
        self.last_sample = None;
    }

    // -- connection lifecycle ----------------------------------------------------

    fn remember_console(&mut self, addr: SocketAddr) {
        let snapshot = {
            let mut s = self.settings.lock().unwrap();
            s.remembered.retain(|r| r.addr != addr);
            s.remembered
                .insert(0, RememberedConsole { addr, label: None });
            if s.remembered.len() > 8 {
                s.remembered.truncate(8);
            }
            let _ = s.save_in(self.data_dir.as_deref());
            s.clone()
        };
        // The UI keeps a cached copy of the settings; broadcast the new
        // remembered-console list so it never goes stale.
        let _ = self.evt.send(Event::SettingsChanged(snapshot));
    }

    /// Start (or restart) the file server on an interface that can reach the
    /// console; never `0.0.0.0` silently. A fixed port may still be held for a
    /// few milliseconds by the previous server's shutdown, so binding is
    /// retried briefly before giving up.
    async fn start_server_for(&mut self, console_addr: SocketAddr) {
        // Stop any previous server: parts are re-registered on demand.
        if let Some(old) = self.server.take() {
            old.shutdown();
        }
        let bind_ip = pick_bind_ip(console_addr.ip());
        let fixed_port = self.settings().server_port;
        let port = fixed_port.unwrap_or(0);
        let bind = SocketAddr::new(bind_ip, port);
        let attempts = if fixed_port.is_some() { 8 } else { 1 };
        let mut last_err = None;
        let mut server = None;
        for attempt in 0..attempts {
            match FileServer::start(bind).await {
                Ok(s) => {
                    server = Some(s);
                    break;
                }
                Err(e) => {
                    last_err = Some(e);
                    if attempt + 1 < attempts {
                        tokio::time::sleep(Duration::from_millis(250)).await;
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        match server {
            Some(s) => {
                let s = Arc::new(s);
                self.log(
                    LogLevel::Info,
                    format!(
                        "Serving packages from {} (console downloads from here)",
                        s.base_url
                    ),
                );
                if bind_ip.is_unspecified() {
                    self.log(
                        LogLevel::Warn,
                        "No local interface matched the console subnet; bound to all interfaces. If the console cannot download, check your firewall.",
                    );
                }
                self.server = Some(s);
            }
            None => {
                self.log(
                    LogLevel::Error,
                    format!("Could not start the local file server on {bind}: {}; installs cannot proceed", last_err.map(|e| e.to_string()).unwrap_or_default()),
                );
            }
        }
    }

    /// Reconcile `Unknown`/`Paused` items against the console after connecting:
    /// re-attach a poller to ONE task that still exists (the engine monitors a
    /// single active task); the rest keep their state with a hint so the user
    /// resumes them deliberately. Never resubmits blindly.
    async fn reconcile(&mut self) {
        if self.v2_mode() {
            self.reconcile_v2().await;
        } else {
            self.reconcile_legacy().await;
        }
    }

    /// v2 reconcile: one jobs request replaces the find_task/per-task-progress
    /// probing. Jobs are matched by task id first, content id second; terminal
    /// job states resolve `Unknown` items directly.
    async fn reconcile_v2(&mut self) {
        let Some(client) = self.client.clone() else {
            return;
        };
        let jobs = match client.v2_jobs().await {
            Ok(jobs) => jobs,
            Err(e) => {
                self.log(
                    LogLevel::Warn,
                    format!("Reconcile: v2 jobs unavailable ({e}); falling back to task probing"),
                );
                self.reconcile_legacy().await;
                return;
            }
        };

        struct Pending {
            id: ItemId,
            task_id: Option<i64>,
            job_id: Option<i64>,
            /// Console the ids were learned on — they only match jobs on that
            /// console (ids collide across machines).
            task_owner: Option<SocketAddr>,
            content_id: Option<String>,
        }
        let current_addr = self.console.as_ref().map(|c| c.addr);
        let items: Vec<Pending> = self
            .queue
            .items()
            .iter()
            .filter(|i| matches!(i.state, ItemState::Unknown | ItemState::Paused))
            .map(|i| Pending {
                id: i.id,
                task_id: i.task_id,
                job_id: i.job_id,
                task_owner: i.task_owner,
                content_id: i.content_id().map(|c| c.to_string()),
            })
            .collect();

        let mut attached = false;
        for pending in items {
            let (id, task_id, job_id, task_owner, content_id) = (
                pending.id,
                pending.task_id,
                pending.job_id,
                pending.task_owner,
                pending.content_id,
            );
            // Match by task id (or remembered job id) — but only when the ids
            // were learned on THIS console; content ids are console-
            // independent and stay a valid cross-console match.
            let ids_match_here = task_belongs_to(task_owner, current_addr);
            let found = jobs.iter().find(|j| {
                ids_match_here
                    && (matches!(task_id, Some(t) if t > 0 && j.bgft_task_id == t)
                        || matches!(job_id, Some(jid) if j.id == jid))
            });
            let found = match found {
                Some(j) => Some(j),
                None => jobs.iter().find(|j| {
                    content_id
                        .as_deref()
                        .is_some_and(|c| !c.is_empty() && j.content_id == c)
                }),
            };
            let Some(job) = found else {
                if let Some(it) = self.queue.get_mut(id) {
                    it.error = Some(
                        "The console does not report this job anymore; verify on the console, then retry or mark completed"
                            .into(),
                    );
                }
                continue;
            };

            let task_id = if job.bgft_task_id > 0 {
                Some(job.bgft_task_id)
            } else {
                task_id
            };
            if let Some(it) = self.queue.get_mut(id) {
                it.task_id = task_id;
                it.job_id = Some(job.id);
                it.task_owner = current_addr;
                it.error = None;
            }

            use crate::rpi::types::v2_states as st;
            let progress = v2_job_progress(job);
            match job.state.as_str() {
                st::COMPLETED | st::ALREADY_INSTALLED => {
                    if let Some(it) = self.queue.get_mut(id) {
                        it.state = ItemState::Completed;
                        it.error = None;
                        it.progress = Some(progress);
                        it.updated_at = unix_ms();
                    }
                    self.log(
                        LogLevel::Info,
                        format!(
                            "Item #{id} already finished on the console (job state: {})",
                            job.state
                        ),
                    );
                }
                st::FAILED => {
                    if let Some(it) = self.queue.get_mut(id) {
                        it.state = ItemState::Failed;
                        it.error = Some(format!("console error 0x{:X}", job.error));
                        it.updated_at = unix_ms();
                    }
                    self.log(
                        LogLevel::Warn,
                        format!(
                            "Item #{id} failed on the console while disconnected (error 0x{:X})",
                            job.error
                        ),
                    );
                }
                st::STOPPED => {
                    if let Some(it) = self.queue.get_mut(id) {
                        it.state = ItemState::Cancelled;
                        it.error = Some("Console reports the job was stopped".into());
                        it.updated_at = unix_ms();
                    }
                }
                st::PAUSED => {
                    if let Some(it) = self.queue.get_mut(id) {
                        it.state = ItemState::Paused;
                    }
                }
                _ => {
                    // Still active console-side.
                    if attached {
                        if let Some(it) = self.queue.get_mut(id) {
                            it.error = Some(
                                "Console still reports this job; it will be resumed after the monitored item finishes"
                                    .into(),
                            );
                        }
                        self.log(
                            LogLevel::Info,
                            format!(
                                "Console still reports item #{id} (job {}); it will be resumed after the monitored item finishes",
                                job.id
                            ),
                        );
                        continue;
                    }
                    if let Some(it) = self.queue.get_mut(id) {
                        it.state = if job.length_total > 0 && job.transferred >= job.length_total {
                            ItemState::Installing
                        } else {
                            ItemState::Transferring
                        };
                        it.progress = Some(progress);
                    }
                    if let Some(tid) = task_id {
                        self.spawn_poller(id, tid, Some(job.id));
                        attached = true;
                        self.log(
                            LogLevel::Info,
                            format!(
                                "Re-attached item #{id} to console job {} (task {tid})",
                                job.id
                            ),
                        );
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        self.persist_queue();
    }

    /// Legacy reconcile: find_task per item, one progress probe by task id.
    async fn reconcile_legacy(&mut self) {
        struct Pending {
            id: ItemId,
            task_id: Option<i64>,
            task_owner: Option<SocketAddr>,
            content_id: Option<String>,
            sub_type: i32,
        }
        let current_addr = self.console.as_ref().map(|c| c.addr);
        let items: Vec<Pending> = self
            .queue
            .items()
            .iter()
            .filter(|i| matches!(i.state, ItemState::Unknown | ItemState::Paused))
            .filter(|i| i.task_id.is_some() || i.content_id().is_some())
            .map(|i| Pending {
                id: i.id,
                task_id: i.task_id,
                task_owner: i.task_owner,
                content_id: i.content_id().map(|c| c.to_string()),
                sub_type: sub_type_for(i),
            })
            .collect();
        let mut attached = false;
        for Pending {
            id,
            task_id,
            task_owner,
            content_id,
            sub_type,
        } in items
        {
            let Some(client) = self.client.clone() else {
                return;
            };
            // Prefer find_task (content-id based) for fresh evidence.
            if let Some(cid) = &content_id
                && let Ok(found) = client.find_task(cid, sub_type).await
            {
                let tid = found.task_id;
                if let Some(it) = self.queue.get_mut(id) {
                    it.task_id = Some(tid);
                    it.task_owner = current_addr;
                    it.error = None;
                }
                if attached {
                    self.log(
                        LogLevel::Info,
                        format!("Console still reports item #{id} (task {tid}); it will be resumed after the monitored item finishes"),
                    );
                    continue;
                }
                if let Some(it) = self.queue.get_mut(id) {
                    it.state = ItemState::Transferring;
                }
                self.spawn_poller(id, tid, None);
                attached = true;
                self.log(
                    LogLevel::Info,
                    format!("Re-attached item #{id} to console task {tid}"),
                );
                continue;
            }
            // Probe progress once by task id — works even when the backend
            // does not implement find_task. Only for ids learned on THIS
            // console: a foreign console's task id would probe (and then
            // monitor) that console's unrelated task with the same id.
            if let Some(tid) = task_id
                && task_belongs_to(task_owner, current_addr)
                && let Ok(pv) = client.task_progress(tid).await
                && pv.raw_error == 0
            {
                if let Some(it) = self.queue.get_mut(id) {
                    if attached {
                        it.error = Some(
                            "Console still reports this task; it will be resumed after the monitored item finishes".into(),
                        );
                        continue;
                    }
                    it.state = if pv.local_copy_percent >= 100 {
                        ItemState::Completed
                    } else if pv.transferred >= pv.total && pv.total > 0 {
                        ItemState::Installing
                    } else {
                        ItemState::Transferring
                    };
                    it.progress = Some(pv);
                    it.task_owner = current_addr;
                }
                if !attached {
                    self.spawn_poller(id, tid, None);
                    attached = true;
                }
                continue;
            }
            // Task unknown to the console: leave as Unknown with a hint.
            if let Some(it) = self.queue.get_mut(id) {
                it.error = Some(
                    "The console does not report this task anymore; verify on the console, then retry or mark completed".into(),
                );
            }
        }
        self.persist_queue();
    }

    // -- shutdown ---------------------------------------------------------------

    async fn shutdown(&mut self) {
        self.running = false;
        // Flush state BEFORE the best-effort remote stop: that call can hang
        // for the client's full timeout (15 s) while `EngineHandle::shutdown`
        // hard-stops the runtime after 5 s — persisting afterwards could be
        // skipped entirely on a black-holed console. All transitions already
        // persist immediately, so this only secures the final flush.
        self.persist_queue();
        // Best-effort console-side cleanup with a short timeout.
        if let Some(active) = self.active.take() {
            let _ = active.poll_cancel.send(true);
            self.stop_remote(active.task_id, active.job_id).await;
        }
        if let Some(server) = self.server.take() {
            server.shutdown();
        }
        self.log(LogLevel::Info, "Engine shut down; state flushed");
    }
}

/// Blend a progress-poll delta into the displayed speed (bytes/s).
/// 40 % fresh + 60 % previous — same idea as the file-server rate EMA.
/// Split out so the math is directly testable.
fn blend_speed(prev_bps: u64, delta_bytes: u64, dt: f64) -> u64 {
    ((delta_bytes as f64 / dt) * 0.4 + prev_bps as f64 * 0.6) as u64
}

/// Best-effort stop of a console task/job through a specific client.
/// Shared by the engine's current-console stop and the late-submit cleanup,
/// which must act on the console the request was actually sent to.
async fn stop_via(client: &RpiClient, use_v2: bool, task_id: i64, job_id: Option<i64>) {
    match (use_v2, job_id) {
        (true, Some(job)) => {
            let _ = client.v2_job_op(job, "stop").await;
        }
        _ => {
            let _ = client.stop_task(task_id).await;
            let _ = client.unregister_task(task_id).await;
        }
    }
}

/// True when a task learned on `owner` may be operated on from `current`.
/// `None` (pre-`task_owner` queue files) stays permissive so legacy queues
/// keep re-attaching after reconnecting to the same console as before.
fn task_belongs_to(owner: Option<SocketAddr>, current: Option<SocketAddr>) -> bool {
    owner.is_none() || owner == current
}

/// Map a v2 job entry to the UI progress view (shared by the poller and the
/// reconcile path).
fn v2_job_progress(job: &V2Job) -> ProgressView {
    ProgressView {
        percent: if job.length_total > 0 {
            (job.transferred as f64 / job.length_total as f64).clamp(0.0, 1.0) * 100.0
        } else {
            job.percent.clamp(0, 100) as f64
        } as f32,
        transferred: job.transferred,
        total: job.length_total,
        speed_bps: job.speed_bps.max(0.0) as u64,
        eta_sec: if job.rest_sec > 0 && job.rest_sec < u32::MAX as u64 {
            Some(job.rest_sec)
        } else {
            None
        },
        piece_index: job.num_index,
        piece_total: job.num_total,
        preparing_percent: 0,
        local_copy_percent: 0,
        raw_error: job.error,
    }
}

/// Map an item's parsed metadata to a BGFT task sub type
/// (Game=6, AC=7, Patch=8, License=9 — `docs/BASELINE.md` §3).
fn sub_type_for(item: &QueueItem) -> i32 {
    use crate::model::ContentKind::*;
    let Some(pkg) = item.primary_part().and_then(|p| p.pkg.as_ref()) else {
        return 6;
    };
    match pkg.content_kind {
        Gd => {
            if pkg.is_patch {
                8
            } else {
                6
            }
        }
        Ac | Al => 7,
        Dp => 8,
        _ => 6,
    }
}

/// Pick the local interface IP whose subnet contains the console; fall back to
/// any non-loopback IPv4, then `0.0.0.0` with an explicit warning.
fn pick_bind_ip(console_ip: IpAddr) -> IpAddr {
    let nets = discovery::local_networks();
    for net in &nets {
        if net.prefix > 31 {
            continue;
        }
        let IpAddr::V4(target) = console_ip else {
            break;
        };
        let t = u32::from_be_bytes(target.octets());
        let mask = if net.prefix == 0 {
            0
        } else {
            u32::MAX << (32 - net.prefix)
        };
        if (t & mask) == net.network
            && let Ok(ifaces) = if_addrs::get_if_addrs()
        {
            for iface in ifaces {
                if let IpAddr::V4(v4) = iface.ip() {
                    let a = u32::from_be_bytes(v4.octets());
                    if (a & mask) == net.network && !v4.is_loopback() {
                        return IpAddr::V4(v4);
                    }
                }
            }
        }
    }
    if let Ok(ifaces) = if_addrs::get_if_addrs() {
        for iface in ifaces {
            if let IpAddr::V4(v4) = iface.ip()
                && !v4.is_loopback()
            {
                return IpAddr::V4(v4);
            }
        }
    }
    IpAddr::from([0, 0, 0, 0])
}

/// Blocking scan: walk paths, collect `.pkg` files (bounded).
fn collect_pkg_files(paths: &[PathBuf], recursive: bool) -> Vec<PathBuf> {
    let mut out = Vec::new();
    for path in paths {
        if path.is_dir() {
            walk_dir(path, recursive, &mut out);
        } else if is_pkg(path) {
            out.push(path.clone());
        }
        if out.len() >= MAX_WALK_FILES {
            break;
        }
    }
    out.sort();
    out
}

fn walk_dir(dir: &Path, recursive: bool, out: &mut Vec<PathBuf>) {
    let Ok(entries) = std::fs::read_dir(dir) else {
        return;
    };
    for entry in entries.flatten() {
        let path = entry.path();
        // `file_type()` (dirent, no symlink follow) decides recursion: a
        // directory symlink would otherwise recurse into itself forever
        // (v0.0.4 fix — a symlink loop in a scanned folder hung the app).
        // Explicitly symlinked *files* still pass through `is_pkg`, which
        // resolves the link.
        let is_dir = entry.file_type().is_ok_and(|t| t.is_dir());
        if is_dir {
            if recursive
                && path
                    .file_name()
                    .map(|n| !n.to_string_lossy().starts_with('.'))
                    .unwrap_or(true)
            {
                walk_dir(&path, recursive, out);
            }
        } else if is_pkg(&path) {
            out.push(path);
        }
        if out.len() >= MAX_WALK_FILES {
            return;
        }
    }
}

fn is_pkg(path: &Path) -> bool {
    path.extension()
        .and_then(|e| e.to_str())
        .map(|e| e.eq_ignore_ascii_case("pkg"))
        .unwrap_or(false)
        && path.is_file()
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[cfg(unix)]
    #[test]
    fn walk_dir_survives_symlink_loops() {
        let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        std::fs::write(dir.path().join("game.pkg"), b"pkg").unwrap();
        // A self-referencing directory symlink: with the old is_dir()-follows-
        // symlinks recursion this walk never terminated (v0.0.4 fix).
        std::os::unix::fs::symlink(dir.path(), dir.path().join("loop")).unwrap();
        let mut out = Vec::new();
        walk_dir(dir.path(), true, &mut out);
        assert_eq!(out.len(), 1, "only the real pkg, and the walk terminated");
        assert!(out[0].ends_with("game.pkg"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn speed_blend_converges_and_decays() {
        // 1 MiB/s instantaneous rate, sampled every second.
        let s1 = blend_speed(0, 1_048_576, 1.0);
        assert!(
            (s1 as i64 - 419_430).abs() < 4,
            "first sample = 40% of 1 MiB/s, got {s1}"
        );
        let s2 = blend_speed(s1, 1_048_576, 1.0);
        assert!(
            s2 > 600_000 && s2 < 1_100_000,
            "converges toward 1 MiB/s, got {s2}"
        );
        // Traffic stops → the readout decays instead of snapping to zero.
        let s3 = blend_speed(s2, 0, 1.0);
        assert!(s3 < s2 && s3 > 0, "decays, got {s3}");
    }

    #[test]
    fn walk_dir_finds_pkgs_in_plain_subdirs() {
        let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        std::fs::write(dir.path().join("a.pkg"), b"x").unwrap();
        let sub = dir.path().join("sub");
        std::fs::create_dir(&sub).unwrap();
        std::fs::write(sub.join("b.pkg"), b"x").unwrap();
        std::fs::write(sub.join("note.txt"), b"x").unwrap();
        let mut out = Vec::new();
        walk_dir(dir.path(), true, &mut out);
        out.sort();
        assert_eq!(out.len(), 2, "recursive scan finds nested packages");
        let mut flat = Vec::new();
        walk_dir(dir.path(), false, &mut flat);
        assert_eq!(flat.len(), 1, "flat scan sees only the top-level pkg");
        assert!(flat[0].ends_with("a.pkg"));
    }
}

```

`crates/rpi-ng-core/src/fileserver.rs`:

```rs
//! Allowlist-scoped local HTTP file server that streams PKG files to the
//! console with Range support.
//!
//! Design points (see `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §4.5):
//! - Only files explicitly registered in the queue are served; URLs use opaque
//!   ids, so path traversal is structurally impossible.
//! - Files are streamed in bounded chunks — never loaded whole.
//! - Single-range and suffix-range requests are supported (required by the
//!   console payload's prerequisite fetcher); multi-range requests fall back
//!   to a full response, which RFC 7233 permits.
//! - Binds the interface reachable from the console (never `0.0.0.0`
//!   silently).

use std::{
    collections::HashMap,
    io::SeekFrom,
    net::SocketAddr,
    path::PathBuf,
    sync::{Arc, Mutex},
    time::Duration,
};

use axum::{
    Router,
    body::Body,
    extract::{Path as AxumPath, Request, State},
    http::{HeaderMap, Method, StatusCode, header},
    middleware::{self, Next},
    response::{IntoResponse, Response},
    routing::get,
};
use tokio::{
    io::{AsyncReadExt, AsyncSeekExt},
    net::TcpListener,
    sync::watch,
};
use tokio_util::io::ReaderStream;

use crate::model::PartId;

/// Percent-encode set for URL path segments: keep RFC 3986 `pchar` unreserved
/// and sub-delims literal (letters, digits, `-._~!$&'()*+,;=:@`), encode the
/// rest. Spaces and specials must be encoded — console installs break on them
/// (pkg-sender issue #6, `docs/RESEARCH.md` §2.3).
const SEGMENT: &percent_encoding::AsciiSet = &percent_encoding::CONTROLS
    .add(b' ')
    .add(b'"')
    .add(b'#')
    .add(b'%')
    .add(b'/')
    .add(b'<')
    .add(b'>')
    .add(b'?')
    .add(b'`')
    .add(b'{')
    .add(b'}')
    .add(b'\\')
    .add(b'^')
    .add(b'|');

/// Concurrency note: the console opens only a handful of connections
/// (prerequisite fetches + the BGFT download), so no artificial open-file gate
/// is imposed; the allowlist bounds exposure to registered files.

#[derive(Default)]
struct Registry {
    /// Opaque id → (canonical path, display file name).
    entries: HashMap<PartId, (PathBuf, String)>,
    next: PartId,
}

impl Registry {
    fn register(&mut self, path: PathBuf, name: String) -> PartId {
        // Re-registering the same (path, name) must not allocate a second
        // entry: the engine re-registers a part on every submit / retry /
        // requeue, and duplicate entries would grow the registry for the
        // whole session while keeping stale URLs alive (v0.0.5 fix). Hand
        // back the existing id instead.
        if let Some((&id, _)) = self
            .entries
            .iter()
            .find(|(_, (p, n))| *p == path && *n == name)
        {
            return id;
        }
        let id = self.next;
        self.next += 1;
        self.entries.insert(id, (path, name));
        id
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Throughput accounting (live in/out Mbps for the UI)
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Live byte counters for the file server, plus a smoothed rate sampler.
///
/// - **tx** = response body bytes (what the console downloads — the number
///   users think of as "transfer speed").
/// - **rx** = request bytes (request line + headers; tiny — control traffic).
///
/// Both are best-effort approximations, documented on purpose: tx counts the
/// promised `Content-Length` (a mid-body disconnect over-counts by the
/// unsent tail), rx estimates the parsed request's wire size.
#[derive(Debug, Default)]
pub struct ServerStats {
    tx_bytes: std::sync::atomic::AtomicU64,
    rx_bytes: std::sync::atomic::AtomicU64,
    requests: std::sync::atomic::AtomicU64,
    rate: Mutex<RateState>,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Default)]
struct RateState {
    /// (t, tx, rx) of the previous sample.
    last: Option<(std::time::Instant, u64, u64)>,
    /// Smoothed bytes/sec (EMA over per-sample deltas).
    tx_ema: f64,
    rx_ema: f64,
}

impl ServerStats {
    fn add_tx(&self, bytes: u64) {
        self.tx_bytes
            .fetch_add(bytes, std::sync::atomic::Ordering::Relaxed);
    }

    fn add_rx(&self, bytes: u64) {
        self.rx_bytes
            .fetch_add(bytes, std::sync::atomic::Ordering::Relaxed);
    }

    fn add_request(&self) {
        self.requests
            .fetch_add(1, std::sync::atomic::Ordering::Relaxed);
    }

    /// Totals since the server started: (tx, rx, requests).
    pub fn totals(&self) -> (u64, u64, u64) {
        (
            self.tx_bytes.load(std::sync::atomic::Ordering::Relaxed),
            self.rx_bytes.load(std::sync::atomic::Ordering::Relaxed),
            self.requests.load(std::sync::atomic::Ordering::Relaxed),
        )
    }

    /// Smoothed transfer rates in **bytes per second**, sampled on demand
    /// (the engine snapshots on each poll tick and on a 250 ms ticker while
    /// the server runs, so the UI stays live without the server itself
    /// keeping any timer).
    pub fn rates(&self) -> (f64, f64) {
        let now = std::time::Instant::now();
        let (tx, rx, _) = self.totals();
        let mut state = self.rate.lock().unwrap();
        update_rates(&mut state, now, tx, rx)
    }
}

/// EMA update, split out so the math is directly testable. α = 0.5 keeps it
/// responsive on speed-up and calm on jitter; samples closer than 100 ms
/// return the previous EMA unchanged (no divide-by-tiny spikes).
fn update_rates(state: &mut RateState, now: std::time::Instant, tx: u64, rx: u64) -> (f64, f64) {
    const MIN_DELTA: Duration = Duration::from_millis(100);
    let Some((t, prev_tx, prev_rx)) = state.last else {
        state.last = Some((now, tx, rx));
        return (state.tx_ema, state.rx_ema);
    };
    let dt = now.saturating_duration_since(t);
    if dt < MIN_DELTA {
        return (state.tx_ema, state.rx_ema);
    }
    let secs = dt.as_secs_f64();
    let tx_rate = tx.saturating_sub(prev_tx) as f64 / secs;
    let rx_rate = rx.saturating_sub(prev_rx) as f64 / secs;
    state.tx_ema = state.tx_ema * 0.5 + tx_rate * 0.5;
    state.rx_ema = state.rx_ema * 0.5 + rx_rate * 0.5;
    state.last = Some((now, tx, rx));
    (state.tx_ema, state.rx_ema)
}

/// Per-request state handed to the handlers.
#[derive(Clone)]
struct ServerState {
    registry: Arc<Mutex<Registry>>,
    stats: Arc<ServerStats>,
}

/// Running file server handle.
pub struct FileServer {
    registry: Arc<Mutex<Registry>>,
    stats: Arc<ServerStats>,
    pub bind: SocketAddr,
    /// Base URL the console should use, e.g. `http://192.168.1.20:53742`.
    pub base_url: String,
    shutdown: watch::Sender<bool>,
}

impl FileServer {
    /// Bind `bind_addr` and serve the registry until [`FileServer::shutdown`].
    pub async fn start(bind_addr: SocketAddr) -> std::io::Result<Self> {
        let registry = Arc::new(Mutex::new(Registry::default()));
        let stats = Arc::new(ServerStats::default());
        let (shutdown_tx, mut shutdown_rx) = watch::channel(false);

        let app = Router::new()
            .route(
                "/pkg/{id}/{name}",
                get(serve_with_name).head(serve_with_name),
            )
            .route("/pkg/{id}", get(serve).head(serve))
            // Count incoming request bytes before routing.
            .layer(middleware::from_fn_with_state(Arc::clone(&stats), track_rx))
            .with_state(ServerState {
                registry: Arc::clone(&registry),
                stats: Arc::clone(&stats),
            });

        let listener = TcpListener::bind(bind_addr).await?;
        let bind = listener.local_addr()?;
        let host = match bind.ip() {
            std::net::IpAddr::V4(ip) => ip.to_string(),
            std::net::IpAddr::V6(ip) => format!("[{ip}]"),
        };
        let base_url = format!("http://{host}:{}", bind.port());

        tokio::spawn(async move {
            let server = axum::serve(listener, app).with_graceful_shutdown(async move {
                let _ = shutdown_rx.wait_for(|&stop| stop).await;
            });
            let _ = server.await;
        });

        Ok(Self {
            registry,
            stats,
            bind,
            base_url,
            shutdown: shutdown_tx,
        })
    }

    /// Live counters and smoothed rates for the UI.
    pub fn stats(&self) -> &Arc<ServerStats> {
        &self.stats
    }

    /// Register a file; returns its id.
    pub fn register(&self, path: PathBuf, name: Option<String>) -> PartId {
        let display = name.unwrap_or_else(|| {
            path.file_name()
                .map(|n| n.to_string_lossy().into_owned())
                .unwrap_or_else(|| "package.pkg".into())
        });
        let mut reg = self.registry.lock().unwrap();
        reg.register(path, display)
    }

    /// URL for a registered part, percent-encoded for safe transport
    /// (spaces/special characters break console installs — see
    /// `docs/RESEARCH.md` §2.3).
    pub fn url_for(&self, id: PartId) -> Option<String> {
        let reg = self.registry.lock().unwrap();
        let (_, name) = reg.entries.get(&id)?.clone();
        let encoded = percent_encoding::utf8_percent_encode(&name, SEGMENT).to_string();
        Some(format!("{}/pkg/{id}/{encoded}", self.base_url))
    }

    /// Remove a part from the allowlist. In-flight downloads finish; new ones 404.
    pub fn unregister(&self, id: PartId) {
        let mut reg = self.registry.lock().unwrap();
        reg.entries.remove(&id);
    }

    /// Signal graceful shutdown; in-flight requests get a grace period.
    pub fn shutdown(&self) {
        let _ = self.shutdown.send(true);
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Handler
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Middleware: approximate the request's wire size (request line + headers)
/// and count it as incoming traffic.
async fn track_rx(State(stats): State<Arc<ServerStats>>, req: Request, next: Next) -> Response {
    stats.add_request();
    // "METHOD /uri HTTP/1.1\r\n" + "Name: value\r\n" per header + final CRLF.
    let mut size = req.method().as_str().len() + req.uri().to_string().len() + 13;
    for (name, value) in req.headers() {
        size += name.as_str().len() + value.as_bytes().len() + 4;
    }
    stats.add_rx(size as u64);
    next.run(req).await
}

async fn serve_with_name(
    state: State<ServerState>,
    AxumPath((id, _name)): AxumPath<(String, String)>,
    headers: HeaderMap,
    method: Method,
) -> Response {
    serve_inner(state, id, headers, method).await
}

async fn serve(
    state: State<ServerState>,
    AxumPath(id): AxumPath<String>,
    headers: HeaderMap,
    method: Method,
) -> Response {
    serve_inner(state, id, headers, method).await
}

async fn serve_inner(
    State(ServerState { registry, stats }): State<ServerState>,
    id_str: String,
    headers: HeaderMap,
    method: Method,
) -> Response {
    // The file is resolved exclusively through the opaque id — the name
    // segment is decorative and never interpreted as a path.
    let Ok(id) = id_str.parse::<PartId>() else {
        return not_found();
    };
    let path = {
        let reg = registry.lock().unwrap();
        match reg.entries.get(&id) {
            Some((path, _)) => path.clone(),
            None => return not_found(),
        }
    };

    // Never trust the client's path: we only serve the registered file.
    let Ok(mut file) = tokio::fs::File::open(&path).await else {
        return not_found();
    };
    let size = match file.metadata().await {
        Ok(m) => m.len(),
        Err(_) => return not_found(),
    };

    let range_header = headers
        .get(header::RANGE)
        .and_then(|v| v.to_str().ok())
        .map(|s| s.to_string());

    let (status, offset, length, content_range) = match range_header
        .as_deref()
        .and_then(|r| r.strip_prefix("bytes="))
    {
        Some(spec) if !spec.contains(',') => match parse_range(spec, size) {
            Some((start, len)) => {
                let cr = if len > 0 {
                    format!("bytes {start}-{}/{}", start + len - 1, size)
                } else {
                    format!("bytes {start}-{start}/{size}")
                };
                (StatusCode::PARTIAL_CONTENT, start, len, Some(cr))
            }
            None => {
                return Response::builder()
                    .status(StatusCode::RANGE_NOT_SATISFIABLE)
                    .header(header::CONTENT_RANGE, format!("bytes */{size}"))
                    .body(Body::empty())
                    .unwrap();
            }
        },
        // Multi-range or no range → full body (200), permitted by RFC 7233.
        _ => (StatusCode::OK, 0u64, size, None),
    };

    if method == Method::HEAD {
        let mut builder = Response::builder()
            .status(status)
            .header(header::CONTENT_TYPE, "application/octet-stream")
            .header(header::CONTENT_LENGTH, length)
            .header(header::ACCEPT_RANGES, "bytes");
        if let Some(cr) = content_range {
            builder = builder.header(header::CONTENT_RANGE, cr);
        }
        return builder.body(Body::empty()).unwrap();
    }

    if offset > 0 && file.seek(SeekFrom::Start(offset)).await.is_err() {
        return not_found();
    }
    // Count the promised body bytes as outgoing traffic (see ServerStats doc
    // for why approximation-by-Content-Length is deliberate).
    stats.add_tx(length);
    // Stream exactly `length` bytes in bounded chunks. 256 KiB keeps
    // per-chunk syscall/wakeup overhead low on gigabit LANs while bounding
    // memory per connection (the console opens only a handful).
    let stream = ReaderStream::with_capacity(file.take(length), 256 * 1024);
    let body = Body::from_stream(stream);

    let mut builder = Response::builder()
        .status(status)
        .header(header::CONTENT_TYPE, "application/octet-stream")
        .header(header::CONTENT_LENGTH, length)
        .header(header::ACCEPT_RANGES, "bytes");
    if let Some(cr) = content_range {
        builder = builder.header(header::CONTENT_RANGE, cr);
    }
    builder.body(body).unwrap()
}

fn not_found() -> Response {
    (StatusCode::NOT_FOUND, "not registered").into_response()
}

/// Parse a single-range spec (`start-end`, `start-`, `-suffix`).
fn parse_range(spec: &str, size: u64) -> Option<(u64, u64)> {
    let spec = spec.trim();
    let (start_s, end_s) = spec.split_once('-')?;
    if start_s.is_empty() {
        // suffix: last N bytes
        let n: u64 = end_s.trim().parse().ok()?;
        if n == 0 || size == 0 {
            return None;
        }
        let n = n.min(size);
        return Some((size - n, n));
    }
    let start: u64 = start_s.trim().parse().ok()?;
    if start >= size {
        return None;
    }
    if end_s.trim().is_empty() {
        return Some((start, size - start));
    }
    let end: u64 = end_s.trim().parse().ok()?;
    if end < start {
        return None;
    }
    let end = end.min(size - 1);
    Some((start, end - start + 1))
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Self-check ("Check File Server" button)
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Outcome of a loopback probe against the local file server.
///
/// The probe deliberately talks to `127.0.0.1` — it answers "is *our* server
/// up and speaking HTTP?", which is exactly the split users need: if this
/// succeeds but the console still cannot download, the problem is between
/// the two machines (firewall, subnet, AP isolation), not the app.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum ServerCheck {
    /// The server answered; the status code it returned (404 for the probe
    /// URL, since it deliberately asks for an unregistered id).
    Responded(u16),
    /// Nothing is listening on the port — the server is not running (it
    /// starts together with a console connection).
    NotRunning,
    /// The port accepted the connection but never answered within the
    /// timeout — typically a firewall/antivirus intercepting loopback.
    NoAnswer,
    /// Anything else (unexpected I/O trouble).
    Error(String),
}

/// Probe the local file server on `bind` (its real bound address), `HEAD`-ing
/// an unregistered URL. Sync + std-only on purpose: the UI calls it from a
/// plain background thread (same pattern as the update checker).
///
/// The server normally binds a *specific* interface IP (the one that can
/// reach the console) — probing loopback there would be refused by design,
/// so the probe targets the bound address itself. A server bound to all
/// interfaces (`0.0.0.0`) is probed via loopback, the one address that is
/// always local.
pub fn self_check(bind: SocketAddr, timeout: Duration) -> ServerCheck {
    use std::io::{Read, Write};
    use std::net::TcpStream;

    let addr = if bind.ip().is_unspecified() {
        SocketAddr::new(
            std::net::IpAddr::V4(std::net::Ipv4Addr::LOCALHOST),
            bind.port(),
        )
    } else {
        bind
    };
    let mut stream = match TcpStream::connect_timeout(&addr, timeout) {
        Ok(s) => s,
        // Refused/reset = nothing (usable) is listening there.
        Err(e) if matches!(e.kind(), std::io::ErrorKind::ConnectionRefused) => {
            return ServerCheck::NotRunning;
        }
        // connect_timeout signals expiry as WouldBlock/TimedOut depending on
        // the platform.
        Err(e)
            if matches!(
                e.kind(),
                std::io::ErrorKind::WouldBlock | std::io::ErrorKind::TimedOut
            ) =>
        {
            return ServerCheck::NoAnswer;
        }
        Err(e) => return ServerCheck::Error(e.to_string()),
    };
    let req =
        format!("HEAD /pkg/0/server-check HTTP/1.1\r\nHost: {addr}\r\nConnection: close\r\n\r\n");
    if let Err(e) = stream.write_all(req.as_bytes()) {
        return ServerCheck::Error(e.to_string());
    }
    let _ = stream.set_read_timeout(Some(timeout));
    let mut buf = [0u8; 128];
    let n = match stream.read(&mut buf) {
        Ok(n) => n,
        Err(e)
            if matches!(
                e.kind(),
                std::io::ErrorKind::WouldBlock | std::io::ErrorKind::TimedOut
            ) =>
        {
            return ServerCheck::NoAnswer;
        }
        Err(e) => return ServerCheck::Error(e.to_string()),
    };
    if n == 0 {
        // Peer closed without answering.
        return ServerCheck::NoAnswer;
    }
    let status = std::str::from_utf8(&buf[..n])
        .ok()
        .and_then(|head| head.split_whitespace().nth(1))
        .and_then(|code| code.parse::<u16>().ok())
        .unwrap_or(0);
    ServerCheck::Responded(status)
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn registering_the_same_file_reuses_its_id() {
        // v0.0.5 regression: every re-registration used to allocate a fresh
        // entry, so submits/retries slowly grew the registry and kept stale
        // URLs alive for the whole session.
        let server = FileServer::start(SocketAddr::from(([127, 0, 0, 1], 0)))
            .await
            .unwrap();
        let path = PathBuf::from("/does/not/need/to/exist.pkg");
        let a = server.register(path.clone(), Some("a.pkg".into()));
        let b = server.register(path.clone(), Some("a.pkg".into()));
        assert_eq!(a, b, "re-registration must return the existing id");
        // A different display name is a different registration.
        let c = server.register(path.clone(), Some("renamed.pkg".into()));
        assert_ne!(a, c);
        // After unregistering, the id is free again: the next registration
        // for the same file gets a fresh entry (old URLs 404).
        server.unregister(a);
        let d = server.register(path, Some("a.pkg".into()));
        assert_ne!(a, d);
        server.shutdown();
    }

    #[test]
    fn rates_track_deltas_and_decay() {
        let t0 = std::time::Instant::now();
        let mut state = RateState::default();
        // First sample just seeds the baseline (rates stay 0).
        assert_eq!(update_rates(&mut state, t0, 0, 0), (0.0, 0.0));
        // 1 s later, 1 MiB sent → EMA = 0.5 * 1_048_576 B/s.
        let (tx, _) = update_rates(&mut state, t0 + Duration::from_secs(1), 1_048_576, 0);
        assert!((tx - 524_288.0).abs() < 1.0, "tx = {tx}");
        // Another second at the same speed → EMA converges upward.
        let (tx, _) = update_rates(&mut state, t0 + Duration::from_secs(2), 2_097_152, 0);
        assert!(tx > 700_000.0 && tx < 1_100_000.0, "tx = {tx}");
        // Traffic stops → EMA halves each sample, decaying to ~0.
        update_rates(&mut state, t0 + Duration::from_secs(3), 2_097_152, 0);
        update_rates(&mut state, t0 + Duration::from_secs(4), 2_097_152, 0);
        let (tx, _) = update_rates(&mut state, t0 + Duration::from_secs(5), 2_097_152, 0);
        assert!(tx < 200_000.0, "tx should decay, got {tx}");
        // Sub-100 ms bursts never divide by a tiny dt.
        let (tx2, _) = update_rates(&mut state, t0 + Duration::from_millis(5040), 2_097_152, 0);
        assert_eq!(tx2, tx);
    }

    #[test]
    fn self_check_reports_a_dead_port_as_not_running() {
        // Grab a definitely-free port by binding and dropping a listener.
        let listener = std::net::TcpListener::bind("127.0.0.1:0").unwrap();
        let port = listener.local_addr().unwrap().port();
        drop(listener);
        // Windows sometimes drops the SYN to a just-released loopback port
        // instead of answering RST (same platform variance as commit 95ef0f4);
        // both outcomes agree on what the user cares about: nothing usable
        // answered there.
        match self_check(
            SocketAddr::from(([127, 0, 0, 1], port)),
            Duration::from_millis(750),
        ) {
            ServerCheck::NotRunning | ServerCheck::NoAnswer => {}
            other => panic!("expected NotRunning/NoAnswer, got {other:?}"),
        }
    }

    #[tokio::test(flavor = "multi_thread", worker_threads = 2)]
    async fn self_check_against_the_real_server_and_stats_count_traffic() {
        let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        let payload = vec![0xA5u8; 64 * 1024];
        let file = dir.path().join("probe.pkg");
        std::fs::write(&file, &payload).unwrap();

        let server = FileServer::start(SocketAddr::from(([127, 0, 0, 1], 0)))
            .await
            .unwrap();

        // The probe URL is unregistered → 404, but that still proves the
        // server is alive and speaking HTTP. (Probed before the register
        // below so the id stays deterministic; in-app, any status answer —
        // even 200 for a real id — means "alive".)
        assert_eq!(
            self_check(server.bind, Duration::from_millis(750)),
            ServerCheck::Responded(404)
        );

        let id = server.register(file, Some("probe.pkg".into()));

        // A real ranged download must land in the stats (tx exact, rx > 0).
        let url = server.url_for(id).unwrap();
        let client = reqwest::Client::new();
        let res = client
            .get(&url)
            .header("Range", "bytes=1024-2047")
            .send()
            .await
            .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(res.status().as_u16(), 206);
        let bytes = res.bytes().await.unwrap();
        assert_eq!(bytes.len(), 1024);
        // The server keeps running until polled; give the counters a beat.
        tokio::time::sleep(Duration::from_millis(50)).await;
        let (tx, rx, requests) = server.stats().totals();
        assert_eq!(tx, 1024, "tx must count exactly the range length");
        assert!(rx > 0, "request bytes must be counted");
        assert!(requests >= 2, "probe + download = at least 2 requests");

        server.shutdown();
    }
}

```

`crates/rpi-ng-core/src/lib.rs`:

```rs
//! Remote Package Installer NG — core library.
//!
//! Contains everything except the desktop UI: the RPI protocol client, PKG/SFO
//! parsing, the local file server, LAN discovery, the persistent queue and the
//! orchestration engine. See `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` for the big picture.
//!
//! Library users drive everything through [`engine::spawn`], which returns an
//! [`engine::EngineHandle`] (commands in, events out). All other modules are
//! also usable standalone (the mock-backend tests do exactly that).

pub mod discovery;
pub mod engine;
pub mod fileserver;
pub mod model;
pub mod pkg;
pub mod queue;
pub mod rpi;
pub mod settings;
pub mod sfo;
pub mod update;

pub use engine::{Command, EngineHandle, spawn};
pub use model::Event;
pub use settings::Settings;

/// Crate version reported in logs and the UI.
pub const VERSION: &str = env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION");

```

`crates/rpi-ng-core/src/model.rs`:

```rs
//! Core domain types shared by the engine, backends, UI and persistence layer.

use std::{
    fmt,
    net::SocketAddr,
    path::PathBuf,
    time::{SystemTime, UNIX_EPOCH},
};

use crate::settings::Settings;

/// Task id as assigned by the console backend (BGFT task id in the RPI dialect).
pub type TaskId = i64;

/// Opaque id of a queue part (file) registered with the local file server.
pub type PartId = u64;

/// Opaque id of a queue item (a package group).
pub type ItemId = u64;

pub fn unix_ms() -> u64 {
    SystemTime::now()
        .duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH)
        .map(|d| d.as_millis() as u64)
        .unwrap_or(0)
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Console side
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Which console generation a backend serves. Never inferred from an IP or port;
/// only from verified backend behavior or explicit user choice.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, serde::Serialize, serde::Deserialize)]
pub enum ConsoleGeneration {
    Ps4,
    Ps5,
}

impl fmt::Display for ConsoleGeneration {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        match self {
            Self::Ps4 => write!(f, "PS4"),
            Self::Ps5 => write!(f, "PS5"),
        }
    }
}

/// The backend dialect detected on a console.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, serde::Serialize, serde::Deserialize, Default)]
pub enum BackendKind {
    /// flatz Remote Package Installer dialect (JSON POST API on port 12800).
    Rpi,
    /// A fork/variant that answered the RPI probes but differs in unknown ways.
    RpiVariant,
    /// Something answered on the port, but it did not speak the RPI dialect.
    #[default]
    Unknown,
}

impl fmt::Display for BackendKind {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        match self {
            Self::Rpi => write!(f, "Remote Package Installer"),
            Self::RpiVariant => write!(f, "RPI variant"),
            Self::Unknown => write!(f, "Unknown"),
        }
    }
}

/// Operations a backend has been *verified* to support. Capabilities are only set
/// after live probing; a UI must disable unsupported operations instead of
/// simulating them.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, serde::Serialize, serde::Deserialize)]
pub struct BackendCaps {
    /// `POST /api/install` answered a well-formed request (probed implicitly on
    /// connect via `is_exists`, never by a fake install).
    pub install_direct: bool,
    /// Progress reporting is available.
    pub progress: bool,
    /// pause/resume endpoints available (assumed true for Rpi until disproven).
    pub pause_resume: bool,
    /// `GET /api/status` answers JSON (newer receivers only).
    pub status_endpoint: bool,
    /// `POST /api/find_task` available for reconciliation.
    pub find_task: bool,
    /// `GET /api/v2/system` answered with an identity — the v2 job API is
    /// available (install, jobs list, per-job lifecycle ops).
    pub api_v2: bool,
}

impl Default for BackendCaps {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self {
            install_direct: false,
            progress: true,
            pause_resume: true,
            status_endpoint: false,
            find_task: true,
            api_v2: false,
        }
    }
}

/// Connection status of the selected console.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, serde::Serialize, serde::Deserialize, Default)]
pub enum ConsoleStatus {
    /// Not connected; no console selected.
    #[default]
    None,
    /// Probe in progress.
    Probing,
    /// Connected and verified.
    Connected,
    /// The endpoint answered but is not a compatible backend (reason string).
    Incompatible(String),
    /// Was connected; the endpoint stopped answering.
    Lost,
}

/// Identity of a v2-capable backend, as reported by `GET /api/v2/system`.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, serde::Serialize, serde::Deserialize)]
pub struct BackendIdentity {
    pub name: String,
    pub version: String,
}

impl fmt::Display for BackendIdentity {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        if self.version.is_empty() {
            write!(f, "{}", self.name)
        } else {
            write!(f, "{} {}", self.name, self.version)
        }
    }
}

/// The console the app talks to.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, serde::Serialize, serde::Deserialize)]
pub struct ConsoleTarget {
    pub addr: SocketAddr,
    pub generation: ConsoleGeneration,
    pub kind: BackendKind,
    pub caps: BackendCaps,
    /// Best-effort friendly label (e.g. remembered alias).
    pub label: Option<String>,
    /// v2 backend identity (name + version) when the backend provides one.
    #[serde(default)]
    pub backend: Option<BackendIdentity>,
}

/// A discovery result before it is promoted to a [`ConsoleTarget`].
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct ConsoleCandidate {
    pub addr: SocketAddr,
    pub kind: BackendKind,
    /// How the candidate was found.
    pub source: DiscoverySource,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum DiscoverySource {
    SubnetProbe,
    Remembered,
    Manual,
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Package side
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Content type byte from the PKG header (offset 0x74).
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, serde::Serialize, serde::Deserialize)]
pub enum ContentKind {
    /// 0x1A — game data (app / patch / remaster).
    Gd,
    /// 0x1B — additional content data (AC data, themes).
    Ac,
    /// 0x1C — additional content without data.
    Al,
    /// 0x1E — delta patch.
    Dp,
    Unknown(u32),
}

impl ContentKind {
    pub fn from_raw(raw: u32) -> Self {
        match raw {
            0x1A => Self::Gd,
            0x1B => Self::Ac,
            0x1C => Self::Al,
            0x1E => Self::Dp,
            other => Self::Unknown(other),
        }
    }

    pub fn package_type(&self) -> Option<&'static str> {
        match self {
            Self::Gd => Some("PS4GD"),
            Self::Ac => Some("PS4AC"),
            Self::Al => Some("PS4AL"),
            Self::Dp => Some("PS4DP"),
            Self::Unknown(_) => None,
        }
    }

    pub fn label(&self) -> String {
        match self {
            Self::Gd => "Game".into(),
            Self::Ac => "Add-on / Theme".into(),
            Self::Al => "Add-on (no data)".into(),
            Self::Dp => "Delta patch".into(),
            Self::Unknown(v) => format!("Unknown (0x{v:X})"),
        }
    }
}

/// Metadata parsed from a PKG file header (and its PARAM.SFO when present).
/// See `docs/BASELINE.md` for the on-disk layout this mirrors.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, serde::Serialize, serde::Deserialize)]
pub struct PkgInfo {
    pub content_id: String,
    pub title_id: Option<String>,
    pub content_kind: ContentKind,
    pub is_patch: bool,
    /// Game title from PARAM.SFO (localized when available).
    pub title: Option<String>,
    pub app_version: Option<String>,
    pub package_version: Option<String>,
    pub category: Option<String>,
    /// `package_size` field from the header (offset 0x430).
    pub header_size: u64,
}

impl PkgInfo {
    /// Derive the title id from a content id like
    /// `UP0001-CUSA03041_00-REDEMPTION000002` (second dash-delimited field,
    /// truncated at the `_` suffix; 9 characters by format).
    pub fn title_id_from_content_id(content_id: &str) -> Option<String> {
        let mut parts = content_id.split('-');
        let _service = parts.next()?;
        let title = parts.next()?;
        let title = title.split('_').next().unwrap_or(title);
        if title.is_empty() {
            return None;
        }
        Some(title.to_string())
    }
}

/// One physical `.pkg` file on disk.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, serde::Serialize, serde::Deserialize)]
pub struct PartFile {
    pub id: PartId,
    pub path: PathBuf,
    /// Display file name (file stem).
    pub file_name: String,
    /// Actual size on disk in bytes.
    pub size: u64,
    /// Index within a multipart group (0-based) when detected.
    pub part_index: Option<u32>,
    /// Parsed metadata; `None` until parsed or if parsing failed.
    pub pkg: Option<PkgInfo>,
    /// Set when the file failed validation (unreadable, bad magic, ...).
    pub invalid_reason: Option<String>,
}

impl PartFile {
    pub fn display_title(&self) -> String {
        if let Some(pkg) = &self.pkg
            && let Some(t) = &pkg.title
        {
            return t.clone();
        }
        self.file_name.clone()
    }

    pub fn title_id(&self) -> Option<String> {
        self.pkg.as_ref().and_then(|p| p.title_id.clone())
    }
}

/// A package group: one or more parts installed as a single console task.
/// Multipart pieces must be in consecutive order.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, serde::Serialize, serde::Deserialize)]
pub struct QueueItem {
    pub id: ItemId,
    pub parts: Vec<PartFile>,
    pub state: ItemState,
    /// Human-readable error detail for failed/invalid states.
    pub error: Option<String>,
    /// Console-assigned task id once an install request succeeded.
    pub task_id: Option<TaskId>,
    /// Console the `task_id`/`job_id` were learned from — they are only
    /// meaningful *on that console* (ids are console-scoped and collide
    /// across machines), so remote ops must not be aimed at a different one.
    /// `None` = pre-field queue file or unknown origin (legacy permissive).
    #[serde(default)]
    pub task_owner: Option<SocketAddr>,
    /// Console-assigned v2 job id (NextGen-class backends); used for the
    /// per-job lifecycle endpoints instead of the task id.
    #[serde(default)]
    pub job_id: Option<i64>,
    /// Title echoed back by the console on install (when provided).
    pub console_title: Option<String>,
    pub progress: Option<ProgressView>,
    pub attempts: u32,
    pub added_at: u64,
    pub updated_at: u64,
}

impl QueueItem {
    pub fn total_size(&self) -> u64 {
        self.parts.iter().map(|p| p.size).sum()
    }

    pub fn is_multipart(&self) -> bool {
        self.parts.len() > 1
    }

    pub fn primary_part(&self) -> Option<&PartFile> {
        self.parts.first()
    }

    pub fn content_id(&self) -> Option<&str> {
        self.primary_part()
            .and_then(|p| p.pkg.as_ref())
            .map(|p| p.content_id.as_str())
    }

    /// A stable grouping key for duplicate detection: the parsed content id when
    /// available, otherwise the canonical path.
    pub fn dedup_key(&self) -> String {
        if let Some(cid) = self.content_id() {
            format!("cid:{cid}")
        } else if let Some(first) = self.parts.first() {
            format!("path:{}", first.path.display())
        } else {
            format!("empty:{}", self.id)
        }
    }
}

/// Explicit queue item state machine. Transitions are driven *only* by observed
/// evidence (HTTP results, progress counters, user commands) — never by
/// assumptions. See `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §4.4.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, serde::Serialize, serde::Deserialize)]
pub enum ItemState {
    /// Added, validated or awaiting validation; not started.
    Queued,
    /// Files being (re)validated / prepared.
    Preparing,
    /// Failed local validation (bad file, unsupported package) — not retryable
    /// without user action.
    Invalid,
    /// Install request in flight.
    Submitting,
    /// Console is downloading (progress observed, not finished).
    Transferring,
    /// Download finished; console reports the local copy phase.
    Installing,
    /// Paused by the user (backend confirmed when possible).
    Paused,
    /// Completed with evidence (full transfer + no error + copy phase done).
    Completed,
    /// Failed after retries / permanent error.
    Failed,
    /// Cancelled by the user.
    Cancelled,
    /// Outcome unknown (app restart, lost connection). Never auto-resubmitted.
    Unknown,
}

impl ItemState {
    pub fn label(&self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            Self::Queued => "Queued",
            Self::Preparing => "Preparing",
            Self::Invalid => "Invalid package",
            Self::Submitting => "Submitting",
            Self::Transferring => "Transferring",
            Self::Installing => "Installing",
            Self::Paused => "Paused",
            Self::Completed => "Completed",
            Self::Failed => "Failed",
            Self::Cancelled => "Cancelled",
            Self::Unknown => "Unknown",
        }
    }

    pub fn is_active(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(
            self,
            Self::Preparing | Self::Submitting | Self::Transferring | Self::Installing
        )
    }

    pub fn is_finished(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(
            self,
            Self::Completed | Self::Failed | Self::Cancelled | Self::Invalid
        )
    }
}

/// Progress as reported by the backend, plus client-side derivation (speed).
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Default, serde::Serialize, serde::Deserialize)]
pub struct ProgressView {
    /// 0.0..=100.0 overall transfer percent (transferred_total/length_total).
    pub percent: f32,
    pub transferred: u64,
    pub total: u64,
    /// Client-side computed throughput in bytes/s (EMA over poll deltas).
    pub speed_bps: u64,
    /// Backend-provided ETA seconds (total) when available.
    pub eta_sec: Option<u64>,
    /// Current multipart piece index (0-based) and count.
    pub piece_index: u32,
    pub piece_total: u32,
    /// Preparation phase percent reported by the console (BGFT).
    pub preparing_percent: i32,
    /// Post-download local copy percent reported by the console (BGFT).
    pub local_copy_percent: i32,
    /// Raw backend error code (non-zero means the task failed console-side).
    pub raw_error: i64,
}

/// A point-in-time view of the whole application state, sent to the UI.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]
pub struct Snapshot {
    pub items: Vec<QueueItem>,
    pub console: ConsoleInfo,
    pub server: Option<ServerInfo>,
    pub engine_busy: bool,
}

/// Console part of a [`Snapshot`].
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]
pub struct ConsoleInfo {
    pub status: ConsoleStatus,
    pub target: Option<ConsoleTarget>,
}

/// Local file server part of a [`Snapshot`].
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct ServerInfo {
    pub bind: SocketAddr,
    /// Base URL the console should use, e.g. `http://192.168.1.20:53742`.
    pub base_url: String,
    /// Smoothed outgoing rate (response body bytes/s) — the download the
    /// console sees. Sampled per poll tick / 250 ms ticker.
    pub tx_bps: f64,
    /// Smoothed incoming rate (request bytes/s) — tiny control traffic.
    pub rx_bps: f64,
    /// Total body bytes served since the server started.
    pub tx_total: u64,
    /// Total request bytes received since the server started.
    pub rx_total: u64,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum LogLevel {
    Info,
    Warn,
    Error,
}

impl fmt::Display for LogLevel {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        match self {
            Self::Info => write!(f, "INFO"),
            Self::Warn => write!(f, "WARN"),
            Self::Error => write!(f, "ERROR"),
        }
    }
}

/// Events emitted by the engine to the UI.
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub enum Event {
    /// Coalesced full-state snapshot (queue + console + server).
    Snapshot(Snapshot),
    /// A single log line for the activity panel.
    Log(LogLevel, String),
    /// Discovery finished (may be empty).
    DiscoveryDone(Vec<ConsoleCandidate>),
    /// Settings changed on the engine side — an echo of `SetSettings` or an
    /// engine-side update (e.g. the remembered-console list). The UI should
    /// refresh its cached copy.
    SettingsChanged(Settings),
}
```

`crates/rpi-ng-core/src/pkg.rs`:

```rs
//! PKG header and entry-table parsing.
//!
//! Mirrors the on-disk format the upstream payload itself parses
//! (`docs/BASELINE.md` §4.7): big-endian fields, header size 0x2000,
//! entry table of 0x20-byte entries located at `entry_table_offset`,
//! PARAM.SFO entry id 0x1000 and icon0.png entry id 0x1200.
//!
//! The parser is intentionally conservative: `entry_count` is capped, every
//! offset is bounds-checked against the real file size, and failures produce
//! an `Err` with a precise reason instead of a panic.

use std::{
    fs::File,
    io::{Read, Seek, SeekFrom},
    path::Path,
};

use crate::{
    model::{ContentKind, PkgInfo},
    sfo::{Sfo, SfoValue},
};

pub const PKG_MAGIC: [u8; 4] = [0x7F, b'P', b'K', b'G'];
pub const HEADER_SIZE: usize = 0x2000;
pub const ENTRY_SIZE: usize = 0x20;
pub const ENTRY_ID_PARAM_SFO: u32 = 0x1000;
pub const ENTRY_ID_ICON0_PNG: u32 = 0x1200;

pub const MAX_ENTRY_COUNT: u32 = 512;
pub const MAX_SFO_SIZE: u32 = 1024 * 1024;
pub const CONTENT_ID_LEN: usize = 0x24;

const CONTENT_FLAGS_FIRST_PATCH: u32 = 0x0010_0000;
const CONTENT_FLAGS_SUBSEQUENT_PATCH: u32 = 0x4000_0000;

#[derive(Debug, thiserror::Error)]
pub enum PkgError {
    #[error("I/O error: {0}")]
    Io(#[from] std::io::Error),
    #[error("file too small ({0} bytes, need at least {1})")]
    TooSmall(u64, u64),
    #[error("bad magic (not a PS4 PKG)")]
    BadMagic,
    #[error("entry count {0} exceeds limit {1}")]
    TooManyEntries(u32, u32),
    #[error("entry table offset 0x{0:X} out of range")]
    EntryTableOffset(u64),
    #[error("PARAM.SFO entry missing")]
    NoSfo,
    #[error("PARAM.SFO size {0} exceeds limit")]
    SfoTooLarge(u32),
    #[error("content id is not valid ASCII")]
    BadContentId,
    #[error("PARAM.SFO parse error: {0}")]
    Sfo(#[from] crate::sfo::SfoError),
}

fn be_u32(b: &[u8], off: usize) -> u32 {
    u32::from_be_bytes([b[off], b[off + 1], b[off + 2], b[off + 3]])
}

fn be_u64(b: &[u8], off: usize) -> u64 {
    let mut v = [0u8; 8];
    v.copy_from_slice(&b[off..off + 8]);
    u64::from_be_bytes(v)
}

/// Parse PKG metadata from an open file, seeking as needed (at most
/// header + entry table + SFO bytes are read; large files are never scanned).
pub fn parse_pkg(file: &mut File, file_size: u64) -> Result<PkgInfo, PkgError> {
    if file_size < HEADER_SIZE as u64 {
        return Err(PkgError::TooSmall(file_size, HEADER_SIZE as u64));
    }

    let mut header = vec![0u8; HEADER_SIZE];
    file.seek(SeekFrom::Start(0))?;
    file.read_exact(&mut header)?;
    if header[0..4] != PKG_MAGIC {
        return Err(PkgError::BadMagic);
    }

    let entry_count = be_u32(&header, 0x10);
    let entry_table_offset = be_u32(&header, 0x18) as u64;
    if entry_count == 0 {
        return Err(PkgError::NoSfo);
    }
    if entry_count > MAX_ENTRY_COUNT {
        return Err(PkgError::TooManyEntries(entry_count, MAX_ENTRY_COUNT));
    }
    let entry_table_size = entry_count as u64 * ENTRY_SIZE as u64;
    if entry_table_offset < HEADER_SIZE as u64
        || entry_table_offset.saturating_add(entry_table_size) > file_size
    {
        return Err(PkgError::EntryTableOffset(entry_table_offset));
    }

    // Entry table.
    let mut table = vec![0u8; entry_table_size as usize];
    file.seek(SeekFrom::Start(entry_table_offset))?;
    file.read_exact(&mut table)?;

    let mut sfo_off: Option<(u64, u32)> = None;
    for i in 0..entry_count as usize {
        let e = i * ENTRY_SIZE;
        let id = be_u32(&table, e);
        let offset = be_u32(&table, e + 0x10) as u64;
        let size = be_u32(&table, e + 0x14);
        if id == ENTRY_ID_PARAM_SFO {
            if size == 0 || size > MAX_SFO_SIZE {
                return Err(PkgError::SfoTooLarge(size));
            }
            if offset.saturating_add(size as u64) > file_size {
                return Err(PkgError::EntryTableOffset(offset));
            }
            sfo_off = Some((offset, size));
        }
    }
    let Some((sfo_offset, sfo_size)) = sfo_off else {
        return Err(PkgError::NoSfo);
    };

    // PARAM.SFO bytes.
    let mut sfo_buf = vec![0u8; sfo_size as usize];
    file.seek(SeekFrom::Start(sfo_offset))?;
    file.read_exact(&mut sfo_buf)?;
    let sfo = Sfo::parse(&sfo_buf)?;

    // Header fields.
    let content_id_raw: [u8; CONTENT_ID_LEN] =
        header[0x40..0x40 + CONTENT_ID_LEN].try_into().unwrap();
    let trim = content_id_raw
        .iter()
        .position(|&b| b == 0)
        .unwrap_or(CONTENT_ID_LEN);
    if !content_id_raw[..trim].is_ascii() {
        return Err(PkgError::BadContentId);
    }
    let content_id = String::from_utf8_lossy(&content_id_raw[..trim]).into_owned();

    let content_type = be_u32(&header, 0x74);
    let content_flags = be_u32(&header, 0x78);
    let header_size = be_u64(&header, 0x430);

    let is_patch = (content_flags & CONTENT_FLAGS_FIRST_PATCH) != 0
        || (content_flags & CONTENT_FLAGS_SUBSEQUENT_PATCH) != 0;

    let title_id = match sfo.get_str("CONTENT_ID") {
        Some(cid) => PkgInfo::title_id_from_content_id(cid)
            .or_else(|| PkgInfo::title_id_from_content_id(&content_id)),
        None => PkgInfo::title_id_from_content_id(&content_id),
    };

    let title = sfo.title(1); // English localization id, mirrors upstream default
    let app_version = sfo.get_str("APP_VER").map(|s| s.to_string());
    let package_version = sfo.get_str("VERSION").map(|s| s.to_string());
    let category = sfo.get_str("CATEGORY").map(|s| s.to_string());
    let _ = SfoValue::U32(0); // keep enum import used

    Ok(PkgInfo {
        content_id,
        title_id,
        content_kind: ContentKind::from_raw(content_type),
        is_patch,
        title,
        app_version,
        package_version,
        category,
        header_size,
    })
}

/// Convenience wrapper that opens the file and parses it.
pub fn parse_pkg_file(path: &Path) -> Result<PkgInfo, PkgError> {
    let mut file = File::open(path)?;
    let size = file.metadata()?.len();
    parse_pkg(&mut file, size)
}

/// Heuristic multipart detection from a file name: `Name_0.pkg`, `Name_1.pkg`, ...
/// Returns `(base, Some(index))` for pieces, `(full_stem, None)` otherwise.
pub fn split_part_name(file_name: &str) -> (String, Option<u32>) {
    let lower = file_name.to_ascii_lowercase();
    if !lower.ends_with(".pkg") {
        return (file_name.to_string(), None);
    }
    let stem = &file_name[..file_name.len() - 4];
    let Some(pos) = stem.rfind('_') else {
        return (stem.to_string(), None);
    };
    let tail = &stem[pos + 1..];
    if tail.is_empty() || !tail.chars().all(|c| c.is_ascii_digit()) {
        return (stem.to_string(), None);
    }
    let idx: u32 = tail.parse().unwrap_or(u32::MAX);
    if idx == u32::MAX {
        return (stem.to_string(), None);
    }
    (stem[..pos].to_string(), Some(idx))
}
```
