`crates/rpi-ng-core/src/queue.rs`:

```rs
//! Persistent queue: items, state transitions, duplicate detection and
//! restart recovery.
//!
//! Recovery rule (`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §4.4): items whose outcome cannot be
//! known after a restart are marked [`ItemState::Unknown`] and are **never**
//! auto-resubmitted — the engine reconciles against the console when one is
//! connected, and resubmission is always a deliberate user act.

use std::{
    collections::HashSet,
    path::{Path, PathBuf},
};

use crate::{
    model::{ItemId, ItemState, PartFile, PartId, QueueItem, unix_ms},
    pkg,
    settings::atomic_write,
};

const QUEUE_FILE: &str = "queue.json";
const QUEUE_BAK: &str = "queue.json.bak";

#[derive(Debug, thiserror::Error)]
pub enum QueueError {
    #[error("I/O error: {0}")]
    Io(#[from] std::io::Error),
    #[error("serialization error: {0}")]
    Serde(#[from] serde_json::Error),
}

/// Structural repairs applied while loading a queue document that was
/// hand-edited or corrupted. Empty for every file the app itself writes —
/// the app never emits duplicate ids, regressed counters or part-less items.
#[derive(Debug, Default, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct LoadRepairs {
    /// Items dropped because a later entry reused an existing id. Every
    /// id lookup (`get`/`remove`/`move_item`, engine state updates, UI row
    /// buttons) resolves to the FIRST match, so a duplicate id made the
    /// later item unreachable — its buttons and progress silently acted on
    /// the wrong row.
    pub dropped_duplicate_ids: Vec<ItemId>,
    /// `next_item` was at or below an existing item id: the next add would
    /// have handed out a colliding id. Raised to max id + 1.
    pub next_item_repaired: bool,
    /// `next_part` was at or below an existing part id. Raised to max id + 1.
    pub next_part_repaired: bool,
    /// Items with no files at all: kept visible but marked Invalid instead
    /// of letting the engine submit an install with zero URLs.
    pub invalidated_empty_items: Vec<ItemId>,
}

impl LoadRepairs {
    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
        *self == Self::default()
    }

    /// One human-readable log line per repair (shown in the activity panel).
    pub fn warnings(&self) -> Vec<String> {
        let mut out = Vec::new();
        if !self.dropped_duplicate_ids.is_empty() {
            out.push(format!(
                "queue.json contained duplicate item ids {} (kept the first entry of each; \
                 hand-edited entries can collide and make rows unmanageable)",
                self.dropped_duplicate_ids
                    .iter()
                    .map(|id| id.to_string())
                    .collect::<Vec<_>>()
                    .join(", ")
            ));
        }
        if self.next_item_repaired {
            out.push(
                "queue.json next_item was behind an existing id; raised past the highest id \
                 so new entries cannot collide"
                    .into(),
            );
        }
        if self.next_part_repaired {
            out.push(
                "queue.json next_part was behind an existing part id; raised past the highest id"
                    .into(),
            );
        }
        if !self.invalidated_empty_items.is_empty() {
            out.push(format!(
                "queue.json items {} had no files (marked invalid); remove them from the queue",
                self.invalidated_empty_items
                    .iter()
                    .map(|id| id.to_string())
                    .collect::<Vec<_>>()
                    .join(", ")
            ));
        }
        out
    }
}

/// The queue, persisted as JSON after every transition (atomic writes).
#[derive(Debug, Default)]
pub struct Queue {
    items: Vec<QueueItem>,
    next_item: ItemId,
    next_part: PartId,
}

impl Queue {
    pub fn load() -> Self {
        Self::load_in(crate::settings::data_dir().as_deref())
    }

    /// Load from `dir` (or the global data dir when `None`), discarding the
    /// repair report (see [`Queue::load_in_reported`]).
    pub fn load_in(dir: Option<&std::path::Path>) -> Self {
        Self::load_in_reported(dir).0
    }

    /// Load from `dir` (or the global data dir when `None`), reporting the
    /// structural repairs applied to a hand-edited or corrupted document.
    pub fn load_in_reported(dir: Option<&std::path::Path>) -> (Self, LoadRepairs) {
        let Some(dir) = dir else {
            return (Self::default(), LoadRepairs::default());
        };
        for candidate in [dir.join(QUEUE_FILE), dir.join(QUEUE_BAK)] {
            if let Ok(text) = std::fs::read_to_string(&candidate)
                && let Ok(doc) = serde_json::from_str::<QueueDoc>(&text)
            {
                return Self::from_doc(doc);
            }
        }
        (Self::default(), LoadRepairs::default())
    }

    /// Load from a JSON document (test/inspection entry point; applies the
    /// same recovery mapping as [`Queue::load`]).
    pub fn from_json(text: &str) -> Result<Self, QueueError> {
        Ok(Self::from_json_reported(text)?.0)
    }

    /// Load from a JSON document, reporting structural repairs (test entry
    /// point mirroring [`Queue::load_in_reported`]).
    pub fn from_json_reported(text: &str) -> Result<(Self, LoadRepairs), QueueError> {
        let doc = serde_json::from_str::<QueueDoc>(text)?;
        Ok(Self::from_doc(doc))
    }

    fn from_doc(doc: QueueDoc) -> (Self, LoadRepairs) {
        let mut repairs = LoadRepairs::default();
        let mut q = Queue {
            items: doc.items,
            next_item: doc.next_item,
            next_part: doc.next_part,
        };

        // Hand-edited documents may repeat an item id. Every lookup resolves
        // to the first match, so later duplicates are unreachable zombies —
        // drop them and keep the first entry (the one all live references
        // already point at).
        let mut seen: HashSet<ItemId> = HashSet::new();
        let mut kept = Vec::with_capacity(q.items.len());
        for item in q.items.drain(..) {
            if !seen.insert(item.id) {
                repairs.dropped_duplicate_ids.push(item.id);
                continue;
            }
            kept.push(item);
        }
        q.items = kept;

        // Counters must sit past every existing id, or the next add hands out
        // a colliding id. Saturating: a hand-entered u64::MAX must not panic.
        let max_item = q.items.iter().map(|i| i.id).max();
        let want_item = max_item.map_or(q.next_item, |m| q.next_item.max(m.saturating_add(1)));
        if want_item != q.next_item {
            q.next_item = want_item;
            repairs.next_item_repaired = true;
        }
        let max_part = q
            .items
            .iter()
            .flat_map(|i| i.parts.iter().map(|p| p.id))
            .max();
        let want_part = max_part.map_or(q.next_part, |m| q.next_part.max(m.saturating_add(1)));
        if want_part != q.next_part {
            q.next_part = want_part;
            repairs.next_part_repaired = true;
        }

        // Recovery: unknown-outcome states become Unknown — never auto-resumed.
        for item in &mut q.items {
            match item.state {
                ItemState::Transferring
                | ItemState::Installing
                | ItemState::Submitting
                | ItemState::Preparing => {
                    item.state = ItemState::Unknown;
                    item.updated_at = unix_ms();
                }
                _ => {}
            }
            // A part-less item (hand edit / corruption) would otherwise be
            // submitted with zero files. Keep it visible and explain why.
            if item.parts.is_empty() && item.state != ItemState::Invalid {
                repairs.invalidated_empty_items.push(item.id);
                item.state = ItemState::Invalid;
                item.error = Some("no files in this entry (hand-edited queue.json?)".into());
                item.updated_at = unix_ms();
            }
        }
        (q, repairs)
    }

    fn to_doc(&self) -> QueueDoc {
        QueueDoc {
            next_item: self.next_item,
            next_part: self.next_part,
            items: self.items.clone(),
        }
    }

    pub fn save(&self) -> Result<(), QueueError> {
        self.save_in(crate::settings::data_dir().as_deref())
    }

    pub fn save_in(&self, dir: Option<&std::path::Path>) -> Result<(), QueueError> {
        let Some(dir) = dir else {
            return Ok(());
        };
        std::fs::create_dir_all(dir)?;
        let path = dir.join(QUEUE_FILE);
        if path.exists() {
            let _ = std::fs::copy(&path, dir.join(QUEUE_BAK));
        }
        let text = serde_json::to_string_pretty(&self.to_doc())?;
        let tmp = dir.join("queue.json.tmp");
        atomic_write(&tmp, &path, text.as_bytes())?;
        Ok(())
    }

    pub fn items(&self) -> &[QueueItem] {
        &self.items
    }

    pub fn items_mut(&mut self) -> &mut [QueueItem] {
        &mut self.items
    }

    pub fn get_mut(&mut self, id: ItemId) -> Option<&mut QueueItem> {
        self.items.iter_mut().find(|i| i.id == id)
    }

    pub fn get(&self, id: ItemId) -> Option<&QueueItem> {
        self.items.iter().find(|i| i.id == id)
    }

    /// Add file paths (already scanned, sizes known via metadata). Files are
    /// parsed lazily by the engine; here they become parts/items with
    /// multipart grouping and duplicate detection.
    ///
    /// Returns the ids of newly added items (duplicates and unreadable files
    /// are skipped with reasons returned for logging).
    pub fn add_paths(&mut self, paths: Vec<PathBuf>) -> (Vec<ItemId>, Vec<String>) {
        let mut added = Vec::new();
        let mut skipped = Vec::new();

        // Existence + size pass.
        let mut files: Vec<(PathBuf, u64)> = Vec::new();
        for path in paths {
            match std::fs::metadata(&path) {
                Ok(m) if m.is_file() => files.push((path, m.len())),
                Ok(_) => skipped.push(format!("not a file: {}", path.display())),
                Err(e) => skipped.push(format!("unreadable: {} ({e})", path.display())),
            }
        }

        // Multipart grouping by (base name, directory).
        #[derive(Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, Clone, Ord, PartialOrd)]
        struct GroupKey {
            base: String,
            dir: PathBuf,
        }
        let mut groups: std::collections::BTreeMap<GroupKey, Vec<(PathBuf, u64, Option<u32>)>> =
            std::collections::BTreeMap::new();
        for (path, size) in files {
            let file_name = path
                .file_name()
                .map(|n| n.to_string_lossy().into_owned())
                .unwrap_or_default();
            let (base, part_index) = pkg::split_part_name(&file_name);
            let dir = path.parent().unwrap_or_else(|| Path::new("")).to_path_buf();
            groups
                .entry(GroupKey { base, dir })
                .or_default()
                .push((path, size, part_index));
        }

        // Existing keys for dedup.
        let existing: HashSet<String> = self.items.iter().map(|i| i.dedup_key()).collect();
        let mut keys_of_new: Vec<String> = Vec::new();

        for (_key, mut group) in groups {
            // Sort pieces by index (None first) then by name for stable order.
            group.sort_by(|a, b| a.2.cmp(&b.2).then_with(|| a.0.cmp(&b.0)));

            let parts: Vec<PartFile> = group
                .into_iter()
                .map(|(path, size, part_index)| {
                    let file_name = path
                        .file_name()
                        .map(|n| n.to_string_lossy().into_owned())
                        .unwrap_or_else(|| "package.pkg".into());
                    PartFile {
                        id: self.next_part,
                        path,
                        file_name,
                        size,
                        part_index,
                        pkg: None,
                        invalid_reason: None,
                    }
                })
                .collect();
            self.next_part += parts.len() as PartId;

            let now = unix_ms();
            let item = QueueItem {
                id: self.next_item,
                parts,
                state: ItemState::Queued,
                error: None,
                task_id: None,
                task_owner: None,
                job_id: None,
                console_title: None,
                progress: None,
                attempts: 0,
                added_at: now,
                updated_at: now,
            };
            self.next_item += 1;

            // Dedup: skip if an equivalent key already exists (in queue or in
            // this batch). Content ids are not parsed yet at add time, so the
            // pre-parse key is the first part's canonical path; the engine
            // re-checks with content ids after parsing.
            let key = item.dedup_key();
            if existing.contains(&key) || keys_of_new.contains(&key) {
                skipped.push(format!("duplicate: {}", key));
                // Parts were registered with ids but never exposed to the
                // server yet — nothing to unregister.
                continue;
            }
            keys_of_new.push(key);
            added.push(item.id);
            self.items.push(item);
        }

        (added, skipped)
    }

    /// Replace a placeholder dedup check after metadata parsing: returns ids
    /// of items whose parsed content id duplicates another item's.
    pub fn duplicates_by_content_id(&self) -> Vec<(ItemId, ItemId)> {
        let mut first_by_cid: std::collections::HashMap<String, ItemId> =
            std::collections::HashMap::new();
        let mut dupes = Vec::new();
        for item in &self.items {
            if item.state.is_finished() {
                continue;
            }
            let Some(cid) = item.content_id() else {
                continue;
            };
            let key = format!("cid:{cid}");
            match first_by_cid.get(&key) {
                Some(first) if *first != item.id => dupes.push((*first, item.id)),
                _ => {
                    first_by_cid.insert(key, item.id);
                }
            }
        }
        dupes
    }

    pub fn remove(&mut self, id: ItemId) -> Option<QueueItem> {
        let pos = self.items.iter().position(|i| i.id == id)?;
        Some(self.items.remove(pos))
    }

    pub fn move_item(&mut self, id: ItemId, delta: i32) -> bool {
        let Some(pos) = self.items.iter().position(|i| i.id == id) else {
            return false;
        };
        let new_pos = pos as i64 + delta as i64;
        if new_pos < 0 || new_pos >= self.items.len() as i64 {
            return false;
        }
        let new_pos = new_pos as usize;
        let item = self.items.remove(pos);
        self.items.insert(new_pos, item);
        true
    }

    /// Requeue a finished/failed/cancelled/unknown item for a fresh attempt.
    pub fn requeue(&mut self, id: ItemId) -> bool {
        let Some(item) = self.get_mut(id) else {
            return false;
        };
        if item.state.is_active() {
            return false;
        }
        item.state = ItemState::Queued;
        item.error = None;
        item.task_id = None;
        // A fresh attempt must not inherit the previous attempt's leftovers:
        // `attempts` only ever grows during a submit cycle, so keeping it
        // meant a user-retried item got *zero* automatic submit retries once
        // one cycle had exhausted the budget (every transient blip then went
        // straight to Failed). The v2 job id belongs to the old console-side
        // job, not to the attempt about to be created.
        item.job_id = None;
        item.task_owner = None;
        item.attempts = 0;
        item.progress = None;
        item.updated_at = unix_ms();
        true
    }

    pub fn clear_finished(&mut self) -> usize {
        let before = self.items.len();
        self.items.retain(|i| !i.state.is_finished());
        before - self.items.len()
    }

    /// Next item to process (first queued, in order).
    pub fn next_queued(&self) -> Option<ItemId> {
        self.items
            .iter()
            .find(|i| i.state == ItemState::Queued)
            .map(|i| i.id)
    }

    pub fn any_active(&self) -> bool {
        self.items.iter().any(|i| i.state.is_active())
    }

    pub fn stats(&self) -> QueueStats {
        let mut stats = QueueStats::default();
        for item in &self.items {
            match item.state {
                ItemState::Queued | ItemState::Preparing | ItemState::Submitting => {
                    stats.pending += 1
                }
                ItemState::Transferring | ItemState::Installing => stats.active += 1,
                ItemState::Paused => stats.paused += 1,
                ItemState::Completed => stats.completed += 1,
                ItemState::Failed | ItemState::Invalid => stats.failed += 1,
                ItemState::Cancelled => stats.cancelled += 1,
                ItemState::Unknown => stats.unknown += 1,
            }
        }
        stats
    }
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct QueueStats {
    pub pending: usize,
    pub active: usize,
    pub paused: usize,
    pub completed: usize,
    pub failed: usize,
    pub cancelled: usize,
    pub unknown: usize,
}

#[derive(Debug, serde::Serialize, serde::Deserialize)]
struct QueueDoc {
    next_item: ItemId,
    next_part: PartId,
    items: Vec<QueueItem>,
}

```

`crates/rpi-ng-core/src/rpi/client.rs`:

```rs
//! HTTP client for the RPI dialect (JSON POST API on the console).

use std::{net::SocketAddr, time::Duration};

use serde::{Deserialize, de::DeserializeOwned};

use super::{json_fix, types::*};
use crate::model::{BackendCaps, BackendKind, ProgressView};

#[derive(Debug, thiserror::Error)]
pub enum RpiError {
    #[error("network error: {0}")]
    Network(String),
    #[error("timeout after {0:?}")]
    Timeout(Duration),
    #[error("connection refused (is the installer app running on the console?)")]
    ConnectionRefused,
    #[error("backend answered HTTP {code}")]
    Http { code: u16 },
    #[error("invalid response (not valid JSON): {0}")]
    InvalidJson(String),
    #[error("backend refused: {code} ({gloss})")]
    BackendError { code: i64, gloss: String },
    #[error("task id missing in response")]
    MissingTaskId,
}

impl RpiError {
    /// Transient errors are worth retrying; permanent ones are not.
    pub fn is_transient(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(
            self,
            Self::Network(_) | Self::Timeout(_) | Self::ConnectionRefused
        )
    }
}

/// The console's answer to a backend probe.
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct ProbeOutcome {
    pub kind: BackendKind,
    pub caps: BackendCaps,
    /// Present when the backend speaks the v2 API (name/version/storage).
    pub identity: Option<V2SystemData>,
}

/// HTTP client for the RPI dialect. Cheap to clone (the underlying
/// connection pool is shared).
#[derive(Clone)]
pub struct RpiClient {
    http: reqwest::Client,
    base: String,
    timeout: Duration,
}

impl RpiClient {
    pub fn new(addr: SocketAddr, timeout: Duration) -> Self {
        let http = reqwest::Client::builder()
            .timeout(timeout)
            .connect_timeout(timeout)
            // Never route console traffic through system proxies — the console
            // is on the LAN.
            .no_proxy()
            .build()
            .expect("reqwest client");
        Self {
            http,
            base: format!("http://{addr}"),
            timeout,
        }
    }

    async fn post_json<T: DeserializeOwned>(
        &self,
        path: &str,
        body: &impl serde::Serialize,
    ) -> Result<T, RpiError> {
        let url = format!("{}{path}", self.base);
        let resp = self.http.post(&url).json(body).send().await;
        let resp = match resp {
            Ok(r) => r,
            Err(e) => return Err(map_transport_err(e, self.timeout)),
        };
        let text = resp.text().await.unwrap_or_default();
        self.parse_response(&text)
    }

    /// GET a JSON document, treating a non-success HTTP status as an error.
    /// Legacy RPI backends answer unknown GET paths with 404 (+ HTML), so this
    /// doubles as the v2-availability probe.
    async fn get_json<T: DeserializeOwned>(&self, path: &str) -> Result<T, RpiError> {
        let url = format!("{}{path}", self.base);
        let resp = self
            .http
            .get(&url)
            .send()
            .await
            .map_err(|e| map_transport_err(e, self.timeout))?;
        let status = resp.status();
        let text = resp.text().await.unwrap_or_default();
        if !status.is_success() {
            return Err(RpiError::Http {
                code: status.as_u16(),
            });
        }
        self.parse_response(&text)
    }

    /// Parse an RPI JSON body uniformly: repair hex literals, reject
    /// `"status":"fail"` bodies as backend errors, then deserialize into `T`.
    fn parse_response<T: DeserializeOwned>(&self, text: &str) -> Result<T, RpiError> {
        let repaired = json_fix::repair(text);
        let value: serde_json::Value = serde_json::from_str(repaired.trim())
            .or_else(|_| serde_json::from_str(text.trim()))
            .map_err(|e| RpiError::InvalidJson(format!("{e} | body: {}", truncate(text, 300))))?;

        if value.get("status").and_then(|s| s.as_str()) == Some("fail") {
            let code = lenient_i64_from_value(value.get("error_code"));
            // Some dialects (v2 install failures, parameter errors) carry a
            // human-readable `error` string instead of / next to `error_code`.
            let gloss = value
                .get("error")
                .and_then(|e| e.as_str())
                .map(str::to_string)
                .or_else(|| explain_error_code(code).map(str::to_string))
                .unwrap_or_else(|| "no known translation".into());
            return Err(RpiError::BackendError { code, gloss });
        }
        serde_json::from_value(value)
            .map_err(|e| RpiError::InvalidJson(format!("{e} | body: {}", truncate(text, 300))))
    }

    /// Probe whether the endpoint speaks the RPI dialect, and what it supports.
    ///
    /// Evidence used (never a bare port check):
    /// 1. `GET /api` must answer the payload's "Unsupported method" body —
    ///    this is how the ecosystem distinguishes RPI servers from random HTTP.
    /// 2. `GET /api/status` answering JSON marks newer receivers (caps).
    /// 3. `POST /api/is_exists` validates the full request/response path.
    pub async fn probe(&self) -> Result<ProbeOutcome, RpiError> {
        // (1) GET /api — RPI answers 400 "Unsupported method" mentioning "fail".
        let get = self.http.get(format!("{}/api", self.base)).send().await;
        let speaks_rpi = match get {
            Ok(resp) => {
                let status = resp.status().as_u16();
                let body = resp.text().await.unwrap_or_default();
                (status == 400 || status == 405)
                    && (body.contains("Unsupported method") || body.contains("\"fail\""))
            }
            Err(_) => false,
        };

        // (2) /api/status — newer receivers answer {"busy":...}; old ones HTML.
        let mut caps = BackendCaps::default();
        if let Ok(resp) = self
            .http
            .get(format!("{}/api/status", self.base))
            .send()
            .await
            && let Ok(text) = resp.text().await
            && text.contains("busy")
            && serde_json::from_str::<StatusResponse>(json_fix::repair(&text).as_str()).is_ok()
        {
            caps.status_endpoint = true;
        }

        // (3) Full request/response path validation (read-only API call).
        // This is also the fallback dialect proof when the /api fingerprint
        // is missing, so it is issued exactly once.
        match self.is_exists("PROBE00000").await {
            Ok(_) => {
                caps.install_direct = true;
            }
            Err(RpiError::BackendError { .. }) if speaks_rpi => {
                // The endpoint understood the dialect but rejected the probe —
                // still an RPI server.
                caps.install_direct = true;
            }
            Err(e) => {
                if speaks_rpi {
                    return Err(e);
                }
                return Err(RpiError::Network(
                    "endpoint did not answer the RPI probes".into(),
                ));
            }
        }

        // (4) v2 API — NextGen-class backends answer /api/v2/system with
        // their identity. One extra GET against an already-verified backend.
        let mut identity = None;
        if caps.install_direct
            && let Ok(sys) = self.v2_system().await
            && !sys.name.is_empty()
        {
            caps.api_v2 = true;
            identity = Some(sys);
        }

        Ok(ProbeOutcome {
            kind: BackendKind::Rpi,
            caps,
            identity,
        })
    }

    pub async fn install_direct(
        &self,
        package_urls: &[String],
    ) -> Result<InstallResponse, RpiError> {
        let req = InstallDirectRequest {
            kind: "direct",
            packages: package_urls.to_vec(),
        };
        let resp: InstallResponse = self.post_json("/api/install", &req).await?;
        if resp.status != "success" {
            return Err(RpiError::BackendError {
                code: 0,
                gloss: "install did not report success".into(),
            });
        }
        check_task_id(&resp)?;
        Ok(resp)
    }

    pub async fn install_ref(&self, url: &str) -> Result<InstallResponse, RpiError> {
        let req = InstallRefRequest {
            kind: "ref_pkg_url",
            url: url.to_string(),
        };
        let resp: InstallResponse = self.post_json("/api/install", &req).await?;
        if resp.status != "success" {
            return Err(RpiError::BackendError {
                code: 0,
                gloss: "install did not report success".into(),
            });
        }
        check_task_id(&resp)?;
        Ok(resp)
    }

    pub async fn task_progress_raw(&self, task_id: i64) -> Result<TaskProgressDto, RpiError> {
        let req = TaskIdRequest { task_id };
        self.post_json("/api/get_task_progress", &req).await
    }

    /// Poll progress and fold it into a UI-ready view.
    pub async fn task_progress(&self, task_id: i64) -> Result<ProgressView, RpiError> {
        let dto = self.task_progress_raw(task_id).await?;
        Ok(progress_dto_to_view(&dto))
    }

    pub async fn pause_task(&self, task_id: i64) -> Result<(), RpiError> {
        let req = TaskIdRequest { task_id };
        let _: FailResponse = self.post_json("/api/pause_task", &req).await?;
        Ok(())
    }

    pub async fn resume_task(&self, task_id: i64) -> Result<(), RpiError> {
        let req = TaskIdRequest { task_id };
        let _: FailResponse = self.post_json("/api/resume_task", &req).await?;
        Ok(())
    }

    pub async fn stop_task(&self, task_id: i64) -> Result<(), RpiError> {
        let req = TaskIdRequest { task_id };
        let _: FailResponse = self.post_json("/api/stop_task", &req).await?;
        Ok(())
    }

    pub async fn start_task(&self, task_id: i64) -> Result<(), RpiError> {
        let req = TaskIdRequest { task_id };
        let _: FailResponse = self.post_json("/api/start_task", &req).await?;
        Ok(())
    }

    pub async fn unregister_task(&self, task_id: i64) -> Result<(), RpiError> {
        let req = TaskIdRequest { task_id };
        let _: FailResponse = self.post_json("/api/unregister_task", &req).await?;
        Ok(())
    }

    pub async fn is_exists(&self, title_id: &str) -> Result<IsExistsResponse, RpiError> {
        let req = TitleIdRequest {
            title_id: title_id.to_string(),
        };
        self.post_json("/api/is_exists", &req).await
    }

    pub async fn find_task(
        &self,
        content_id: &str,
        sub_type: i32,
    ) -> Result<FindTaskResponse, RpiError> {
        let req = FindTaskRequest {
            content_id: content_id.to_string(),
            sub_type,
        };
        self.post_json("/api/find_task", &req).await
    }

    // -- v2 API ------------------------------------------------------------

    /// `GET /api/v2/system` — identity and storage of a v2 backend.
    pub async fn v2_system(&self) -> Result<V2SystemData, RpiError> {
        let resp: V2SystemResponse = self.get_json("/api/v2/system").await?;
        Ok(resp.data)
    }

    /// `POST /api/v2/install` — `url` for single files, `urls` for multipart.
    /// Maps the v2 response into the shared [`InstallResponse`] shape
    /// (`job_id` filled, `task_id: -1` = already installed).
    pub async fn v2_install(&self, package_urls: &[String]) -> Result<InstallResponse, RpiError> {
        if package_urls.is_empty() {
            return Err(RpiError::BackendError {
                code: 0,
                gloss: "no package urls to install".into(),
            });
        }
        let req = if package_urls.len() == 1 {
            V2InstallRequest {
                url: Some(package_urls[0].clone()),
                urls: None,
            }
        } else {
            V2InstallRequest {
                url: None,
                urls: Some(package_urls.to_vec()),
            }
        };
        let resp: V2InstallResponse = self.post_json("/api/v2/install", &req).await?;
        if resp.status != "success" {
            return Err(RpiError::BackendError {
                code: 0,
                gloss: "install did not report success".into(),
            });
        }
        let d = resp.data;
        let out = InstallResponse {
            status: "success".into(),
            task_id: d.task_id,
            title: d.title,
            job_id: Some(d.job_id),
        };
        check_task_id(&out)?;
        Ok(out)
    }

    /// `GET /api/v2/jobs` — the console's whole job registry in one request.
    pub async fn v2_jobs(&self) -> Result<Vec<V2Job>, RpiError> {
        let resp: V2JobsResponse = self.get_json("/api/v2/jobs").await?;
        Ok(resp.data.jobs)
    }

    /// `POST /api/v2/jobs/<id>/{pause|resume|stop}`.
    ///
    /// The API documents this as POST, but NextGen 0.0.1/0.0.2 only route the
    /// operation under GET/HEAD — a POST answers 404. Try POST first (correct
    /// for fixed builds and future versions), then fall back to GET once.
    pub async fn v2_job_op(&self, job_id: i64, op: &str) -> Result<(), RpiError> {
        let url = format!("{}/api/v2/jobs/{job_id}/{op}", self.base);
        for attempt in 0..2 {
            let req = if attempt == 0 {
                self.http.post(&url)
            } else {
                self.http.get(&url)
            };
            let resp = req
                .send()
                .await
                .map_err(|e| map_transport_err(e, self.timeout))?;
            let status = resp.status();
            let text = resp.text().await.unwrap_or_default();
            if status.is_success() {
                let _: StatusAck = self.parse_response(&text)?;
                return Ok(());
            }
            if attempt == 0 && status.as_u16() == 404 {
                continue; // documented-POST / routed-GET mismatch: retry as GET
            }
            return Err(RpiError::Http {
                code: status.as_u16(),
            });
        }
        Err(RpiError::Http { code: 404 })
    }
}

/// Acknowledgment body of lifecycle endpoints: `{"status":"success"}`.
#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]
struct StatusAck {
    #[allow(dead_code)]
    status: String,
}

/// Install responses must carry a usable task id: `> 0` is a real BGFT task,
/// `-1` means "already installed" (both dialects). Anything else (0, absent,
/// or other negative sentinels from fork dialects) is untrackable.
fn check_task_id(resp: &InstallResponse) -> Result<(), RpiError> {
    if resp.task_id > 0 || resp.task_id == -1 {
        Ok(())
    } else {
        Err(RpiError::MissingTaskId)
    }
}

fn map_transport_err(e: reqwest::Error, timeout: Duration) -> RpiError {
    if e.is_timeout() {
        RpiError::Timeout(timeout)
    } else if e.is_connect() {
        RpiError::ConnectionRefused
    } else {
        RpiError::Network(e.to_string())
    }
}

fn lenient_i64_from_value(v: Option<&serde_json::Value>) -> i64 {
    match v {
        Some(serde_json::Value::Number(n)) => n.as_i64().unwrap_or(0),
        Some(serde_json::Value::String(s)) => {
            let t = s.trim();
            if let Some(hex) = t.strip_prefix("0x").or_else(|| t.strip_prefix("0X")) {
                i64::from_str_radix(hex, 16).unwrap_or(0)
            } else {
                t.parse::<i64>().unwrap_or(0)
            }
        }
        _ => 0,
    }
}

fn truncate(s: &str, n: usize) -> String {
    if s.len() <= n {
        s.to_string()
    } else {
        let mut end = n;
        while end > 0 && !s.is_char_boundary(end) {
            end -= 1;
        }
        format!("{}…", &s[..end])
    }
}

/// Fold a progress DTO into the UI view. Only observed evidence is used.
pub fn progress_dto_to_view(dto: &TaskProgressDto) -> ProgressView {
    let total = dto.length_total;
    let transferred = dto.transferred_total;
    let percent = if total > 0 {
        (transferred as f64 / total as f64).clamp(0.0, 1.0) * 100.0
    } else {
        0.0
    };
    ProgressView {
        percent: percent as f32,
        transferred,
        total,
        speed_bps: 0, // computed by the engine from poll deltas
        eta_sec: if dto.rest_sec_total > 0 && dto.rest_sec_total < u32::MAX as u64 {
            Some(dto.rest_sec_total)
        } else {
            None
        },
        piece_index: dto.num_index.min(u32::MAX as u64) as u32,
        piece_total: dto.num_total.min(u32::MAX as u64) as u32,
        preparing_percent: dto.preparing_percent.clamp(0, 100) as i32,
        local_copy_percent: dto.local_copy_percent.clamp(0, 100) as i32,
        raw_error: dto.error,
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn dto_accepts_all_three_dialects() {
        // strict decimal
        let a: TaskProgressDto =
            serde_json::from_str(r#"{"status":"success","bits":32,"error":0,"length":4096,"transferred":0,"length_total":4096,"transferred_total":0,"num_index":0,"num_total":1,"rest_sec":0,"rest_sec_total":0,"preparing_percent":0,"local_copy_percent":0}"#).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(a.bits, 32);
        // quoted hex
        let b: TaskProgressDto =
            serde_json::from_str(r#"{"status":"success","bits":"0x20","error":"0","length":"0x1000","transferred":"0","length_total":"0x1000","transferred_total":"0","num_index":"0","num_total":"1"}"#).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(b.bits, 32);
        assert_eq!(b.length_total, 4096);
        // unquoted hex after repair
        let raw = r#"{"status":"success","bits": 0x20, "error":0, "length": 0x1000, "transferred":0x0, "length_total": 0x1000, "transferred_total":0x0, "num_index":0, "num_total":1, "rest_sec":0, "rest_sec_total":0, "preparing_percent":0, "local_copy_percent":0}"#;
        let c: TaskProgressDto = serde_json::from_str(json_fix::repair(raw).trim()).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(c.bits, 32);
    }

    #[test]
    fn error_glossary_known_codes() {
        assert!(explain_error_code(0x8099_0006).is_some());
        assert!(explain_error_code(1).is_none());
    }
}

`crates/rpi-ng-core/src/rpi/json_fix.rs`:

```rs
//! Tolerant JSON preprocessing for the RPI dialect.
//!
//! The upstream payload writes unquoted hexadecimal literals
//! (`{"bits":0x20,...}`), which is invalid JSON (`docs/BASELINE.md` §3.1).
//! [`repair`] rewrites such literals to decimal integers while staying aware of
//! JSON strings (a `": 0x…"` *inside* a quoted value must not be touched).

/// Rewrite unquoted `0x…` number literals into plain decimal.
///
/// String-aware: scans the document tracking whether we are inside a quoted
/// string (handling `\` escapes), and only rewrites number tokens that appear
/// in value position (after `:` or `,` or `[`), never inside strings.
pub fn repair(body: &str) -> String {
    let bytes = body.as_bytes();
    let mut out = String::with_capacity(body.len() + 16);
    let mut i = 0usize;
    let mut in_string = false;
    let mut escaped = false;

    while i < bytes.len() {
        let b = bytes[i];
        if in_string {
            if b < 0x80 {
                out.push(b as char);
                if escaped {
                    escaped = false;
                } else if b == b'\\' {
                    escaped = true;
                } else if b == b'"' {
                    in_string = false;
                }
                i += 1;
            } else {
                // Multi-byte UTF-8 inside a string (game titles are often
                // non-ASCII): copy the whole char. Pushing raw bytes as chars
                // would Latin-1 them into mojibake. The escape state machine
                // is unaffected — continuation bytes are never '"' or '\'.
                let rest = &body[i..];
                let ch = rest.chars().next().unwrap_or('\u{FFFD}');
                out.push(ch);
                i += ch.len_utf8();
            }
            continue;
        }
        match b {
            b'"' => {
                in_string = true;
                out.push('"');
                i += 1;
            }
            b':' | b',' | b'[' => {
                out.push(b as char);
                i += 1;
                // skip whitespace
                while i < bytes.len()
                    && (bytes[i] == b' '
                        || bytes[i] == b'\t'
                        || bytes[i] == b'\r'
                        || bytes[i] == b'\n')
                {
                    out.push(bytes[i] as char);
                    i += 1;
                }
                // hex literal?
                if i + 1 < bytes.len()
                    && bytes[i] == b'0'
                    && (bytes[i + 1] == b'x' || bytes[i + 1] == b'X')
                {
                    let mut j = i + 2;
                    while j < bytes.len() && bytes[j].is_ascii_hexdigit() {
                        j += 1;
                    }
                    if j > i + 2 {
                        let hex = &body[i + 2..j];
                        match u64::from_str_radix(hex, 16) {
                            Ok(v) => out.push_str(&v.to_string()),
                            Err(_) => out.push_str(&body[i..j]), // unreachable for hexdigits
                        }
                        i = j;
                    }
                }
            }
            _ => {
                // copy ASCII byte; non-ASCII must pass through as part of a
                // UTF-8 sequence — handled since we only special-case ASCII above.
                if b < 0x80 {
                    out.push(b as char);
                    i += 1;
                } else {
                    // Find the full UTF-8 sequence start; copy char by char.
                    let rest = &body[i..];
                    let ch = rest.chars().next().unwrap_or('\u{FFFD}');
                    out.push(ch);
                    i += ch.len_utf8();
                }
            }
        }
    }
    out
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::repair;

    #[test]
    fn repairs_unquoted_hex() {
        let raw = r#"{"status":"success","bits": 0x20, "error":0, "length": 0x1000, "transferred":0x0, "num_index":0, "num_total":3}"#;
        let fixed = repair(raw);
        let v: serde_json::Value = serde_json::from_str(&fixed).expect("valid json");
        assert_eq!(v["bits"], 32);
        assert_eq!(v["length"], 4096);
        assert_eq!(v["num_total"], 3);
    }

    #[test]
    fn repairs_error_code_hex() {
        let raw = r#"{ "status": "fail", "error_code": 0x80990005 }"#;
        let v: serde_json::Value = serde_json::from_str(&repair(raw)).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(v["error_code"], 0x8099_0005_u64);
    }

    #[test]
    fn leaves_strings_alone() {
        let raw = r#"{"status":"success","title":"Weird: 0xdead value"}"#;
        let v: serde_json::Value = serde_json::from_str(&repair(raw)).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(v["title"], "Weird: 0xdead value");
    }

    #[test]
    fn handles_escaped_strings() {
        let raw = r#"{"title":"quote \" then : 0x1 still string"}"#;
        let v: serde_json::Value = serde_json::from_str(&repair(raw)).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(v["title"], r#"quote " then : 0x1 still string"#);
    }

    #[test]
    fn passes_valid_json_through_unchanged_semantically() {
        let raw = r#"{"status":"success","task_id":7,"title":"Game"}"#;
        let v: serde_json::Value = serde_json::from_str(&repair(raw)).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(v["task_id"], 7);
    }

    #[test]
    fn uppercase_hex_and_arrays() {
        let raw = r#"{"bits": 0XAB, "pieces":[0x1, 0x2, 0x10]}"#;
        let v: serde_json::Value = serde_json::from_str(&repair(raw)).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(v["bits"], 0xAB);
        assert_eq!(v["pieces"][2], 16);
    }

    #[test]
    fn preserves_multibyte_utf8_inside_strings() {
        // Regression (v0.0.4): the in-string branch used to push raw bytes as
        // chars (Latin-1), corrupting non-ASCII titles into mojibake.
        let raw = r#"{"status":"success","title":"龍が如く極 Save","bits":0x20}"#;
        let v: serde_json::Value = serde_json::from_str(&repair(raw)).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(v["title"], "龍が如く極 Save");
        assert_eq!(v["bits"], 32);
    }

    #[test]
    fn multibyte_after_escaped_quote_and_after_hex() {
        // Non-ASCII both after an escaped quote inside the same string and in
        // a value that follows a repaired hex literal.
        let raw = "{\"a\":\"x\\\"é€\", \"b\": 0x1, \"c\":\"日本語\"}";
        let v: serde_json::Value = serde_json::from_str(&repair(raw)).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(v["a"], "x\"é€");
        assert_eq!(v["b"], 1);
        assert_eq!(v["c"], "日本語");
    }
}

`crates/rpi-ng-core/src/rpi/types.rs`:

```rs
//! Request/response DTOs for the RPI dialect, with lenient numeric fields.
//!
//! Numbers may arrive as plain integers (strict JSON), unquoted hex literals
//! (repaired by [`super::json_fix`] before deserialization), or quoted
//! strings (`"0x20"`, `"32"`) — some forks quote them. [`LenientNum`] accepts
//! all three.

use serde::{Deserialize, Deserializer, Serialize};

pub fn deserialize_lenient_u64<'de, D>(deserializer: D) -> Result<u64, D::Error>
where
    D: Deserializer<'de>,
{
    #[derive(Deserialize)]
    #[serde(untagged)]
    enum Repr {
        Num(u64),
        Neg(i64),
        Str(String),
    }
    match Repr::deserialize(deserializer)? {
        Repr::Num(v) => Ok(v),
        Repr::Neg(v) => {
            if v < 0 {
                // The backend sometimes reports -1 style sentinels; keep the raw
                // bits visible rather than failing.
                Ok(v as u64)
            } else {
                Ok(v as u64)
            }
        }
        Repr::Str(s) => {
            let t = s.trim();
            if let Some(hex) = t.strip_prefix("0x").or_else(|| t.strip_prefix("0X")) {
                u64::from_str_radix(hex, 16).map_err(serde::de::Error::custom)
            } else {
                t.parse::<u64>().map_err(serde::de::Error::custom)
            }
        }
    }
}

pub fn deserialize_lenient_i64<'de, D>(deserializer: D) -> Result<i64, D::Error>
where
    D: Deserializer<'de>,
{
    #[derive(Deserialize)]
    #[serde(untagged)]
    enum Repr {
        Num(i64),
        Str(String),
    }
    match Repr::deserialize(deserializer)? {
        Repr::Num(v) => Ok(v),
        Repr::Str(s) => {
            let t = s.trim();
            if let Some(hex) = t.strip_prefix("0x").or_else(|| t.strip_prefix("0X")) {
                i64::from_str_radix(hex, 16).map_err(serde::de::Error::custom)
            } else if let Some(hex) = t.strip_prefix("-0x").or_else(|| t.strip_prefix("-0X")) {
                let v = i64::from_str_radix(hex, 16).map_err(serde::de::Error::custom)?;
                Ok(-v)
            } else {
                t.parse::<i64>().map_err(serde::de::Error::custom)
            }
        }
    }
}

}

/// `GET /api/v2/system` — backend identity and health.
#[derive(Debug, Deserialize, Clone)]
pub struct V2SystemResponse {
    pub status: String,
    pub data: V2SystemData,
}

#[derive(Debug, Deserialize, Clone, Default)]
pub struct V2SystemData {
    pub name: String,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub version: String,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub title_id: String,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub ip: String,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub port: u16,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub network: bool,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub storage: V2Storage,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub active_jobs: i64,
}

pub struct V2Storage {

    #[serde(default)]

}

/// `POST /api/v2/install` — accepts `url` (single) or `urls` (multipart).
#[derive(Debug, Serialize)]
pub struct V2InstallRequest {
    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub url: Option<String>,
    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub urls: Option<Vec<String>>,
}

    pub data: V2InstallData,
}

    pub task_id: i64,

    #[serde(default)]
    pub content_id: Option<String>,
    #[serde(default, deserialize_with = "deserialize_lenient_u64")]

}

/// `GET /api/v2/jobs` — every job the console still remembers (terminal
/// states included; jobs vanish only when the console app restarts or its
/// registry reclaims a slot).
#[derive(Debug, Deserialize, Clone)]
pub struct V2JobsResponse {
    pub status: String,
    pub data: V2JobsData,
}

#[derive(Debug, Deserialize, Clone, Default)]
pub struct V2JobsData {
    #[serde(default)]
    pub jobs: Vec<V2Job>,
}

/// One entry of the v2 jobs list. Field names mirror the wire format.
#[derive(Debug, Deserialize, Clone)]
pub struct V2Job {
    pub id: i64,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub bgft_task_id: i64,
    pub state: String,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub title: Option<String>,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub content_id: String,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub app_ver: String,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub content_type: String,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub pieces: u32,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub package_size: u64,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub transferred: u64,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub length_total: u64,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub percent: i32,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub speed_bps: f64,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub is_patch: bool,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub error: i64,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub rest_sec: u64,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub num_index: u32,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub num_total: u32,
}

/// v2 job states, as emitted by the console (`taskman.c`).
pub mod v2_states {
    pub const PENDING: &str = "pending";
    pub const PREPARING: &str = "preparing";
    pub const REGISTERED: &str = "registered";
    pub const RUNNING: &str = "running";
    pub const PAUSED: &str = "paused";
    pub const COMPLETED: &str = "completed";
    pub const ALREADY_INSTALLED: &str = "already_installed";
    pub const FAILED: &str = "failed";
    pub const STOPPED: &str = "stopped";
}

    pub local_copy_percent: i64,
}

/// u32 fields that may arrive as u64 on lenient paths — stored as u64, read as u32.
#[allow(dead_code)]
fn clamp_u32(v: u64) -> u32 {
    v.min(u32::MAX as u64) as u32
}

#[derive(Debug, Deserialize, Clone)]
pub struct IsExistsResponse {
    pub status: String,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub exists: String,
    /// Size on disk when the title exists; 0 when unknown/absent.
    #[serde(default, deserialize_with = "deserialize_lenient_u64")]
    pub size: u64,
}

#[derive(Debug, Deserialize, Clone)]
pub struct FindTaskResponse {
    pub status: String,
    #[serde(default, deserialize_with = "deserialize_lenient_i64")]
    pub task_id: i64,
}

#[derive(Debug, Deserialize, Clone)]
pub struct StatusResponse {
    #[serde(default)]
    pub busy: bool,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub active: Option<u64>,
}

/// Task sub types (BGFT), from upstream README.
pub mod sub_types {
    pub const GAME: i32 = 6;
    pub const AC: i32 = 7;
    pub const PATCH: i32 = 8;
    pub const LICENSE: i32 = 9;
}

/// Glossary of console-side error codes observed in the wild. Only codes with a
/// corroborating source are listed; unknown codes are surfaced verbatim.
pub fn explain_error_code(code: i64) -> Option<&'static str> {
    match code {
        0x8099_0006 => Some("Console reports the operation is not supported (BGFT not available)."),
        0x8099_0004 => Some("Console-side install error (BGFT rejected the task)."),
        0x8099_0007 => Some("Console reports invalid task data (package manifest rejected)."),
        0x8099_000A => {
            Some("Console-side transfer failed (network or disk error during download).")
        }
        0x8099_0016 => Some("Console reports the task is already running or registered."),
        0x8099_0019 => Some(
            "Console reports the task was not found (it may have finished or been unregistered).",
        ),
        _ => None,
    }
}

`crates/rpi-ng-core/src/settings.rs`:

```rs
//! Persisted user settings (atomic JSON, corruption-tolerant).

use std::{net::SocketAddr, path::PathBuf, time::Duration};

use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};

#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub struct Settings {
    /// `System`, `Light` or `Dark` UI theme.
    pub theme: ThemeMode,
    /// Local file server port: `None` = OS-assigned ephemeral port (default).
    pub server_port: Option<u16>,
    /// Enable the bounded subnet probe during discovery.
    pub discovery_enabled: bool,
    /// Maximum candidate addresses probed per discovery sweep.
    pub discovery_max_hosts: u32,
    /// TCP connect timeout for discovery probes.
    pub discovery_timeout: Duration,
    /// Progress poll interval for an active task.
    pub poll_interval: Duration,
    /// Default for the recursive flag when adding folders.
    pub folder_scan_recursive: bool,
    /// Consoles the user selected before (validated again on use).
    pub remembered: Vec<RememberedConsole>,
    /// Auto-start the next queued item when the console finishes one.
    pub auto_advance: bool,
    /// What happens to the rest of the queue when a transfer fails.
    pub on_failure: FailurePolicy,
    /// Look for a newer release on GitHub when the app starts (the check
    /// sends nothing but a plain GET to the releases API).
    pub auto_check_updates: bool,
}

/// Queue behavior after a failed transfer.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, Default)]
#[serde(rename_all = "lowercase")]
pub enum FailurePolicy {
    /// Keep going: mark the item failed, start the next queued one.
    #[default]
    Continue,
    /// Park the queue: nothing else starts until the user says so.
    Pause,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, Default)]
pub enum ThemeMode {
    #[default]
    System,
    Light,
    Dark,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct RememberedConsole {
    pub addr: SocketAddr,
    pub label: Option<String>,
}

impl Default for Settings {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self {
            theme: ThemeMode::default(),
            server_port: None,
            discovery_enabled: true,
            discovery_max_hosts: 1024,
            discovery_timeout: Duration::from_millis(350),
            poll_interval: Duration::from_millis(1000),
            folder_scan_recursive: true,
            remembered: Vec::new(),
            auto_advance: true,
            on_failure: FailurePolicy::default(),
            auto_check_updates: true,
        }
    }
}

/// Serializable mirror of [`Settings`] (durations as millis).
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]
struct SettingsDoc {
    #[serde(default)]
    theme: ThemeMode,
    #[serde(default)]
    server_port: Option<u16>,
    #[serde(default = "default_true")]
    discovery_enabled: bool,
    #[serde(default = "default_max_hosts")]
    discovery_max_hosts: u32,
    #[serde(default = "default_disc_timeout")]
    discovery_timeout_ms: u64,
    #[serde(default = "default_poll_ms")]
    poll_interval_ms: u64,
    #[serde(default = "default_true")]
    folder_scan_recursive: bool,
    #[serde(default)]
    remembered: Vec<RememberedConsole>,
    #[serde(default = "default_true")]
    auto_advance: bool,
    #[serde(default)]
    on_failure: FailurePolicy,
    #[serde(default = "default_true")]
    auto_check_updates: bool,
}

fn default_true() -> bool {
    true
}
fn default_max_hosts() -> u32 {
    1024
}
fn default_disc_timeout() -> u64 {
    350
}
fn default_poll_ms() -> u64 {
    1000
}

impl From<Settings> for SettingsDoc {
    fn from(s: Settings) -> Self {
        Self {
            theme: s.theme,
            server_port: s.server_port,
            discovery_enabled: s.discovery_enabled,
            discovery_max_hosts: s.discovery_max_hosts,
            discovery_timeout_ms: s.discovery_timeout.as_millis() as u64,
            poll_interval_ms: s.poll_interval.as_millis() as u64,
            folder_scan_recursive: s.folder_scan_recursive,
            remembered: s.remembered,
            auto_advance: s.auto_advance,
            on_failure: s.on_failure,
            auto_check_updates: s.auto_check_updates,
        }
    }
}

impl From<SettingsDoc> for Settings {
    fn from(d: SettingsDoc) -> Self {
        Self {
            theme: d.theme,
            server_port: d.server_port,
            discovery_enabled: d.discovery_enabled,
            discovery_max_hosts: d.discovery_max_hosts.clamp(64, 4096),
            discovery_timeout: Duration::from_millis(d.discovery_timeout_ms.clamp(100, 2000)),
            poll_interval: Duration::from_millis(d.poll_interval_ms.clamp(250, 10_000)),
            folder_scan_recursive: d.folder_scan_recursive,
            remembered: d.remembered,
            auto_advance: d.auto_advance,
            on_failure: d.on_failure,
            auto_check_updates: d.auto_check_updates,
        }
    }

/// Files we persist in the data dir (migrated from the legacy location on
/// first run of a newer build).
const PERSISTED_FILES: [&str; 2] = ["settings.json", "queue.json"];

/// Where settings/queue documents live.
///
/// Resolution order:
/// 1. `RPI_NG_DATA_DIR` env var (tests, headless embedding);
/// 2. **the directory of the executable** — the app is portable, so the
///    config travels with it (e.g. `D:\\RPING\\rpi-ng.exe` → `D:\\RPING\\`);
/// 3. per-OS user data dir (fallback when the exe path cannot be determined).
pub fn data_dir() -> Option<PathBuf> {
    resolve_data_dir(
        std::env::var("RPI_NG_DATA_DIR").ok().as_deref(),
        std::env::current_exe().ok().as_deref(),
    )
}

/// Pure resolution logic (parameterized for tests). An empty env value is
/// treated as unset — same contract as the real `RPI_NG_DATA_DIR` handling.
fn resolve_data_dir(env: Option<&str>, exe: Option<&std::path::Path>) -> Option<PathBuf> {
    if let Some(dir) = env.filter(|v| !v.is_empty()) {
        return Some(PathBuf::from(dir));
    }
    if let Some(exe) = exe
        && let Some(parent) = exe.parent()
        // Guard against the degenerate `parent() == ""` some launchers report.
        && !parent.as_os_str().is_empty()
    {
        return Some(parent.to_path_buf());
    }
    legacy_data_dir()
}

/// The pre-portable location (per-OS user data dir). Kept only so an existing
/// install can be migrated — see [`init_data_dir`].
pub fn legacy_data_dir() -> Option<PathBuf> {
    directories::ProjectDirs::from("", "", "rpi-ng").map(|d| d.data_dir().to_path_buf())
}

/// Resolve the data dir, create it, and migrate any pre-portable files into
/// it (once: files already present are never overwritten). Called at startup,
/// before anything reads or writes the data dir.
///
/// The legacy migration only runs under the *default* resolution — an
/// `RPI_NG_DATA_DIR` override (tests, headless embedding) stays hermetic and
/// never reads the host user's real files.
pub fn init_data_dir() -> Option<PathBuf> {
    let env = std::env::var("RPI_NG_DATA_DIR")
        .ok()
        .filter(|v| !v.is_empty());
    let dir = resolve_data_dir(env.as_deref(), std::env::current_exe().ok().as_deref())?;
    if std::fs::create_dir_all(&dir).is_err() {
        // Read-only location (e.g. Program Files without elevation): keep the
        // dir anyway — reads still work, and any later write failure is
        // surfaced by the engine ("settings save failed: …") with the path.
    }
    if env.is_none()
        && let Some(legacy) = legacy_data_dir()
        && legacy != dir
    {
        for file in PERSISTED_FILES {
            let from = legacy.join(file);
            let to = dir.join(file);
            if !to.exists() && from.is_file() {
                // Best effort: a failed copy just means we start fresh.
                let _ = std::fs::copy(&from, &to);
            }
        }
    }
    Some(dir)
}

impl Settings {
    pub fn load() -> Self {
        Self::load_in(data_dir().as_deref())
    }

    pub fn load_in(dir: Option<&std::path::Path>) -> Self {
        let Some(dir) = dir else {
            return Self::default();
        };
        let path = dir.join("settings.json");
        for candidate in [&path, &dir.join("settings.json.bak")] {
            if let Ok(text) = std::fs::read_to_string(candidate)
                && let Ok(doc) = serde_json::from_str::<SettingsDoc>(&text)
            {
                return doc.into();
            }
        }
        Self::default()
    }

    /// Atomic write: serialize → write tmp → fsync → rename over destination,
    /// keeping a `.bak` of the previous file.
    pub fn save(&self) -> std::io::Result<()> {
        self.save_in(data_dir().as_deref())
    }

    pub fn save_in(&self, dir: Option<&std::path::Path>) -> std::io::Result<()> {
        let Some(dir) = dir else {
            return Ok(());
        };
        std::fs::create_dir_all(dir)?;
        let path = dir.join("settings.json");
        if path.exists() {
            let _ = std::fs::copy(&path, dir.join("settings.json.bak"));
        }
        let text = serde_json::to_string_pretty(&SettingsDoc::from(self.clone()))
            .map_err(std::io::Error::other)?;
        let tmp = dir.join("settings.json.tmp");
        atomic_write(&tmp, &path, text.as_bytes())
    }
}

/// Write bytes to `tmp`, fsync, then rename to `dest`.
pub fn atomic_write(tmp: &PathBuf, dest: &PathBuf, bytes: &[u8]) -> std::io::Result<()> {
    use std::io::Write;
    {
        let mut f = std::fs::File::create(tmp)?;
        f.write_all(bytes)?;
        f.sync_all()?;
    }
    std::fs::rename(tmp, dest)
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn env_override_wins_over_exe_dir() {
        let dir = resolve_data_dir(
            Some("/tmp/data"),
            Some(std::path::Path::new("/opt/app/rpi-ng.exe")),
        );
        assert_eq!(dir, Some(PathBuf::from("/tmp/data")));
    }

    #[test]
    fn exe_dir_is_used_when_no_env() {
        let exe = if cfg!(windows) {
            std::path::Path::new("D:\\RPING\\rpi-ng.exe")
        } else {
            std::path::Path::new("/home/user/RPING/rpi-ng")
        };
        let dir = resolve_data_dir(None, Some(exe));
        assert_eq!(dir, Some(exe.parent().unwrap().to_path_buf()));
    }

    #[test]
    fn empty_env_is_ignored() {
        let dir = resolve_data_dir(Some(""), Some(std::path::Path::new("/opt/app/rpi-ng")));
        assert_eq!(dir, Some(PathBuf::from("/opt/app")));
    }

    #[test]
    fn falls_back_to_user_dir_without_exe() {
        // No env, no exe path: still resolves to the per-OS user dir.
        assert!(resolve_data_dir(None, None).is_some());
    }

    #[test]
    fn init_migrates_legacy_files_once() {
        let new_dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        let legacy = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        std::fs::write(legacy.path().join("settings.json"), "{\"theme\":\"Dark\"}").unwrap();
        std::fs::write(legacy.path().join("queue.json"), "{\"items\":[]}").unwrap();

        migrate(legacy.path(), new_dir.path());
        // Both files copied.
        assert!(new_dir.path().join("settings.json").is_file());
        assert!(new_dir.path().join("queue.json").is_file());
        // And loaded correctly.
        let s = Settings::load_in(Some(new_dir.path()));
        assert_eq!(s.theme, ThemeMode::Dark);

        // Second run: existing files are never overwritten.
        std::fs::write(
            new_dir.path().join("settings.json"),
            "{\"theme\":\"Light\"}",
        )
        .unwrap();
        migrate(legacy.path(), new_dir.path());
        assert_eq!(
            std::fs::read_to_string(new_dir.path().join("settings.json")).unwrap(),
            "{\"theme\":\"Light\"}"
        );
    }

    /// The migration half of [`init_data_dir`] with explicit dirs (the full
    /// function reads process-global state: env + current exe).
    fn migrate(legacy: &std::path::Path, dir: &std::path::Path) {
        for file in PERSISTED_FILES {
            let from = legacy.join(file);
            let to = dir.join(file);
            if !to.exists() && from.is_file() {
                std::fs::copy(&from, &to).unwrap();
            }
        }
    }
}

`crates/rpi-ng-core/src/sfo.rs`:

```rs
//! PARAM.SFO parser (format used by PlayStation packages).
//!
//! Layout (all big-endian), mirrored from the upstream payload's `sfo.c` and
//! hardened with explicit bounds checks:
//!
//! ```text
//! header (0x14 bytes): magic "\0PSF", version u32, key_table_offset u32,
//!                      value_table_offset u32, entry_count u32
//! entries  (0x20 * entry_count bytes... actually 0x10 each):
//!   key_offset u16, data_format u16, used_size u32, max_size u32, value_offset u32
//! ```

use std::collections::BTreeMap;

pub const SFO_MAGIC: &[u8; 4] = b"\0PSF";
const MAX_ENTRIES: usize = 256;
const MAX_ENTRY_SIZE: usize = 64 * 1024;
const MAX_TOTAL_SIZE: usize = 1024 * 1024;

#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum SfoValue {
    String(String),
    U32(u32),
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]
pub struct Sfo {
    pub entries: BTreeMap<String, SfoValue>,
}

#[derive(Debug, thiserror::Error)]
pub enum SfoError {
    #[error("too short ({len} bytes)")]
    TooShort { len: usize },
    #[error("bad magic")]
    BadMagic,
    #[error("entry count {0} exceeds limit {1}")]
    TooManyEntries(usize, usize),
    #[error("entry table exceeds buffer")]
    EntryTableOverflow,
    #[error("key offset out of bounds (key {key}, offset {offset})")]
    KeyOutOfBounds { key: usize, offset: usize },
    #[error("value out of bounds (key {key}, offset {offset}, size {size})")]
    ValueOutOfBounds {
        key: usize,
        offset: usize,
        size: usize,
    },
    #[error("entry size {size} exceeds limit for key {key}")]
    EntryTooLarge { key: usize, size: usize },
    #[error("unterminated key string (key {key})")]
    UnterminatedKey { key: usize },
}

fn be_u16(b: &[u8], off: usize) -> u16 {
    u16::from_be_bytes([b[off], b[off + 1]])
}

fn be_u32(b: &[u8], off: usize) -> u32 {
    u32::from_be_bytes([b[off], b[off + 1], b[off + 2], b[off + 3]])
}

impl Sfo {
    /// Parse a PARAM.SFO buffer. Trailing garbage is ignored; all offsets are
    /// validated against the buffer length.
    pub fn parse(buf: &[u8]) -> Result<Self, SfoError> {
        if buf.len() < 0x14 {
            return Err(SfoError::TooShort { len: buf.len() });
        }
        if &buf[0..4] != SFO_MAGIC {
            return Err(SfoError::BadMagic);
        }
        let key_table = be_u32(buf, 0x08) as usize;
        let value_table = be_u32(buf, 0x0C) as usize;
        let entry_count = be_u32(buf, 0x10) as usize;
        if entry_count > MAX_ENTRIES {
            return Err(SfoError::TooManyEntries(entry_count, MAX_ENTRIES));
        }
        if entry_count
            .checked_mul(0x10)
            .map(|n| 0x14 + n)
            .unwrap_or(usize::MAX)
            > buf.len()
        {
            return Err(SfoError::EntryTableOverflow);
        }

        let mut entries = BTreeMap::new();
        for i in 0..entry_count {
            let e = 0x14 + i * 0x10;
            let key_offset = be_u16(buf, e) as usize;
            let data_format = be_u16(buf, e + 2);
            let used_size = be_u32(buf, e + 4) as usize;
            let value_offset = be_u32(buf, e + 0x0C) as usize;

            let key_abs = key_table
                .checked_add(key_offset)
                .ok_or(SfoError::KeyOutOfBounds {
                    key: i,
                    offset: usize::MAX,
                })?;
            if key_abs >= buf.len() {
                return Err(SfoError::KeyOutOfBounds {
                    key: i,
                    offset: key_abs,
                });
            }
            let key_end = buf[key_abs..]
                .iter()
                .position(|&b| b == 0)
                .ok_or(SfoError::UnterminatedKey { key: i })?
                + key_abs;
            if key_end > buf.len() {
                return Err(SfoError::UnterminatedKey { key: i });
            }
            let key = String::from_utf8_lossy(&buf[key_abs..key_end]).into_owned();

            if used_size > MAX_ENTRY_SIZE {
                return Err(SfoError::EntryTooLarge {
                    key: i,
                    size: used_size,
                });
            }
            let value_abs =
                value_table
                    .checked_add(value_offset)
                    .ok_or(SfoError::ValueOutOfBounds {
                        key: i,
                        offset: usize::MAX,
                        size: used_size,
                    })?;
            let value_end = value_abs
                .checked_add(used_size)
                .ok_or(SfoError::ValueOutOfBounds {
                    key: i,
                    offset: usize::MAX,
                    size: used_size,
                })?;
            if value_abs > buf.len() || value_end > buf.len() {
                return Err(SfoError::ValueOutOfBounds {
                    key: i,
                    offset: value_abs,
                    size: used_size,
                });
            }

            let value = match data_format {
                0x0204 => {
                    let raw = &buf[value_abs..value_end];
                    let len = raw.iter().position(|&b| b == 0).unwrap_or(raw.len());
                    SfoValue::String(String::from_utf8_lossy(&raw[..len]).into_owned())
                }
                0x0404 => {
                    // The bounds check above guarantees `value_abs + used_size`
                    // fits, but a malformed entry can claim used_size < 4 —
                    // reading a full u32 there would run past the buffer and
                    // panic the parse thread (v0.0.5 fix; a truncated SFO
                    // triggered exactly this). Skip such entries instead.
                    if value_abs + 4 <= buf.len() {
                        SfoValue::U32(be_u32(buf, value_abs))
                    } else {
                        continue;
                    }
                }
                0x0004 => {
                    // Special/int8-ish format; treat as u32 read when possible.
                    if value_abs + 4 <= buf.len() {
                        SfoValue::U32(be_u32(buf, value_abs))
                    } else {
                        SfoValue::U32(buf[value_abs] as u32)
                    }
                }
                _ => continue, // unknown formats are skipped, not fatal
            };
            entries.insert(key, value);
        }
        Ok(Self { entries })
    }

    pub fn get_str(&self, key: &str) -> Option<&str> {
        match self.entries.get(key) {
            Some(SfoValue::String(s)) => Some(s.as_str()),
            _ => None,
        }
    }

    pub fn get_u32(&self, key: &str) -> Option<u32> {
        match self.entries.get(key) {
            Some(SfoValue::U32(v)) => Some(*v),
            _ => None,
        }
    }

    /// Localized title: prefers `TITLE_XX` for `lang_id`, falls back to `TITLE`.
    pub fn title(&self, lang_id: u8) -> Option<String> {
        let localized = format!("TITLE_{lang_id:02}");
        if let Some(t) = self.get_str(&localized)
            && !t.is_empty()
        {
            return Some(t.to_string());
        }
        self.get_str("TITLE").map(|t| t.to_string())
    }

    /// Total buffer size cap used by callers.
    pub fn max_total_size() -> usize {
        MAX_TOTAL_SIZE
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    /// Minimal SFO with one entry; `fmt` picks the data format, `used` the
    /// used-size, and the value table gets exactly `used` bytes appended.
    fn sfo_with_entry(fmt: u16, used: u32, value_bytes: &[u8]) -> Vec<u8> {
        let key_table = 0x14 + 0x10;
        let value_table = key_table + 2; // "X\0"
        let mut buf = Vec::new();
        buf.extend_from_slice(SFO_MAGIC);
        buf.extend_from_slice(&1u32.to_be_bytes());
        buf.extend_from_slice(&(key_table as u32).to_be_bytes());
        buf.extend_from_slice(&(value_table as u32).to_be_bytes());
        buf.extend_from_slice(&1u32.to_be_bytes());
        // entry: key_off 0, fmt, used, max = used, value_off 0
        buf.extend_from_slice(&0u16.to_be_bytes());
        buf.extend_from_slice(&fmt.to_be_bytes());
        buf.extend_from_slice(&used.to_be_bytes());
        buf.extend_from_slice(&used.to_be_bytes());
        buf.extend_from_slice(&0u32.to_be_bytes());
        buf.extend_from_slice(b"X\0");
        buf.extend_from_slice(value_bytes);
        buf
    }

    /// v0.0.5 regression: a u32 (0x0404) entry whose used_size is shorter
    /// than 4 bytes and sits at the very end of the buffer used to index out
    /// of bounds and panic — silently killing the background parse thread.
    #[test]
    fn short_u32_entry_at_buffer_end_is_skipped_not_panicked() {
        let buf = sfo_with_entry(0x0404, 2, &[0xAA, 0xBB]);
        let sfo = Sfo::parse(&buf).expect("malformed entry must be skipped, not panic");
        assert!(sfo.entries.is_empty(), "the broken entry is dropped");
    }

    #[test]
    fn wellformed_u32_entry_still_parses() {
        let buf = sfo_with_entry(0x0404, 4, &0x1122_3344u32.to_be_bytes());
        let sfo = Sfo::parse(&buf).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(sfo.get_u32("X"), Some(0x1122_3344));
    }

    #[test]
    fn string_entries_still_parse() {
        let val = b"gd\0";
        let buf = sfo_with_entry(0x0204, val.len() as u32, val);
        let sfo = Sfo::parse(&buf).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(sfo.get_str("X"), Some("gd"));
    }
}

```

`crates/rpi-ng-core/src/update.rs`:

```rs
//! Update check against the project's GitHub releases.
//!
//! The check is a single GET to the releases API — nothing else is sent (no
//! telemetry, no identifiers; the request carries only the app's User-Agent).
//! Versions come from the release tag (`v0.0.2`) and are compared with a
//! conservative numeric parser: anything that does not parse cleanly is
//! treated as "not newer" so a malformed tag can never nag the user.

use serde::Deserialize;

/// Releases API endpoint for the latest published release.
pub const RELEASES_API_URL: &str =
    "https://api.github.com/repos/AldySan/Remote-Package-Installer-NG/releases/latest";

/// Browser-facing releases page (used when the API omits `html_url`).
pub const RELEASES_PAGE: &str = "https://github.com/AldySan/Remote-Package-Installer-NG/releases";

#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct UpdateInfo {
    /// Normalized version (`v` prefix stripped), e.g. `0.0.3`.
    pub version: String,
    /// The release tag exactly as published, e.g. `v0.0.3`.
    pub tag: String,
    /// Human-facing release page URL.
    pub url: String,
}

#[derive(Debug, thiserror::Error)]
pub enum UpdateError {
    #[error("network error: {0}")]
    Network(#[from] reqwest::Error),
    #[error("unexpected API status {status}: {message}")]
    Api { status: u16, message: String },
    #[error("GitHub API rate limit exceeded; try again later")]
    RateLimited,
    #[error("malformed release response: {0}")]
    Malformed(&'static str),
}

/// A client suitable for the update check (User-Agent is mandatory for the
/// GitHub API; without one it answers 403).
pub fn http_client() -> reqwest::Client {
    reqwest::Client::builder()
        .user_agent(format!("Remote-Package-Installer-NG/{}", crate::VERSION))
        .timeout(std::time::Duration::from_secs(15))
        .build()
        .expect("TLS client with default roots builds")
}

#[derive(Deserialize)]
struct GithubRelease {
    tag_name: String,
    #[serde(default)]
    html_url: String,
}

/// Fetch the latest release and report whether it is newer than `current`.
///
/// Returns `Ok(None)` when the endpoint reports no releases yet (404).
pub async fn check_latest(
    client: &reqwest::Client,
    api_url: &str,
    current: &str,
) -> Result<Option<UpdateInfo>, UpdateError> {
    let resp = client
        .get(api_url)
        .header("Accept", "application/vnd.github+json")
        .send()
        .await?;
    let status = resp.status();
    if !status.is_success() {
        return match status.as_u16() {
            404 => Ok(None),
            403 | 429 => Err(UpdateError::RateLimited),
            other => Err(UpdateError::Api {
                status: other,
                message: resp
                    .text()
                    .await
                    .unwrap_or_default()
                    .chars()
                    .take(200)
                    .collect(),
            }),
        };
    }
    let release: GithubRelease = resp
        .json()
        .await
        .map_err(|_| UpdateError::Malformed("expected a release object"))?;
    if release.tag_name.trim().is_empty() {
        return Err(UpdateError::Malformed("empty tag_name"));
    }
    let tag = release.tag_name.trim().to_string();
    let version = normalize_version(&tag);
    let url = if release.html_url.is_empty() {
        format!("{RELEASES_PAGE}/tag/{tag}")
    } else {
        release.html_url
    };
    if is_newer(current, &version) {
        Ok(Some(UpdateInfo { version, tag, url }))
    } else {
        Ok(None)
    }
}

/// Strip the conventional `v`/`V` prefix from a version or tag.
pub fn normalize_version(raw: &str) -> String {
    let raw = raw.trim();
    raw.strip_prefix(['v', 'V']).unwrap_or(raw).to_string()
}

/// True only when `latest` is *strictly* newer than `current`.
///
/// Numeric, dot-separated segments compared left to right (`0.0.10` beats
/// `0.0.2`). A trailing `-something` suffix makes a version *older* than the
/// same number without one (pre-releases). Anything unparseable is never
/// newer — an upstream tagging mistake must not produce update nags.
pub fn is_newer(current: &str, latest: &str) -> bool {
    match (parse(current), parse(latest)) {
        (Some(cur), Some(new)) => {
            if cur.numbers != new.numbers {
                return new.numbers > cur.numbers;
            }
            // Same release number: a pre-release is not newer than the
            // release; the release is newer than a pre-release.
            match (cur.pre, new.pre) {
                (None, Some(_)) => false,
                (Some(_), None) => true,
                _ => false,
            }
        }
        _ => false,
    }
}

#[derive(Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
struct Parsed {
    numbers: Vec<u64>,
    pre: Option<String>,
}

fn parse(raw: &str) -> Option<Parsed> {
    let raw = normalize_version(raw);
    let (main, pre) = match raw.split_once('-') {
        Some((main, pre)) => (main, Some(pre.to_string())),
        None => (raw.as_str(), None),
    };
    if main.is_empty() {
        return None;
    }
    let mut numbers = Vec::with_capacity(main.split('.').count());
    for segment in main.split('.') {
        if segment.is_empty() || !segment.chars().all(|c| c.is_ascii_digit()) {
            return None;
        }
        numbers.push(segment.parse().ok()?);
    }
    // Cap the shape: 1 to 4 numeric segments, sane magnitudes.
    if numbers.is_empty() || numbers.len() > 4 || numbers.iter().any(|&n| n > 1_000_000) {
        return None;
    }
    Some(Parsed { numbers, pre })
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn plain_versions_compare_numerically() {
        assert!(is_newer("0.0.1", "0.0.2"));
        assert!(is_newer("0.0.2", "0.0.10")); // not lexicographic
        assert!(is_newer("0.0.9", "0.1.0"));
        assert!(is_newer("0.9.9", "1.0.0"));
        assert!(!is_newer("0.0.2", "0.0.2"));
        assert!(!is_newer("0.0.3", "0.0.2"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn v_prefix_and_padding_are_normalized() {
        assert!(is_newer("v0.0.1", "v0.0.2"));
        assert!(is_newer("0.0.1", "v0.0.2"));
        assert!(is_newer("v0.1", "0.1.1")); // 0.1 == 0.1.0.0 padded
        assert!(!is_newer("0.1.0", "0.1"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn pre_releases_rank_below_releases() {
        assert!(!is_newer("0.0.2", "0.0.2-beta.1"));
        assert!(is_newer("0.0.2-beta.1", "0.0.2"));
        assert!(is_newer("0.0.1", "0.0.2-beta.1")); // different number still wins
    }

    #[test]
    fn garbage_never_counts_as_newer() {
        assert!(!is_newer("0.0.2", "latest"));
        assert!(!is_newer("0.0.2", ""));
        assert!(!is_newer("0.0.2", "0.0.x"));
        assert!(!is_newer("0.0.2", "v"));
        assert!(!is_newer("0.0.2", "0.0.2.0.0.0.0")); // too many segments
        assert!(!is_newer("not-a-version", "0.0.3"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn normalize_strips_prefix_once() {
        assert_eq!(normalize_version("v1.2.3"), "1.2.3");
        assert_eq!(normalize_version("V1.2.3"), "1.2.3");
        assert_eq!(normalize_version("1.2.3"), "1.2.3");
        assert_eq!(normalize_version(" v1.2.3 "), "1.2.3");
    }
}
