`crates/rpi-ng-core/tests/fileserver_test.rs`:

```rs
//! File server tests: allowlist enforcement, Range requests, HEAD, encoding.

use std::{net::SocketAddr, path::PathBuf, time::Duration};

use rpi_ng_core::fileserver::FileServer;

async fn start_server() -> (FileServer, SocketAddr) {
    // Bind loopback with an ephemeral port.
    let server = FileServer::start(SocketAddr::from(([127, 0, 0, 1], 0)))
        .await
        .unwrap();
    let bind = server.bind;
    (server, bind)
}

async fn get(
    addr: SocketAddr,
    path: &str,
    headers: &[(&str, &str)],
) -> (u16, Vec<u8>, Option<String>, Option<String>) {
    let mut req = reqwest::Client::builder()
        .no_proxy()
        .timeout(Duration::from_secs(10))
        .build()
        .unwrap()
        .get(format!("http://{addr}{path}"));
    for (k, v) in headers {
        req = req.header(*k, *v);
    }
    let resp = req.send().await.unwrap();
    let status = resp.status().as_u16();
    let content_range = resp
        .headers()
        .get("content-range")
        .map(|v| v.to_str().unwrap().into());
    let accept_ranges = resp
        .headers()
        .get("accept-ranges")
        .map(|v| v.to_str().unwrap().into());
    let body = resp.bytes().await.unwrap().to_vec();
    (status, body, content_range, accept_ranges)
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn serves_registered_file_completely() {
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let path = tmp.path().join("game.pkg");
    let data: Vec<u8> = (0u8..255u8).cycle().take(100_000).collect();
    std::fs::write(&path, &data).unwrap();

    let (server, addr) = start_server().await;
    let id = server.register(path, Some("game.pkg".into()));
    let url = server.url_for(id).unwrap();
    assert!(url.ends_with("/pkg/0/game.pkg"));

    let (status, body, _, accept) = get(addr, &format!("/pkg/{id}/game.pkg"), &[]).await;
    assert_eq!(status, 200);
    assert_eq!(body.len(), 100_000);
    assert_eq!(accept.as_deref(), Some("bytes"));
    assert_eq!(body[..16], data[..16]);
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn unregistered_ids_404() {
    let (server, addr) = start_server().await;
    let _ = server;
    let (status, _, _, _) = get(addr, "/pkg/999/nope.pkg", &[]).await;
    assert_eq!(status, 404);
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn no_traversal_even_with_dots() {
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let secret = tmp.path().join("secret.txt");
    std::fs::write(&secret, "secret").unwrap();
    let pkg = tmp.path().join("a.pkg");
    std::fs::write(&pkg, "pkg-bytes").unwrap();

    let (server, addr) = start_server().await;
    let id = server.register(pkg, None);

    // Unregistered numeric id with traversal-shaped name → 404.
    let (status, _, _, _) = get(addr, "/pkg/999/..%2Fsecret.txt", &[]).await;
    assert_eq!(status, 404);

    // Path not matching any route → 404 (secret never reachable).
    let (status, body, _, _) = get(addr, "/pkg/../secret.txt", &[]).await;
    assert_eq!(status, 404);
    assert!(body != b"secret");

    // A traversal-shaped *name* on a registered id still serves only the
    // registered file — never anything from disk outside the allowlist.
    let (status, body, _, _) = get(addr, &format!("/pkg/{id}/%2e%2e%2fsecret.txt"), &[]).await;
    assert!(status == 200 || status == 404, "status was {status}");
    assert!(body != b"secret", "traversal must never leak other files");
    if status == 200 {
        assert_eq!(body, b"pkg-bytes");
    }
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn range_requests_single_and_suffix() {
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let path = tmp.path().join("game.pkg");
    let data: Vec<u8> = (0u8..=255u8).cycle().take(10_000).collect();
    std::fs::write(&path, &data).unwrap();

    let (server, addr) = start_server().await;
    let id = server.register(path, Some("game.pkg".into()));

    // Middle range.
    let (status, body, cr, _) = get(
        addr,
        &format!("/pkg/{id}/game.pkg"),
        &[("Range", "bytes=100-199")],
    )
    .await;
    assert_eq!(status, 206);
    assert_eq!(body.len(), 100);
    assert_eq!(body[..10], data[100..110]);
    assert_eq!(cr.as_deref(), Some("bytes 100-199/10000"));

    // Open-ended range.
    let (status, body, _, _) = get(
        addr,
        &format!("/pkg/{id}/game.pkg"),
        &[("Range", "bytes=9900-")],
    )
    .await;
    assert_eq!(status, 206);
    assert_eq!(body.len(), 100);

    // Suffix range: last 50 bytes.
    let (status, body, cr, _) = get(
        addr,
        &format!("/pkg/{id}/game.pkg"),
        &[("Range", "bytes=-50")],
    )
    .await;
    assert_eq!(status, 206);
    assert_eq!(body.len(), 50);
    assert_eq!(body, data[9950..]);
    assert_eq!(cr.as_deref(), Some("bytes 9950-9999/10000"));

    // Unsatisfiable range → 416.
    let (status, _, _, _) = get(
        addr,
        &format!("/pkg/{id}/game.pkg"),
        &[("Range", "bytes=99999-")],
    )
    .await;
    assert_eq!(status, 416);

    // Multi-range falls back to a full 200 response (RFC-permitted).
    let (status, body, _, _) = get(
        addr,
        &format!("/pkg/{id}/game.pkg"),
        &[("Range", "bytes=0-1,5-6")],
    )
    .await;
    assert_eq!(status, 200);
    assert_eq!(body.len(), 10_000);
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn head_requests_have_headers_no_body() {
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let path = tmp.path().join("game.pkg");
    std::fs::write(&path, vec![7u8; 4096]).unwrap();

    let (server, addr) = start_server().await;
    let id = server.register(path, Some("game.pkg".into()));

    let resp = reqwest::Client::builder()
        .no_proxy()
        .build()
        .unwrap()
        .head(format!("http://{addr}/pkg/{id}/game.pkg"))
        .send()
        .await
        .unwrap();
    assert_eq!(resp.status().as_u16(), 200);
    assert_eq!(resp.headers().get("content-length").unwrap(), "4096");
    assert_eq!(resp.headers().get("accept-ranges").unwrap(), "bytes");
    let body = resp.bytes().await.unwrap();
    assert!(body.is_empty());
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn url_encoding_escapes_special_characters() {
    let (server, _addr) = start_server().await;
    let id = server.register(
        PathBuf::from("/tmp/my game+.pkg"),
        Some("my game+.pkg".into()),
    );
    let url = server.url_for(id).unwrap();
    assert!(url.contains("my%20game+.pkg"), "url was: {url}");
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn unregister_removes_access() {
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let path = tmp.path().join("game.pkg");
    std::fs::write(&path, vec![1u8; 16]).unwrap();

    let (server, addr) = start_server().await;
    let id = server.register(path, Some("game.pkg".into()));
    let (status, _, _, _) = get(addr, &format!("/pkg/{id}/game.pkg"), &[]).await;
    assert_eq!(status, 200);

    server.unregister(id);
    let (status, _, _, _) = get(addr, &format!("/pkg/{id}/game.pkg"), &[]).await;
    assert_eq!(status, 404);
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn concurrent_requests_stream_correctly() {
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let path = tmp.path().join("game.pkg");
    let data: Vec<u8> = (0u8..=255u8).cycle().take(300_000).collect();
    std::fs::write(&path, &data).unwrap();

    let (server, addr) = start_server().await;
    let id = server.register(path, Some("game.pkg".into()));

    let mut handles = Vec::new();
    for i in 0..4 {
        let url = format!("http://{addr}/pkg/{id}/game.pkg");
        let offset = i * 1000;
        let range = format!("bytes={offset}-{}", offset + 999);
        handles.push(tokio::spawn(async move {
            let resp = reqwest::Client::builder()
                .no_proxy()
                .timeout(Duration::from_secs(15))
                .build()
                .unwrap()
                .get(url)
                .header("Range", range)
                .send()
                .await
                .unwrap();
            assert_eq!(resp.status().as_u16(), 206);
            resp.bytes().await.unwrap().to_vec()
        }));
    }
    // All four requests ran concurrently; collect sequentially (join).
    let mut results = Vec::new();
    for h in handles {
        results.push(h.await.unwrap());
    }
    for (i, body) in results.iter().enumerate() {
        let offset = i * 1000;
        assert_eq!(&body[..10], &data[offset..offset + 10]);
    }
}
```

`crates/rpi-ng-core/tests/pkg_test.rs`:

```rs
//! PKG and SFO parser tests with synthesized fixtures.
//!
//! The fixtures are generated in-memory from the documented layout
//! (`docs/BASELINE.md` §2, §4.7) — no copyrighted sample data is used.

use std::io::Write;

use rpi_ng_core::{pkg, sfo::Sfo};

/// Build a minimal but valid PARAM.SFO with the given string entries.
fn build_sfo(entries: &[(&str, &str)]) -> Vec<u8> {
    let mut keys = Vec::new();
    let mut values = Vec::new();
    let mut index = Vec::new(); // (key_off, fmt, used, max, val_off)

    let key_base = 0x14 + entries.len() * 0x10;
    for (k, v) in entries {
        let key_off = keys.len();
        keys.extend_from_slice(k.as_bytes());
        keys.push(0);
        let val_off = values.len();
        values.extend_from_slice(v.as_bytes());
        values.push(0);
        index.push((
            key_off as u16,
            0x0204u16,
            (v.len() + 1) as u32,
            (v.len() + 1) as u32,
            val_off as u32,
        ));
    }

    let mut out = Vec::new();
    out.extend_from_slice(b"\0PSF");
    out.extend_from_slice(&1u32.to_be_bytes());
    out.extend_from_slice(&(key_base as u32).to_be_bytes());
    out.extend_from_slice(&((key_base + keys.len()) as u32).to_be_bytes());
    out.extend_from_slice(&(entries.len() as u32).to_be_bytes());
    for (k, f, u, m, v) in index {
        out.extend_from_slice(&k.to_be_bytes());
        out.extend_from_slice(&f.to_be_bytes());
        out.extend_from_slice(&u.to_be_bytes());
        out.extend_from_slice(&m.to_be_bytes());
        out.extend_from_slice(&v.to_be_bytes());
    }
    out.extend_from_slice(&keys);
    out.extend_from_slice(&values);
    out
}

/// Build a PKG file (header + entry table + SFO payload) in a temp file.
fn build_pkg(
    dir: &std::path::Path,
    name: &str,
    content_id: &str,
    entries: &[(&str, &str)],
) -> std::path::PathBuf {
    let sfo = build_sfo(entries);

    // Entry table: one PARAM.SFO entry located after the header (0x20 bytes).
    let sfo_offset: u32 = 0x2000;
    let entry_table_offset: u32 = 0x2000 + sfo.len() as u32;
    let mut entry_table = Vec::new();
    entry_table.extend_from_slice(&0x1000u32.to_be_bytes()); // id @0x00
    entry_table.extend_from_slice(&[0u8; 0x0C]); // reserved
    entry_table.extend_from_slice(&sfo_offset.to_be_bytes()); // offset @0x10
    entry_table.extend_from_slice(&(sfo.len() as u32).to_be_bytes()); // size @0x14
    entry_table.extend_from_slice(&[0u8; 0x0C]); // trailing padding to 0x20

    let mut header = vec![0u8; 0x2000];
    header[0..4].copy_from_slice(&pkg::PKG_MAGIC);
    header[0x10..0x14].copy_from_slice(&1u32.to_be_bytes()); // entry_count
    header[0x18..0x1C].copy_from_slice(&entry_table_offset.to_be_bytes());
    let cid = content_id.as_bytes();
    header[0x40..0x40 + cid.len()].copy_from_slice(cid);
    header[0x74..0x78].copy_from_slice(&0x1Au32.to_be_bytes()); // GD
    header[0x430..0x438].copy_from_slice(&0x1000u64.to_be_bytes()); // package_size

    let path = dir.join(name);
    let mut f = std::fs::File::create(&path).unwrap();
    f.write_all(&header).unwrap();
    f.write_all(&sfo).unwrap();
    f.write_all(&entry_table).unwrap();
    // Pad so the file is big enough.
    let cur = 0x2000 + sfo.len() + entry_table.len();
    let target = 0x1000usize; // package_size declared
    if cur < target {
        f.write_all(&vec![0u8; target - cur]).unwrap();
    }
    f.sync_all().unwrap();
    path
}

#[test]
fn parses_full_pkg_with_sfo_metadata() {
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let path = build_pkg(
        tmp.path(),
        "UP0001-CUSA01234_00-GAME000000000000.pkg",
        "UP0001-CUSA01234_00-GAME000000000000",
        &[
            ("TITLE", "Test Game"),
            ("TITLE_01", "Test Game (EN)"),
            ("CONTENT_ID", "UP0001-CUSA01234_00-GAME000000000000"),
            ("CATEGORY", "gd"),
            ("APP_VER", "01.00"),
        ],
    );
    let info = pkg::parse_pkg_file(&path).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(info.content_id, "UP0001-CUSA01234_00-GAME000000000000");
    assert_eq!(info.title_id.as_deref(), Some("CUSA01234"));
    assert_eq!(info.title.as_deref(), Some("Test Game (EN)"));
    assert_eq!(info.app_version.as_deref(), Some("01.00"));
    assert_eq!(info.category.as_deref(), Some("gd"));
    assert!(!info.is_patch);
    assert_eq!(info.header_size, 0x1000);
    assert_eq!(info.content_kind.package_type(), Some("PS4GD"));
}

#[test]
fn detects_patch_flag() {
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let path = build_pkg(
        tmp.path(),
        "patch.pkg",
        "UP0001-CUSA01234_00-PATCH00000000000",
        &[("TITLE", "P")],
    );
    // Flip the FIRST_PATCH content flag bit (offset 0x78).
    let mut buf = std::fs::read(&path).unwrap();
    buf[0x78..0x7C].copy_from_slice(&0x0010_0000u32.to_be_bytes());
    std::fs::write(&path, &buf).unwrap();
    let info = pkg::parse_pkg_file(&path).unwrap();
    assert!(info.is_patch);
}

#[test]
fn rejects_bad_magic() {
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let path = tmp.path().join("bad.pkg");
    std::fs::write(&path, vec![0u8; 0x2000]).unwrap();
    let err = pkg::parse_pkg_file(&path).unwrap_err();
    assert!(matches!(err, pkg::PkgError::BadMagic));
}

#[test]
fn rejects_oversized_entry_count() {
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let path = tmp.path().join("evil.pkg");
    let mut buf = vec![0u8; 0x2000];
    buf[0..4].copy_from_slice(&pkg::PKG_MAGIC);
    buf[0x10..0x14].copy_from_slice(&100_000u32.to_be_bytes()); // absurd entry count
    std::fs::write(&path, &buf).unwrap();
    let err = pkg::parse_pkg_file(&path).unwrap_err();
    assert!(matches!(err, pkg::PkgError::TooManyEntries(_, _)));
}

#[test]
fn rejects_entry_table_out_of_range() {
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let path = tmp.path().join("evil.pkg");
    let mut buf = vec![0u8; 0x2000];
    buf[0..4].copy_from_slice(&pkg::PKG_MAGIC);
    buf[0x10..0x14].copy_from_slice(&1u32.to_be_bytes());
    buf[0x18..0x1C].copy_from_slice(&0xFFFF_F000u32.to_be_bytes()); // wild offset
    std::fs::write(&path, &buf).unwrap();
    let err = pkg::parse_pkg_file(&path).unwrap_err();
    assert!(matches!(err, pkg::PkgError::EntryTableOffset(_)));
}

#[test]
fn sfo_bounds_and_formats() {
    // Truncated header.
    assert!(Sfo::parse(&[0u8; 4]).is_err());
    // Bad magic.
    assert!(Sfo::parse(&[b'X'; 0x14]).is_err());
    // Normal parse with a u32 entry.
    let sfo_buf = build_sfo(&[("TITLE", "Hello"), ("PARENTAL_LEVEL", "1")]);
    let sfo = Sfo::parse(&sfo_buf).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(sfo.get_str("TITLE"), Some("Hello"));
    assert_eq!(sfo.get_str("PARENTAL_LEVEL"), Some("1"));
    // Localized title fallback.
    assert_eq!(sfo.title(7).as_deref(), Some("Hello"));
}

#[test]
fn multipart_name_splitting() {
    assert_eq!(pkg::split_part_name("Game_0.pkg"), ("Game".into(), Some(0)));
    assert_eq!(pkg::split_part_name("Game_1.pkg"), ("Game".into(), Some(1)));
    assert_eq!(
        pkg::split_part_name("Game_12.pkg"),
        ("Game".into(), Some(12))
    );
    assert_eq!(pkg::split_part_name("Game.pkg"), ("Game".into(), None));
    assert_eq!(
        pkg::split_part_name("Game_v2.pkg"),
        ("Game_v2".into(), None)
    );
    assert_eq!(pkg::split_part_name("GAME_3.PKG"), ("GAME".into(), Some(3)));
}
```

`crates/rpi-ng-core/tests/queue_test.rs`:

```rs
//! Queue tests: grouping, duplicate detection, state recovery, persistence.

use std::path::PathBuf;

use rpi_ng_core::{model::ItemState, queue::Queue};

fn write_pkg(dir: &std::path::Path, name: &str, size: u64) -> PathBuf {
    let path = dir.join(name);
    std::fs::write(&path, vec![0u8; size as usize]).unwrap();
    path
}

#[test]
fn groups_multipart_pieces_in_order() {
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let p0 = write_pkg(tmp.path(), "Game_1.pkg", 10);
    let p2 = write_pkg(tmp.path(), "Game_0.pkg", 10);
    let p1 = write_pkg(tmp.path(), "Game_2.pkg", 10);

    let mut q = Queue::default();
    let (added, skipped) = q.add_paths(vec![p0, p1, p2]);
    assert_eq!(added.len(), 1);
    assert!(skipped.is_empty());
    let item = q.get(added[0]).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(item.parts.len(), 3);
    // Ordered by piece index 0,1,2 regardless of insertion order.
    assert_eq!(item.parts[0].file_name, "Game_0.pkg");
    assert_eq!(item.parts[1].file_name, "Game_1.pkg");
    assert_eq!(item.parts[2].file_name, "Game_2.pkg");
    assert!(item.is_multipart());
}

#[test]
fn separate_items_for_unrelated_files() {
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let a = write_pkg(tmp.path(), "Alpha.pkg", 5);
    let b = write_pkg(tmp.path(), "Beta.pkg", 5);
    let mut q = Queue::default();
    let (added, _) = q.add_paths(vec![a, b]);
    assert_eq!(added.len(), 2);
}

#[test]
fn detects_duplicates_by_path() {
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let a = write_pkg(tmp.path(), "Alpha.pkg", 5);
    let mut q = Queue::default();
    let (added, _) = q.add_paths(vec![a.clone()]);
    assert_eq!(added.len(), 1);
    let (added2, skipped) = q.add_paths(vec![a]);
    assert!(added2.is_empty());
    assert_eq!(skipped.len(), 1);
    assert!(skipped[0].contains("duplicate"));
}

#[test]
fn skips_missing_files() {
    let mut q = Queue::default();
    let (added, skipped) = q.add_paths(vec![PathBuf::from("/nonexistent/pkg.pkg")]);
    assert!(added.is_empty());
    assert_eq!(skipped.len(), 1);
}

#[test]
fn requeue_and_clear_finished() {
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let a = write_pkg(tmp.path(), "A.pkg", 5);
    let b = write_pkg(tmp.path(), "B.pkg", 5);
    let mut q = Queue::default();
    let (added, _) = q.add_paths(vec![a, b]);
    let (id_a, id_b) = (added[0], added[1]);

    q.get_mut(id_a).unwrap().state = ItemState::Completed;
    q.get_mut(id_b).unwrap().state = ItemState::Failed;

    assert_eq!(q.clear_finished(), 2);
    assert!(q.items().is_empty());

    // Requeue path.
    let (added, _) = {
        let tmp2 = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        let c = write_pkg(tmp2.path(), "C.pkg", 5);
        q.add_paths(vec![c])
    };
    let id = added[0];
    q.get_mut(id).unwrap().state = ItemState::Failed;
    assert!(q.requeue(id));
    assert_eq!(q.get(id).unwrap().state, ItemState::Queued);
}

#[test]
fn requeue_resets_the_previous_attempts_bookkeeping() {
    // A submit cycle that exhausted its transient-retry budget (attempts
    // grows to MAX_SUBMIT_ATTEMPTS - 1) used to leak into every later Retry:
    // the requeued item then had *zero* automatic submit retries left, so the
    // first transient blip failed it permanently. The stale v2 job id of the
    // old console-side job must go too.
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let a = write_pkg(tmp.path(), "A.pkg", 5);
    let mut q = Queue::default();
    let (added, _) = q.add_paths(vec![a]);
    let id = added[0];

    {
        let item = q.get_mut(id).unwrap();
        item.state = ItemState::Failed;
        item.error = Some("network error".into());
        item.task_id = Some(7);
        item.job_id = Some(3);
        item.attempts = 2; // what a fully-spent submit cycle leaves behind
    }
    assert!(q.requeue(id));
    let item = q.get(id).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(item.state, ItemState::Queued);
    assert_eq!(item.attempts, 0, "a fresh attempt gets a full retry budget");
    assert_eq!(
        item.job_id, None,
        "the old console job id must not carry over"
    );
    assert_eq!(item.task_id, None);
    assert!(item.error.is_none());
    assert!(item.progress.is_none());
}

#[test]
fn move_item_bounds() {
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let paths: Vec<_> = ["A.pkg", "B.pkg", "C.pkg"]
        .iter()
        .map(|n| write_pkg(tmp.path(), n, 5))
        .collect();
    let mut q = Queue::default();
    let (added, _) = q.add_paths(paths);
    let (a, b, c) = (added[0], added[1], added[2]);
    assert!(q.move_item(c, -1)); // C before B
    assert_eq!(
        q.items().iter().map(|i| i.id).collect::<Vec<_>>(),
        vec![a, c, b]
    );
    assert!(!q.move_item(a, -1)); // already first
    assert!(!q.move_item(b, 1)); // already last (after moves: a,c,b)
}

#[test]
fn recovery_marks_active_states_unknown() {
    // Round-trip a queue document with an active item through from_json:
    // Transferring/Installing/Submitting/Preparing must become Unknown —
    // the never-auto-resubmit invariant.
    let doc = r#"{
      "next_item": 2,
      "next_part": 1,
      "items": [
        {"id":1,"parts":[{"id":0,"path":"/tmp/a.pkg","file_name":"a.pkg","size":10,"part_index":null,"pkg":null,"invalid_reason":null}],
         "state":"Transferring","error":null,"task_id":7,"console_title":null,"progress":null,"attempts":0,"added_at":0,"updated_at":0}
      ]
    }"#;
    let q = Queue::from_json(doc).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(q.items().len(), 1);
    assert_eq!(q.items()[0].state, ItemState::Unknown);
    assert_eq!(q.items()[0].task_id, Some(7)); // evidence retained for reconciliation

    // Finished states must survive recovery untouched.
    let doc2 = doc.replace("\"Transferring\"", "\"Completed\"");
    let q2 = Queue::from_json(&doc2).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(q2.items()[0].state, ItemState::Completed);

    // Corrupt documents fail cleanly.
    assert!(Queue::from_json("not json").is_err());
}

#[test]
fn stats_count_states() {
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let paths: Vec<_> = ["A.pkg", "B.pkg", "C.pkg", "D.pkg", "E.pkg"]
        .iter()
        .map(|n| write_pkg(tmp.path(), n, 5))
        .collect();
    let mut q = Queue::default();
    let (added, _) = q.add_paths(paths);
    q.get_mut(added[0]).unwrap().state = ItemState::Completed;
    q.get_mut(added[1]).unwrap().state = ItemState::Failed;
    q.get_mut(added[2]).unwrap().state = ItemState::Unknown;
    q.get_mut(added[3]).unwrap().state = ItemState::Cancelled;
    let stats = q.stats();
    assert_eq!(stats.completed, 1);
    assert_eq!(stats.failed, 1);
    assert_eq!(stats.unknown, 1);
    assert_eq!(stats.cancelled, 1);
    assert_eq!(stats.pending, 1);
}

/// Queue files written before the `task_owner` field existed (v0.0.6 and
/// earlier) must keep loading: the field defaults to `None`, which the
/// engine treats permissively so legacy queues re-attach exactly as before.
#[test]
fn legacy_queue_doc_without_task_owner_loads() {
    let doc = r#"{
        "next_item": 1,
        "next_part": 1,
        "items": [
            {
                "id": 0,
                "parts": [
                    {
                        "id": 0,
                        "path": "/tmp/legacy.pkg",
                        "file_name": "legacy.pkg",
                        "size": 10,
                        "part_index": null
                    }
                ],
                "state": "Unknown",
                "error": null,
                "task_id": 7,
                "job_id": null,
                "console_title": null,
                "progress": null,
                "attempts": 0,
                "added_at": 1,
                "updated_at": 1
            }
        ]
    }"#;
    let q = Queue::from_json(doc).expect("legacy queue doc loads");
    let item = q.get(0).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(item.task_id, Some(7));
    assert_eq!(
        item.task_owner, None,
        "missing owner defaults to permissive"
    );
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Hand-edited queue.json (structural sanitization on load)
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

fn hand_edited_item(id: u64, part_id: u64, path: &str) -> String {
    let file_name = path.rsplit('/').next().unwrap_or(path);
    format!(
        r#"{{"id":{id},"parts":[{{"id":{part_id},"path":"{path}","file_name":"{file_name}","size":10,"part_index":null,"pkg":null,"invalid_reason":null}}],
            "state":"Queued","error":null,"task_id":null,"task_owner":null,"job_id":null,"console_title":null,"progress":null,"attempts":0,"added_at":0,"updated_at":0}}"#
    )
}

fn hand_edited_doc(next_item: u64, next_part: u64, items: &[String]) -> String {
    format!(
        r#"{{"next_item":{next_item},"next_part":{next_part},"items":[{}]}}"#,
        items.join(",")
    )
}

/// A pristine document — everything the app itself writes — reports zero
/// repairs. This pins that sanitization never touches normal files.
#[test]
fn well_formed_doc_reports_no_repairs() {
    let doc = hand_edited_doc(
        3,
        3,
        &[
            hand_edited_item(0, 0, "/tmp/a.pkg"),
            hand_edited_item(1, 1, "/tmp/b.pkg"),
            hand_edited_item(2, 2, "/tmp/c.pkg"),
        ],
    );
    let (_, repairs) = Queue::from_json_reported(&doc).unwrap();
    assert!(repairs.is_empty(), "unexpected repairs: {repairs:?}");
    assert!(repairs.warnings().is_empty());
}

/// The user-iseng case: an entry copy-pasted so two items share one id. The
/// second entry is unreachable (every lookup resolves to the first match) —
/// its buttons would have acted on the wrong row. Keep the first, drop the
/// rest, and say so.
#[test]
fn duplicate_item_ids_are_dropped_first_wins() {
    let doc = hand_edited_doc(
        3,
        2,
        &[
            hand_edited_item(1, 0, "/tmp/real.pkg"),
            hand_edited_item(2, 1, "/tmp/other.pkg"),
            // Copy of item 1 with a different file — must disappear.
            hand_edited_item(1, 1, "/tmp/copy.pkg"),
        ],
    );
    let (q, repairs) = Queue::from_json_reported(&doc).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(q.items().len(), 2);
    assert_eq!(repairs.dropped_duplicate_ids, vec![1]);
    // The survivor of id 1 is the FIRST entry.
    assert_eq!(q.get(1).unwrap().parts[0].file_name, "real.pkg");
    assert_eq!(q.get(2).unwrap().parts[0].file_name, "other.pkg");
    // The warning mentions the id, so the activity panel explains the loss.
    assert!(
        repairs
            .warnings()
            .iter()
            .any(|w| w.contains("duplicate item ids 1"))
    );
}

/// next_item behind an existing id (user renumbered entries): the next add
/// would have reused a live id and corrupted that item. Raised past the max.
#[test]
fn regressed_next_item_is_raised_past_the_highest_id() {
    let doc = hand_edited_doc(
        2, // behind: items already sit at 1..=3
        3,
        &[
            hand_edited_item(1, 0, "/tmp/a.pkg"),
            hand_edited_item(2, 1, "/tmp/b.pkg"),
            hand_edited_item(3, 2, "/tmp/c.pkg"),
        ],
    );
    let (q, repairs) = Queue::from_json_reported(&doc).unwrap();
    assert!(repairs.next_item_repaired);

    // The proof: add a real file and its id must not collide with 1..=3.
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let new = write_pkg(tmp.path(), "New.pkg", 5);
    let mut q = q;
    let (added, _) = q.add_paths(vec![new]);
    assert_eq!(
        added,
        vec![4],
        "fresh ids continue past the highest existing"
    );
    // And the new item does not shadow any restored one.
    assert!(q.get(1).is_some());
    assert!(q.get(2).is_some());
    assert!(q.get(3).is_some());
    assert_eq!(q.items().len(), 4);
}

/// Same repair for the part counter (observable on the next add).
#[test]
fn regressed_next_part_is_raised_past_the_highest_part_id() {
    let doc = hand_edited_doc(
        2,
        0, // behind: parts already sit at 5..=6
        &[
            hand_edited_item(1, 5, "/tmp/a.pkg"),
            hand_edited_item(2, 6, "/tmp/b.pkg"),
        ],
    );
    let (q, repairs) = Queue::from_json_reported(&doc).unwrap();
    assert!(repairs.next_part_repaired);
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let new = write_pkg(tmp.path(), "New.pkg", 5);
    let mut q = q;
    let (added, _) = q.add_paths(vec![new]);
    let part_id = q.get(added[0]).unwrap().parts[0].id;
    assert!(
        part_id > 6,
        "new part ids continue past the highest existing"
    );
}

/// An entry with no files would be submitted as an install with zero URLs;
/// it stays visible as Invalid (with a reason) instead of vanishing silently.
#[test]
fn partless_item_is_marked_invalid_not_dropped() {
    let doc = r#"{"next_item":2,"next_part":1,"items":[
        {"id":1,"parts":[],"state":"Queued","error":null,"task_id":null,"task_owner":null,"job_id":null,"console_title":null,"progress":null,"attempts":0,"added_at":0,"updated_at":0}
    ]}"#;
    let (q, repairs) = Queue::from_json_reported(doc).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(q.items().len(), 1, "the entry stays visible");
    let item = q.get(1).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(item.state, ItemState::Invalid);
    assert!(item.error.as_deref().unwrap().contains("no files"));
    assert_eq!(repairs.invalidated_empty_items, vec![1]);
}

/// A hand-entered u64::MAX id must not panic in debug builds (overflow) —
/// the counter saturates instead.
#[test]
fn saturating_counter_repair_on_max_id() {
    let doc = hand_edited_doc(0, 0, &[hand_edited_item(u64::MAX, 0, "/tmp/max.pkg")]);
    let (q, repairs) = Queue::from_json_reported(&doc).unwrap();
    assert!(repairs.next_item_repaired);
    assert_eq!(q.items().len(), 1);
}

/// Repairs survive a save/load round trip: what the app writes back after
/// fixing a hand-edited file is pristine (second load reports nothing).
#[test]
fn sanitized_queue_round_trips_clean() {
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let doc = hand_edited_doc(
        1,
        0,
        &[
            hand_edited_item(1, 5, "/tmp/a.pkg"),
            hand_edited_item(1, 6, "/tmp/dup.pkg"),
        ],
    );
    let (q, repairs) = Queue::from_json_reported(&doc).unwrap();
    assert!(!repairs.is_empty());
    q.save_in(Some(tmp.path())).unwrap();

    let (q2, repairs2) = Queue::load_in_reported(Some(tmp.path()));
    assert!(
        repairs2.is_empty(),
        "second load must be pristine: {repairs2:?}"
    );
    assert_eq!(q2.items().len(), 1);
    assert_eq!(q2.items()[0].id, 1);
}
```

`crates/rpi-ng-core/tests/rpi_mock.rs`:

```rs
//! Integration tests against a faithful mock of the RPI (flatz) backend.
//!
//! The mock reproduces the documented behavior of the original payload
//! (`docs/BASELINE.md`), including its **invalid JSON with unquoted hex
//! literals** — so these tests lock in the quirks real consoles exhibit.
//! Mocks are protocol-conformance evidence only, **not** evidence of
//! real-console compatibility.

use std::{
    net::SocketAddr,
    sync::{Arc, Mutex},
    time::Duration,
};

use axum::{
    Json, Router,
    body::Body,
    extract::{Path, State},
    http::{StatusCode, header},
    response::{IntoResponse, Response},
    routing::{get, post},
};
use serde_json::{Value, json};

use rpi_ng_core::{
    engine::Command,
    model::{Event, ItemState, Snapshot},
    rpi::{RpiClient, RpiError},
    settings::{FailurePolicy, Settings, ThemeMode},
};

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Mock console
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq)]
enum Dialect {
    /// The original payload: unquoted hex literals (invalid JSON).
    BrokenHex,
    /// Forks that emit strict JSON.
    Strict,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]
struct MockTask {
    total: u64,
    transferred: u64,
    local_copy: i32,
    error: i64,
}

/// A v2 job, mirroring the NextGen task manager's in-memory registry:
/// jobs persist (with terminal states) until the "app restarts".
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
struct MockJob {
    id: i64,
    task_id: i64,
    state: String,
    total: u64,
    transferred: u64,
    title: String,
    content_id: String,
    /// Simulated console-side pause: jobs paused via the op endpoint stop
    /// advancing on polls.
    paused: bool,
}

#[derive(Debug)]
struct MockState {
    next_task_id: i64,
    tasks: std::collections::HashMap<i64, MockTask>,
    dialect: Dialect,
    /// Advance transferred by this much per progress poll.
    step: u64,
    /// On install, actually fetch the first package URL's header (Range),
    /// like the real prerequisite fetcher does.
    fetch_urls: bool,
    /// Captured install requests for assertions.
    installs: Vec<Value>,
    /// Content id → task id map for find_task.
    by_content: std::collections::HashMap<String, i64>,
    /// When set, install answers with this failure code.
    fail_installs: Option<i64>,
    /// When set, install answers success WITHOUT a task id (v3.x fork
    /// dialect).
    omit_task_id: bool,
    /// Serve the v2 API (NextGen-class backend) alongside the legacy one.
    v2: bool,
    next_job_id: i64,
    jobs: std::collections::HashMap<i64, MockJob>,
    /// When set, v2 install reports task_id -1 (already installed).
    already_installed: bool,
    /// Count POSTs seen on /api/v2/jobs/<id>/<op> — proves the client
    /// exercised the POST-first fallback (NextGen answers them with 404).
    v2_op_posts: std::collections::HashMap<String, usize>,
    /// Delay before /api/install answers — widens the engine's submit
    /// window so submit-window regressions are deterministic.
    install_delay_ms: u64,
    /// Task ids that received a stop_task call (best-effort cleanup proof).
    stopped_tasks: Vec<i64>,
}

struct MockConsole {
    state: Arc<Mutex<MockState>>,
    addr: SocketAddr,
}

impl MockConsole {
    async fn start(dialect: Dialect) -> Self {
        Self::start_opt(dialect, false).await
    }

    /// Start with the v2 API enabled (NextGen-class backend).
    async fn start_v2() -> Self {
        Self::start_opt(Dialect::Strict, true).await
    }

    async fn start_opt(dialect: Dialect, v2: bool) -> Self {
        let state = Arc::new(Mutex::new(MockState {
            next_task_id: 1,
            tasks: Default::default(),
            dialect,
            step: 1024 * 1024,
            fetch_urls: true,
            installs: Vec::new(),
            by_content: Default::default(),
            fail_installs: None,
            omit_task_id: false,
            v2,
            next_job_id: 1,
            jobs: Default::default(),
            already_installed: false,
            v2_op_posts: Default::default(),
            install_delay_ms: 0,
            stopped_tasks: Vec::new(),
        }));
        let app = Router::new()
            .route("/", get(v2_system))
            .route("/api", get(unsupported))
            .route("/api/status", get(status))
            .route("/api/is_exists", post(is_exists))
            .route("/api/install", post(install))
            .route("/api/get_task_progress", post(progress))
            .route("/api/find_task", post(find_task))
            .route("/api/pause_task", post(ok_task))
            .route("/api/resume_task", post(ok_task))
            .route("/api/stop_task", post(stop_task))
            .route("/api/start_task", post(ok_task))
            .route("/api/unregister_task", post(ok_task))
            .route("/api/v2/system", get(v2_system))
            .route("/api/v2/install", post(v2_install))
            .route("/api/v2/jobs", get(v2_jobs))
            .route(
                "/api/v2/jobs/{id}/{op}",
                post(v2_op_post_404).get(v2_job_op),
            )
            .with_state(Arc::clone(&state));

        let listener = tokio::net::TcpListener::bind(SocketAddr::from(([127, 0, 0, 1], 0)))
            .await
            .unwrap();
        let addr = listener.local_addr().unwrap();
        tokio::spawn(async move {
            axum::serve(listener, app).await.unwrap();
        });
        Self { state, addr }
    }

    fn installs(&self) -> Vec<Value> {
        self.state.lock().unwrap().installs.clone()
    }

    /// Captured v2 install bodies (`url` / `urls` requests only).
    fn v2_installs(&self) -> Vec<Value> {
        self.state
            .lock()
            .unwrap()
            .installs
            .iter()
            .filter(|v| v.get("urls").is_some() || v.get("url").is_some())
            .cloned()
            .collect()
    }

    fn tasks(&self) -> std::collections::HashMap<i64, MockTask> {
        self.state.lock().unwrap().tasks.clone()
    }

    fn jobs(&self) -> Vec<MockJob> {
        let mut jobs: Vec<MockJob> = self.state.lock().unwrap().jobs.values().cloned().collect();
        jobs.sort_by_key(|j| j.id);
        jobs
    }

    fn v2_op_posts(&self, op: &str) -> usize {
        *self.state.lock().unwrap().v2_op_posts.get(op).unwrap_or(&0)
    }

    fn stopped_tasks(&self) -> Vec<i64> {
        self.state.lock().unwrap().stopped_tasks.clone()
    }
}

async fn unsupported() -> Response {
    // The original answers GET /api with 400 + "Unsupported method" (this is
    // what ecosystem senders probe for).
    (
        StatusCode::BAD_REQUEST,
        [(header::CONTENT_TYPE, "text/plain")],
        "Unsupported method",
    )
        .into_response()
}

async fn status() -> Response {
    // Newer receivers answer JSON; the original answers HTML → unsupported.
    Json(json!({"busy": false, "active": 0})).into_response()
}

async fn is_exists(Json(_body): Json<Value>) -> Response {
    Json(json!({"status": "success", "exists": "false"})).into_response()
}

async fn find_task(
    State(state): State<Arc<Mutex<MockState>>>,
    Json(body): Json<Value>,
) -> Response {
    let cid = body["content_id"].as_str().unwrap_or_default().to_string();
    let st = state.lock().unwrap();
    match st.by_content.get(&cid) {
        Some(id) => Json(json!({"status": "success", "task_id": id})).into_response(),
        None => fail_body(&st.dialect, 0x8099_0019),
    }
}

async fn ok_task(Json(_body): Json<Value>) -> Response {
    Json(json!({"status": "success"})).into_response()
}

/// Like ok_task, but records the stopped task id — evidence that the
/// engine's best-effort remote cleanup actually reached the console.
async fn stop_task(
    State(state): State<Arc<Mutex<MockState>>>,
    Json(body): Json<Value>,
) -> Response {
    if let Some(id) = body["task_id"].as_i64() {
        state.lock().unwrap().stopped_tasks.push(id);
    }
    Json(json!({"status": "success"})).into_response()
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// v2 API (NextGen-class mock)
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// The fork's 404 body for unknown paths.
fn not_found_text() -> Response {
    (
        StatusCode::NOT_FOUND,
        [(header::CONTENT_TYPE, "text/plain")],
        "Page not found",
    )
        .into_response()
}

async fn v2_system(State(state): State<Arc<Mutex<MockState>>>) -> Response {
    let st = state.lock().unwrap();
    if !st.v2 {
        return not_found_text();
    }
    Json(json!({
        "status": "success",
        "data": {
            "name": "Remote PKG Installer NextGen",
            "version": "0.0.2-mock",
            "title_id": "RPIN00001",
            "ip": "127.0.0.1",
            "port": 12800,
            "network": true,
            "storage": {"free": 274877906944u64, "total": 500107862016u64},
            "active_jobs": st.jobs.len() as i64
        }
    }))
    .into_response()
}

async fn v2_install(
    State(state): State<Arc<Mutex<MockState>>>,
    Json(body): Json<Value>,
) -> Response {
    {
        let mut st = state.lock().unwrap();
        st.installs.push(body.clone());
        if !st.v2 {
            return not_found_text();
        }
        if let Some(code) = st.fail_installs {
            // v2 parameter/installer failures: HTTP 500 + error string.
            return (
                StatusCode::INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR,
                Json(json!({"status": "fail", "error": format!("mock failure 0x{code:X}")})),
            )
                .into_response();
        }
    }
    let urls: Vec<String> = body["urls"]
        .as_array()
        .map(|a| {
            a.iter()
                .map(|v| v.as_str().unwrap_or_default().to_string())
                .collect()
        })
        .or_else(|| body["url"].as_str().map(|u| vec![u.to_string()]))
        .unwrap_or_default();
    if urls.is_empty() {
        return (
            StatusCode::INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR,
            Json(json!({"status": "fail", "error": "Provide 'url', 'urls' or 'ref_pkg_url'."})),
        )
            .into_response();
    }

    // Prerequisite fetch, like the console-side installer performs.
    let fetch_urls = state.lock().unwrap().fetch_urls;
    let total = if fetch_urls {
        match fetch_range_total(&urls[0]).await {
            Some(t) => t,
            None => {
                return (
                    StatusCode::INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR,
                    Json(json!({"status": "fail", "error": "prerequisite fetch failed"})),
                )
                    .into_response();
            }
        }
    } else {
        10 * 1024 * 1024
    };

    let mut st = state.lock().unwrap();
    let job_id = st.next_job_id;
    st.next_job_id += 1;
    if st.already_installed {
        let title = "Mock Game".to_string();
        st.jobs.insert(
            job_id,
            MockJob {
                id: job_id,
                task_id: -1,
                state: "already_installed".into(),
                total: 0,
                transferred: 0,
                title: title.clone(),
                content_id: format!("CONTENT-ID-V2-{job_id}"),
                paused: false,
            },
        );
        return Json(json!({
            "status": "success",
            "data": {"job_id": job_id, "task_id": -1, "title": title,
                     "content_id": format!("CONTENT-ID-V2-{job_id}"), "package_size": 0}
        }))
        .into_response();
    }
    let task_id = st.next_task_id;
    st.next_task_id += 1;
    let title = "Mock Game".to_string();
    let content_id = format!("CONTENT-ID-{task_id}");
    st.tasks.insert(
        task_id,
        MockTask {
            total,
            transferred: 0,
            local_copy: 0,
            error: 0,
        },
    );
    st.by_content.insert(content_id.clone(), task_id);
    st.jobs.insert(
        job_id,
        MockJob {
            id: job_id,
            task_id,
            state: "running".into(),
            total,
            transferred: 0,
            title: title.clone(),
            content_id,
            paused: false,
        },
    );
    Json(json!({
        "status": "success",
        "data": {"job_id": job_id, "task_id": task_id, "title": title,
                 "content_id": format!("CONTENT-ID-{task_id}"), "package_size": total}
    }))
    .into_response()
}

fn job_json(j: &MockJob) -> Value {
    let percent = (j.transferred * 100)
        .checked_div(j.total.max(1))
        .unwrap_or(0)
        .min(100) as i64;
    json!({
        "id": j.id,
        "bgft_task_id": j.task_id,
        "state": j.state,
        "title": j.title,
        "content_id": j.content_id,
        "app_ver": "01.00",
        "content_type": "PS4GD",
        "pieces": 1,
        "package_size": j.total,
        "transferred": j.transferred,
        "length_total": j.total,
        "percent": percent,
        "speed_bps": 11258991,
        "is_patch": false,
        "error": 0,
        "rest_sec": 27,
        "num_index": 0,
        "num_total": 1
    })
}

async fn v2_jobs(State(state): State<Arc<Mutex<MockState>>>) -> Response {
    let mut st = state.lock().unwrap();
    if !st.v2 {
        return not_found_text();
    }
    // Advance running jobs, like the console's 1s taskman poll.
    let step = st.step;
    for job in st.jobs.values_mut() {
        if job.state == "running" && !job.paused && job.transferred < job.total {
            job.transferred = (job.transferred + step).min(job.total);
            if job.transferred >= job.total {
                job.state = "completed".into();
            }
        }
    }
    let jobs: Vec<Value> = st.jobs.values().map(job_json).collect();
    Json(json!({"status": "success", "data": {"jobs": jobs}})).into_response()
}

/// Reproduces the NextGen routing quirk exactly: the POST router misses
/// `/api/v2/jobs/<id>/<op>`, so POST answers 404 — only GET performs the op.
async fn v2_op_post_404(
    State(state): State<Arc<Mutex<MockState>>>,
    Path((_id, op)): Path<(i64, String)>,
) -> Response {
    {
        let mut st = state.lock().unwrap();
        if !st.v2 {
            return not_found_text();
        }
        *st.v2_op_posts.entry(op).or_insert(0) += 1;
    }
    not_found_text()
}

async fn v2_job_op(
    State(state): State<Arc<Mutex<MockState>>>,
    Path((id, op)): Path<(i64, String)>,
) -> Response {
    let mut st = state.lock().unwrap();
    if !st.v2 {
        return not_found_text();
    }
    let Some(job) = st.jobs.get_mut(&id) else {
        return Json(json!({"status": "fail", "error_code": 0x80990019i64})).into_response();
    };
    match op.as_str() {
        "pause" => {
            job.paused = true;
            job.state = "paused".into();
        }
        "resume" => {
            job.paused = false;
            if job.state == "paused" {
                job.state = "running".into();
            }
        }
        "stop" => {
            job.state = "stopped".into();
        }
        _ => {
            return (
                StatusCode::BAD_REQUEST,
                Json(json!({"status": "fail", "error": "Unknown operation"})),
            )
                .into_response();
        }
    }
    Json(json!({"status": "success"})).into_response()
}

async fn install(State(state): State<Arc<Mutex<MockState>>>, Json(body): Json<Value>) -> Response {
    let delay_ms = state.lock().unwrap().install_delay_ms;
    if delay_ms > 0 {
        // Simulate a slow console answer: the engine's submit window is
        // wide open while this is pending.
        tokio::time::sleep(Duration::from_millis(delay_ms)).await;
    }
    {
        let mut st = state.lock().unwrap();
        st.installs.push(body.clone());
        if let Some(code) = st.fail_installs {
            return fail_body(&st.dialect, code);
        }
    }
    let urls: Vec<String> = body["packages"]
        .as_array()
        .map(|a| {
            a.iter()
                .map(|v| v.as_str().unwrap_or_default().to_string())
                .collect()
        })
        .unwrap_or_default();
    if urls.is_empty() || body["type"].as_str() != Some("direct") {
        return fail_body(&state.lock().unwrap().dialect, 0x8099_0007);
    }

    // Prerequisite fetch: range-request the first 0x2000 bytes of piece 0,
    // exactly like the real payload (docs/BASELINE.md §2).
    let total = if state.lock().unwrap().fetch_urls {
        match fetch_range_total(&urls[0]).await {
            Some(t) => t,
            None => return fail_body(&state.lock().unwrap().dialect, 0x8099_000A),
        }
    } else {
        10 * 1024 * 1024
    };

    let mut st = state.lock().unwrap();
    let task_id = st.next_task_id;
    st.next_task_id += 1;
    st.tasks.insert(
        task_id,
        MockTask {
            total,
            transferred: 0,
            local_copy: 0,
            error: 0,
        },
    );
    st.by_content
        .insert(format!("CONTENT-ID-{task_id}"), task_id);
    if st.omit_task_id {
        return Json(json!({"status": "success"})).into_response();
    }
    Json(json!({"status": "success", "task_id": task_id, "title": "Mock Game"})).into_response()
}

async fn progress(State(state): State<Arc<Mutex<MockState>>>, Json(body): Json<Value>) -> Response {
    let task_id = body["task_id"].as_i64().unwrap_or(-1);
    let dialect = state.lock().unwrap().dialect;
    let mut st = state.lock().unwrap();
    // Advance the simulated download on each poll.
    let step = st.step;
    let (total, transferred, copy, error) = {
        let Some(task) = st.tasks.get_mut(&task_id) else {
            return fail_body(&dialect, 0x8099_0019);
        };
        if task.error == 0 && task.transferred < task.total {
            task.transferred = (task.transferred + step).min(task.total);
            if task.transferred >= task.total {
                task.local_copy = 50; // copy phase begins
            }
        } else if task.local_copy > 0 && task.local_copy < 100 {
            task.local_copy = 100;
        }
        (task.total, task.transferred, task.local_copy, task.error)
    };
    let rest = if total > transferred {
        (total - transferred) / step.max(1)
    } else {
        0
    };

    match dialect {
        Dialect::Strict => Json(json!({
            "status": "success",
            "bits": 32,
            "error": error,
            "length": total,
            "transferred": transferred,
            "length_total": total,
            "transferred_total": transferred,
            "num_index": 0,
            "num_total": 1,
            "rest_sec": rest,
            "rest_sec_total": rest,
            "preparing_percent": 0,
            "local_copy_percent": copy,
        }))
        .into_response(),
        Dialect::BrokenHex => {
            // Byte-for-byte the original payload's format string (invalid JSON).
            let body = format!(
                "{{ \"status\": \"success\", \"bits\": 0x{bits:X}, \"error\": {error}, \"length\": 0x{len:X}, \"transferred\": 0x{tr:X}, \"length_total\": 0x{lt:X}, \"transferred_total\": 0x{tt:X}, \"num_index\": 0, \"num_total\": 1, \"rest_sec\": {rest}, \"rest_sec_total\": {rest}, \"preparing_percent\": 0, \"local_copy_percent\": {copy} }}\n",
                bits = 32u32,
                error = error,
                len = total,
                tr = transferred,
                lt = total,
                tt = transferred,
                rest = rest,
                copy = copy,
            );
            Response::builder()
                .status(StatusCode::OK)
                .header(header::CONTENT_TYPE, "application/json")
                .body(Body::from(body))
                .unwrap()
        }
    }
}

fn fail_body(dialect: &Dialect, code: i64) -> Response {
    match dialect {
        Dialect::Strict => Json(json!({"status": "fail", "error_code": code})).into_response(),
        Dialect::BrokenHex => Response::builder()
            .status(StatusCode::OK)
            .header(header::CONTENT_TYPE, "application/json")
            .body(Body::from(format!(
                "{{ \"status\": \"fail\", \"error_code\": 0x{code:08X} }}\n"
            )))
            .unwrap(),
    }
}

/// Fetch `bytes=0-8191` of a URL and return the total size from Content-Range.
async fn fetch_range_total(url: &str) -> Option<u64> {
    let client = reqwest::Client::builder()
        .no_proxy()
        .timeout(Duration::from_secs(10))
        .build()
        .ok()?;
    let resp = client
        .get(url)
        .header("Range", "bytes=0-8191")
        .send()
        .await
        .ok()?;
    if resp.status().as_u16() != 206 {
        return None;
    }
    let cr = resp
        .headers()
        .get("content-range")?
        .to_str()
        .ok()?
        .to_string();
    resp.bytes().await.ok()?;
    cr.split('/').nth(1)?.parse().ok()
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Client-level protocol tests
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

fn client_for(addr: SocketAddr) -> RpiClient {
    RpiClient::new(addr, Duration::from_secs(5))
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn probe_recognizes_rpi_dialect() {
    let mock = MockConsole::start(Dialect::BrokenHex).await;
    let probe = client_for(mock.addr).probe().await.unwrap();
    assert_eq!(probe.kind, rpi_ng_core::model::BackendKind::Rpi);
    assert!(probe.caps.install_direct);
    assert!(probe.caps.status_endpoint);
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn install_and_progress_with_broken_hex_dialect() {
    let mock = MockConsole::start(Dialect::BrokenHex).await;
    let client = client_for(mock.addr);

    // Serve a small file so the prerequisite fetch succeeds.
    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let pkg_path = tmp.path().join("game.pkg");
    std::fs::write(&pkg_path, vec![0u8; 256 * 1024]).unwrap();
    let server = rpi_ng_core::fileserver::FileServer::start(SocketAddr::from(([127, 0, 0, 1], 0)))
        .await
        .unwrap();
    let id = server.register(pkg_path, Some("game.pkg".into()));
    let url = server.url_for(id).unwrap();

    let resp = client.install_direct(&[url]).await.unwrap();
    assert_eq!(resp.status, "success");
    assert!(resp.task_id > 0);
    assert_eq!(resp.title.as_deref(), Some("Mock Game"));

    // Poll until completion; the progress bodies are invalid JSON with hex.
    let mut completed = false;
    for _ in 0..20 {
        let pv = client.task_progress(resp.task_id).await.unwrap();
        if pv.local_copy_percent >= 100 && pv.transferred >= pv.total && pv.total > 0 {
            completed = true;
            break;
        }
        tokio::time::sleep(Duration::from_millis(10)).await;
    }
    assert!(completed, "mock task should complete through polls");
    let last = client.task_progress(resp.task_id).await.unwrap();
    assert_eq!(last.total, 256 * 1024);
    assert_eq!(last.percent, 100.0);
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn install_with_strict_dialect() {
    let mock = MockConsole::start(Dialect::Strict).await;
    let client = client_for(mock.addr);
    // Unreachable package URL → the mock's prerequisite fetch fails →
    // the backend surfaces the console-side error code.
    let err = client
        .install_direct(&["http://127.0.0.1:1/pkg".into()])
        .await
        .unwrap_err();
    match err {
        RpiError::BackendError { code, .. } => assert_eq!(code, 0x8099_000A),
        other => panic!("expected BackendError, got {other:?}"),
    }
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn backend_failures_surface_error_codes() {
    let mock = MockConsole::start(Dialect::BrokenHex).await;
    mock.state.lock().unwrap().fail_installs = Some(0x8099_0007);
    let client = client_for(mock.addr);
    let err = client
        .install_direct(&["http://127.0.0.1:1/x.pkg".into()])
        .await
        .unwrap_err();
    match err {
        RpiError::BackendError { code, .. } => assert_eq!(code, 0x8099_0007),
        other => panic!("expected BackendError, got {other:?}"),
    }
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn unknown_task_maps_to_task_gone() {
    let mock = MockConsole::start(Dialect::BrokenHex).await;
    let client = client_for(mock.addr);
    let err = client.task_progress(999).await.unwrap_err();
    match err {
        RpiError::BackendError { code, .. } => assert_eq!(code, 0x8099_0019),
        other => panic!("expected BackendError, got {other:?}"),
    }
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn connection_refused_is_transparent() {
    // Bind then drop a listener to get a refused port.
    let listener = tokio::net::TcpListener::bind(SocketAddr::from(([127, 0, 0, 1], 0)))
        .await
        .unwrap();
    let addr = listener.local_addr().unwrap();
    drop(listener);
    let client = RpiClient::new(addr, Duration::from_secs(2));
    let err = client.is_exists("CUSA00000").await.unwrap_err();
    // The concrete transport error differs by platform (RST vs silently
    // dropped SYN → timeout on some Windows setups); what must hold is that
    // the failure is classified as transient and retryable.
    assert!(
        err.is_transient(),
        "error should be transient, was: {err:?}"
    );
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn pause_resume_roundtrip() {
    let mock = MockConsole::start(Dialect::Strict).await;
    let client = client_for(mock.addr);
    // No task registered; the mock answers success for lifecycle commands.
    client.pause_task(1).await.unwrap();
    client.resume_task(1).await.unwrap();
    client.stop_task(1).await.unwrap();
    client.unregister_task(1).await.unwrap();
}
```
