`crates/rpi-ng/build.rs`:

```rs
//! Embeds the brand icon and version metadata into the Windows executable's
//! resource section, so Explorer, the taskbar and jumplists show the app icon
//! before any window exists. The other platforms get their icon differently:
//! at runtime from `assets/icon_256.png` (Windows/X11 title bar, see
//! `main.rs`) or from the packaging assets (`packaging/`, macOS .app bundle
//! and Linux desktop integration) — see `docs/BUILDING.md`.

fn main() {
    // build.rs also runs while Linux/macOS CI builds the workspace; only
    // Windows binaries carry a Win32 resource section.
    let target_os = std::env::var("CARGO_CFG_TARGET_OS").unwrap_or_default();
    if target_os == "windows" {
        let mut res = winresource::WindowsResource::new();
        res.set_icon("assets/icon.ico");
        let version = std::env::var("CARGO_PKG_VERSION").unwrap_or_default();
        res.set(
            "FileDescription",
            "Remote Package Installer NG - PS4 remote PKG installer",
        );
        res.set("ProductName", "Remote Package Installer NG");
        res.set("ProductVersion", &version);
        res.set("FileVersion", &version);
        res.compile()
            .expect("failed to embed the Windows icon resource");
    }
    println!("cargo:rerun-if-changed=build.rs");
    println!("cargo:rerun-if-changed=assets/icon.ico");
}

```

`crates/rpi-ng/src/app.rs`:

```rs
//! Top-level application: layout, engine wiring, first-run experience.

use std::{collections::VecDeque, path::PathBuf, time::Duration};

use eframe::egui;

use rpi_ng_core::{
    Command, EngineHandle,
    fileserver::ServerCheck,
    model::{ConsoleCandidate, ConsoleStatus, Event, ItemState, LogLevel, QueueItem, Snapshot},
    settings::{Settings, ThemeMode},
    update::UpdateInfo,
};

use crate::{
    connect_view, dialogs, log_view, palette, queue_view, servercheck::ServerChecker,
    settings_view, updater::Updater,
};

const MAX_LOG_LINES: usize = 500;

/// State of the in-app update check.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
enum UpdateStatus {
    Idle,
    Checking,
    UpToDate,
    Available(UpdateInfo),
    Failed(String),
}

pub struct App {
    engine: Option<EngineHandle>,
    pub(crate) settings: Settings,
    /// Editable copy shown while the settings window is open. It must live
    /// here (not as a per-frame local) or every edit is dropped before the
    /// user can press Save — that was the v0.0.1 settings bug.
    pub(crate) settings_draft: Option<Settings>,
    pub(crate) snapshot: Snapshot,
    pub(crate) logs: VecDeque<(u64, LogLevel, String)>,
    /// Latest discovery results (empty until a scan finishes).
    pub(crate) discovered: Vec<ConsoleCandidate>,
    pub(crate) discovery_running: bool,
    pub(crate) manual_addr: String,
    pub(crate) search: String,
    show_settings: bool,
    show_log: bool,
    show_about: bool,
    /// In-app update checker (background thread + polled channel).
    updater: Updater,
    update_status: UpdateStatus,
    show_update_window: bool,
    /// File-server self-check ("Check File Server" button).
    pub(crate) server_checker: ServerChecker,
    /// Outcome of the most recent self-check, shown as a status chip.
    pub(crate) server_check_result: Option<rpi_ng_core::fileserver::ServerCheck>,
    pub(crate) dialog: Option<dialogs::DialogPick>,
    /// Folder awaiting a recursive/flat choice.
    pub(crate) pending_folder: Option<PathBuf>,
    /// Show the connection screen even though a console is connected.
    pub(crate) force_connect_view: bool,
}

impl App {
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        // Resolve/create/migrate the data dir BEFORE loading anything, so a
        // first run on an upgraded install picks up the old files.
        let data_dir = rpi_ng_core::settings::init_data_dir();
        let settings = Settings::load();
        let auto_discovery = settings.discovery_enabled;
        let auto_update_check = settings.auto_check_updates;
        let engine = rpi_ng_core::spawn(settings.clone());
        // Kick off a discovery sweep on first run if enabled.
        if auto_discovery {
            engine.send(Command::Discover);
        }
        let mut updater = Updater::new();
        let mut update_status = UpdateStatus::Idle;
        if auto_update_check {
            updater.kick();
            update_status = UpdateStatus::Checking;
        }
        // First log line: where the config lives, so users can find (and
        // back up) their settings — the app is portable, they travel with
        // the executable's folder.
        let mut logs = VecDeque::new();
        let location = match &data_dir {
            Some(dir) => format!("Settings and the queue are stored in {}", dir.display()),
            None => {
                "Settings storage could not be resolved. Changes will not".to_string()
                    + " persist across restarts"
            }
        };
        logs.push_back((rpi_ng_core::model::unix_ms(), LogLevel::Info, location));
        Self {
            engine: Some(engine),
            settings,
            settings_draft: None,
            snapshot: Snapshot::default(),
            logs,
            discovered: Vec::new(),
            discovery_running: auto_discovery,
            manual_addr: String::new(),
            search: String::new(),
            show_settings: false,
            show_log: true,
            show_about: false,
            updater,
            update_status,
            show_update_window: false,
            server_checker: ServerChecker::new(),
            server_check_result: None,
            dialog: None,
            pending_folder: None,
            force_connect_view: false,
        }
    }

    fn engine(&self) -> &EngineHandle {
        self.engine.as_ref().expect("engine lives until on_exit")
    }

    pub(crate) fn send(&self, cmd: Command) {
        self.engine().send(cmd);
    }

    fn apply_theme(&self, ctx: &egui::Context) {
        // Live preview: while the settings window is open, render with the
        // draft's theme so the user sees the change before pressing Save.
        let theme = self
            .settings_draft
            .as_ref()
            .map_or(self.settings.theme, |draft| draft.theme);
        let pref = match theme {
            ThemeMode::System => egui::ThemePreference::System,
            ThemeMode::Light => egui::ThemePreference::Light,
            ThemeMode::Dark => egui::ThemePreference::Dark,
        };
        ctx.set_theme(pref);
        // egui's dark weak text (text color x 0.6) is ~2.3:1 on the dark
        // panel, far below WCAG AA. Raising the alpha keeps a hint of
        // hierarchy while staying readable (see palette.rs tests). The light
        // theme keeps egui's default, which passes comfortably.
        ctx.style_mut_of(egui::Theme::Dark, |s| {
            s.visuals.weak_text_alpha = palette::DARK_WEAK_TEXT_ALPHA;
        });
    }

    fn poll_engine(&mut self) -> bool {
        let mut busy = false;
        for ev in self.engine().poll_events() {
            match ev {
                Event::Snapshot(s) => {
                    busy |= s.engine_busy;
                    self.snapshot = s;
                }
                Event::Log(level, msg) => {
                    self.logs
                        .push_back((rpi_ng_core::model::unix_ms(), level, msg));
                    while self.logs.len() > MAX_LOG_LINES {
                        self.logs.pop_front();
                    }
                }
                Event::DiscoveryDone(found) => {
                    self.discovered = found;
                    self.discovery_running = false;
                }
                Event::SettingsChanged(s) => {
                    // Authoritative refresh from the engine (SetSettings echo,
                    // remembered-console updates). The settings-window draft
                    // is separate state and is intentionally left alone.
                    self.settings = s;
                }
            }
        }
        busy
    }

    fn poll_dialog(&mut self) {
        let Some(dialog) = &self.dialog else { return };
        let Some(outcome) = dialog.try_result() else {
            return;
        };
        self.dialog = None;
        match outcome {
            dialogs::DialogOutcome::Files(files) => {
                self.send(Command::AddPaths {
                    paths: files,
                    recursive: false,
                });
            }
            dialogs::DialogOutcome::Folder(path) => {
                self.pending_folder = Some(path);
            }
            dialogs::DialogOutcome::Cancelled => {}
        }
    }

    fn poll_dropped_files(&mut self, ctx: &egui::Context) {
        let dropped: Vec<PathBuf> = ctx
            .input(|i| i.raw.dropped_files.clone())
            .into_iter()
            .map(|f| f.path().to_path_buf())
            .collect();
        if dropped.is_empty() {
            return;
        }
        let files: Vec<PathBuf> = dropped.iter().filter(|p| p.is_file()).cloned().collect();
        if let Some(first_dir) = dropped.iter().find(|p| p.is_dir()) {
            self.pending_folder = Some(first_dir.clone());
        }
        if !files.is_empty() {
            self.send(Command::AddPaths {
                paths: files,
                recursive: false,
            });
        }
    }

    fn folder_choice(&mut self, ctx: &egui::Context) {
        let Some(path) = self.pending_folder.clone() else {
            return;
        };
        // None = still deciding, Some(None) = cancelled, Some(Some(rec)) = scan.
        let mut action: Option<Option<bool>> = None;
        let window = egui::Window::new("Add folder")
            .collapsible(false)
            .resizable(false)
            .show(ctx, |ui| {
                ui.label(format!("Add packages from:\n{}", path.display()));
                ui.add_space(8.0);
                ui.horizontal(|ui| {
                    if ui.button("Scan subfolders").clicked() {
                        action = Some(Some(true));
                    }
                    if ui.button("Scan top level only").clicked() {
                        action = Some(Some(false));
                    }
                    if ui.button("Cancel").clicked() {
                        action = Some(None);
                    }
                });
            });
        if action.is_none() && escape_closes_top_window(ctx, window.map(|w| w.response.layer_id)) {
            action = Some(None);
        }
        match action {
            Some(Some(recursive)) => {
                self.pending_folder = None;
                self.send(Command::AddPaths {
                    paths: vec![path],
                    recursive,
                });
            }
            Some(None) => self.pending_folder = None,
            None => {}
        }
    }

    fn connected(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self.snapshot.console.status, ConsoleStatus::Connected)
    }

    /// Poll the update-checker channel and fold results into the UI state.
    /// Every outcome is logged to the activity panel so even the silent
    /// startup check is visible to anyone who cares.
    fn poll_update(&mut self) {
        let Some(result) = self.updater.poll() else {
            return;
        };
        let entry = match result {
            Ok(Some(info)) => {
                self.update_status = UpdateStatus::Available(info.clone());
                self.show_update_window = true;
                (
                    LogLevel::Info,
                    format!(
                        "Update available: {} (you have {}). See the download page for \
                         installers",
                        info.tag,
                        rpi_ng_core::VERSION
                    ),
                )
            }
            Ok(None) => {
                self.update_status = UpdateStatus::UpToDate;
                (
                    LogLevel::Info,
                    format!("You are on the latest release ({})", rpi_ng_core::VERSION),
                )
            }
            Err(e) => {
                self.update_status = UpdateStatus::Failed(e.clone());
                (LogLevel::Warn, format!("Update check failed: {e}"))
            }
        };
        self.logs
            .push_back((rpi_ng_core::model::unix_ms(), entry.0, entry.1));
        while self.logs.len() > MAX_LOG_LINES {
            self.logs.pop_front();
        }
    }

    /// Poll the file-server self-check channel, fold the result into the UI
    /// state and log an actionable line — the whole point of the button is
    /// that the outcome explains *where* the problem likely is.
    fn poll_server_check(&mut self) {
        let Some(result) = self.server_checker.poll() else {
            return;
        };
        let (level, msg) = match &result {
            ServerCheck::Responded(code) => (
                LogLevel::Info,
                format!(
                    "File server self-check: answering normally (HTTP {code}). If the console \
                     still cannot download, the block is between the two machines: check the \
                     firewall allows this app on the local network."
                ),
            ),
            ServerCheck::NotRunning => (
                LogLevel::Warn,
                "File server self-check: not running: it starts together with a console \
                 connection."
                    .to_string(),
            ),
            ServerCheck::NoAnswer => (
                LogLevel::Warn,
                "File server self-check: no answer within the timeout: the server may have \
                 stopped, or a firewall/antivirus is intercepting the connection."
                    .to_string(),
            ),
            ServerCheck::Error(e) => (
                LogLevel::Warn,
                format!("File server self-check failed: {e}"),
            ),
        };
        self.logs
            .push_back((rpi_ng_core::model::unix_ms(), level, msg));
        while self.logs.len() > MAX_LOG_LINES {
            self.logs.pop_front();
        }
        self.server_check_result = Some(result);
    }

    fn update_controls(&mut self, ui: &mut egui::Ui) {
        match self.update_status.clone() {
            UpdateStatus::Checking => {
                ui.add_enabled(false, egui::Button::new("Checking…"));
            }
            UpdateStatus::Available(ref info) => {
                if ui
                    .button(
                        egui::RichText::new(format!("New: {}", info.tag))
                            .strong()
                            .color(palette::Palette::of(ui).ok),
                    )
                    .on_hover_text("A newer release is available (click for details)")
                    .clicked()
                {
                    self.show_update_window = true;
                }
            }
            status => {
                let label = match status {
                    UpdateStatus::UpToDate => "Check for updates ✓",
                    _ => "Check for updates",
                };
                if ui
                    .button(label)
                    .on_hover_text("Ask GitHub for a newer release of this app")
                    .clicked()
                {
                    self.updater.kick();
                    self.update_status = UpdateStatus::Checking;
                    self.show_update_window = true;
                }
            }
        }
    }

    fn update_window(&mut self, ctx: &egui::Context) {
        let mut open = self.show_update_window;
        let status = self.update_status.clone();
        let mut check_again = false;
        let window = egui::Window::new("Software update")
            .open(&mut open)
            .collapsible(false)
            .resizable(false)
            .min_width(400.0)
            .show(ctx, |ui| {
                match &status {
                    UpdateStatus::Idle | UpdateStatus::Checking => {
                        ui.horizontal(|ui| {
                            ui.add(egui::Spinner::new());
                            ui.label("Checking GitHub for a newer release…");
                        });
                    }
                    UpdateStatus::Available(info) => {
                        ui.heading("Update available");
                        ui.add_space(6.0);
                        ui.label(format!(
                            "Version {} is available (you have {}).",
                            info.version,
                            rpi_ng_core::VERSION
                        ));
                        ui.add_space(8.0);
                        ui.hyperlink_to("Open the download page", &info.url);
                        ui.add_space(4.0);
                        ui.label(
                            egui::RichText::new(
                                "The page lists the installers for Windows, macOS and Linux.",
                            )
                            .small()
                            .weak(),
                        );
                    }
                    UpdateStatus::UpToDate => {
                        ui.heading("You are up to date");
                        ui.add_space(6.0);
                        ui.label(format!(
                            "{} is the latest published release.",
                            rpi_ng_core::VERSION
                        ));
                    }
                    UpdateStatus::Failed(reason) => {
                        ui.heading("Could not check for updates");
                        ui.add_space(6.0);
                        ui.label(
                            egui::RichText::new(reason.clone())
                                .small()
                                .color(palette::Palette::of(ui).error),
                        );
                        ui.add_space(4.0);
                        ui.label(
                            egui::RichText::new(
                                "This is usually a connection problem or a temporary GitHub \
                                 rate limit; trying again later is fine.",
                            )
                            .small()
                            .weak(),
                        );
                    }
                }
                ui.add_space(10.0);
                if ui.button("Check again").clicked() {
                    check_again = true;
                }
            });
        if escape_closes_top_window(ctx, window.map(|w| w.response.layer_id)) {
            open = false;
        }
        self.show_update_window = open;
        if check_again {
            self.updater.kick();
            self.update_status = UpdateStatus::Checking;
        }
    }
}

impl eframe::App for App {
    fn ui(&mut self, ui: &mut egui::Ui, _frame: &mut eframe::Frame) {
        self.apply_theme(ui.ctx());
        let busy = self.poll_engine();
        self.poll_update();
        self.poll_server_check();
        self.poll_dialog();
        self.poll_dropped_files(ui.ctx());
        self.folder_choice(ui.ctx());

        egui::Panel::top("top_bar").show(ui, |ui| {
            ui.add_space(4.0);
            if two_row_top_bar(ui.available_width()) {
                // Narrow: the identity row and the controls row split, the
                // same deal the queue toolbar gets; a long console chip can
                // no longer collide with the right-aligned buttons.
                ui.horizontal(|ui| {
                    ui.strong("Remote Package Installer NG");
                    ui.separator();
                    self.console_status_chip(ui);
                });
                ui.horizontal(|ui| {
                    self.top_bar_controls(ui);
                });
            } else {
                ui.horizontal(|ui| {
                    ui.strong("Remote Package Installer NG");
                    ui.separator();
                    self.console_status_chip(ui);
                    self.top_bar_controls(ui);
                });
            }
            ui.add_space(4.0);
        });

        if self.show_log {
            egui::Panel::bottom("log_panel")
                .resizable(true)
                .default_size(140.0)
                .show(ui, |ui| {
                    log_view::show(ui, &self.logs);
                });
        }

        let show_connect = !self.connected() || self.force_connect_view;
        if show_connect {
            egui::CentralPanel::default().show(ui, |ui| {
                connect_view::show(self, ui);
            });
        } else {
            egui::CentralPanel::default().show(ui, |ui| {
                queue_view::show(self, ui);
            });
        }

        if self.show_settings {
            let mut open = self.show_settings;
            settings_view::show(self, ui.ctx(), &mut open);
            self.show_settings = open;
            if !self.show_settings {
                // Closed via ✕ or Escape — discard unsaved edits.
                self.settings_draft = None;
            }
        }
        if self.show_about {
            let mut open = self.show_about;
            about_window(ui.ctx(), &mut open);
            self.show_about = open;
        }
        if self.show_update_window {
            self.update_window(ui.ctx());
        }

        // Repaint cadence: only while something is actually happening —
        // an idle queue costs zero CPU (no busy redraw loop). While the
        // file server is up the engine emits a snapshot every 250 ms, and
        // the live in/out rates below the toolbar need that repaint.
        let active = busy
            || self.snapshot.engine_busy
            || self.discovery_running
            || matches!(self.snapshot.console.status, ConsoleStatus::Probing)
            || self.dialog.is_some()
            || self.pending_folder.is_some()
            || self.updater.busy
            || self.server_checker.busy
            || self.snapshot.server.is_some();
        if active {
            ui.ctx().request_repaint_after(Duration::from_millis(150));
        }
    }

    fn on_exit(&mut self) {
        // Deterministic engine shutdown before the process exits: cancels
        // work, stops the file server, flushes the queue.
        if let Some(engine) = self.engine.take() {
            engine.shutdown();
        }
    }
}

impl App {
    fn top_bar_controls(&mut self, ui: &mut egui::Ui) {
        ui.with_layout(egui::Layout::right_to_left(egui::Align::Center), |ui| {
            if ui.button("About").clicked() {
                self.show_about = !self.show_about;
            }
            let log_label = if self.show_log {
                "Hide log"
            } else {
                "Show log"
            };
            if ui.button(log_label).clicked() {
                self.show_log = !self.show_log;
            }
            if ui.button("Settings…").clicked() {
                self.show_settings = !self.show_settings;
                // Seed the draft from the (engine-kept-fresh) settings
                // on open; discard it on toggle-off.
                self.settings_draft = if self.show_settings {
                    Some(self.settings.clone())
                } else {
                    None
                };
            }
            ui.separator();
            self.update_controls(ui);
        });
    }

    fn console_status_chip(&mut self, ui: &mut egui::Ui) {
        let (color, label) = {
            let p = palette::Palette::of(ui);
            let color = p.console_status(&self.snapshot.console.status);
            let label = match &self.snapshot.console.status {
                ConsoleStatus::None => "No console".to_string(),
                ConsoleStatus::Probing => "Connecting…".into(),
                ConsoleStatus::Connected => self
                    .snapshot
                    .console
                    .target
                    .as_ref()
                    .map(|t| {
                        // Prefer the backend's own identity (v2 backends
                        // report name + version), else the dialect kind.
                        let who = match &t.backend {
                            Some(b) => b.to_string(),
                            None => t.kind.to_string(),
                        };
                        format!("{who} ({})", t.addr)
                    })
                    .unwrap_or_else(|| "Connected".into()),
                ConsoleStatus::Incompatible(reason) => format!("Incompatible: {reason}"),
                ConsoleStatus::Lost => "Console lost".into(),
            };
            (color, label)
        };
        let full = format!("● {label}");
        let chip = ui
            .scope(|ui| {
                ui.set_max_width(MAX_CHIP_WIDTH);
                ui.add(egui::Label::new(egui::RichText::new(full.clone()).color(color)).truncate())
            })
            .inner;
        chip.on_hover_text(full);
        if self.connected()
            && ui
                .small_button("Switch")
                .on_hover_text("Pick a different console")
                .clicked()
        {
            self.force_connect_view = true;
        }
    }
}

fn about_window(ctx: &egui::Context, open: &mut bool) {
    let window = egui::Window::new("About")
        .open(open)
        .collapsible(false)
        .resizable(false)
        .show(ctx, |ui| {
            ui.heading("Remote Package Installer NG");
            ui.label(format!("Version {}", rpi_ng_core::VERSION));
            ui.add_space(6.0);
            ui.label(
                "A cross-platform installer for PS4 packages over the local network, \
                 speaking the Remote Package Installer protocol.",
            );
            ui.add_space(6.0);
            ui.label("Distributed under GPL-3.0. See LICENSE and docs/ for details.");
            ui.add_space(6.0);
            ui.hyperlink_to(
                "Repository",
                "https://github.com/AldySan/Remote-Package-Installer-NG",
            );
        });
    if escape_closes_top_window(ctx, window.map(|w| w.response.layer_id)) {
        *open = false;
    }
}

/// Escape closes a window, but only the front-most one: `layer` must be
/// the topmost window layer (`Order::Middle`) this frame. egui 0.37
/// `Window` closes only via its ✕ button, so apps wire Escape themselves;
/// without the layer check, stacked windows (e.g. Settings behind the
/// update prompt) would all close at once instead of just the front one.
pub(crate) fn escape_closes_top_window(ctx: &egui::Context, layer: Option<egui::LayerId>) -> bool {
    let Some(layer) = layer else {
        return false;
    };
    if !ctx.input(|i| i.key_down(egui::Key::Escape)) {
        return false;
    }
    let is_top = ctx.memory(|m| m.areas().top_layer_id(egui::Order::Middle)) == Some(layer);
    if is_top {
        // Consume so a lower window checking later this frame cannot also act.
        ctx.input_mut(|i| i.consume_key(egui::Modifiers::NONE, egui::Key::Escape));
    }
    is_top
}

/// Below this available width the top bar wraps into two rows (identity,
/// then controls). Measured headlessly with real egui metrics: the worst
/// controls row ("Check for updates" plus the up-to-date checkmark) needs
/// ~364px, the title plus separator ~229px, and the chip is capped at
/// [`MAX_CHIP_WIDTH`], so a single row needs ~930px. The 1120px default
/// window stays single-row; the 860px minimum wraps.
const TOP_BAR_SINGLE_ROW_MIN_WIDTH: f32 = 960.0;

/// Long backend identities ("Remote PKG Installer NextGen 0.0.3
/// (192.168.1.42:12800)") and incompatibility reasons must not push the
/// chip into the right-aligned controls: the label truncates at this many
/// pixels and the full text stays one hover away.
const MAX_CHIP_WIDTH: f32 = 320.0;

fn two_row_top_bar(available_width: f32) -> bool {
    available_width < TOP_BAR_SINGLE_ROW_MIN_WIDTH
}

pub(crate) fn queue_summary(items: &[QueueItem]) -> String {
    let total = items.len();
    let done = items
        .iter()
        .filter(|i| i.state == ItemState::Completed)
        .count();
    let failed = items
        .iter()
        .filter(|i| matches!(i.state, ItemState::Failed | ItemState::Invalid))
        .count();
    let active = items.iter().filter(|i| i.state.is_active()).count();
    format!("{total} item(s) · {done} done · {active} active · {failed} failed")
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use rpi_ng_core::model::{ItemState, PartFile, QueueItem};

    fn item(state: ItemState) -> QueueItem {
        QueueItem {
            id: 0,
            parts: vec![PartFile {
                id: 0,
                path: "/tmp/a.pkg".into(),
                file_name: "a.pkg".into(),
                size: 1,
                part_index: None,
                pkg: None,
                invalid_reason: None,
            }],
            state,
            error: None,
            task_id: None,
            task_owner: None,
            job_id: None,
            console_title: None,
            progress: None,
            attempts: 0,
            added_at: 0,
            updated_at: 0,
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn summary_counts_the_four_visible_buckets() {
        // Exactly what the toolbar line promises: total, done, active, failed.
        // Cancelled/unknown/paused are total-only — that is the contract the
        // first user-testing round settled on.
        let items = vec![
            item(ItemState::Completed),
            item(ItemState::Completed),
            item(ItemState::Transferring),
            item(ItemState::Failed),
            item(ItemState::Invalid),
            item(ItemState::Cancelled),
            item(ItemState::Unknown),
        ];
        assert_eq!(
            queue_summary(&items),
            "7 item(s) · 2 done · 1 active · 2 failed"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn summary_on_empty_queue() {
        assert_eq!(
            queue_summary(&[]),
            "0 item(s) · 0 done · 0 active · 0 failed"
        );
    }

    // -- Escape closes the front-most window (R-32) -------------------------

    fn escape_frame() -> egui::RawInput {
        let mut input = egui::RawInput::default();
        input.events.push(egui::Event::Key {
            key: egui::Key::Escape,
            physical_key: Some(egui::Key::Escape),
            pressed: true,
            repeat: false,
            modifiers: egui::Modifiers::NONE,
        });
        input
    }

    fn render_two_windows(ctx: &egui::Context) -> (Option<egui::LayerId>, Option<egui::LayerId>) {
        let back = egui::Window::new("back window").show(ctx, |ui| {
            ui.label("back");
        });
        let front = egui::Window::new("front window").show(ctx, |ui| {
            ui.label("front");
        });
        (
            back.map(|w| w.response.layer_id),
            front.map(|w| w.response.layer_id),
        )
    }

    #[test]
    fn escape_closes_only_the_top_window() {
        let ctx = egui::Context::default();
        // Settle the window layers first (areas register over frames).
        for _ in 0..2 {
            ctx.run_pass(egui::RawInput::default(), |ctx| {
                render_two_windows(ctx);
            })
            .drop_without_applying_deltas();
        }
        let output = ctx.run_pass(escape_frame(), |ctx| {
            let (back, front) = render_two_windows(ctx);
            let front_closes = escape_closes_top_window(ctx, front);
            let back_closes = escape_closes_top_window(ctx, back);
            assert!(front_closes, "the front window must close on Escape");
            assert!(!back_closes, "the back window must stay open");
        });
        output.drop_without_applying_deltas();
    }

    #[test]
    fn no_escape_no_close_and_none_layer_is_safe() {
        let ctx = egui::Context::default();
        let output = ctx.run_pass(egui::RawInput::default(), |ctx| {
            let (_, front) = render_two_windows(ctx);
            assert!(!escape_closes_top_window(ctx, front));
            // A window that did not render (None layer) must never close.
            assert!(!escape_closes_top_window(ctx, None));
        });
        output.drop_without_applying_deltas();
    }

    // -- top bar fits the minimum window (R-03) -----------------------------

    #[test]
    fn top_bar_wraps_below_its_threshold() {
        assert!(two_row_top_bar(844.0), "minimum window must wrap");
        assert!(!two_row_top_bar(1104.0), "default window stays single-row");
        assert!(!two_row_top_bar(TOP_BAR_SINGLE_ROW_MIN_WIDTH));
    }

    /// Measured with real egui metrics, not estimates: the wrapped rows must
    /// fit the 860x560 minimum window, and the single-row threshold must
    /// cover what the single row actually needs (worst-case controls label,
    /// truncated chip).
    #[test]
    fn top_bar_rows_fit_the_minimum_window_when_measured() {
        let ctx = egui::Context::default();
        let output = ctx.run_ui(egui::RawInput::default(), |ui| {
            let min_usable = 860.0 - 2.0 * 8.0;

            let controls = ui
                .horizontal(|ui| {
                    let _ = ui.button("About");
                    let _ = ui.button("Hide log");
                    let _ = ui.button("Settings…");
                    ui.separator();
                    // Worst-case update-controls label.
                    let _ = ui.button("Check for updates ✓");
                    ui.min_rect()
                })
                .inner;
            assert!(
                controls.width() <= min_usable,
                "controls row {:.0}px exceeds the {:.0}px minimum window",
                controls.width(),
                min_usable
            );

            let long_label = "● Incompatible: the address answered, but the response did not \
                 look like a Remote Package Installer app, so the connection was refused \
                 before any package was sent";
            let identity = ui
                .horizontal(|ui| {
                    ui.strong("Remote Package Installer NG");
                    ui.separator();
                    let chip = ui
                        .scope(|ui| {
                            ui.set_max_width(MAX_CHIP_WIDTH);
                            ui.add(
                                egui::Label::new(egui::RichText::new(long_label).weak()).truncate(),
                            )
                        })
                        .inner;
                    assert!(
                        chip.rect.width() <= MAX_CHIP_WIDTH + 1.0,
                        "chip {:.0}px exceeds its {:.0}px budget",
                        chip.rect.width(),
                        MAX_CHIP_WIDTH
                    );
                    ui.min_rect()
                })
                .inner;
            assert!(
                identity.width() <= min_usable,
                "identity row {:.0}px exceeds the {:.0}px minimum window",
                identity.width(),
                min_usable
            );

            let gap = ui.style().spacing.item_spacing.x;
            let single_row_needs = identity.width() + gap + controls.width();
            assert!(
                TOP_BAR_SINGLE_ROW_MIN_WIDTH >= single_row_needs + 30.0,
                "threshold {:.0}px leaves <30px over the {:.0}px single-row need",
                TOP_BAR_SINGLE_ROW_MIN_WIDTH,
                single_row_needs
            );
        });
        output.drop_without_applying_deltas();
    }
}
```

`crates/rpi-ng/src/connect_view.rs`:

```rs
//! Connection view: discovery results, remembered consoles, manual fallback,
//! and actionable troubleshooting — the first-run experience.

use std::net::SocketAddr;

use eframe::egui;

use rpi_ng_core::model::{ConsoleStatus, DiscoverySource};

use crate::{app::App, palette::Palette};

pub fn show(app: &mut App, ui: &mut egui::Ui) {
    // Scrolled, not squeezed: with a handful of discovered and remembered
    // consoles the content measures taller than the central panel at the
    // 860x560 minimum window (446px of content against roughly 380px with
    // the log panel open), which used to push the manual-address fallback
    // and the troubleshooting section out of reach with no way back.
    egui::ScrollArea::vertical()
        .auto_shrink([false, false])
        .show(ui, |ui| {
            content(app, ui);
        });
}

fn content(app: &mut App, ui: &mut egui::Ui) {
    let frame = egui::Frame::new()
        .inner_margin(egui::Margin::same(24))
        .corner_radius(8.0);
    frame.show(ui, |ui| {
        ui.set_width(ui.available_width());
        ui.vertical_centered(|ui| {
            ui.heading("Connect to your console");
            ui.add_space(4.0);
            ui.label(
                egui::RichText::new(
                    "Run the Remote Package Installer app (or a compatible backend) on your \
                     PS4 and keep it in the foreground while sending.",
                )
                .weak(),
            );
        });
        ui.add_space(12.0);

        if app.force_connect_view && matches!(app.snapshot.console.status, ConsoleStatus::Connected)
        {
            ui.horizontal(|ui| {
                ui.label("A console is already connected.");
                if ui.button("Back to the queue").clicked() {
                    app.force_connect_view = false;
                }
                if ui.button("Disconnect").clicked() {
                    app.force_connect_view = false;
                    app.send(rpi_ng_core::Command::Disconnect);
                }
            });
            ui.add_space(8.0);
        }

        status_line(app, ui);
        ui.add_space(8.0);

        candidates(app, ui);
        ui.add_space(8.0);

        remembered(app, ui);
        ui.add_space(12.0);

        manual(app, ui);
        ui.add_space(12.0);
        troubleshooting(ui);
    });
}

fn status_line(app: &mut App, ui: &mut egui::Ui) {
    ui.horizontal(|ui| {
        if app.discovery_running {
            ui.add(egui::Spinner::new());
            ui.label("Scanning the local network for consoles…");
        } else {
            ui.label(format!(
                "Found {} console(s) on the network.",
                app.discovered.len()
            ));
        }
        // One sweep at a time (the engine also guards this): a second
        // click while scanning would stack another full subnet probe.
        if ui
            .add_enabled(!app.discovery_running, egui::Button::new("Scan again"))
            .clicked()
        {
            app.discovered.clear();
            app.discovery_running = true;
            app.send(rpi_ng_core::Command::Discover);
        }
    });
    if let ConsoleStatus::Incompatible(reason) = &app.snapshot.console.status {
        ui.label(
            egui::RichText::new(format!(
                "The last address did not answer like an installer: {reason}"
            ))
            .color(Palette::of(ui).error),
        );
    }
    if matches!(app.snapshot.console.status, ConsoleStatus::Lost) {
        ui.label(
            egui::RichText::new(
                "The console stopped answering. Keep the installer app in the foreground on \
                 the console (the PS4 suspends background apps), then reconnect.",
            )
            .color(Palette::of(ui).warn),
        );
    }
}

fn candidates(app: &mut App, ui: &mut egui::Ui) {
    if app.discovered.is_empty() {
        return;
    }
    // Double-clicking Connect while a probe is already running just stacks
    // probe work and restarts the session churn; the chip up top already
    // says "Connecting…".
    let probing = matches!(app.snapshot.console.status, ConsoleStatus::Probing);
    ui.group(|ui| {
        ui.label(egui::RichText::new("Discovered consoles").strong());
        for cand in app.discovered.clone() {
            ui.horizontal(|ui| {
                let source = match cand.source {
                    DiscoverySource::SubnetProbe => "network scan",
                    DiscoverySource::Remembered => "remembered",
                    DiscoverySource::Manual => "manual",
                };
                ui.colored_label(Palette::of(ui).ok, "●");
                ui.strong(cand.addr.to_string());
                ui.label(
                    egui::RichText::new(format!("{} · {}", cand.kind, source))
                        .small()
                        .weak(),
                );
                if ui
                    .add_enabled(!probing, egui::Button::new("Connect"))
                    .clicked()
                {
                    app.send(rpi_ng_core::Command::Connect(cand.addr));
                }
            });
        }
    });
}

fn remembered(app: &mut App, ui: &mut egui::Ui) {
    let remembered: Vec<SocketAddr> = app.settings.remembered.iter().map(|r| r.addr).collect();
    if remembered.is_empty() {
        return;
    }
    let probing = matches!(app.snapshot.console.status, ConsoleStatus::Probing);
    ui.group(|ui| {
        ui.label(egui::RichText::new("Previously used").strong());
        for addr in remembered {
            ui.horizontal(|ui| {
                ui.label(addr.to_string());
                if ui
                    .add_enabled(!probing, egui::Button::new("Connect").small())
                    .clicked()
                {
                    app.send(rpi_ng_core::Command::Connect(addr));
                }
            });
        }
    });
}

fn manual(app: &mut App, ui: &mut egui::Ui) {
    let probing = matches!(app.snapshot.console.status, ConsoleStatus::Probing);
    ui.group(|ui| {
        ui.label(egui::RichText::new("Manual address (fallback)").strong());
        ui.horizontal(|ui| {
            let edit = egui::TextEdit::singleline(&mut app.manual_addr)
                .hint_text("192.168.1.42 or 192.168.1.42:12800")
                .desired_width(240.0);
            ui.add(edit);
            let parsed = parse_addr(&app.manual_addr);
            let enabled = parsed.is_some() && !probing;
            if ui
                .add_enabled(enabled, egui::Button::new("Connect"))
                .on_disabled_hover_text("Enter a valid IP address first")
                .clicked()
                && let Some(addr) = parsed
            {
                app.send(rpi_ng_core::Command::Connect(addr));
            }
        });
    });
}

fn parse_addr(text: &str) -> Option<SocketAddr> {
    let text = text.trim();
    if text.is_empty() {
        return None;
    }
    if let Ok(addr) = text.parse::<SocketAddr>() {
        return Some(addr);
    }
    if let Ok(ip) = text.parse::<std::net::IpAddr>() {
        return Some(SocketAddr::new(ip, 12800));
    }
    None
}

fn troubleshooting(ui: &mut egui::Ui) {
    egui::CollapsingHeader::new("Nothing found? Troubleshooting")
        .default_open(false)
        .show(ui, |ui| {
            // Regular size (v0.0.2 used ui.small() — unreadably tiny).
            ui.label(
                egui::RichText::new(
                    "• Check that the PS4 and this computer are on the same network (no \
                     guest/AP isolation).\n\
                     • On the PS4, start the Remote Package Installer app and keep it in the \
                     foreground: the PS4 suspends background apps.\n\
                     • GoldHEN users: start the installer from the GoldHEN menu, or note that \
                     some setups use port 9090 instead of 12800 (try 192.168.1.42:9090).\n\
                     • Verify the console IP in Settings → Network → View Connection Status.\n\
                     • Firewalls: this app serves files from your computer to the console; allow \
                     it on the local network when prompted.\n\
                     • Large subnets: the scan covers a bounded number of addresses: use the \
                     manual address for unusual networks.",
                )
                .weak(),
            );
        });
}
```

`crates/rpi-ng/src/dialogs.rs`:

```rs
//! Native file/folder dialogs on a helper thread.
//!
//! `rfd`'s async dialog needs a running async runtime; the UI thread has none,
//! so each dialog runs on its own single-threaded runtime and reports back
//! through a channel that the UI polls per frame (non-blocking).

use std::path::PathBuf;
use std::sync::mpsc;

pub enum DialogOutcome {
    Files(Vec<PathBuf>),
    Folder(PathBuf),
    Cancelled,
}

pub struct DialogPick {
    rx: mpsc::Receiver<DialogOutcome>,
}

impl DialogPick {
    /// Non-blocking check; `true` when the dialog finished.
    pub fn try_result(&self) -> Option<DialogOutcome> {
        self.rx.try_recv().ok()
    }
}

/// Show a multi-select PKG file dialog.
pub fn pick_pkg_files() -> DialogPick {
    let (tx, rx) = mpsc::channel();
    // Clone kept for the spawn-failure path: `tx` is moved into the thread.
    let fallback_tx = tx.clone();
    let spawned = std::thread::Builder::new()
        .name("rpi-ng-dialog".into())
        .spawn(move || {
            let Ok(rt) = tokio::runtime::Builder::new_current_thread()
                .enable_all()
                .build()
            else {
                let _ = tx.send(DialogOutcome::Cancelled);
                return;
            };
            rt.block_on(async move {
                let handle = rfd::AsyncFileDialog::new()
                    .set_title("Select PKG files")
                    .add_filter("PlayStation packages", &["pkg"])
                    .pick_files()
                    .await;
                let outcome = match handle {
                    Some(files) if !files.is_empty() => DialogOutcome::Files(
                        files.into_iter().map(|f| f.path().to_path_buf()).collect(),
                    ),
                    _ => DialogOutcome::Cancelled,
                };
                let _ = tx.send(outcome);
            });
        })
        .is_ok();
    if !spawned {
        // Thread-spawn failure must still resolve the promise (same contract
        // as the update/server-check helpers) instead of panicking the GUI.
        let _ = fallback_tx.send(DialogOutcome::Cancelled);
    }
    DialogPick { rx }
}

/// Show a folder-pick dialog.
pub fn pick_folder() -> DialogPick {
    let (tx, rx) = mpsc::channel();
    let fallback_tx = tx.clone();
    let spawned = std::thread::Builder::new()
        .name("rpi-ng-dialog".into())
        .spawn(move || {
            let Ok(rt) = tokio::runtime::Builder::new_current_thread()
                .enable_all()
                .build()
            else {
                let _ = tx.send(DialogOutcome::Cancelled);
                return;
            };
            rt.block_on(async move {
                let handle = rfd::AsyncFileDialog::new()
                    .set_title("Select a folder with packages")
                    .pick_folder()
                    .await;
                let outcome = match handle {
                    Some(folder) => DialogOutcome::Folder(folder.path().to_path_buf()),
                    _ => DialogOutcome::Cancelled,
                };
                let _ = tx.send(outcome);
            });
        })
        .is_ok();
    if !spawned {
        let _ = fallback_tx.send(DialogOutcome::Cancelled);
    }
    DialogPick { rx }
}
```

`crates/rpi-ng/src/fmt.rs`:

```rs
//! Formatting helpers (bytes, durations, addresses).

use std::time::Duration;

pub fn bytes(n: u64) -> String {
    const UNITS: [&str; 6] = ["B", "KiB", "MiB", "GiB", "TiB", "PiB"];
    let mut value = n as f64;
    let mut unit = 0usize;
    while value >= 1024.0 && unit < UNITS.len() - 1 {
        value /= 1024.0;
        unit += 1;
    }
    if unit == 0 {
        format!("{n} B")
    } else {
        let unit = UNITS[unit];
        format!("{value:.1} {unit}")
    }
}

pub fn bps(n: u64) -> String {
    if n == 0 {
        String::new()
    } else {
        format!("{}/s", bytes(n))
    }
}

/// Megabits per second (network-style), from a bytes-per-second rate.
/// Adaptive precision so both a gigabit LAN and an idle server read well.
pub fn mbps(bytes_per_sec: f64) -> String {
    let mbps = bytes_per_sec * 8.0 / 1_000_000.0;
    if mbps >= 100.0 {
        format!("{mbps:.0} Mbps")
    } else if mbps >= 10.0 {
        format!("{mbps:.1} Mbps")
    } else {
        format!("{mbps:.2} Mbps")
    }
}

pub fn duration(sec: u64) -> String {
    let d = Duration::from_secs(sec.min(99 * 3600 + 3599));
    let h = d.as_secs() / 3600;
    let m = (d.as_secs() % 3600) / 60;
    let s = d.as_secs() % 60;
    if h > 0 {
        format!("{h}h {m}m")
    } else if m > 0 {
        format!("{m}m {s:02}s")
    } else {
        format!("{s}s")
    }
}

pub fn clock(unix_ms: u64) -> String {
    // No timezone dependency: log timestamps render UTC and *say so* —
    // otherwise a UTC+7 user reads every entry seven hours off their wall
    // clock with no hint (v0.0.5 fix: the marker used to be missing).
    let secs = unix_ms / 1000;
    let (h, m, s) = (secs / 3600 % 24, secs / 60 % 60, secs % 60);
    format!("{h:02}:{m:02}:{s:02} UTC")
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn bytes_units_and_boundaries() {
        assert_eq!(bytes(0), "0 B");
        assert_eq!(bytes(1023), "1023 B");
        assert_eq!(bytes(1024), "1.0 KiB");
        // A PS4 game: tens of GiB with one decimal.
        assert_eq!(bytes(50 * 1024 * 1024 * 1024), "50.0 GiB");
        // Unit growth stops at PiB; huge values clamp, never panic.
        assert!(bytes(u64::MAX).ends_with("PiB"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn bps_zero_renders_empty() {
        // A zero rate shows nothing (not "0 B/s") — the label it feeds is
        // only drawn while a transfer is live.
        assert_eq!(bps(0), "");
        assert_eq!(bps(1024), "1.0 KiB/s");
    }

    #[test]
    fn mbps_tiers_and_precision() {
        // Network-style megabits from a bytes/s rate — the v0.0.3 Mbps fix.
        // 2.5 MiB/s = 20.97152 Mbps → one decimal in the 10..100 tier.
        assert_eq!(mbps(2.5 * 1024.0 * 1024.0), "21.0 Mbps");
        assert_eq!(
            mbps(125_000_000.0),
            "1000 Mbps",
            ">=100 tier drops decimals"
        );
        assert_eq!(mbps(15.0 * 1_000_000.0 / 8.0), "15.0 Mbps");
        assert_eq!(
            mbps(1.0 * 1_000_000.0 / 8.0),
            "1.00 Mbps",
            "<10 tier keeps two decimals"
        );
        // Idle server must not render "-0.00 Mbps" style noise.
        assert_eq!(mbps(0.0), "0.00 Mbps");
    }

    #[test]
    fn duration_renders_h_m_s() {
        assert_eq!(duration(0), "0s");
        assert_eq!(duration(59), "59s");
        assert_eq!(duration(60), "1m 00s");
        assert_eq!(duration(125), "2m 05s");
        // Past an hour only h+m render (seconds drop, they are noise there).
        assert_eq!(duration(3_725), "1h 2m");
        assert_eq!(duration(3600), "1h 0m");
        // The 99h59m59s cap keeps a garbage ETA from printing absurd hours.
        assert_eq!(duration(400 * 3600), "99h 59m");
        assert_eq!(duration(u64::MAX), "99h 59m");
    }

    #[test]
    fn clock_marks_utc_and_wraps_at_24h() {
        // The UTC marker is the fix for the "logs are 7 hours off" report:
        // it must survive every value, and the day wraps.
        assert_eq!(clock(0), "00:00:00 UTC");
        assert_eq!(clock(86_399_000), "23:59:59 UTC");
        assert_eq!(clock(86_400_000), "00:00:00 UTC");
        // 2025-01-01 07:15:30 UTC in ms.
        assert_eq!(clock(1_735_715_730_000), "07:15:30 UTC");
    }
}
```

`crates/rpi-ng/src/log_view.rs`:

```rs
//! Activity log panel (bounded, colored by level).

use std::collections::VecDeque;

use eframe::egui;

use rpi_ng_core::model::LogLevel;

use crate::{fmt, palette::Palette};

pub fn show(ui: &mut egui::Ui, logs: &VecDeque<(u64, LogLevel, String)>) {
    ui.heading(egui::RichText::new("Activity").small().strong());
    ui.separator();
    egui::ScrollArea::vertical()
        .auto_shrink([false, false])
        .stick_to_bottom(true)
        .show(ui, |ui| {
            if logs.is_empty() {
                ui.label(egui::RichText::new("Nothing yet.").weak());
            }
            let p = Palette::of(ui);
            for (ts, level, msg) in logs {
                let color = p.log_level(ui, *level);
                ui.monospace(
                    egui::RichText::new(format!("{} [{level:>5}] {msg}", fmt::clock(*ts)))
                        .small()
                        .color(color),
                );
            }
        });
}
```

`crates/rpi-ng/src/main.rs`:

```rs
//! Remote Package Installer NG — desktop application entry point.
#![cfg_attr(not(debug_assertions), windows_subsystem = "windows")]
// Release builds on Windows open no console window (the GUI is the app).
// Debug builds keep the console so `cargo run` output stays visible.

mod app;
mod connect_view;
mod dialogs;
mod fmt;
mod log_view;
mod palette;
mod queue_view;
mod servercheck;
mod settings_view;
mod updater;

use eframe::egui;

/// Matches the binary name, the Linux `.desktop` file and its `Icon=` key.
/// X11 docks match `WM_CLASS` and Wayland compositors match the toplevel
/// `app_id` against this value (see `packaging/linux/`).
const APP_ID: &str = "remote-package-installer-ng";

fn main() -> eframe::Result<()> {
    let options = eframe::NativeOptions {
        viewport: egui::ViewportBuilder::default()
            .with_inner_size([1120.0, 760.0])
            .with_min_inner_size([860.0, 560.0])
            .with_title("Remote Package Installer NG")
            .with_app_id(APP_ID)
            .with_icon(window_icon()),
        ..Default::default()
    };
    eframe::run_native(
        "Remote Package Installer NG",
        options,
        Box::new(|_cc| Ok(Box::new(app::App::new()))),
    )
}

/// Brand icon for the window title bar and taskbar. Windows and X11 read it
/// at runtime; the `.exe` file icon (Windows) and the macOS `.app` bundle
/// icon come from the build/packaging assets instead — see `docs/BUILDING.md`.
fn window_icon() -> egui::IconData {
    let bytes = include_bytes!("../assets/icon_256.png");
    let rgba = image::load_from_memory(bytes)
        .expect("embedded window icon is a valid PNG")
        .to_rgba8();
    let (width, height) = (rgba.width(), rgba.height());
    egui::IconData {
        rgba: rgba.into_raw(),
        width,
        height,
    }
}
```
