`crates/rpi-ng/src/palette.rs`:

```rs
//! Semantic color palette: one color per meaning, with a per-theme value.
//!
//! Every colored text in the app resolves through here so it passes WCAG AA
//! (>= 4.5:1) on the panel it renders on, in BOTH themes (the pure primaries
//! egui ships (`Color32::GREEN` etc.) are unreadable in light mode: yellow on
//! a near-white panel is 1.0:1). Centralizing also makes the contrast
//! contract testable in one place: see the tests at the bottom of this file.
//!
//! Mapping notes (deliberate, vs. the pre-palette ad-hoc colors):
//! * "Connecting" (probing) and Preparing/Submitting/Transferring share the
//!   in-progress blue; the label text tells them apart, one blue is calmer
//!   than three.
//! * Unknown maps to warn, not violet: like Paused, it waits for a user
//!   decision (mark completed or retry), and amber is the needs-attention
//!   color. "Console lost" is warn for the same reason (reconnect action).
//! * Queued and Cancelled share the neutral gray: no signal either way.

use eframe::egui;

use rpi_ng_core::{
    fileserver::ServerCheck,
    model::{ConsoleStatus, ItemState, LogLevel},
};

/// egui's default dark weak text (0.6 alpha) is ~2.3:1 on the dark panel,
/// far below AA. 0.95 keeps a hint of hierarchy while passing.
pub(crate) const DARK_WEAK_TEXT_ALPHA: f32 = 0.95;

pub(crate) struct Palette {
    /// Positive outcome: connected, completed, server OK, upload rate.
    pub ok: egui::Color32,
    /// Needs user attention: paused, unknown outcome, warnings, patch tag.
    pub warn: egui::Color32,
    /// Failure: failed/invalid items, errors, incompatible console.
    pub error: egui::Color32,
    /// Work in progress: connecting, preparing, submitting, transferring.
    pub info: egui::Color32,
    /// The console is installing the package (distinct from transferring).
    pub installing: egui::Color32,
    /// No signal: queued, cancelled, no console, server not running.
    pub neutral: egui::Color32,
}

impl Palette {
    /// Resolve against the visuals the current frame is rendering with.
    pub(crate) fn of(ui: &egui::Ui) -> Self {
        Self::for_dark_mode(ui.visuals().dark_mode)
    }

    pub(crate) fn for_dark_mode(dark: bool) -> Self {
        if dark {
            Self {
                ok: egui::Color32::from_rgb(0x4A, 0xDE, 0x80),
                warn: egui::Color32::from_rgb(0xFB, 0xBF, 0x24),
                error: egui::Color32::from_rgb(0xF8, 0x71, 0x71),
                info: egui::Color32::from_rgb(0x93, 0xC5, 0xFD),
                installing: egui::Color32::from_rgb(0xA7, 0x8B, 0xFA),
                neutral: egui::Color32::from_rgb(0xA1, 0xA1, 0xAA),
            }
        } else {
            Self {
                ok: egui::Color32::from_rgb(0x16, 0x65, 0x34),
                warn: egui::Color32::from_rgb(0xB4, 0x53, 0x09),
                error: egui::Color32::from_rgb(0xB9, 0x1C, 0x1C),
                info: egui::Color32::from_rgb(0x1D, 0x4E, 0xD8),
                installing: egui::Color32::from_rgb(0x6D, 0x28, 0xD9),
                neutral: egui::Color32::from_rgb(0x57, 0x53, 0x4E),
            }
        }
    }

    /// Queue item state -> semantic color.
    pub(crate) fn item_state(&self, state: &ItemState) -> egui::Color32 {
        match state {
            ItemState::Queued | ItemState::Cancelled => self.neutral,
            ItemState::Preparing | ItemState::Submitting | ItemState::Transferring => self.info,
            ItemState::Installing => self.installing,
            ItemState::Paused | ItemState::Unknown => self.warn,
            ItemState::Completed => self.ok,
            ItemState::Failed | ItemState::Invalid => self.error,
        }
    }

    /// Top-bar console status chip color.
    pub(crate) fn console_status(&self, status: &ConsoleStatus) -> egui::Color32 {
        match status {
            ConsoleStatus::None => self.neutral,
            ConsoleStatus::Probing => self.info,
            ConsoleStatus::Connected => self.ok,
            ConsoleStatus::Incompatible(_) => self.error,
            ConsoleStatus::Lost => self.warn,
        }
    }

    /// File-server self-check outcome chip color.
    pub(crate) fn server_check(&self, check: &ServerCheck) -> egui::Color32 {
        match check {
            ServerCheck::Responded(_) => self.ok,
            ServerCheck::NotRunning => self.neutral,
            ServerCheck::NoAnswer => self.warn,
            ServerCheck::Error(_) => self.error,
        }
    }

    /// Activity log line color by level (Info keeps the normal text color).
    pub(crate) fn log_level(&self, ui: &egui::Ui, level: LogLevel) -> egui::Color32 {
        match level {
            LogLevel::Info => ui.style().visuals.text_color(),
            LogLevel::Warn => self.warn,
            LogLevel::Error => self.error,
        }
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    /// WCAG 2.x relative-luminance contrast ratio.
    fn contrast(fg: egui::Color32, bg: egui::Color32) -> f32 {
        fn channel(c: u8) -> f32 {
            let c = c as f32 / 255.0;
            if c <= 0.04045 {
                c / 12.92
            } else {
                ((c + 0.055) / 1.055).powf(2.4)
            }
        }
        fn luminance(c: egui::Color32) -> f32 {
            0.2126 * channel(c.r()) + 0.7152 * channel(c.g()) + 0.0722 * channel(c.b())
        }
        let (a, b) = (luminance(fg), luminance(bg));
        (a.max(b) + 0.05) / (a.min(b) + 0.05)
    }

    fn entries(p: &Palette) -> [(&'static str, egui::Color32); 6] {
        [
            ("ok", p.ok),
            ("warn", p.warn),
            ("error", p.error),
            ("info", p.info),
            ("installing", p.installing),
            ("neutral", p.neutral),
        ]
    }

    /// The reason this module exists: every semantic color must stay readable
    /// as text on its theme's panel. `ok` additionally renders as the "New:
    /// {tag}" button label, so it must also pass on the button fill
    /// (`weak_bg_fill`); it is darkened to green-800 in light mode for that.
    #[test]
    fn every_color_passes_aa_on_its_background() {
        for dark in [true, false] {
            let p = Palette::for_dark_mode(dark);
            let visuals = if dark {
                egui::Visuals::dark()
            } else {
                egui::Visuals::light()
            };
            let theme = if dark { "dark" } else { "light" };
            for (name, c) in entries(&p) {
                assert!(
                    contrast(c, visuals.panel_fill) >= 4.5,
                    "{name} on {theme} panel: {:.2}",
                    contrast(c, visuals.panel_fill)
                );
            }
            let btn_bg = visuals.widgets.inactive.weak_bg_fill;
            assert!(
                contrast(p.ok, btn_bg) >= 4.5,
                "ok on {theme} button: {:.2}",
                contrast(p.ok, btn_bg)
            );
        }
    }

    /// Secondary (`.weak()`) text must also pass: light mode does on egui's
    /// default 0.6 alpha; dark mode needs the raised alpha `apply_theme`
    /// installs (gray(133) at 0.95 vs gray(84) at 0.6, which is ~2.3:1).
    #[test]
    fn weak_text_passes_aa_in_both_themes() {
        let light = egui::Visuals::light();
        assert!(
            contrast(light.weak_text_color(), light.panel_fill) >= 4.5,
            "light weak text: {:.2}",
            contrast(light.weak_text_color(), light.panel_fill)
        );
        let mut dark = egui::Visuals::dark();
        dark.weak_text_alpha = DARK_WEAK_TEXT_ALPHA;
        assert!(
            contrast(dark.weak_text_color(), dark.panel_fill) >= 4.5,
            "dark weak text: {:.2}",
            contrast(dark.weak_text_color(), dark.panel_fill)
        );
    }

    /// The state mapping must keep the three outcomes a user scans for
    /// (done / failed / waiting) distinct from each other.
    #[test]
    fn terminal_states_map_to_distinct_colors() {
        for dark in [true, false] {
            let p = Palette::for_dark_mode(dark);
            let done = p.item_state(&ItemState::Completed);
            let failed = p.item_state(&ItemState::Failed);
            let waiting = p.item_state(&ItemState::Unknown);
            assert_ne!(done, failed);
            assert_ne!(done, waiting);
            assert_ne!(failed, waiting);
        }
    }
}

```

`crates/rpi-ng/src/queue_view.rs`:

```rs
//! Queue view: toolbar, item list with live progress.
//!
//! The list is a plain scroll area — rows have variable heights (error
//! lines, wrapping titles, per-state action rows), and egui's virtualized
//! `show_rows` contractually requires a FIXED row height: rendering 88-101px
//! rows into 74px slots made every row overlap the next. Measured headlessly
//! during the fourth audit round: a plain row is ~88px, one with an error
//! line ~101px. egui lays out thousands of widgets per frame comfortably,
//! and the log panel already renders up to 500 entries unvirtualized, so the
//! simplicity is the right trade here. Rows with a live progress bar render
//! two extra lines (the bar, then the transferred/speed readout) so they can
//! never push their readout past the fixed right-hand column.

use eframe::egui;

use rpi_ng_core::{
    fileserver::ServerCheck,
    model::{ItemState, ProgressView, QueueItem},
};

use crate::{
    app::{App, queue_summary},
    dialogs, fmt,
    palette::Palette,
};

/// Which per-item action buttons a queue row offers, derived ONLY from the
/// item state — the exact mapping the UI renders. Extracted from the row
/// layout code so the "which button for which state" contract is testable
/// without a GUI harness (this is where "button does nothing" bugs live).
///
/// Note the deliberate asymmetry carried over from the layout: pausing an
/// active item pauses the WHOLE queue (one console task at a time), while
/// resuming is per-item.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub(crate) struct RowActions {
    /// "Pause" — pauses the whole queue (only while this item is active).
    pub pause: bool,
    /// "Resume" — resumes just this item (only while paused).
    pub resume_item: bool,
    /// "Mark completed" — outcome unknown, user confirms it finished.
    pub mark_completed: bool,
    /// "Retry" — requeue for a fresh attempt.
    pub retry: bool,
    /// "Remove" — delete from the queue (never while active).
    pub remove: bool,
    /// "Cancel" — stop the in-flight install (only while active).
    pub cancel: bool,
    /// "Up" — reorder (never while active).
    pub move_up: bool,
    /// "Down" — reorder (never while active).
    pub move_down: bool,
    /// Row sits at the top of the queue: "Up" renders disabled with a reason
    /// instead of doing nothing (the engine rejects the move silently).
    pub at_top: bool,
    /// Row sits at the bottom of the queue: "Down" renders disabled.
    pub at_bottom: bool,
}

impl RowActions {
    pub(crate) fn for_state(state: ItemState) -> Self {
        let active = state.is_active();
        Self {
            pause: active,
            resume_item: state == ItemState::Paused,
            mark_completed: state == ItemState::Unknown,
            retry: state.is_finished() || state == ItemState::Unknown,
            remove: !active,
            cancel: active,
            move_up: !active,
            move_down: !active,
            at_top: false,
            at_bottom: false,
        }
    }

    /// Position-aware variant: `is_first` / `is_last` are the item's real
    /// position in the whole queue (not the filtered view), because that is
    /// what the engine's `move_item` bounds itself by.
    pub(crate) fn for_row(state: ItemState, is_first: bool, is_last: bool) -> Self {
        let mut actions = Self::for_state(state);
        actions.at_top = is_first;
        actions.at_bottom = is_last;
        actions
    }
}

/// Toolbar button enablement, derived from engine/busy state and the queue —
/// the exact contract the toolbar renders (plus the hover reason shown when
/// Add is disabled). Extracted for the same reason as [`RowActions`].
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub(crate) struct ToolbarState {
    /// "Add PKG files…" / "Add folder…" enabled.
    pub can_add: bool,
    /// Why Add is disabled (already accounts for which gate is blocking).
    pub add_disabled_reason: &'static str,
    /// "Start queue" enabled (nothing running, engine idle).
    pub can_start: bool,
    /// Label swaps to "Queue running" while anything is active.
    pub start_label: &'static str,
    /// "Pause" enabled.
    pub can_pause: bool,
    /// "Resume" enabled (a paused item exists).
    pub can_resume: bool,
    /// "Clear finished" enabled.
    pub can_clear_finished: bool,
}

impl ToolbarState {
    pub(crate) fn derive(busy: bool, dialog_open: bool, items: &[QueueItem]) -> Self {
        let any_active = items.iter().any(|i| i.state.is_active());
        Self {
            can_add: !busy && !dialog_open,
            add_disabled_reason: if dialog_open {
                "Close the open file dialog first"
            } else {
                "Wait for the current operation to finish"
            },
            can_start: !any_active && !busy,
            start_label: if any_active {
                "Queue running"
            } else {
                "Start queue"
            },
            can_pause: any_active,
            can_resume: items.iter().any(|i| i.state == ItemState::Paused),
            can_clear_finished: items.iter().any(|i| i.state.is_finished()),
        }
    }
}

pub fn show(app: &mut App, ui: &mut egui::Ui) {
    toolbar(app, ui);
    server_strip(app, ui);
    ui.add_space(4.0);

    // Filter to row indices once per frame: no QueueItem clones, and the
    // search needle is lowered exactly once (not once per item).
    let needle = app.search.trim().to_lowercase();
    let indices: Vec<usize> = app
        .snapshot
        .items
        .iter()
        .enumerate()
        .filter(|(_, item)| matches_search(&needle, item))
        .map(|(idx, _)| idx)
        .collect();
    let items_len = app.snapshot.items.len();

    if indices.is_empty() {
        if app.snapshot.items.is_empty() {
            empty_state(ui);
        } else {
            // A filter that hides everything is not an empty queue: saying
            // "drop files here" while items exist looks like they vanished.
            no_matches(ui, &needle);
        }
        return;
    }

    egui::ScrollArea::vertical()
        .auto_shrink([false, false])
        .show(ui, |ui| {
            for idx in indices {
                let item = &app.snapshot.items[idx];
                item_row(app, ui, item, idx == 0, idx + 1 == items_len);
                ui.separator();
            }
        });
}

fn matches_search(needle: &str, item: &QueueItem) -> bool {
    if needle.is_empty() {
        return true;
    }
    let primary = item.primary_part();
    let title = primary.map(|p| p.display_title()).unwrap_or_default();
    let file = primary.map(|p| p.file_name.clone()).unwrap_or_default();
    let title_id = primary.and_then(|p| p.title_id()).unwrap_or_default();
    let cid = item.content_id().unwrap_or_default().to_string();
    [title, file, title_id, cid]
        .iter()
        .any(|hay| hay.to_lowercase().contains(needle))
}

/// Below this available width the toolbar wraps into two rows. Measured
/// headlessly with real egui metrics (see the layout tests): the buttons
/// need 575px in the worst case ("Queue running" label) and the right-aligned
/// search + summary group 431px (6-digit counts), so a single row needs
/// ~1015px. The 1120px default window stays single-row; the 860px minimum
/// window wraps.
const SINGLE_ROW_MIN_WIDTH: f32 = 1050.0;

fn two_row_toolbar(available_width: f32) -> bool {
    available_width < SINGLE_ROW_MIN_WIDTH
}

fn toolbar(app: &mut App, ui: &mut egui::Ui) {
    let tb = ToolbarState::derive(
        app.snapshot.engine_busy,
        app.dialog.is_some(),
        &app.snapshot.items,
    );
    if two_row_toolbar(ui.available_width()) {
        // Narrow: buttons get their own row, search + summary the next one,
        // so the right-aligned group can never paint over the buttons.
        ui.horizontal(|ui| toolbar_buttons(app, ui, &tb));
        ui.horizontal(|ui| toolbar_search(app, ui));
    } else {
        ui.horizontal(|ui| {
            toolbar_buttons(app, ui, &tb);
            toolbar_search(app, ui);
        });
    }
}

fn toolbar_buttons(app: &mut App, ui: &mut egui::Ui, tb: &ToolbarState) {
    if ui
        .add_enabled(tb.can_add, egui::Button::new("Add PKG files…"))
        .on_hover_text("Select one or more .pkg files")
        .on_disabled_hover_text(tb.add_disabled_reason)
        .clicked()
    {
        app.dialog = Some(dialogs::pick_pkg_files());
    }
    if ui
        .add_enabled(tb.can_add, egui::Button::new("Add folder…"))
        .on_hover_text("Scan a folder for packages")
        .on_disabled_hover_text(tb.add_disabled_reason)
        .clicked()
    {
        app.dialog = Some(dialogs::pick_folder());
    }
    ui.separator();

    if ui
        .add_enabled(tb.can_start, egui::Button::new(tb.start_label))
        .on_hover_text("Install queued items in order")
        .clicked()
    {
        app.send(rpi_ng_core::Command::Start);
    }
    if ui
        .add_enabled(tb.can_pause, egui::Button::new("Pause"))
        .on_hover_text("Pause the active console task and stop auto-advance")
        .clicked()
    {
        app.send(rpi_ng_core::Command::Pause);
    }
    if ui
        .add_enabled(tb.can_resume, egui::Button::new("Resume"))
        .clicked()
    {
        app.send(rpi_ng_core::Command::Resume);
    }
    ui.separator();
    if ui
        .add_enabled(tb.can_clear_finished, egui::Button::new("Clear finished"))
        .clicked()
    {
        app.send(rpi_ng_core::Command::ClearFinished);
    }
}

fn toolbar_search(app: &mut App, ui: &mut egui::Ui) {
    ui.with_layout(egui::Layout::right_to_left(egui::Align::Center), |ui| {
        let summary = queue_summary(&app.snapshot.items);
        ui.label(egui::RichText::new(summary).small().weak());
        ui.add(
            egui::TextEdit::singleline(&mut app.search)
                .hint_text("Search queue…")
                .desired_width(180.0),
        );
    });
}

/// Below this available width the server strip wraps into two rows.
/// Measured headlessly with real egui metrics (see the layout tests): the
/// rates group needs ~463px in the worst case and the controls group
/// ~433px, so a single row needs ~903px. The 1120px default window stays
/// single-row; the 860px minimum wraps.
const SERVER_STRIP_SINGLE_ROW_MIN_WIDTH: f32 = 940.0;

fn two_row_server_strip(available_width: f32) -> bool {
    available_width < SERVER_STRIP_SINGLE_ROW_MIN_WIDTH
}

/// Live file-server line: where packages are served from, live in/out
/// transfer rates, totals and the self-check button. Visible whenever the
/// server runs (i.e. while a console session is up) — the natural home for
/// "is the pipe actually working?" questions.
fn server_strip(app: &mut App, ui: &mut egui::Ui) {
    let Some(server) = app.snapshot.server.clone() else {
        return;
    };
    ui.add_space(2.0);
    if two_row_server_strip(ui.available_width()) {
        // Narrow: the rates row and the controls row split, so the session
        // totals can never paint over the URL and transfer rates.
        ui.horizontal(|ui| {
            server_strip_rates(ui, &server);
        });
        ui.horizontal(|ui| {
            server_strip_controls(app, ui, &server);
        });
    } else {
        ui.horizontal(|ui| {
            server_strip_rates(ui, &server);
            server_strip_controls(app, ui, &server);
        });
    }
}

/// Left group: serving URL plus live transfer rates.
fn server_strip_rates(ui: &mut egui::Ui, server: &rpi_ng_core::model::ServerInfo) {
    let p = Palette::of(ui);
    ui.colored_label(p.ok, "●");
    ui.strong("File server");
    ui.monospace(&server.base_url);
    ui.separator();
    ui.colored_label(p.ok, format!("↑ {}", fmt::mbps(server.tx_bps)))
        .on_hover_text("Out: what the console is downloading from you (smoothed)");
    ui.colored_label(p.info, format!("↓ {}", fmt::mbps(server.rx_bps)))
        .on_hover_text("In: request/control traffic (tiny by design)");
}

/// Right group: self-check button + outcome chip + session totals.
fn server_strip_controls(
    app: &mut App,
    ui: &mut egui::Ui,
    server: &rpi_ng_core::model::ServerInfo,
) {
    let totals = format!(
        "sent {} · received {}",
        fmt::bytes(server.tx_total),
        fmt::bytes(server.rx_total)
    );
    ui.with_layout(egui::Layout::right_to_left(egui::Align::Center), |ui| {
        let busy = app.server_checker.busy;
        let label = if busy {
            "Checking…"
        } else {
            "Check File Server"
        };
        if ui
            .button(label)
            .on_hover_text(
                "Test whether the app's own file server is answering on this \
                 computer. If it is OK but the console still cannot download, the \
                 block is between the two machines (firewall / network)",
            )
            .clicked()
        {
            // Probe the actual bound address: the server usually binds a
            // specific interface (the one that can reach the console),
            // which a loopback probe would wrongly report as dead.
            app.server_checker.kick(server.bind);
        }
        if busy {
            ui.add(egui::Spinner::new());
        } else if let Some(result) = app.server_check_result.clone() {
            check_chip(ui, &result);
        }
        ui.label(egui::RichText::new(totals).weak());
    });
}

/// Compact outcome chip for the most recent self-check.
fn check_chip(ui: &mut egui::Ui, result: &ServerCheck) {
    let p = Palette::of(ui);
    match result {
        ServerCheck::Responded(code) => {
            ui.colored_label(p.ok, format!("OK (HTTP {code})"))
                .on_hover_text("The server answered the probe: it is running normally");
        }
        ServerCheck::NotRunning => {
            ui.colored_label(p.neutral, "not running")
                .on_hover_text("Nothing is listening: the server starts with a console connection");
        }
        ServerCheck::NoAnswer => {
            ui.colored_label(p.warn, "no answer").on_hover_text(
                "The port accepted the connection but never replied: a firewall or antivirus may be intercepting it",
            );
        }
        ServerCheck::Error(e) => {
            ui.colored_label(p.error, "check failed")
                .on_hover_text(format!("Unexpected I/O error: {e}"));
        }
    }
}

fn empty_state(ui: &mut egui::Ui) {
    let frame = egui::Frame::new()
        .fill(ui.style().visuals.faint_bg_color)
        .inner_margin(egui::Margin::same(28))
        .corner_radius(10.0);
    frame.show(ui, |ui| {
        ui.set_width(ui.available_width());
        ui.vertical_centered(|ui| {
            ui.add_space(20.0);
            ui.heading("Drop PKG files here");
            ui.label(egui::RichText::new("…or use “Add PKG files…” / “Add folder…” above.").weak());
            // The live file-server strip above the list already shows the
            // serving URL — no need to repeat it here.
            ui.add_space(20.0);
        });
    });
}

/// The queue is not empty — the search box is hiding everything.
fn no_matches(ui: &mut egui::Ui, needle: &str) {
    let frame = egui::Frame::new()
        .fill(ui.style().visuals.faint_bg_color)
        .inner_margin(egui::Margin::same(28))
        .corner_radius(10.0);
    frame.show(ui, |ui| {
        ui.set_width(ui.available_width());
        ui.vertical_centered(|ui| {
            ui.add_space(20.0);
            ui.heading("No packages match this search");
            ui.label(egui::RichText::new(format!("Nothing matches “{needle}”.")));
            ui.label(
                egui::RichText::new("Clear the search box to see the whole queue again.").weak(),
            );
            ui.add_space(20.0);
        });
    });
}

fn item_row(app: &App, ui: &mut egui::Ui, item: &QueueItem, is_first: bool, is_last: bool) {
    let id = item.id;
    let state = item.state;
    let color = Palette::of(ui).item_state(&state);
    let primary = item.primary_part();
    let title = primary
        .map(|p| p.display_title())
        .unwrap_or_else(|| "No name".into());
    let size = fmt::bytes(item.total_size());

    ui.horizontal(|ui| {
        ui.vertical(|ui| {
            ui.set_min_width(ui.available_width() - 240.0);
            ui.horizontal(|ui| {
                ui.strong(egui::RichText::new(title).color(ui.style().visuals.strong_text_color()));
                if item.is_multipart() {
                    ui.label(
                        egui::RichText::new(format!("{} parts", item.parts.len()))
                            .small()
                            .weak(),
                    );
                }
                if let Some(p) = primary.and_then(|p| p.pkg.as_ref()) {
                    if p.is_patch {
                        ui.label(
                            egui::RichText::new("patch")
                                .small()
                                .color(Palette::of(ui).warn),
                        );
                    }
                    if let Some(kind) = p.content_kind.package_type() {
                        ui.label(egui::RichText::new(kind).small().weak());
                    }
                    // Version straight from the package's PARAM.SFO: the app
                    // version for games/patches, the package version otherwise
                    // (themes and some add-ons only carry the latter).
                    if let Some(av) = &p.app_version {
                        ui.label(egui::RichText::new(format!("App v{av}")).small().weak());
                    } else if let Some(pv) = &p.package_version {
                        ui.label(egui::RichText::new(format!("Pkg v{pv}")).small().weak());
                    }
                }
            });
            ui.horizontal(|ui| {
                if let Some(t) = primary.and_then(|p| p.title_id()) {
                    ui.monospace(egui::RichText::new(t).small());
                }
                if let Some(cid) = item.content_id() {
                    ui.monospace(egui::RichText::new(cid).small().weak());
                }
            });
            ui.label(egui::RichText::new(size).small().weak());
        });
        ui.vertical(|ui| {
            ui.set_width(230.0);
            ui.with_layout(egui::Layout::top_down(egui::Align::Min), |ui| {
                state_and_progress(ui, item, color);
            });
        });
    });

    // Error / hint line.
    if let Some(err) = &item.error {
        ui.label(
            egui::RichText::new(err)
                .small()
                .color(Palette::of(ui).error),
        );
    }

    // Actions.
    let actions = RowActions::for_row(state, is_first, is_last);
    ui.horizontal(|ui| {
        if actions.resume_item && ui.small_button("Resume").clicked() {
            app.send(rpi_ng_core::Command::ResumeItem(id));
        }
        if actions.pause && ui.small_button("Pause").clicked() {
            app.send(rpi_ng_core::Command::Pause);
        }
        if actions.mark_completed
            && ui
                .small_button("Mark completed")
                .on_hover_text("You verified on the console that it finished")
                .clicked()
        {
            app.send(rpi_ng_core::Command::MarkCompleted(id));
        }
        if actions.retry && ui.small_button("Retry").clicked() {
            app.send(rpi_ng_core::Command::Retry(id));
        }
        if actions.remove && ui.small_button("Remove").clicked() {
            app.send(rpi_ng_core::Command::Remove(id));
        }
        if actions.cancel && ui.small_button("Cancel").clicked() {
            app.send(rpi_ng_core::Command::Cancel(id));
        }
        if actions.move_up
            && ui
                .add_enabled(!actions.at_top, egui::Button::new("Up").small())
                .on_disabled_hover_text("Already at the top of the queue")
                .clicked()
        {
            app.send(rpi_ng_core::Command::Move { id, delta: -1 });
        }
        if actions.move_down
            && ui
                .add_enabled(!actions.at_bottom, egui::Button::new("Down").small())
                .on_disabled_hover_text("Already at the bottom of the queue")
                .clicked()
        {
            app.send(rpi_ng_core::Command::Move { id, delta: 1 });
        }
    });
}

fn state_and_progress(ui: &mut egui::Ui, item: &QueueItem, color: egui::Color32) {
    ui.horizontal(|ui| {
        ui.colored_label(color, item.state.label());
        if item.progress.is_none() && item.state == ItemState::Submitting {
            ui.add(egui::Spinner::new());
        }
    });
    let Some(p) = &item.progress else { return };
    if item.state == ItemState::Transferring || item.state == ItemState::Installing {
        let pct = p.percent;
        // egui's ProgressBar claims ALL remaining width when no desired
        // width is set, so anything placed after it in the same horizontal
        // paints past the fixed 230px right-hand column (measured: the old
        // single-row layout needed 365px). The bar gets its own row and
        // the readout wraps below it instead.
        let bar = egui::ProgressBar::new(pct.clamp(0.0, 100.0) / 100.0)
            .text(format!("{pct:.1}%"))
            .desired_height(14.0);
        ui.add(bar);
        let speed = if p.speed_bps > 0 {
            format!(" · {}", fmt::bps(p.speed_bps))
        } else {
            String::new()
        };
        ui.label(
            egui::RichText::new(format!(
                "{} / {}{speed}{}",
                fmt::bytes(p.transferred),
                fmt::bytes(p.total),
                eta_suffix(p),
            ))
            .small()
            .weak(),
        );
    } else if p.preparing_percent > 0 && p.preparing_percent < 100 {
        ui.label(
            egui::RichText::new(format!("preparing {}%", p.preparing_percent))
                .small()
                .weak(),
        );
    }
}

fn eta_suffix(p: &ProgressView) -> String {
    match p.eta_sec {
        Some(sec) if sec > 0 => format!(" · {}", fmt::duration(sec)),
        _ => String::new(),
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Pure UI-state logic tests (no GUI harness needed — these pin the contracts
// the egui code renders: which rows are visible, which buttons exist).
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use rpi_ng_core::model::{ContentKind, PartFile, PkgInfo};

    fn item_with(id: u64, state: ItemState) -> QueueItem {
        QueueItem {
            id,
            parts: vec![PartFile {
                id,
                path: "/tmp/game.pkg".into(),
                file_name: "game.pkg".into(),
                size: 10,
                part_index: None,
                pkg: None,
                invalid_reason: None,
            }],
            state,
            error: None,
            task_id: None,
            task_owner: None,
            job_id: None,
            console_title: None,
            progress: None,
            attempts: 0,
            added_at: 0,
            updated_at: 0,
        }
    }

    fn item_with_pkg(
        id: u64,
        state: ItemState,
        title: &str,
        cid: &str,
        title_id: &str,
    ) -> QueueItem {
        let mut item = item_with(id, state);
        let part = &mut item.parts[0];
        part.pkg = Some(PkgInfo {
            content_id: cid.into(),
            title_id: Some(title_id.into()),
            content_kind: ContentKind::Gd,
            is_patch: false,
            title: Some(title.into()),
            app_version: None,
            package_version: None,
            category: None,
            header_size: 0,
        });
        item
    }

    // -- search visibility ("item not showing" bug class) -------------------

    #[test]
    fn empty_needle_shows_everything() {
        // The caller passes search.trim().to_lowercase(); an empty or
        // whitespace-only box must show the whole queue.
        assert!(matches_search("", &item_with(0, ItemState::Queued)));
        assert!(matches_search(
            "   ".trim(),
            &item_with(1, ItemState::Failed)
        ));
    }

    #[test]
    fn search_matches_title_file_title_id_and_content_id() {
        let item = item_with_pkg(
            0,
            ItemState::Queued,
            "Red Dead Redemption",
            "UP0001-CUSA03041_00-REDEMPTION000002",
            "CUSA03041",
        );
        // Parsed metadata wins the display title, but the file name must
        // still match — users search what they see on disk.
        assert!(matches_search("red dead", &item), "title match");
        assert!(matches_search("game.pkg", &item), "file name match");
        assert!(matches_search("cusa03041", &item), "title id match");
        assert!(
            matches_search("up0001-cusa03041", &item),
            "content id match"
        );
        assert!(
            matches_search("redemption000002", &item),
            "content id tail match"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn search_no_match_is_false_not_panic() {
        let item = item_with(0, ItemState::Queued);
        assert!(!matches_search("zzz-not-there", &item));
        // An unparsed item (pkg: None) must not match on empty fields.
        assert!(!matches_search("CUSA", &item));
    }

    // -- per-row action buttons ("button does nothing" bug class) ----------

    #[test]
    fn row_actions_match_every_state() {
        // (state, expected RowActions)
        let cases: Vec<(ItemState, RowActions)> = vec![
            // Freshly queued: manage freely, nothing to pause or cancel.
            (
                ItemState::Queued,
                RowActions {
                    pause: false,
                    resume_item: false,
                    mark_completed: false,
                    retry: false,
                    remove: true,
                    cancel: false,
                    move_up: true,
                    move_down: true,
                    at_top: false,
                    at_bottom: false,
                },
            ),
            // Preparing/Submitting/Transferring/Installing: the engine owns
            // the item — only whole-queue Pause and per-item Cancel exist.
            (
                ItemState::Preparing,
                RowActions {
                    pause: true,
                    resume_item: false,
                    mark_completed: false,
                    retry: false,
                    remove: false,
                    cancel: true,
                    move_up: false,
                    move_down: false,
                    at_top: false,
                    at_bottom: false,
                },
            ),
            (
                ItemState::Submitting,
                RowActions {
                    pause: true,
                    resume_item: false,
                    mark_completed: false,
                    retry: false,
                    remove: false,
                    cancel: true,
                    move_up: false,
                    move_down: false,
                    at_top: false,
                    at_bottom: false,
                },
            ),
            (
                ItemState::Transferring,
                RowActions {
                    pause: true,
                    resume_item: false,
                    mark_completed: false,
                    retry: false,
                    remove: false,
                    cancel: true,
                    move_up: false,
                    move_down: false,
                    at_top: false,
                    at_bottom: false,
                },
            ),
            (
                ItemState::Installing,
                RowActions {
                    pause: true,
                    resume_item: false,
                    mark_completed: false,
                    retry: false,
                    remove: false,
                    cancel: true,
                    move_up: false,
                    move_down: false,
                    at_top: false,
                    at_bottom: false,
                },
            ),
            // Paused: per-item Resume (NOT whole-queue Pause) + management.
            (
                ItemState::Paused,
                RowActions {
                    pause: false,
                    resume_item: true,
                    mark_completed: false,
                    retry: false,
                    remove: true,
                    cancel: false,
                    move_up: true,
                    move_down: true,
                    at_top: false,
                    at_bottom: false,
                },
            ),
            // Completed/Failed/Cancelled/Invalid: only management + Retry.
            (
                ItemState::Completed,
                RowActions {
                    pause: false,
                    resume_item: false,
                    mark_completed: false,
                    retry: true,
                    remove: true,
                    cancel: false,
                    move_up: true,
                    move_down: true,
                    at_top: false,
                    at_bottom: false,
                },
            ),
            (
                ItemState::Failed,
                RowActions {
                    pause: false,
                    resume_item: false,
                    mark_completed: false,
                    retry: true,
                    remove: true,
                    cancel: false,
                    move_up: true,
                    move_down: true,
                    at_top: false,
                    at_bottom: false,
                },
            ),
            (
                ItemState::Cancelled,
                RowActions {
                    pause: false,
                    resume_item: false,
                    mark_completed: false,
                    retry: true,
                    remove: true,
                    cancel: false,
                    move_up: true,
                    move_down: true,
                    at_top: false,
                    at_bottom: false,
                },
            ),
            (
                ItemState::Invalid,
                RowActions {
                    pause: false,
                    resume_item: false,
                    mark_completed: false,
                    retry: true,
                    remove: true,
                    cancel: false,
                    move_up: true,
                    move_down: true,
                    at_top: false,
                    at_bottom: false,
                },
            ),
            // Unknown: user decides the outcome — Mark completed OR Retry.
            (
                ItemState::Unknown,
                RowActions {
                    pause: false,
                    resume_item: false,
                    mark_completed: true,
                    retry: true,
                    remove: true,
                    cancel: false,
                    move_up: true,
                    move_down: true,
                    at_top: false,
                    at_bottom: false,
                },
            ),
        ];
        for (state, expected) in cases {
            assert_eq!(RowActions::for_state(state), expected, "state {state:?}");
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn row_actions_never_mix_cancel_and_remove() {
        // The pair is mutually exclusive per state: Cancel needs an in-flight
        // task, Remove must never yank one out from under the engine.
        for state in [
            ItemState::Queued,
            ItemState::Preparing,
            ItemState::Submitting,
            ItemState::Transferring,
            ItemState::Installing,
            ItemState::Paused,
            ItemState::Completed,
            ItemState::Failed,
            ItemState::Cancelled,
            ItemState::Invalid,
            ItemState::Unknown,
        ] {
            let a = RowActions::for_state(state);
            assert_ne!(a.cancel, a.remove, "state {state:?}");
            assert_eq!(a.cancel, a.pause, "cancel tracks active (pause) state");
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn row_actions_know_the_queue_boundaries() {
        // Middle of the queue: no caps.
        let a = RowActions::for_row(ItemState::Queued, false, false);
        assert!(!a.at_top && !a.at_bottom);
        assert_eq!(a, RowActions::for_state(ItemState::Queued));
        // First row: Up is capped; the state flags stay untouched.
        let a = RowActions::for_row(ItemState::Queued, true, false);
        assert!(a.at_top && !a.at_bottom);
        assert!(a.move_up && a.move_down, "state flags remain state-only");
        // Last row: Down is capped.
        let a = RowActions::for_row(ItemState::Completed, false, true);
        assert!(!a.at_top && a.at_bottom);
        // A single-item queue caps both sides: two honest disabled buttons
        // instead of two dead ones.
        let a = RowActions::for_row(ItemState::Queued, true, true);
        assert!(a.at_top && a.at_bottom);
    }

    // -- toolbar gating -----------------------------------------------------

    #[test]
    fn toolbar_on_empty_queue() {
        let tb = ToolbarState::derive(false, false, &[]);
        assert!(tb.can_add);
        assert!(tb.can_start);
        assert_eq!(tb.start_label, "Start queue");
        assert!(!tb.can_pause);
        assert!(!tb.can_resume);
        assert!(!tb.can_clear_finished);
    }

    #[test]
    fn toolbar_while_queue_running() {
        let items = vec![
            item_with(0, ItemState::Transferring),
            item_with(1, ItemState::Queued),
        ];
        let tb = ToolbarState::derive(false, false, &items);
        assert!(tb.can_add, "adding while running is allowed");
        assert!(!tb.can_start, "no double-start");
        assert_eq!(tb.start_label, "Queue running");
        assert!(tb.can_pause);
        assert!(!tb.can_resume);
    }

    #[test]
    fn toolbar_with_paused_item() {
        let items = vec![item_with(0, ItemState::Paused)];
        let tb = ToolbarState::derive(false, false, &items);
        assert!(
            tb.can_start,
            "start is allowed when only paused items exist"
        );
        assert!(!tb.can_pause);
        assert!(tb.can_resume);
    }

    #[test]
    fn toolbar_with_finished_item() {
        let items = vec![item_with(0, ItemState::Completed)];
        let tb = ToolbarState::derive(false, false, &items);
        assert!(tb.can_clear_finished);
        assert!(!tb.can_pause);
    }

    #[test]
    fn toolbar_busy_and_dialog_gates() {
        // Engine busy: everything that starts work goes off.
        let tb = ToolbarState::derive(true, false, &[]);
        assert!(!tb.can_add);
        assert_eq!(
            tb.add_disabled_reason,
            "Wait for the current operation to finish"
        );
        assert!(!tb.can_start);

        // A native dialog open: Add must go off AND the disabled hover must
        // blame the dialog (the one gate the user can actually close).
        let tb = ToolbarState::derive(false, true, &[]);
        assert!(!tb.can_add);
        assert_eq!(tb.add_disabled_reason, "Close the open file dialog first");
        // Start does not depend on the dialog.
        assert!(tb.can_start);
    }

    // -- toolbar layout (R-03: no collisions at the 860x560 minimum) -------

    #[test]
    fn toolbar_wraps_below_the_single_row_threshold() {
        // Usable central-panel width at the 860x560 minimum window
        // (8px panel margin each side) and at the 1120x760 default.
        assert!(two_row_toolbar(844.0), "minimum window must wrap");
        assert!(!two_row_toolbar(1104.0), "default window stays single-row");
        assert!(!two_row_toolbar(SINGLE_ROW_MIN_WIDTH));
        assert!(
            !two_row_toolbar(1920.0 - 16.0),
            "wide windows stay single-row"
        );
    }

    /// Measured with real egui metrics (headless pass), not estimates: the
    /// two wrapped rows must fit the minimum window, and the single-row
    /// threshold must cover what the single row actually needs.
    #[test]
    fn toolbar_rows_fit_the_minimum_window_when_measured() {
        let ctx = egui::Context::default();
        let output = ctx.run_ui(egui::RawInput::default(), |ui| {
            let min_usable = 860.0 - 2.0 * 8.0;
            let buttons_row = ui
                .horizontal(|ui| {
                    for label in [
                        "Add PKG files…",
                        "Add folder…",
                        "Queue running",
                        "Pause",
                        "Resume",
                        "Clear finished",
                    ] {
                        let _ = ui.button(label);
                    }
                    ui.separator();
                    ui.separator();
                    ui.min_rect()
                })
                .inner;
            eprintln!("MEASURE buttons_row {:.0}", buttons_row.width());
            assert!(
                buttons_row.width() <= min_usable,
                "buttons row {:.0}px exceeds the {:.0}px minimum window",
                buttons_row.width(),
                min_usable
            );

            let search_row = ui
                .horizontal(|ui| {
                    // Worst-case summary text (6-digit counts in every slot).
                    ui.label(
                        egui::RichText::new(
                            "999999 item(s) · 999999 done · 999999 active · 999999 failed",
                        )
                        .small()
                        .weak(),
                    );
                    let mut search = String::new();
                    ui.add(
                        egui::TextEdit::singleline(&mut search)
                            .hint_text("Search queue…")
                            .desired_width(180.0),
                    );
                    ui.min_rect()
                })
                .inner;
            eprintln!("MEASURE search_row {:.0}", search_row.width());
            assert!(
                search_row.width() <= min_usable,
                "search row {:.0}px exceeds the {:.0}px minimum window",
                search_row.width(),
                min_usable
            );

            // A single row needs both groups plus one inter-group gap; the
            // threshold must cover it or the wrap would be pointless. The
            // 30px headroom absorbs small metric drift across platforms.
            let gap = ui.style().spacing.item_spacing.x;
            let single_row_needs = buttons_row.width() + gap + search_row.width();
            assert!(
                SINGLE_ROW_MIN_WIDTH >= single_row_needs + 30.0,
                "threshold {:.0}px leaves <30px over the {:.0}px single-row need",
                SINGLE_ROW_MIN_WIDTH,
                single_row_needs
            );
        });
        output.drop_without_applying_deltas();
    }

    // -- progress readout stays inside its column (R-03) -------------------

    fn item_with_progress(state: ItemState) -> QueueItem {
        let mut item = item_with(0, state);
        item.progress = Some(ProgressView {
            percent: 45.3,
            transferred: 26_367_164_620,
            total: 53_687_091_200,
            speed_bps: 117_000_000,
            eta_sec: Some(302),
            piece_index: 0,
            piece_total: 1,
            preparing_percent: 100,
            local_copy_percent: 0,
            raw_error: 0,
        });
        item
    }

    /// The right-hand column of a queue row is a fixed 230px. The progress
    /// bar claims all remaining width of its row, so the size/speed readout
    /// must live on its own line below it: the pre-fix single-row layout
    /// measured 365px and painted the readout 135px past the column edge,
    /// clipped by the central panel.
    #[test]
    fn progress_readout_stays_inside_the_column() {
        let ctx = egui::Context::default();
        let output = ctx.run_ui(egui::RawInput::default(), |ui| {
            for state in [ItemState::Transferring, ItemState::Installing] {
                let item = item_with_progress(state);
                let rect = ui
                    .allocate_ui(egui::vec2(230.0, 300.0), |ui| {
                        state_and_progress(ui, &item, egui::Color32::WHITE);
                        ui.min_rect()
                    })
                    .inner;
                assert!(
                    rect.width() <= 230.0 + 1.0,
                    "{state:?} progress row {:.0}px overflows the 230px column",
                    rect.width()
                );
            }
        });
        output.drop_without_applying_deltas();
    }

    /// The readout must keep carrying every number the old layout showed:
    /// percent, transferred, total, speed and ETA (the transferred bytes
    /// used to be printed twice; now they appear once).
    #[test]
    fn progress_readout_shows_all_the_numbers() {
        // The bar text is "{pct:.1}%"; the readout line is built from the
        // same fmt helpers the row renders with, so pin their combination.
        let p = ProgressView {
            percent: 45.3,
            transferred: 26_367_164_620,
            total: 53_687_091_200,
            speed_bps: 117_000_000,
            eta_sec: Some(302),
            piece_index: 0,
            piece_total: 1,
            preparing_percent: 100,
            local_copy_percent: 0,
            raw_error: 0,
        };
        let speed = format!(" · {}", fmt::bps(p.speed_bps));
        let readout = format!(
            "{} / {}{speed}{}",
            fmt::bytes(p.transferred),
            fmt::bytes(p.total),
            eta_suffix(&p)
        );
        for part in ["24.6 GiB", "50.0 GiB", "111.6 MiB/s", "5m 02s"] {
            assert!(readout.contains(part), "readout {readout} lacks {part}");
        }
    }

    // -- server strip fits the minimum window (R-03) ------------------------

    #[test]
    fn server_strip_wraps_below_its_threshold() {
        assert!(two_row_server_strip(844.0), "minimum window must wrap");
        assert!(
            !two_row_server_strip(1104.0),
            "default window stays single-row"
        );
        assert!(!two_row_server_strip(SERVER_STRIP_SINGLE_ROW_MIN_WIDTH));
    }

    /// Measured with real egui metrics, not estimates: the wrapped rows must
    /// fit the 860x560 minimum window, and the single-row threshold must
    /// cover what the single row actually needs (worst-case rates, chip and
    /// totals).
    #[test]
    fn server_strip_rows_fit_the_minimum_window_when_measured() {
        let ctx = egui::Context::default();
        let output = ctx.run_ui(egui::RawInput::default(), |ui| {
            let min_usable = 860.0 - 2.0 * 8.0;

            let rates = ui
                .horizontal(|ui| {
                    ui.colored_label(egui::Color32::WHITE, "●");
                    ui.strong("File server");
                    ui.monospace("http://192.168.1.42:12899/");
                    ui.separator();
                    ui.colored_label(egui::Color32::WHITE, "↑ 1000 Mbps");
                    ui.colored_label(egui::Color32::WHITE, "↓ 1000 Mbps");
                    ui.min_rect()
                })
                .inner;
            eprintln!("MEASURE server rates row {:.0}", rates.width());
            assert!(
                rates.width() <= min_usable,
                "rates row {:.0}px exceeds the {:.0}px minimum window",
                rates.width(),
                min_usable
            );

            let controls = ui
                .horizontal(|ui| {
                    let _ = ui.button("Check File Server");
                    ui.colored_label(egui::Color32::WHITE, "OK (HTTP 400)");
                    ui.label("sent 1023.9 GiB · received 1023.9 MiB");
                    ui.min_rect()
                })
                .inner;
            eprintln!("MEASURE server controls row {:.0}", controls.width());
            assert!(
                controls.width() <= min_usable,
                "controls row {:.0}px exceeds the {:.0}px minimum window",
                controls.width(),
                min_usable
            );

            let gap = ui.style().spacing.item_spacing.x;
            let single_row_needs = rates.width() + gap + controls.width();
            eprintln!("MEASURE server single-row need {:.0}", single_row_needs);
            assert!(
                SERVER_STRIP_SINGLE_ROW_MIN_WIDTH >= single_row_needs + 30.0,
                "threshold {:.0}px leaves <30px over the {:.0}px single-row need",
                SERVER_STRIP_SINGLE_ROW_MIN_WIDTH,
                single_row_needs
            );
        });
        output.drop_without_applying_deltas();
    }
}
```

`crates/rpi-ng/src/servercheck.rs`:

```rs
//! "Check File Server" — loopback self-test of the local file server.
//!
//! The check runs on a plain background thread (the probe is a single tiny
//! TCP exchange, std-only — no runtime needed) and reports through a channel
//! the app polls once per frame, exactly like the update checker. Its purpose
//! is diagnostic: it tells the user whether the app's own server is alive,
//! which separates "our server is down" from "something between the two
//! machines is blocking" (firewall, subnet, AP isolation).

use std::{
    net::SocketAddr,
    sync::mpsc::{Receiver, Sender, channel},
    time::Duration,
};

use rpi_ng_core::fileserver::{self, ServerCheck};

/// How long the probe waits for a connect/read before declaring `NoAnswer`.
const TIMEOUT: Duration = Duration::from_millis(1500);

pub struct ServerChecker {
    tx: Sender<ServerCheck>,
    rx: Receiver<ServerCheck>,
    /// True while a probe is in flight (prevents stacking, drives repaints).
    pub busy: bool,
}

impl ServerChecker {
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        let (tx, rx) = channel();
        Self {
            tx,
            rx,
            busy: false,
        }
    }

    /// Start a background probe of the server's bound address unless one is
    /// already running.
    pub fn kick(&mut self, bind: SocketAddr) {
        if self.busy {
            return;
        }
        self.busy = true;
        let tx = self.tx.clone();
        let fallback_tx = tx.clone();
        let build = std::thread::Builder::new().name("rpi-ng-server-check".into());
        if build
            .spawn(move || {
                let result = fileserver::self_check(bind, TIMEOUT);
                let _ = tx.send(result);
            })
            .is_err()
        {
            // Thread spawn failed: still honor the promise of a result.
            let _ = fallback_tx.send(ServerCheck::Error(
                "could not start the file-server check".into(),
            ));
        }
    }

    /// Poll for a finished probe. Returns the outcome exactly once.
    pub fn poll(&mut self) -> Option<ServerCheck> {
        match self.rx.try_recv() {
            Ok(result) => {
                self.busy = false;
                Some(result)
            }
            Err(_) => None,
        }
    }
}

impl Default for ServerChecker {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self::new()
    }
}

```

`crates/rpi-ng/src/settings_view.rs`:

```rs
//! Settings window (persisted via the engine).
//!
//! The editable draft lives on [`App`] so it survives across frames: a
//! per-frame local clone would reset on every repaint and silently drop
//! every edit before the user could press Save (the v0.0.1 settings bug).

use eframe::egui;

use rpi_ng_core::{
    fileserver::ServerCheck,
    settings::{FailurePolicy, ThemeMode},
};

use crate::{
    app::{App, escape_closes_top_window},
    palette::Palette,
};

pub fn show(app: &mut App, ctx: &egui::Context, open: &mut bool) {
    let Some(draft) = app.settings_draft.as_mut() else {
        // No draft means the window is not supposed to be open.
        *open = false;
        return;
    };
    // Values the window body needs from `app` without fighting the draft
    // borrow (the closure only captures `draft` + these copies).
    let server_bind = app.snapshot.server.as_ref().map(|s| s.bind);
    let check_busy = app.server_checker.busy;
    let check_result = app.server_check_result.clone();
    let mut save = false;
    let mut kick_check: Option<std::net::SocketAddr> = None;
    let window = egui::Window::new("Settings")
        .open(open)
        .collapsible(false)
        .resizable(false)
        .min_width(460.0)
        .show(ctx, |ui| {
            egui::Grid::new("settings_grid")
                .num_columns(2)
                .spacing([16.0, 8.0])
                .show(ui, |ui| {
                    ui.label("Theme");
                    ui.horizontal(|ui| {
                        egui::ComboBox::from_id_salt("theme")
                            .selected_text(theme_label(draft.theme))
                            .show_ui(ui, |ui| {
                                ui.selectable_value(&mut draft.theme, ThemeMode::System, "System");
                                ui.selectable_value(&mut draft.theme, ThemeMode::Light, "Light");
                                ui.selectable_value(&mut draft.theme, ThemeMode::Dark, "Dark");
                            });
                    });
                    ui.end_row();

                    ui.label("File server port");
                    ui.horizontal(|ui| {
                        let mut fixed = draft.server_port.is_some();
                        if ui.checkbox(&mut fixed, "Fixed port").changed() {
                            draft.server_port = if fixed { Some(12899) } else { None };
                        }
                        if let Some(port) = &mut draft.server_port {
                            ui.add(egui::DragValue::new(port).range(1024..=65535).speed(1));
                        } else {
                            ui.label(egui::RichText::new("automatic").weak());
                        }
                    });
                    ui.end_row();

                    ui.label("File server");
                    ui.horizontal(|ui| {
                        let label = if check_busy {
                            "Checking…"
                        } else {
                            "Check File Server"
                        };
                        if ui
                            .add_enabled(server_bind.is_some(), egui::Button::new(label))
                            .on_disabled_hover_text(
                                "The file server starts when you connect to a console; \
                                 connect first, then check",
                            )
                            .on_hover_text(
                                "Test whether the app's own file server is answering on this \
                                 computer. If it is OK but the console still cannot download, the \
                                 block is between the two machines (firewall / network)",
                            )
                            .clicked()
                        {
                            kick_check = server_bind;
                        }
                        if check_busy {
                            ui.add(egui::Spinner::new());
                        } else if let Some(result) = &check_result {
                            let p = Palette::of(ui);
                            let color = p.server_check(result);
                            match result {
                                ServerCheck::Responded(code) => {
                                    ui.colored_label(color, format!("OK (HTTP {code})"))
                                }
                                ServerCheck::NotRunning => ui.colored_label(color, "not running"),
                                ServerCheck::NoAnswer => ui.colored_label(color, "no answer"),
                                ServerCheck::Error(_) => ui.colored_label(color, "check failed"),
                            };
                        }
                    });
                    ui.end_row();

                    ui.label("Network scan");
                    ui.checkbox(
                        &mut draft.discovery_enabled,
                        "Scan for consoles automatically",
                    );
                    ui.end_row();

                    ui.label("Progress polling");
                    ui.horizontal(|ui| {
                        let mut ms = draft.poll_interval.as_millis() as i64;
                        ui.add(
                            egui::DragValue::new(&mut ms)
                                .range(250..=5000)
                                .speed(50)
                                .suffix(" ms"),
                        );
                        draft.poll_interval =
                            std::time::Duration::from_millis(ms.clamp(250, 5000) as u64);
                    });
                    ui.end_row();

                    ui.label("Folder scans");
                    ui.checkbox(
                        &mut draft.folder_scan_recursive,
                        "Include subfolders by default",
                    );
                    ui.end_row();

                    ui.label("Queue");
                    ui.horizontal(|ui| {
                        ui.checkbox(&mut draft.auto_advance, "Start the next item automatically");
                    });
                    ui.end_row();

                    ui.label("When a transfer fails");
                    ui.horizontal(|ui| {
                        egui::ComboBox::from_id_salt("on_failure")
                            .selected_text(failure_label(draft.on_failure))
                            .show_ui(ui, |ui| {
                                ui.selectable_value(
                                    &mut draft.on_failure,
                                    FailurePolicy::Continue,
                                    "Continue with the next package",
                                );
                                ui.selectable_value(
                                    &mut draft.on_failure,
                                    FailurePolicy::Pause,
                                    "Pause the queue",
                                );
                            });
                    });
                    ui.end_row();

                    ui.label("Updates");
                    ui.horizontal(|ui| {
                        ui.checkbox(
                            &mut draft.auto_check_updates,
                            "Check for updates on startup",
                        );
                    });
                    ui.end_row();
                });

            ui.add_space(10.0);
            ui.horizontal(|ui| {
                if ui.button("Save").clicked() {
                    save = true;
                }
            });

            // Footnotes: full width, regular size, quiet color — visible when
            // you look for them, silent when you don't. (v0.0.2 had these
            // crammed next to Save in `.small()`, which users couldn't read.)
            ui.add_space(6.0);
            ui.separator();
            ui.label(
                egui::RichText::new("The file-server port applies the next time you connect.")
                    .weak(),
            );
            ui.label(
                egui::RichText::new(
                    "The update check only asks GitHub for the latest release tag; \
                     nothing else is sent.",
                )
                .weak(),
            );
        });
    if let Some(w) = window
        && escape_closes_top_window(ctx, Some(w.response.layer_id))
    {
        *open = false;
    }
    if save && let Some(draft) = app.settings_draft.clone() {
        app.settings = draft.clone();
        app.send(rpi_ng_core::Command::SetSettings(Box::new(draft)));
    }
    if let Some(bind) = kick_check {
        // Probe the bound address — the server usually binds a specific
        // interface, which a loopback probe would wrongly report as dead.
        app.server_checker.kick(bind);
    }
}

fn theme_label(mode: ThemeMode) -> &'static str {
    match mode {
        ThemeMode::System => "System",
        ThemeMode::Light => "Light",
        ThemeMode::Dark => "Dark",
    }
}

fn failure_label(policy: FailurePolicy) -> &'static str {
    match policy {
        FailurePolicy::Continue => "Continue with the next package",
        FailurePolicy::Pause => "Pause the queue",
    }
}
```

`crates/rpi-ng/src/updater.rs`:

```rs
//! In-app update checks against the project's GitHub releases.
//!
//! The check runs on a dedicated thread with its own tiny tokio runtime so it
//! can never block the UI thread, and reports back through a channel the app
//! polls once per frame. Only a plain GET to the releases API is ever sent —
//! no telemetry, no identifiers.

use std::sync::mpsc::{Receiver, Sender, channel};

use rpi_ng_core::update::{self, UpdateInfo};

/// A finished check: `Ok(None)` = no newer release, `Ok(Some)` = update
/// available, `Err` = the check itself failed (network, rate limit, ...).
pub type CheckResult = Result<Option<UpdateInfo>, String>;

pub struct Updater {
    tx: Sender<CheckResult>,
    rx: Receiver<CheckResult>,
    /// True while a background check is in flight (used to avoid stacking
    /// checks and to keep the UI repainting).
    pub busy: bool,
}

impl Updater {
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        let (tx, rx) = channel();
        Self {
            tx,
            rx,
            busy: false,
        }
    }

    /// Start a background check unless one is already running.
    pub fn kick(&mut self) {
        if self.busy {
            return;
        }
        self.busy = true;
        let tx = self.tx.clone();
        spawn_check(tx);
    }

    /// Poll for a finished check. Returns the result of the most recent
    /// completed check, exactly once.
    pub fn poll(&mut self) -> Option<CheckResult> {
        match self.rx.try_recv() {
            Ok(result) => {
                self.busy = false;
                Some(result)
            }
            Err(_) => None,
        }
    }
}

impl Default for Updater {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self::new()
    }
}

fn spawn_check(tx: Sender<CheckResult>) {
    // Kept for the spawn-failure path: the thread never runs, so the promise
    // of a result must still be honored or the UI would wait forever.
    let fallback_tx = tx.clone();
    let build = std::thread::Builder::new().name("rpi-ng-update-check".into());
    if build
        .spawn(move || {
            let rt = match tokio::runtime::Builder::new_current_thread()
                .enable_all()
                .build()
            {
                Ok(rt) => rt,
                Err(e) => {
                    let _ = tx.send(Err(format!("could not start the check: {e}")));
                    return;
                }
            };
            let result = rt.block_on(async {
                let client = update::http_client();
                match update::check_latest(&client, update::RELEASES_API_URL, rpi_ng_core::VERSION)
                    .await
                {
                    Ok(opt) => Ok(opt),
                    Err(e) => Err(e.to_string()),
                }
            });
            let _ = tx.send(result);
        })
        .is_err()
    {
        // Thread spawn failed (resource exhaustion): report instead of hanging.
        let _ = fallback_tx.send(Err("could not start the update check".into()));
    }
}

```

`crates/rpi-ng/tests/assets_test.rs`:

```rs
//! Guards for the shipped icon and packaging assets (see `docs/BUILDING.md`).
//!
//! The app shows a real brand icon everywhere a platform allows one:
//! embedded as a Win32 resource in the `.exe` (`build.rs`), decoded at
//! runtime for the window title bar (`main.rs`), inside the macOS `.app`
//! bundle (`packaging/macos/Info.plist` + `AppIcon.icns`), and as freedesktop
//! icon-theme files for Linux (`packaging/linux/`). These tests pin every
//! file and the cross-file naming (binary == app_id == `Icon=` ==
//! `StartupWMClass`) so a regeneration or rename mistake fails CI instead
//! of shipping a broken or orphaned icon.

use std::collections::BTreeMap;
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};

/// The name that ties the binary, the X11/Wayland `app_id` (`main.rs`), the
/// `.desktop` file name, its `Icon=`/`StartupWMClass` keys and the hicolor
/// icon file names together.
const APP_NAME: &str = "remote-package-installer-ng";

fn repo_file(rel: &[&str]) -> PathBuf {
    let mut path = Path::new(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")).to_path_buf();
    for part in rel {
        path.push(part);
    }
    path
}

fn asset(name: &str) -> Vec<u8> {
    let path = repo_file(&["assets", name]);
    std::fs::read(&path).unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("missing asset {}: {e}", path.display()))
}

#[test]
fn master_icon_is_a_1024_square() {
    let img = image::load_from_memory(&asset("icon.png")).expect("master icon decodes");
    assert_eq!((img.width(), img.height()), (1024, 1024));
}

#[test]
fn runtime_window_icon_decodes_the_way_main_rs_does() {
    let bytes = asset("icon_256.png");
    let rgba = image::load_from_memory(&bytes)
        .expect("runtime window icon decodes")
        .to_rgba8();
    assert_eq!((rgba.width(), rgba.height()), (256, 256));
    assert_eq!(rgba.as_raw().len(), 256 * 256 * 4);
}

#[test]
fn windows_ico_is_a_multi_size_resource() {
    let data = asset("icon.ico");
    assert!(data.len() >= 6, "truncated ICONDIR");
    assert_eq!(&data[0..4], &[0, 0, 1, 0], "ICO magic (type 1 = icon)");
    let count = u16::from_le_bytes([data[4], data[5]]) as usize;
    assert_eq!(count, 7, "expected 16..256 plus common in-betweens");

    let mut sizes = Vec::new();
    for i in 0..count {
        let e = 6 + i * 16;
        let w = if data[e] == 0 { 256 } else { data[e] as usize };
        let h = if data[e + 1] == 0 {
            256
        } else {
            data[e + 1] as usize
        };
        assert_eq!(w, h, "entry {i} is not square");
        assert_eq!(
            u16::from_le_bytes([data[e + 6], data[e + 7]]),
            32,
            "entry {i} is not 32 bpp"
        );
        let len =
            u32::from_le_bytes([data[e + 8], data[e + 9], data[e + 10], data[e + 11]]) as usize;
        let off =
            u32::from_le_bytes([data[e + 12], data[e + 13], data[e + 14], data[e + 15]]) as usize;
        assert!(off + len <= data.len(), "entry {i} points outside the file");
        assert_eq!(
            &data[off..off + 4],
            &[0x89, b'P', b'N', b'G'],
            "entry {i} is not PNG data"
        );
        sizes.push(w);
    }
    sizes.sort_unstable();
    assert_eq!(sizes, vec![16, 24, 32, 48, 64, 128, 256]);
}

#[test]
fn macos_icns_is_a_valid_png_container() {
    let data = asset("AppIcon.icns");
    assert_eq!(&data[0..4], b"icns", "icns magic");
    let total = u32::from_be_bytes([data[4], data[5], data[6], data[7]]) as usize;
    assert_eq!(total, data.len(), "length field != file size");

    let mut off = 8;
    let mut types = Vec::new();
    while off < data.len() {
        assert!(off + 8 <= data.len(), "truncated chunk header");
        let typ = String::from_utf8_lossy(&data[off..off + 4]).into_owned();
        let len = u32::from_be_bytes([data[off + 4], data[off + 5], data[off + 6], data[off + 7]])
            as usize;
        assert!(len >= 8 && off + len <= data.len(), "corrupt chunk {typ}");
        assert_eq!(
            &data[off + 8..off + 12],
            &[0x89, b'P', b'N', b'G'],
            "chunk {typ} is not PNG"
        );
        types.push(typ);
        off += len;
    }
    assert_eq!(off, data.len(), "chunks must tile the file exactly");
    for want in ["ic11", "ic12", "ic07", "ic08", "ic09", "ic10"] {
        assert!(
            types.iter().any(|t| t == want),
            "missing {want} (have {types:?})"
        );
    }
}

fn desktop_entry() -> BTreeMap<String, String> {
    let path = repo_file(&[
        "..",
        "..",
        "packaging",
        "linux",
        "remote-package-installer-ng.desktop",
    ]);
    let text = std::fs::read_to_string(&path)
        .unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("missing desktop entry {}: {e}", path.display()));
    let mut map = BTreeMap::new();
    for line in text.lines() {
        let line = line.trim();
        if line.is_empty() || line.starts_with('#') || line.starts_with('[') {
            continue;
        }
        if let Some((key, value)) = line.split_once('=') {
            assert!(
                map.insert(key.trim().to_owned(), value.trim().to_owned())
                    .is_none(),
                "duplicate key {key}"
            );
        }
    }
    map
}

#[test]
fn linux_desktop_entry_is_wellformed_and_matches_the_app() {
    let entry = desktop_entry();
    assert_eq!(entry.get("Type").map(String::as_str), Some("Application"));
    assert_eq!(entry.get("Exec").map(String::as_str), Some(APP_NAME));
    assert_eq!(entry.get("Icon").map(String::as_str), Some(APP_NAME));
    assert_eq!(entry.get("Terminal").map(String::as_str), Some("false"));
    assert_eq!(
        entry.get("StartupWMClass").map(String::as_str),
        Some(APP_NAME)
    );
    assert!(entry.get("Name").is_some_and(|n| !n.is_empty()));

    // The Icon= key resolves inside a freedesktop icon theme: every declared
    // hicolor size must actually ship.
    for size in [48, 64, 128, 256] {
        let dir = format!("{size}x{size}");
        let file = format!("{APP_NAME}.png");
        let icon = repo_file(&[
            "..",
            "..",
            "packaging",
            "linux",
            "icons",
            "hicolor",
            &dir,
            "apps",
            &file,
        ]);
        assert!(icon.is_file(), "missing hicolor icon {}", icon.display());
    }

    // main.rs must set the same app_id (X11 WM_CLASS / Wayland app_id), or
    // docks and taskbars will not associate the window with this entry.
    let main_rs =
        std::fs::read_to_string(repo_file(&["src", "main.rs"])).expect("main.rs readable");
    let needle = format!("const APP_ID: &str = \"{APP_NAME}\";");
    assert!(
        main_rs.contains(&needle),
        "main.rs APP_ID no longer matches the desktop entry (expected {needle})"
    );
}

#[test]
fn macos_bundle_plist_wires_up_the_icns() {
    let path = repo_file(&["..", "..", "packaging", "macos", "Info.plist"]);
    let text = std::fs::read_to_string(&path)
        .unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("missing Info.plist {}: {e}", path.display()));

    assert!(text.contains("<key>CFBundleIconFile</key><string>AppIcon</string>"));
    assert!(text.contains(&format!(
        "<key>CFBundleExecutable</key><string>{APP_NAME}</string>"
    )));
    assert!(text.contains("<key>CFBundleIdentifier</key><string>io.github.aldysan."));
    // The release workflow substitutes the tag version into these fields.
    assert!(text.contains("__VERSION__"));

    // CFBundleIconFile must point at an asset that actually ships next to
    // the executable inside the .app bundle (see release.yml).
    assert!(!asset("AppIcon.icns").is_empty());
}
```

`crates/rpi-ng/tests/source_hygiene.rs`:

```rs
//! Source hygiene: no em dashes in string literals (antislop R-02).
//!
//! Round 1 of the UI audit swept the app crate only, and the core crate's
//! log/error strings (rendered verbatim in the Activity panel and status
//! labels) silently kept theirs. This test tokenizes every workspace
//! source file and fails on any em dash inside a string, char or raw
//! string literal, so the contract holds for both crates from now on.
//! Em dashes in comments are fine: comments never reach the user.

use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};

#[test]
fn no_em_dash_in_string_literals() {
    let app = Path::new(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"));
    let mut roots: Vec<PathBuf> = vec![app.join("src"), app.join("build.rs"), app.join("tests")];
    let core = app.join("../rpi-ng-core");
    roots.extend([core.join("src"), core.join("tests"), core.join("examples")]);

    let mut files: Vec<PathBuf> = Vec::new();
    for root in &roots {
        collect_rs_files(root, &mut files);
    }
    assert!(
        !files.is_empty(),
        "scanner found no sources; the layout changed"
    );

    let mut offenders: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
    for file in &files {
        // This test's own fixtures deliberately contain em dashes.
        if file.file_name().is_some_and(|n| n == "source_hygiene.rs") {
            continue;
        }
        let text = std::fs::read_to_string(file).expect("readable source file");
        for line in em_dash_lines(&text) {
            offenders.push(format!("{}:{}", file.display(), line));
        }
    }
    assert!(
        offenders.is_empty(),
        "em dash in user-facing string literals (use a period, colon, \
         semicolon or parentheses instead):\n  {}",
        offenders.join("\n  ")
    );
}

fn collect_rs_files(dir: &Path, out: &mut Vec<PathBuf>) {
    let Ok(entries) = std::fs::read_dir(dir) else {
        return;
    };
    for entry in entries.flatten() {
        let path = entry.path();
        if path.is_dir() {
            collect_rs_files(&path, out);
        } else if path.extension().is_some_and(|e| e == "rs") {
            out.push(path);
        }
    }
}

/// Line number of every em dash that sits inside a string/char/raw-string
/// literal. A tiny Rust tokenizer: enough states to tell literals from
/// comments, handling escapes, string line-continuations, nested block
/// comments, raw strings (`r"…"`, `r#"…"#`) and lifetimes.
fn em_dash_lines(text: &str) -> Vec<usize> {
    #[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq)]
    enum State {
        Code,
        Str,
        RawStr(usize), // number of leading `#`
        LineComment,
        BlockComment(usize),
    }

    let mut hits = Vec::new();
    let chars: Vec<char> = text.chars().collect();
    let mut state = State::Code;
    let mut line = 1usize;
    let mut i = 0usize;

    while i < chars.len() {
        let c = chars[i];
        let next = chars.get(i + 1).copied();

        if c == '\n' {
            line += 1;
            i += 1;
            if state == State::LineComment {
                state = State::Code;
            }
            continue;
        }

        match state {
            State::Code => match c {
                '/' if next == Some('/') => {
                    state = State::LineComment;
                    i += 2;
                }
                '/' if next == Some('*') => {
                    state = State::BlockComment(1);
                    i += 2;
                }
                '"' => {
                    state = State::Str;
                    i += 1;
                }
                'r' if next == Some('"') => {
                    state = State::RawStr(0);
                    i += 2;
                }
                'r' if next == Some('#') => {
                    let mut hashes = 0usize;
                    let mut j = i + 1;
```
