- `local_copy_percent` — percent of the post-download local copy (install) phase.

### 3.1 Critical quirk: the original emits invalid JSON

`[CONFIRMED]` — `server.c` writes `bits`, `length`, `transferred`, `length_total`,
`transferred_total` and `error_code` as **unquoted hexadecimal literals**
(e.g. `{"bits":0x20,...}`), which strict JSON parsers reject. Any client that claims to
speak to the original payload must therefore parse these responses leniently (accept
`0x…` literals, quoted hex, or plain integers). This project implements a tolerant
parser for exactly this reason, and its mock backend reproduces the quirk faithfully
(see `docs/TESTING.md`).

## 4. Verified limitations and defects of the original

### 4.1 Busy-spin server loop — 100% CPU on console

`[CONFIRMED]` — `server.c server_listen()` is:

```c
for (;;) {
    sb_poll_server(s_server);
}
```

with the server configured via `opts.timeout = "0"` (non-blocking poll). Combined, this
spins one CPU core continuously while the app is open, matching the v3.53 fork's public
claim ("100% CPU busy-spin & screaming fans"). This is a **console-side** defect; a PC
sender cannot fix it, and this project makes no such claim.

### 4.2 App must remain in the foreground to receive commands

`[DOCUMENTED]` — the PS4 suspends background applications, which stops networking. The
upstream README instructs users to keep the app in focus until the "waiting to install"
phase completes, and describes recovery (re-open the app, resume the task) if the
connection is lost. A sender can mitigate this only by detecting task progress and
guiding the user; it cannot prevent suspension.

### 4.3 Progress `bits` are undocumented

`[CONFIRMED]` — the payload forwards BGFT's raw `bits` bitmask with a TODO
("make bits field more user-friendly") and no definition. No first-party documentation
of the bit layout exists in the repository. Community senders variously ignore `bits`
and infer state from byte counters, error codes, and pause/resume calls. This project
does the same (see `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §Progress interpretation) and does **not**
invent bit meanings.

### 4.4 Small JSON parser pool

`[CONFIRMED]` — every handler allocates a 256-node `tiny-json` pool. Long `packages`
arrays (many multipart pieces) or long URLs can exhaust it, failing the request. The
v3.55 fork's changelog (pool raised to 2048 nodes) is consistent with this limit. This
is a console-side constraint; senders should keep install requests small.

### 4.5 No discovery mechanism

`[CONFIRMED]` — the payload exposes no beacon, mDNS, SSDP, or UDP service; the server
listens on plain TCP 12800 only. Auto-discovery from the PC side can therefore only be
a bounded port probe plus API verification, or a fork-specific beacon (e.g. pkg-sender's
receiver broadcasts UDP 12801, which the original does not).

### 4.6 Console-side error surfaces are terse

`[CONFIRMED]` — failures are reported as `{"status":"fail","error_code":0x…}` with raw
Orbis error codes and no message. The PC client must map known codes to actionable
text and leave unknown codes visible verbatim.

### 4.7 Reported but not verifiable in this source

- `[REPORTED]` **CE-32871-1 after closing the app** — the v3.53 fork attributes this to
  root escalation orphaning the `/app0` mount and claims a fix. The public source here
  escalates via custom syscall 410 and contains **no libjbc reference**, so the fork's
  description does not match this source tree; possibly it refers to different base
  binaries. Recorded as unverified; not reproduced.
- `[REPORTED]` **Multipart installs "unstable/truncated"** in the base — the source
  handles multiple pieces correctly in principle (pieces are passed in order and summed
  into `package_size`); no reproducing evidence is available here. Recorded as
  unverified.
- `[REPORTED]` **SIGPIPE crashes on client disconnect mid-transfer** — would live in
  sandbird's send paths; not reproduced (requires console hardware). The fork's claim
  that `MSG_NOSIGNAL` constants were wrong for Orbis is plausible but unverifiable here.

### 4.8 Prerequisites make installs fail late

`[CONFIRMED]` — the payload validates packages (header parse, SFO extraction) **after**
the install request is submitted. A malformed PKG surfaces as a 500 "Internal server
error" with a terse reason. A PC-side sender that parses headers up front (as this
project does) catches most of these before touching the console.

## 5. Baseline reproduction status

| Activity | Status | Reason |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Build original payload | **Not attempted** | Requires the proprietary Orbis SDK + publishing tools under Wine; not available in this environment. Environmental limitation, documented rather than worked around. |
| Run original payload | Not possible | Requires a jailbroken PS4 with HEN-class kernel payload (custom syscall 410). No console hardware in this environment. |
| Idle CPU / memory measurements of original | Not possible | Follows from the above. No numbers are claimed anywhere in this project. |
| Protocol behavior tests | Via mock backend | `rpi-ng-core` integration tests implement a faithful mock of the documented API (including the invalid-JSON quirk). Mocks are **not** evidence of real-console compatibility. |

## 6. Compatibility contract this project must preserve

1. Target address form `http://<console-ip>:12800`, plain HTTP, JSON POST commands.
2. `install` request shapes: `{"type":"direct","packages":[...]}` (multipart pieces in
   order) and `{"type":"ref_pkg_url","url":...}`.
3. Lenient parsing of success/error bodies, including unquoted hex values.
4. Progress polling via `/api/get_task_progress` with byte-counter semantics.
5. Task lifecycle commands: start/stop/pause/resume/unregister.
6. Serving local packages over HTTP with **Range support** and concurrent request
   handling, at a stable URL.
7. Not fabricating completion: an install is complete only when progress reports full
   transfer and no error, never merely when the HTTP request succeeded.

```

`docs/BUILDING.md`:

```md
# Building from source

The README covers normal use with the prebuilt downloads. This page is for
developers who want to build, test or package the app themselves.

## Toolchain

Rust 1.95 or newer — install via [rustup](https://rustup.rs). No other
prerequisites for building and testing: the GUI stack and everything else
are plain Cargo dependencies. (Only regenerating the icon assets needs
Python 3 with Pillow — see the icon section below.)

## Build

```sh
git clone https://github.com/AldySan/Remote-Package-Installer-NG.git
cd Remote-Package-Installer-NG
cargo build --release --locked
```

The binary lands in `target/release/remote-package-installer-ng`
(`remote-package-installer-ng.exe` on Windows). The first build takes a few
minutes.

## Test and lint

```sh
cargo test --workspace --locked
cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings
cargo fmt --all -- --check
```

The suite is self-contained: a mock console implements the wire protocol
(both the legacy flatz dialect and the NextGen v2 job API), and PKG/SFO
fixtures are generated on the fly. No PS4 is needed.

## Release profile

Published binaries are built with maximum compiler optimization, configured
in the workspace `Cargo.toml`:

- `opt-level = 3`, `lto = "fat"`, `codegen-units = 1` — full cross-crate
  optimization
- `strip = true` — symbols stripped, smaller downloads
- Windows builds statically link the C runtime (`.cargo/config.toml`), so no
  "VCRUNTIME140.dll missing" errors on machines without the Visual C++
  redistributable
- The update checker's TLS stack (rustls + aws-lc-rs) builds from source with
  just a C compiler. On Windows x64 it would normally want NASM for its
  assembly routines; `.cargo/config.toml` sets `AWS_LC_SYS_PREBUILT_NASM=1`
  so builds fall back to the prebuilt objects when NASM is not installed
  (Linux and macOS builds are unaffected either way).

## Application icon and platform packaging

The brand icon exists once, as `crates/rpi-ng/assets/icon.png` (1024x1024
RGBA). Every format the platforms need is derived from it by
`python3 packaging/icons/generate.py` (requires Pillow; `--redraw` redraws
the master from the description in the script):

- `crates/rpi-ng/assets/icon.ico` — embedded into the Windows `.exe` as a
  Win32 resource by `crates/rpi-ng/build.rs` (the `winresource` build
  dependency, Windows targets only). This is what Explorer, the taskbar and
  jumplists show before any window exists; the same resource also carries
  FileDescription/ProductName/version strings for Task Manager.
- `crates/rpi-ng/assets/icon_256.png` — decoded at startup in `main.rs` and
  passed to egui as the window icon (`ViewportBuilder::with_icon`): title
  bar and taskbar on Windows and X11. macOS does not support runtime window
  icons — the bundle icon covers it there.
- `crates/rpi-ng/assets/AppIcon.icns` + `packaging/macos/Info.plist` — the
  release workflow builds a universal `RemotePackageInstallerNG.app` bundle
  (lipo + ad-hoc codesign) so Finder, Dock and cmd-tab show the icon.
- `packaging/linux/` — a `.desktop` entry plus hicolor icons shipped in the
  Linux tarball. `main.rs` sets the app_id to the binary name, which X11
  docks (WM_CLASS) and Wayland compositors (app_id) match against the
  desktop file to resolve the window's icon.

`crates/rpi-ng/tests/assets_test.rs` pins every derived file and the naming
convention that ties them together (binary == app_id == `Icon=` ==
`StartupWMClass`), so a regeneration or rename mistake fails CI.

CI builds the same release binary on every push to `dev`/`main`
(`.github/workflows/ci.yml`). Tag pushes (`v*`) trigger
`.github/workflows/release.yml`, which packages a Windows zip, a Linux
tarball (with the optional desktop-integration files) and a universal macOS
app bundle (Intel + Apple Silicon merged with `lipo`) and attaches them to
a GitHub Release.

```

`docs/PS5_ROADMAP.md`:

```md
# PS5 Roadmap

Research summary and go/no-go assessment for PlayStation 5 support. Evidence
levels follow `docs/RESEARCH.md`: **[VERIFIED]** = read in published source;
**[DOCUMENTED]** = stated by the project itself; **[REPORTED]** = third-party
claims.

## 1. Verified capabilities and sources

| Capability | Evidence | Source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| PS5 removed the BGFT install path the PS4 tool relies on (`sceBgftServiceIntDownloadRegisterTaskBy*` → `0x80990006 SCE_BGFT_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED`) | [VERIFIED] | etaHEN `PS5 technical writeups/pkg-writeup.md` |
| PS5 installs go through `libSceAppInstUtil` with ShellCore authid escalation (`0x3800000000000010`) or etaHEN jailbreak IPC | [VERIFIED] | same writeup |
| **etaHEN DPI v1**: JSON API on port **9090** — POST with `url`, `content_name`, `content_id`, `playgo_scenario_id`, `ex_uri`, `icon_url`; replies `{"res": N}` | [VERIFIED] | etaHEN `Source Code/util/source/DirectPKGInstaller.cpp` |
| **etaHEN DPI v2**: web UI + API on port **12800** (libmicrohttpd) | [VERIFIED] | same source |
| **LoopDPI receiver** (`pkg-receiver.elf`, shipped by pkg-sender): listens on **12800** on PS5, installed via the browser exploit once, registers a home-screen shortcut; accepts the RPI-shaped `{"type":"direct","packages":[...]}` install request plus optional `name` / `icon_url` | [VERIFIED] | Loopayeh/pkg-sender source + README |
| A third sender (DirectPackageInstaller) implements PS5 installs via **etaHEN DPI v2** | [DOCUMENTED] | its README |
| Firmware requirements | [REPORTED] | etaHEN supports current firmware ranges; see its releases |

**Conclusion: two independent, source-verified PS5 remote-install paths exist.
PS5 support is feasible — it is a compatibility/engineering problem, not a
research blocker.**

## 2. Differences from the PS4 workflow

| Aspect | PS4 (RPI) | PS5 (etaHEN / LoopDPI) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Transport | JSON POST API, port 12800 | v1: JSON POST on 9090; v2: HTTP on 12800; LoopDPI: RPI-shaped API on 12800 |
| Task registration | BGFT task id + progress polling | DPI returns `{"res": N}` — **no task id, no documented progress polling** in DPI v1 |
| Package types | PS4GD/AC/AL/DP + sub types | PS5 PKG layout, playgo scenario ids, entitlements |
| Package validation | PKG header + SFO (documented here) | PS5 PKG format differs; the PC side must not assume PS4 layouts |
| Console-side preconditions | HEN + RPI app in foreground | etaHEN (or receiver elf) running; receiver persists across app switches |
| Progress reporting | `get_task_progress` counters | v2 web UI has progress; API contract not source-verified here |

## 3. What the current architecture already gives us

- The queue engine, file server, discovery, persistence and UI are
  generation-agnostic — `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §5 was designed for this.
- `ConsoleGeneration` is an explicit model field; nothing in `queue.rs`,
  `fileserver.rs` or `engine.rs` branches on “is PS4”.
- The local file server's Range/streaming contract is exactly what a
  console-pull architecture needs (both PS5 paths pull from the PC).

## 4. What a PS5 adapter needs (dedicated work)

1. **`EtaHenDpiV1Backend`** (9090): install submission, response semantics,
   capability probing, and — critically — an honest progress story (polling
   the DPI v2 endpoints or reporting “submitted, no progress available”).
2. **`LoopDpiBackend`** (12800): RPI-shaped install + `/api/status`; the
   receiver also exposes `/api/files/pull` for non-package files (out of
   scope).
3. **PS5 package validation**: reject PS4 PKGs when a PS5 console is selected
   and vice versa (`pkg::validate_for(generation)` seam already sketched).
4. **Discovery deltas**: etaHEN DPI v2 on 12800 answers like a web app, not
   necessarily like RPI — probes must stay per-generation.
5. **Hardware validation** on a PS5 with etaHEN and with a LoopDPI receiver.

## 5. Go/no-go decision

**GO — as a separate post-v1 milestone.** The PS4 application ships first and
must not depend on PS5 feasibility (it doesn't). PS5 work starts by porting
the two adapters above behind the existing `ConsoleBackend` trait, guarded by
capability detection, with no UI changes beyond rendering new backend rows.

## 6. Blockers / open questions

- DPI v1's `{"res": N}` codes are not documented in the source we inspected —
  needs empirical mapping (hardware).
- Progress reporting quality varies by backend; the UI must degrade honestly
  (show “unknown progress” instead of a fabricated bar).
- Licensing: etaHEN is GPL-3.0 (compatible with our license for *interaction*;
  we do not copy its code); pkg-sender's receiver elf is MIT-distributed
  software — we never redistribute console binaries ourselves.

```

`docs/RESEARCH.md`:

```md
# Ecosystem Research

Phase 1 research of the PlayStation remote-package ecosystem. Findings are separated by
confidence: **[VERIFIED]** (read in published source code during this research),
**[DOCUMENTED]** (stated by the project's own README/releases), **[REPORTED]** (user
reports or third-party claims), **[UNVERIFIED]** (could not be confirmed).

Sources were inspected between 2026-10-27 via the GitHub/Codeberg REST APIs and raw
file fetches; repository state (license, stars, activity) was captured at that time.

## 1. Survey overview

| Project | Kind | Platform | License | Status | Verdict for this project |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| flatz/ps4_remote_pkg_installer | Console payload (API server) | PS4 | none | dormant (2 commits) | **Protocol source of truth** — see `docs/BASELINE.md` |
| rawblue33/Remote-PKG-Installer v3.53/v3.55 | Console payload fork | PS4 | none (binary-only repo) | active releases | Claims catalog; no source to verify — treat as untrusted backend variant |
| Loopayeh/pkg-sender | Desktop sender | Win/Linux | **MIT** | active (2026-10) | Best-engineered open sender; discovery + PS5 receiver; adopt patterns |
| marcussacana/DirectPackageInstaller | Desktop sender/downloader | Win/Linux | **GPL-3.0** | active (2026-06) | URL/debrid workflows, 3 backends incl. etaHEN; out of scope for v1, informs roadmap |
| NookieAI/PS4-Vault | Library manager + sender | Windows | none | active (2026-07) | Library/metadata UX ideas; no reusable protocol code (no license) |
| cy33hc/ps4-ezremote-client | Console-side client | PS4 | GPL-2.0 | very active (2026-10) | Alternative console workflow (no PC needed); informs feature honesty, not our path |
| ItsJokerZz/FPKGi | Console-side installer | PS4/PS5 | GPL-2.0 | **archived** (2026-01) | JSON-list driven installs; archived — reference only |
| GoldHEN/GoldHEN | Kernel payload / HEN | PS4 | unclear (repo ships `.bin` only) | active | Ships "Remote Package Install" feature `[DOCUMENTED]`; closed payload — treat as RPI-compatible backend family |
| etaHEN/etaHEN | Kernel payload / HEN | PS5 | **GPL-3.0** | active (2026-05) | DPI v1 (9090 JSON) + DPI v2 (12800 web) install APIs; core of PS5 feasibility |
| Gkiokan/ps4-remote-pkg-sender | Web sender | browser | none | inactive (2025-01) | Popular web sender; no license — ideas only |
| LightningMods/PS4-Store, Itemzflow | Console-side stores | PS4 | various | active | Contain RPI/BGFT-derived install code `[VERIFIED]` (appear in code search for RPI progress structs) — alternative backends family |

## 2. Project findings

### 2.1 flatz ps4_remote_pkg_installer (original)

Covered fully in `docs/BASELINE.md`. Key protocol facts reused throughout this document:
JSON POST API on port 12800; install via direct URLs or CDN manifest URL; task
lifecycle commands; progress via `get_task_progress`; **the original emits invalid JSON
with unquoted hex literals** `[VERIFIED]`; no discovery beacon `[VERIFIED]`.

### 2.2 Remote-PKG-Installer v3.53/v3.55 (Codeberg, "by Ari")

The repository contains **only a README and a donation image** — the payload is
distributed as release binaries with no source `[VERIFIED by API contents listing]`.
Therefore **none of its claims can be source-verified**. Its changelog is nonetheless
useful as a defect catalog for the original, and several claims cross-check:

- Busy-spin CPU fix (`usleep` when idle) — **consistent with the confirmed `for(;;)
  sb_poll_server()` loop** in the original `[VERIFIED in original]`.
- Web dashboard on port **12801**, mobile-first install flow, `/api/exit` endpoint,
  TV notifications, human-readable progress fields — `[DOCUMENTED]` by the fork only;
  presence of a second port is relevant: senders should not assume 12800 is the only
  service.
- CE-32871-1 exit crash root-caused to privilege escalation orphaning `/app0` —
  `[REPORTED]`; the public flatz source uses syscall 410 and contains no libjbc, so the
  claim does not match the public tree. Console-side regardless — a PC sender cannot fix
  or cause it.
- FreeBSD `MSG_NOSIGNAL`, `SO_REUSEPORT`, `SO_LINGER`, threaded lifetime rework —
  `[REPORTED]`, plausible, unverifiable without source.
- "PS5-safety": not applicable; PS4-only backend.

**Decision:** support it as an opaque RPI-dialect backend (same endpoints), verify
compatibility at runtime via capability probing, and never hard-code assumptions from
its README.

### 2.3 Loopayeh/pkg-sender (MIT) — primary engineering reference

`[VERIFIED]` from source (`LoopDPI.Core/NetDiscovery.cs`, `ConsoleClient.cs`):

- **PS4 and PS5 from one client.** PS4 path: "Remote Package Installer homebrew (port
  12800) or GoldHEN with Payload Server (ports 9090/9021/9020)". PS5 path: ships a
  `pkg-receiver.elf` (branded LoopDPI) sent once via the browser exploit; it keeps
  listening on **12800** and installs a home-screen shortcut.
- **Discovery done right:** enumerates real NICs with real netmasks (never assumes
  /24), computes network/broadcast, caps host enumeration (4096), runs a UDP beacon
  listener on **12801** (magic `PKGSENDER`) with a short timeout, and falls back to a
  parallel TCP sweep of port 12800; dedupes by IP. It also broadcasts a PC-announce on
  **12802** (`PKGSENDER-PC`) so console-side browsers can find the PC.
- **Capability probing pattern:** `GET /api` on the receiver returns the RPI
  "Unsupported method" body — used as a liveness check; `GET /api/status` returns
  `{"busy":true,"active":1}` on newer receivers and HTML on old ones — used as a
  capability check.
- **Protocol quirks handled:** URLs must be encoded exactly once (spaces/special
  characters break installs — their issue #6); `https://` URLs are downgraded to
  `http://` for the console; optional `name` and `icon_url` fields are appended to the
  install JSON (receiver-dependent extras).
- **Extra receiver API** `[VERIFIED]`: `POST /api/files/pull
  {"url","path","mode":"resume"|"overwrite"}` lets the console pull arbitrary PC files
  to `/data/homebrew` (used for disc images).
- Ships self-contained installers (no .NET requirement), AppImage/deb, About-first-run
  UX.

**Adopted by this project:** real-netmask bounded subnet probing, capability probing
instead of port-only detection, single-encoding URL discipline, ports 12800/9090
probing order, and the "don't assume one backend dialect" mindset.

### 2.4 marcussacana/DirectPackageInstaller (GPL-3.0)

`[DOCUMENTED]` from README (source not deep-inspected in this pass):

- Three send methods: **RPI homebrew**, **GoldHEN Payload Server**, **etaHEN DPIv2
  API** — i.e. it independently confirms the etaHEN DPIv2 install path on PS5.
- Feature set: URL-based installs (direct links, AllDebrid/RealDebrid/DebridLink,
  Google Drive, Mediafire, PixelDrain, 1Fichier), PSN update manifests (split PKGs),
  RAR/7z support, resumable and segmented downloads, a local proxy "to speed up PS4
  downloads", CLI mode, JDownloader Click'n'Load, NFS sources, auto LAN cable
  connection (Windows).
- The "automatic proxying" concept is interesting: PC-side buffering can smooth console
  download speeds when the upstream is slow. Not needed for local-file v1.

**Verdict:** strong evidence that multi-backend (RPI/GoldHEN/etaHEN) is the right
adapter shape; URL-source workflows deferred (v1 is local files only).

### 2.5 NookieAI/PS4-Vault (no license)

`[DOCUMENTED]`: Windows-only PKG library manager — scans drives, remembers library,
extracts game name/cover/version/region (i.e. full SFO+icon parsing on PC), duplicate
detection, bulk rename, copy/move with live speed/ETA, and send-to-console. Good UX
reference for library features; **no license → no code reuse**. Our v1 keeps a light
queue-oriented metadata model (title/title-id/size/type), not a full library.

### 2.6 cy33hc/ps4-ezremote-client (GPL-2.0)

`[DOCUMENTED]`: console-side app that browses remote FTP/SFTP/SMB/NFS/WebDAV/HTTP/Google
Drive sources and installs/transfers, with an embedded web server acting as a **proxy**
between the console and remote servers (no PC app needed at all). Background installs
via a companion `ezRemote Server` payload started through GoldHEN's BinLoader; pause/
resume; rest-mode and crash caveats documented honestly ("Do not submit too many
background installs, it can crash ezRemote Server").

**Relevance:** demonstrates the console-pull architecture and honest limitation
reporting. Also confirms GoldHEN BinLoader (9090) as the payload-loading channel used
by companion installers.

### 2.7 ItsJokerZz/FPKGi (GPL-2.0, archived)

`[DOCUMENTED]`: console-side installer driven by JSON content files (like PKGi for
PSP/Vita/PS3), PS4+PS5, downloads from URLs. **Archived 2026-01** — reference only.

### 2.8 GoldHEN (PS4 HEN)

`[VERIFIED]`: the public repo ships prebuilt binaries (`goldhen_2.3_*.bin`) and docs,
not payload source; README lists **"Remote Package Install"** among features, plus FTP
(2121), BinLoader (9090), Klog (3232), internal `/data/pkg` install support.
`[REPORTED]` by both pkg-sender and DirectPackageInstaller: a "Payload Server" mode on
ports 9020/9021 (with 9090) into which senders inject their own installer payload.
Mechanism details are not published in repo docs we could verify.

**Decision for v1:** treat GoldHEN consoles as *possible* RPI-family backends, detected
by probing (12800 first, then 9090) and verified through API responses — never assumed
from brand or port alone. Native "Payload Server" injection is documented as a future
adapter in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`.

### 2.9 etaHEN (PS5) — the PS5 path

`[VERIFIED]` from `Source Code/util/source/DirectPKGInstaller.cpp` and
`PS5 technical writeups/pkg-writeup.md`:

- **BGFT is dead on PS5**: `sceBgftServiceIntDownloadRegisterTaskBy*` returns
  `0x80990006 (SCE_BGFT_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED)` even with correct auth — Sony removed the
  path the PS4 tool relied on. PS5 installs go through `libSceAppInstUtil` with
  ShellCore-authid privilege escalation (or etaHEN jailbreak IPC / plugin system).
- **DPI v1**: JSON API on port **9090** — accepts a JSON object with `url`,
  `content_name`, `content_id`, `playgo_scenario_id`, `ex_uri`, `icon_url`; replies
  `{"res": N}`.
- **DPI v2**: MHD-based web UI + API on port **12800** (asset requests, file download,
  clear-tmp handlers).
- Privilege requirements (authid `0x3800000000000010` or etaHEN jailbreak IPC) are
  documented in the writeup.
- Loopayeh's receiver independently implements an RPI-shaped `/api/install` on 12800 on
  PS5 `[VERIFIED]` — two independent PS5 remote-install implementations exist.

Full feasibility analysis and go/no-go: `docs/PS5_ROADMAP.md`.

### 2.10 Other senders (PS4RPIGUI, FrostySo Ps4-Pkg-Sender, Gkiokan web sender, CPM)

`[VERIFIED]` via code search: all define the same progress model
(`bits/error/length/transferred/length_total/transferred_total/num_index/num_total/
rest_sec/rest_sec_total/preparing_percent/local_copy_percent`) — the field set is
stable across the ecosystem. None of the inspected senders decode `bits` into named
states; they infer state from counters, error codes and the pause/resume commands they
issued (CPM models only Paused/Installing/Finished). This project follows the same
honest approach. Gkiokan's and PS4-Vault's lack of a license blocks any code reuse.

## 3. Cross-cutting conclusions

1. **The wire protocol is the product.** The console payload's API (flatz dialect) is
   the de-facto standard; ecosystem differentiation happens in the PC client.
2. **Backend fragmentation is real but narrow**: RPI dialect (12800) is the common
   denominator; GoldHEN variants and etaHEN add ports 9090 and extra fields. An
   adapter + runtime capability probing is mandatory — hardcoding "GoldHEN ⇒ works" or
   "port open ⇒ PS4" is exactly how other tools end up lying to users.
3. **Every implementer hits the same traps**: invalid hex JSON from the original,
   URL-encoding of package URLs, foreground-suspension, terse error codes. These are
   first-class requirements for our client, not edge cases.
4. **No one has an authoritative `bits` mapping** — do not invent one.
5. **Discovery has no standard on PS4**; pkg-sender's beacon only works with its own
   PS5 receiver. For PS4 the honest answer is bounded probing + API verification +
   manual IP fallback + remembered hosts.
6. **Performance claims are untrustworthy across the board** (v3.55's "<0.5% CPU",
   "<50ms prerequisites" are closed-source claims). We publish only what we measure,
   and clearly separate mock-based results from hardware results.

## 4. Feature adoption decisions

**Adopted for v1 (each traceable to evidence above):**
- Lenient hex-tolerant JSON parsing (flatz quirk) — `BASELINE.md §3.1`.
- Local HTTP file server with Range + concurrency + allowlist scoping — `BASELINE.md §2`.
- Bounded, netmask-aware subnet probe on port 12800 with API verification, manual IP
  fallback, remembered hosts — pkg-sender `NetDiscovery.cs`.
- Capability probing (`/api` liveness, `/api/status` when available) — pkg-sender
  `ConsoleClient.cs`.
- PKG header/SFO pre-validation on the PC before submission — closes the
  fail-late defect (`BASELINE.md §4.8`).
- Queue with explicit states derived only from observed evidence (counters, error
  codes, issued commands) — ecosystem-wide practice.
- Ports probed in order 12800, then 9090 — pkg-sender; avoids false negatives on
  GoldHEN-family setups.

**Deferred (documented, not built):**
- Direct URL / debrid / archive sources (DirectPackageInstaller's domain) — v1 is
  local-file only; adapter seam exists.
- GoldHEN Payload-Server payload injection — mechanism not source-verified; requires
  shipping a payload binary; licensing unclear.
- PS5 adapters — see `docs/PS5_ROADMAP.md` (feasible, separate milestone).
- Library/cover-art management (PS4-Vault's domain) — post-v1.

**Rejected:**
- Claiming any fix for console-side defects (busy-spin, CE-32871-1, suspension) —
  impossible from the PC side; we only detect and explain them.
- Web-dashboard-on-console features (v3.55) — out of scope for a PC sender.
- Inventing `bits` state names.

## 5. Licensing and attribution

| Component | License | Use in this project |
| --- | --- | --- |
| flatz upstream | none | **No code copied.** Protocol facts re-implemented clean-room in Rust; format constants (offsets, endpoints) are unprotectable facts documented in `docs/BASELINE.md`. |
| pkg-sender (Loopayeh) | MIT | Ideas and constants reused (probe order, discovery approach); re-implemented in Rust. Attribution given here and in README. |
| DirectPackageInstaller, ezRemote, FPKGi, etaHEN | GPL-2.0/GPL-3.0 | Documentation/evidence only; **no code copied** (keeps our GPL-3.0 tree free of incompatible mixing — GPL-2.0 code would be problematic; GPL-3.0 code would obligate carrying derived work notices we don't need). |
| PS4-Vault, Gkiokan sender, codeberg v3.x | none | Ideas only; no code reuse. |
| This project's dependencies | MIT / Apache-2.0 / BSD / ISC (egui, tokio, axum, reqwest, serde, if-addrs, rfd, …) | Permissive; GPL-3.0-compatible. Final license audit happens at release (`docs/TESTING.md` CI gate). |

This repository is licensed **GPL-3.0** (see `LICENSE`).

```

`docs/TESTING.md`:

```md
# Testing

Testing is a first-class part of this project, not an afterthought. The suite
runs on every push, on all three desktop platforms (see
`.github/workflows/ci.yml`).

## Philosophy

1. **Protocol conformance is tested against a faithful mock.** The original
   payload's API is reproduced byte-for-byte in `tests/rpi_mock.rs` — including
   its **invalid JSON with unquoted hex literals** (`docs/BASELINE.md` §3.1).
   A client that only parses strict JSON would pass a naive mock and fail on a
   real console; ours does not.
2. **Mocks are protocol evidence, not compatibility evidence.** A green mock
   test proves we speak the documented dialect. It does **not** prove a real
   PS4 accepts us — that requires hardware (§Hardware validation).
3. **State transitions require evidence.** The engine tests assert that items
   reach `Completed` only when the (mock) console reports full transfer *plus*
   the copy phase, and that failures surface the console's error codes
   verbatim.
4. **Recovery is tested, not assumed.** Queue documents with active items are
   round-tripped and must come back as `Unknown` with their task ids retained.

## Layout

| Suite | File | Covers |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Unit | `src/rpi/json_fix.rs` (inline) | Hex-literal repair, string-awareness, escapes |
| Unit | `src/rpi/client.rs` (inline) | DTO parsing in all three dialects (decimal, quoted hex, unquoted hex); error glossary |
| Integration | `tests/rpi_mock.rs` | Probe/dialect recognition; install + progress over both dialects; backend error codes; task-gone; connection-refused; **engine end-to-end** (connect → add → parse → submit → poll → completed with evidence, persistence round-trip); **engine failure path** (console-side error → `Failed` with code) |
| Integration | `tests/fileserver_test.rs` | Full/suffix/open-ended/multi ranges (206/416/200), HEAD, allowlist enforcement, traversal attempts, unregister, URL encoding, concurrent streams |
| Integration | `tests/queue_test.rs` | Multipart grouping order, duplicate detection, skip-missing, move/requeue/clear, stats, recovery mapping via `Queue::from_json` |
| Integration | `tests/pkg_test.rs` | Generated PKG/SFO fixtures: full metadata parse, patch flag, bad magic, entry-count/offset hardening, SFO bounds, multipart name splitting |

Run everything:

```sh
cargo test --workspace
```

The suite is fully self-contained: no console, no root, no network beyond
loopback. Engine tests use isolated data directories (`spawn_with`), so they
run in parallel without polluting user state.

## What the mock console reproduces (and why)

From the source-verified behavior of the original payload:

- `GET /api` answers 400 “Unsupported method” — the liveness probe used by the
  ecosystem.
- `POST /api/install` requires `{"type":"direct","packages":[...]}` and
  performs a **Range request against the first package URL** before accepting
  (the prerequisite fetch, `docs/BASELINE.md` §2) — so the tests also prove our
  file server serves ranges to a realistic client.
- `POST /api/get_task_progress` returns the original's broken-hex body shape
  by default (configurable to strict JSON) with per-poll transfer advancement
  and a post-transfer copy phase.
- Task lifecycle endpoints, `find_task`, `is_exists`, and a `/api/status`
  capability probe.

## Hardware validation

**Status: not yet performed — no PS4 hardware was available in the development
environment.** This limitation is stated rather than papered over:

- All protocol claims in this repository are source-derived
  (`docs/BASELINE.md`) or mock-verified (this suite).
- Real-console validation must cover, at minimum: flatz RPI on a HEN console;
  a v3.x fork; a GoldHEN 2.4b console via both 12800 and 9090; a multipart
  patch install; suspension/reconnect mid-install; and console-side error
  paths (e.g. `CE-32871-1`-adjacent failures, which are console-side and not
  fixable by any sender).
- Results will be recorded here with firmware/backend versions when hardware
  is available.

## Performance & benchmarking

The master plan calls for measured claims only. What we currently assert:

- **Idle CPU: structurally zero.** The UI repaints only on interaction or
  engine events (`request_repaint_after` is issued solely while work is
  active); the engine has no polling loops when no task is active — a direct
  answer to the original payload's confirmed busy-spin defect
  (`docs/BASELINE.md` §4.1). This is enforced by design and review, not
  measured on target hardware yet.
- **Transfer throughput:** bounded 64 KiB chunks streamed from disk
  (`fileserver.rs`), verified for correctness under 4 concurrent range
  streams in tests. Sender-side throughput on real hardware is a console-side
  network property — to be benchmarked with hardware and reported honestly.
- **Startup time / memory footprint:** not yet measured; will be added to this
  document together with platform numbers from release builds.

No fabricated benchmark numbers appear anywhere in this project.

```

`packaging/icons/generate.py`:

```py
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Regenerates every icon format shipped with Remote Package Installer NG.

The single source of truth is ``crates/rpi-ng/assets/icon.png`` (1024x1024,
RGBA, transparent corners). This script derives every other format from it:

- ``crates/rpi-ng/assets/icon_256.png`` — runtime window icon decoded by the
  app itself (title bar / taskbar on Windows and X11).
- ``crates/rpi-ng/assets/icon.ico`` — multi-size Win32 icon resource embedded
  into the ``.exe`` by ``crates/rpi-ng/build.rs`` (Explorer / taskbar /
  jumplist before the window exists).
- ``crates/rpi-ng/assets/AppIcon.icns`` — macOS bundle icon referenced by
  ``packaging/macos/Info.plist`` (``CFBundleIconFile``).
- ``packaging/linux/icons/hicolor/<size>/apps/remote-package-installer-ng.png``
  — freedesktop icon-theme sizes for the ``.desktop`` file.

Run from anywhere inside the repo::

    python3 packaging/icons/generate.py

Requires Pillow (``pip install pillow``). With ``--redraw`` the 1024 master is
redrawn from the vector description below instead of being reused, so
tweaking colors/geometry only means editing this file.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import io
import struct
import sys
from pathlib import Path

from PIL import Image, ImageDraw

REPO = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
ASSETS = REPO / "crates" / "rpi-ng" / "assets"
HICOLOR = REPO / "packaging" / "linux" / "icons" / "hicolor"

MASTER_SIZE = 1024  # edge length of the master PNG
SS = 4  # supersampling factor: draw at 4096, downscale with Lanczos

# Brand palette: deep PS-blue gradient, white install glyph.
BG_TOP = (18, 57, 107)  # #12396B
BG_BOTTOM = (31, 111, 191)  # #1F6FBF
EDGE = (12, 39, 75)  # #0C274B, subtle inner stroke for light backgrounds
WHITE = (255, 255, 255, 255)

ICO_SIZES = [16, 24, 32, 48, 64, 128, 256]
ICNS_CHUNKS = [
    ("ic11", 32),
    ("ic12", 64),
    ("ic07", 128),
    ("ic08", 256),
    ("ic09", 512),
    ("ic10", 1024),
]
HICOLOR_SIZES = [48, 64, 128, 256]

APP_NAME = "remote-package-installer-ng"  # binary, app_id and icon key


def redraw_master() -> Image.Image:
    """Draws the 1024 master: rounded square + download-into-console glyph."""
    size = MASTER_SIZE * SS
    cx = size / 2

    # Vertical gradient clipped to a rounded square (transparent corners).
    column = Image.new("RGB", (1, size))
    for y in range(size):
        t = y / (size - 1)
        column.putpixel(
            (0, y),
            tuple(round(a + (b - a) * t) for a, b in zip(BG_TOP, BG_BOTTOM)),
        )
    gradient = column.resize((size, size))

    corner_mask = Image.new("L", (size, size), 0)
    ImageDraw.Draw(corner_mask).rounded_rectangle(
        [0, 0, size - 1, size - 1], radius=int(size * 0.22), fill=255
    )
    icon = Image.new("RGBA", (size, size), (0, 0, 0, 0))
    icon.paste(gradient, (0, 0), corner_mask)

    # Subtle inner stroke so the icon keeps an edge on light backgrounds.
    stroke = Image.new("RGBA", (size, size), (0, 0, 0, 0))
    inset = int(size * 0.006)
    ImageDraw.Draw(stroke).rounded_rectangle(
        [inset, inset, size - 1 - inset, size - 1 - inset],
        radius=int(size * 0.214),
        outline=EDGE + (110,),
        width=max(1, int(size * 0.004)),
    )
    icon = Image.alpha_composite(icon, stroke)

    # White glyph: a download arrow above a console base bar, one polygon
    # each (no seams), sized to stay readable down to 16 px.
    glyph = Image.new("RGBA", (size, size), (0, 0, 0, 0))
    draw = ImageDraw.Draw(glyph)
    shaft_half = 0.075 * size  # arrow stem half-width
    head_half = 0.28 * size  # arrowhead half-width
    top, mid, tip = 0.20 * size, 0.50 * size, 0.70 * size
    draw.polygon(
        [
            (cx - shaft_half, top),
            (cx + shaft_half, top),
            (cx + shaft_half, mid),
            (cx + head_half, mid),
            (cx, tip),
            (cx - head_half, mid),
            (cx - shaft_half, mid),
        ],
        fill=WHITE,
    )
    bar_half = 0.29 * size
    draw.rounded_rectangle(
        [cx - bar_half, 0.775 * size, cx + bar_half, 0.875 * size],
        radius=int(0.05 * size),
        fill=WHITE,
    )
    icon = Image.alpha_composite(icon, glyph)

    return icon.resize((MASTER_SIZE, MASTER_SIZE), Image.LANCZOS)


def png_bytes(frame: Image.Image) -> bytes:
    buf = io.BytesIO()
    frame.save(buf, format="PNG", optimize=True)
    return buf.getvalue()


def build_icns(master: Image.Image, path: Path) -> None:
    """Minimal modern icns: one PNG chunk per type (ic07..ic10, ic11, ic12)."""
    chunks = bytearray()
    for chunk_type, px in ICNS_CHUNKS:
        frame = master.resize((px, px), Image.LANCZOS)
        png = png_bytes(frame)
        chunks += chunk_type.encode("ascii")
        chunks += struct.pack(">I", 8 + len(png))
        chunks += png
    blob = b"icns" + struct.pack(">I", 8 + len(chunks)) + bytes(chunks)
    path.write_bytes(blob)


def main() -> int:
    ASSETS.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    master_path = ASSETS / "icon.png"
    if "--redraw" in sys.argv or not master_path.exists():
        master = redraw_master()
        master.save(master_path, format="PNG", optimize=True)
        print(f"wrote {master_path} ({master.width}x{master.height})")
    else:
        master = Image.open(master_path).convert("RGBA")
        if master.size != (MASTER_SIZE, MASTER_SIZE):
            print(
                f"error: {master_path} is {master.width}x{master.height}, "
                f"expected {MASTER_SIZE}x{MASTER_SIZE} — rerun with --redraw"
            )
            return 1
        print(f"reusing {master_path} ({master.width}x{master.height})")

    # Runtime window icon (embedded via include_bytes!, decoded with `image`).
    runtime = master.resize((256, 256), Image.LANCZOS)
    runtime.save(ASSETS / "icon_256.png", format="PNG", optimize=True)
    print(f"wrote {ASSETS / 'icon_256.png'} (256x256)")

    # Win32 multi-size icon resource.
    master.save(
        ASSETS / "icon.ico",
        format="ICO",
        sizes=[(s, s) for s in ICO_SIZES],
    )
    print(f"wrote {ASSETS / 'icon.ico'} (sizes {ICO_SIZES})")

    # macOS bundle icon.
    build_icns(master, ASSETS / "AppIcon.icns")
    print(f"wrote {ASSETS / 'AppIcon.icns'} (chunks {[t for t, _ in ICNS_CHUNKS]})")

    # Linux freedesktop icon-theme sizes.
    for px in HICOLOR_SIZES:
        dest = HICOLOR / f"{px}x{px}" / "apps" / f"{APP_NAME}.png"
        dest.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        master.resize((px, px), Image.LANCZOS).save(dest, format="PNG", optimize=True)
        print(f"wrote {dest} ({px}x{px})")

    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    raise SystemExit(main())

```
